## PROMULGATED TO BE USED

Civil Code, which was approved by the National Assembly on October 05, 2007, during the 7th session of the 3rd legislature, and which the Senate agreed upon in the same form and purpose without any amendment on November 21, 2007, during the 4th plenary session of the 2nd legislature, the full text of which is as follows:

# Civil Code

# BOOK 1

# General Provisions

## General Chapter

## General Provisions

#### Article 1.- General law of private law

This Code provides for general principles regarding legal relations in civil matters. The provisions of this Civil Code shall apply to property relations and family relations, except where otherwise provided in special laws.

#### Article 2.- Fundamental principles

This Code determines the principles of guaranteeing human dignity, equality between men and women, and property rights as determined in the Constitution.

#### Article 3.- Principle of autonomy of private persons

This Code respects the freedom of will of persons and provides for legal relations with equality between private persons, including legal entities. Public legal entities that are in commercial relations shall be considered as private persons.

#### Article 4.- Prohibition of abuse of rights

No right shall be permitted to be used abusively. In cases where a right is used abusively beyond the scope of protection provided for, the effect of the exercise of such right shall not be recognized.

#### Article 5.- Principle of good faith and integrity

The exercise of rights and performance of obligations must be done in good faith based on integrity.

# BOOK 2

# PERSONS

## CHAPTER 1

## NATURAL PERSONS

### SECTION 1

### CAPACITY TO ENJOY RIGHTS

#### Article 6.- Principle of equality of capacity to enjoy rights

Every natural person is qualified to be the subject of rights and obligations.

#### Article 7.- Limitation of capacity to enjoy rights for foreigners

Foreigners shall not enjoy or possess certain rights specified in cases where there are other provisions in national laws, or international treaties or conventions.

#### Article 8.- Commencement and termination of capacity to enjoy rights

Each natural person has the capacity to enjoy rights at birth and loses all such capacity to enjoy rights upon death.

#### Article 9.- Fetus in the womb

1-A fetus in the womb at the time of a tortious act, if the fetus is born later, may claim damages for harm occurred while in the womb due to such tortious act.

2-A fetus in the womb at the time of commencement of succession, if the fetus

is born later, may receive the inheritance by such succession.

3-A fetus in the womb at the time the testator dies, if the fetus is born later, may receive the effect of such will.

### SECTION 2

### PERSONAL RIGHTS

#### Article 10.- Meaning of personal rights

Personal rights refer to rights that have meaning related to life, body, health, freedom, name, honor, privacy, or other rights that are interests related to individual characteristics.

#### Article 11.- Right to demand cessation

If there is a concern that there will be an unlawful infringement of personal rights, or a concern that an infringement of personal rights that has already occurred will continue or will happen again unlawfully, the person who has the personal rights may demand the cessation of such infringement.

#### Article 12.- Right to demand removal of results of infringing acts

If the state of infringement of personal rights still continues due to the remaining results from the infringing act, the person who has suffered the unlawful infringement of personal rights may demand the removal of the results of such infringing act, provided that the results can be removed.

#### Article 13.- Right to demand compensation for damages

The provisions of Article 11 (Right to demand cessation) and Article 12 (Right to demand removal of results of infringing acts) of this Code shall not prevent the demand for compensation for damages

by the person who has suffered an infringement of interests related to individual characteristics, based on the provisions regarding torts.

### SECTION 3

### CAPACITY OF WILL

#### Article 14.- Lack of capacity of will

An act performed by a party in a state where they were unable to understand and judge the legal consequences of their act may be rescinded.

#### Article 15.- Definition of act

The term "act" as used in Section 3 (Capacity of will), Section 4 (Capacity to act), and Section 6 (Management of property of absent persons and declaration of disappearance) of this Chapter 1 refers to contracts and unilateral acts.

### SECTION 4

### CAPACITY TO ACT

#### Article 16.- Meaning of persons with limited capacity to act

Persons with limited capacity to act refer to minors, persons under general guardianship, and persons under curatorship.

## SUB-PART 1

## MINORS

#### Article 17.- Meaning of minors

Minors refer to persons under the age of 18 (eighteen) years.

#### Article 18.- Right to rescind act

An act performed by a minor without the consent of the parental power holder or guardian for the minor may be rescinded. However, this provision shall not apply to acts that merely result in the acquisition of rights or release from obligations, or acts related to daily life.

#### Article 19.- Disposition of property permitted by parental power holder or guardian for minor

A minor may dispose of property that the parental power holder or guardian for the minor has permitted to be disposed of for a specified purpose, within the scope of such purpose. Furthermore, a minor may also dispose of property that the parental power holder or guardian for the minor has permitted to be disposed of without specifying any purpose.

#### Article 20.- Minor permitted to conduct business

1-A minor who has received permission to conduct one or more types of business from the parental power holder or guardian for the minor shall have the same capacity as a person of of legal age with respect to such business.

2-In the cases specified in paragraph 1 above, if it is clear that the minor is unable to conduct such business, the parental power holder or guardian for the minor may rescind or limit the permission for conducting such business.

#### Article 21.- Conditions for emancipation

1-In cases where a minor who has reached the age of 16 (sixteen) years maintains an independent livelihood, the court may declare the emancipation of such person

upon the application of such minor, if the court finds that emancipation is in the best interest of such minor. In this case, the court shall consult with the parental power holder.

2-A minor who has entered into a marriage shall be emancipated without the need for a declaration from the court.

3-In the case specified in paragraph 2 above, even if the parties divorce, the effect of emancipation shall not be extinguished.

#### Article 22.- Effect of emancipation

A minor who has been emancipated shall be deemed to have reached the age of majority.

#### Article 23.- Labor contract of minor

1-Notwithstanding the provisions of Article 1053 (Management of property and representation) and Article 1080 (Management of property and representation) of this Code, the parental power holder or the guardian for a minor may not enter into a labor contract on behalf of the minor.

2-A contract that contradicts the provision of paragraph 1 above shall have no effect on the minor concerned. However, this provision shall not apply to cases where the minor concerned has given consent to such contract.

3-In cases where it is found that the minor's labor contract may be detrimental to the minor's interests, the parental power holder, the guardian for the minor, or an administrative agency may terminate such contract for the future.

## SUB-PART 2

## PERSONS UNDER GENERAL GUARDIANSHIP

#### Article 24.- Declaration of commencement of general guardianship

1-With respect to a person who is in a permanent state of lacking the capacity to understand and judge the legal consequences of their acts due to mental disability, the court may declare the commencement of general guardianship upon the application of the person themselves, their spouse, relatives within the 4th (fourth) degree, the guardian for a minor, the supervisor of the guardian for a minor, the curator, the head of the commune or Sangkat of the person's residence, or a prosecutor. However, this provision shall not apply to cases where the person concerned has not yet reached the age of 15 (fifteen) years at the time of the application.

2-In cases where the declaration specified in paragraph 1 above is made, if the person concerned is a person under curatorship, the court must rescind the declaration of commencement of curatorship for such person.

#### Article 25.- Meaning of person under general guardianship and appointment of general guardian

A person for whom a declaration of commencement of general guardianship has been made shall be called a "person under general guardianship" and shall be placed under a general guardian.

#### Article 26.- Right to rescind act

An act performed by a person under general guardianship may be rescinded. However, this provision shall not apply to acts related to daily life.

#### Article 27.- Rescission of declaration of commencement of general guardianship

If the grounds specified in Article 24 (Declaration of commencement of general guardianship) of this Code have ceased to exist, the court must rescind the declaration of commencement of general guardianship upon the application of the person themselves, their spouse, relatives within the 4th (fourth) degree, the general guardian, the supervisor of the general guardian, the head of the commune or Sangkat of the person's residence, or a prosecutor.

## SUB-PART 3

## PERSONS UNDER CURATORSHIP

#### Article 28.- Declaration of commencement of curatorship

1-With respect to a person whose capacity to understand and judge the legal consequences of their acts is significantly insufficient due to mental disability, the court may declare the commencement of curatorship for such person upon the application of the person themselves, their spouse, relatives within the 4th (fourth) degree, the general guardian, the supervisor of the general guardian, the head of the commune or Sangkat of the person's residence, or a prosecutor.

2-In cases where the declaration specified in paragraph 1 above is made, if the person concerned is a person under general guardianship, the court must rescind the declaration of commencement of general guardianship for such person.

#### Article 29.- Meaning of person under curatorship and appointment of curator

A person for whom a declaration of commencement of curatorship has been made shall be called a "person under curatorship" and shall be placed under the care of a curator.

#### Article 30.- Right to rescind act

Except for acts related to daily life, acts that

performed by a person under curatorship without the consent of the curator as listed below may be rescinded:

a-Receiving or using capital.

b-Borrowing property or providing a guarantee.

c-Acts aimed at acquiring or losing rights related to immovable property or other significant property.

d-Performing acts related to litigation.

e-Making gifts, or entering into compromise agreements or arbitration agreements.

f-Accepting succession or renouncing succession rights or partitioning an inheritance.

g-Rejecting gifts or legacies or accepting gifts or legacies subject to a burden.

h-Constructing new buildings, reconstruction, expansion, or major repairs.

i-Entering into a lease for a period exceeding 3 (three) years for land, 2 (two) years for buildings, or 6 (six) months for movable property.

j-Acts that the court has specifically declared as requiring the consent of the curator, upon the application of the persons mentioned in Article 28 (Declaration of commencement of curatorship) of this Code, or the curator, or the supervisor of the curator.

#### Article 31.- Rescission of declaration of commencement of curatorship

If the grounds specified in Article 28 (Declaration of commencement of curatorship) of this Code have ceased to exist, the court must rescind the declaration of commencement of curatorship upon the application of the person themselves, their spouse, relatives within the 4th (fourth) degree, the guardian for a minor, or the supervisor of the guardian for a minor

curator, supervisor of the curator, head of the commune or Sangkat of the person's residence, or a prosecutor.

## SUB-PART 4

## PROTECTION OF THE OTHER PARTY TO A PERSON WITH LIMITED CAPACITY

#### Article 32.- Right to demand

1-The other party to a person with limited capacity may demand that such person answer whether or not they give consent to an act that the person may rescind, within a period exceeding 1 (one) month after such person has recovered full capacity. If the person who has recovered full capacity does not respond within such period, they shall be deemed to have given consent to such act.

2-The provision of paragraph 1 above shall also apply to cases where the other party has made the demand as specified in paragraph 1 above to a parental power holder, a guardian, or a curator regarding an act within the scope of their authority, while the person with limited capacity has not yet recovered full capacity, but the parental power holder, guardian, or curator does not respond within such period.

3-The other party may demand that a person under curatorship obtain the consent of the curator within the period specified in paragraph 1 above. If the person under curatorship does not give notice that they have obtained the consent within such period, they shall be deemed to have rescinded such act.

#### Article 33.- Fraud by person with limited capacity

If a person with limited capacity has used fraudulent means

to make people believe that they possess full capacity, such person may not rescind their act.

### SECTION 5

### DOMICILE

#### Article 34.- Meaning of domicile

The place where each person has the center of their livelihood shall be their domicile.

#### Article 35.- Place of residence

In cases where the center of livelihood is unclear, the place of residence shall be deemed to be the domicile.

#### Article 36.- Cases where there is no domicile in Cambodia

With respect to a person who has no domicile in Cambodia, whether they are a Cambodian national or a foreigner, the place of residence of such person in Cambodia shall be deemed to be their domicile. However, this provision shall not apply to cases where the law of domicile applies.

### SECTION 6

### MANAGEMENT OF PROPERTY OF ABSENT PERSONS AND DECLARATION OF DISAPPEARANCE

## SUB-PART 1

## MANAGEMENT OF PROPERTY OF ABSENT PERSONS

#### Article 37.- Appointment of property manager by the court

In cases where a person has left their domicile or residence and

has no prospect of returning easily and has not appointed a manager for their property, the court may appoint a property manager or issue an order necessary for the management of such property upon the application of any interested person, the head of the commune or Sangkat where the person's domicile is located, or a prosecutor. This provision shall also apply to cases where the authority of the manager has expired while the person is absent.

#### Article 38.- Rescission of order for management of property

In the cases specified in Article 37 (Appointment of property manager by the court) of this Code, if the absent person subsequently appoints a manager, the court may rescind the order upon the application of the manager, any interested person, the head of the commune or Sangkat where the person's domicile is located, or a prosecutor.

#### Article 39.- Replacement of manager

In cases where an absent person has appointed a manager, if it is unclear whether the absent person is alive or dead, the court may replace the manager upon the application of any interested person, the head of the commune or Sangkat where the person's domicile is located, or a prosecutor.

#### Article 40.- Rights of property manager, etc.

1-An appointed manager has the right to perform acts specified in paragraph 2 of Article 366 (Scope of authority of representation) of this Code. If it is necessary to perform other acts, the manager may do so with the permission of the court.

2-In cases where it is unclear whether the absent person is alive or

dead, and the manager of an absent person needs to perform an act that exceeds the authority determined by the absent person, the manager may do so with the permission of the court.

3-If it is found appropriate to pay remuneration to the manager, considering the relationship between the absent person and the manager and other circumstances, the court may provide a reasonable amount of remuneration to the manager from the property of the absent person.

## SUB-PART 2

## DECLARATION OF DISAPPEARANCE

#### Article 41.- Conditions for making declaration of disappearance

1-If it is unclear whether an absent person is alive or dead for a period of 5 (five) years, the court may declare that such person has disappeared upon the application of the spouse, any person presumed to be an heir, a legatee, a beneficiary of life insurance, a parental power holder, a guardian, parents, or any other person who has significant legal interest in the declaration of disappearance.

2-The provision of paragraph 1 above shall also apply to cases where a person has entered a war zone, was on a ship that has sunk, or has encountered any other danger that could lead to death, and it is unclear whether the absent person is alive or dead for a period of 1 (one) year after the end of the war, the sinking of the ship, or the passing of such other danger.

#### Article 42.- Public demand

A declaration of disappearance shall be made following the procedures for public demand

public demand.

#### Article 43.- Effect of declaration of disappearance

In cases where a declaration of disappearance has been made in accordance with the provision of paragraph 1 of Article 41 (Conditions for making declaration of disappearance) of this Code after the period specified in said paragraph 1 has elapsed, and in cases where a declaration of disappearance has been made in accordance with the provision of paragraph 2 of the same Article after the danger has passed, the disappeared person shall be deemed to have died. This provision shall only apply to legal relations surrounding the previous domicile or residence until that time.

#### Article 44.- Disposition when disappeared person returns

1-If it is confirmed that the disappeared person is alive, or if it is confirmed that they died at a time different from the time specified in Article 43 (Effect of declaration of disappearance) above, the court must rescind the declaration of disappearance upon the application of the person themselves or any interested person.

2-A person who has personally acquired property as a result of the effect of the declaration of disappearance shall lose their rights due to the rescission of such declaration of disappearance. However, a person who did not know that the declaration of disappearance was contrary to the facts at the time they acquired the property shall be obligated to return it only to the extent of the benefit they still enjoy.

3-The effect of acts performed in good faith based on the declaration of disappearance after the declaration was made but before it was rescinded shall not be affected by the rescission of the declaration of disappearance.

4-In cases where the declaration of disappearance is rescinded after

the spouse of the person who was declared disappeared has remarried another person, the previous marriage shall be dissolved by such subsequent marriage.

### SECTION 7

### PRESUMPTION OF SIMULTANEOUS DEATH

#### Article 45.- Presumption of simultaneous death

Among several persons who have died, if it is unclear whether any one of them was still alive after the others have died, such persons shall be presumed to have died at the same time.

# CHAPTER 2

# JURIDICAL PERSONS

### SECTION 1

### GENERAL PROVISIONS

## SUB-PART 1

## DEFINITION, TYPES, AND PRINCIPLES OF FORMATION

#### Article 46.- Definition, types, and principles of formation of juridical persons

1-In this Code, a group of members that is granted the status of an independent subject of rights and obligations shall be called a "juridical association," and an endowed property that is granted the status of an independent subject of rights and obligations shall be called a "juridical foundation."

2-In this Code, a juridical person that does not have the purpose of making a profit shall be called

a "non-profit juridical person," and a juridical person that has the purpose of making a profit shall be called a "for-profit juridical person." Among non-profit juridical persons, a juridical person whose main purpose is to serve the public interest shall be called a "public interest juridical person."

3-In this Code, a "juridical partnership" where members bear responsibility for the obligations of the juridical person to the extent of the property they have contributed shall be called a "limited liability juridical partnership," and a "juridical partnership" where members bear responsibility for the obligations of the juridical person with their personal property shall be called an "unlimited liability juridical partnership."

4-A juridical person cannot be an unlimited liability member of another juridical partnership.

5-Non-profit juridical persons may be established in accordance with this Code or other legal norms.

6-For-profit juridical persons may be established in accordance with the provisions of other laws.

## SUB-PART 2

## NAME OF NON-PROFIT JURIDICAL PERSON

#### Article 47.- Name of non-profit juridical person

1-An entity that is not a juridical association or a juridical foundation may not use the words "juridical association" or "juridical foundation" in its name.

2-A limited liability juridical partnership must indicate that it is a "limited liability juridical partnership" in its name, while an unlimited liability juridical partnership

unlimited liability shall be indicated as an "unlimited liability juridical partnership" in its name.

#### Article 48.- Foreign juridical person

1-In this Code, a juridical person established in accordance with the laws of a foreign country shall be called a "foreign juridical person."

2-A foreign juridical person, other than a state, a state's administrative division, or a commercial company, shall not be recognized as a juridical person. However, if there is permission under Cambodian law, a treaty, or a convention, it may be recognized as a juridical person.

3-A foreign juridical person recognized as a juridical person under the provision of paragraph 2 above shall have private law rights identical to those of a juridical person of a similar type established based on Cambodian law. However, this provision shall not apply to rights that foreigners cannot possess or in cases where there are special provisions in laws, treaties, or conventions.

## SUB-PART 3

## REGISTRATION OF FORMATION

#### Article 49.- Registration of formation and time of birth of juridical person

A juridical person shall be formed by registration at the place where the principal office of such juridical person is located.

#### Article 50.- Items to be registered

1-To register a juridical person, each of the following items must be registered:

a-Purpose.

b-Name.

c-Principal office and branch offices.

d-Grounds for dissolution, if any are specified in the articles of incorporation.

e-Names and domiciles of directors and supervisors. However, in the case of an unlimited liability juridical partnership, the names and domiciles of the members must be registered.

f-Names of the directors who are authorized to represent the juridical person, if there are directors who do not represent it.

g-A provision for joint representation, if any, that multiple directors represent the juridical person jointly.

2-If there is any change in any of the items specified in paragraph 1 above, such change must be registered at the place of the principal office within 2 (two) weeks and at the place of other offices within 3 (three) weeks. Before such registration, the change may not be set up against a third party.

3-If a provisional disposition order suspending the performance of duties by a director or appointing a substitute director is made, or if such order is changed or rescinded, such fact must be registered at the place of the principal office and other offices. In this case, the second sentence of paragraph 2 above shall apply.

## SUB-PART 4

## DOMICILE OF JURIDICAL PERSON

#### Article 51.- Domicile of juridical person

The domicile of a juridical person shall be at the place of its principal office.

#### Article 52.- Registration of relocation of office

1-When a juridical person relocates its principal office, such relocation must be registered at the place of the former office, and the items specified in Article 50 (Items to be registered) of this Code must be registered at the place of the new office within 2 (two) weeks. When a juridical person relocates any of its other offices, such relocation must be registered at the place of the former office within 3 (three) weeks, and the items specified in Article 50 (Items to be registered) must be registered at the place of the new office within 4 (four) weeks.

2-Notwithstanding the provision of paragraph 1 above, if the relocation of an office is within the jurisdiction of the same registry office, it shall be sufficient to register the relocation.

#### Article 53.- Registration of establishment of new office

1-In cases where a juridical person establishes a new office, the items specified in Article 50 (Items to be registered) of this Code must be registered at the place of such new office within 2 (two) weeks.

2-In the cases specified in paragraph 1 above, the establishment of the new office must be registered at the place of the principal office and other offices within 3 (three) weeks from the date the new office was established.

## SUB-PART 5

## REGISTRATION OF FOREIGN JURIDICAL PERSON

#### Article 54.- Registration of foreign juridical person

1-The provisions of Article 50 (Items to be registered), Article 52 (Registration of relocation of office) and Article 53 (Registration of establishment of new office) of this Code shall apply to cases where a foreign juridical person establishes an office in Cambodia.

2-In cases where a foreign juridical person establishes its first office in Cambodia, any other person may deny existence of such juridical person until it has been registered at the place of such office.

## SUB-PART 6

## MANAGEMENT AND ADMINISTRATION OF JURIDICAL PERSON

#### Article 55.- Inventory of property and list of members

1-A juridical person must prepare an inventory of property at the time of its formation and within the first 3 (three) months of each fiscal year, and must keep such inventory at its office at all times.

2-A juridical association must prepare a list of members and keep it at its office at all times, and must update the list whenever there is a change in membership.

#### Article 56.- Number, appointment, dismissal, and duties of directors

1-A juridical person must have directors as an organ to conduct the business of the juridical person. However, an unlimited liability juridical partnership shall not have directors.

2-There may be one or more directors. However, a juridical foundation must have at least 3 (three) directors.

3-In cases where there are multiple directors, the business of the juridical person shall be decided by a majority of the directors, unless otherwise provided in the articles of incorporation.

4-Directors shall be appointed by the articles of incorporation or by a resolution of the general meeting of members.

5-The directors of a juridical association may be dismissed by a resolution of the general meeting of members.

#### Article 57.- Duties of directors

1-Directors have the duty to comply with laws and legal norms as well as the articles of incorporation, and to perform the business of the juridical person faithfully.

2-In addition to the provision of paragraph 1 above, the relationship between the directors and the juridical person shall be governed by the provisions on mandates.

#### Article 58.- Authority of representation of directors

1-Directors represent the juridical person. However, directors may not act in any manner that contradicts the articles of incorporation.

2-In cases where there are multiple directors, each director shall have the authority of representation. However, it may be provided otherwise in the articles of incorporation.

3-With respect to a juridical association, directors must comply with the resolutions of the general meeting of members.

#### Article 59.- Restriction on authority of representation

A restriction on the authority of representation of a director or any other representative of a juridical person may not be set up against a third party in good faith.

#### Article 60.- Provisional director

In cases where there is a vacancy for a director and there is a concern that damage may result from a delay in the business of the juridical person, the court may appoint

a provisional director upon the application of any interested person or a prosecutor.

#### Article 61.- Special representative

With respect to matters where the interests of a director and those of the juridical person conflict, the director shall not have the authority of representation. In cases where there is no representative for the juridical person due to this reason, a special representative shall be appointed in accordance with the procedures specified in Article 60 (Provisional director) of this Code.

#### Article 62.- Supervisors

1-A juridical person must have one or more supervisors. However, an unlimited liability juridical partnership may choose not to have a supervisor.

2-Supervisors shall be appointed in accordance with the articles of incorporation or by a resolution of the general meeting of members or the board of directors.

3-A director or an employee of the juridical person may not serve as a supervisor.

4-A juridical partnership may serve as a supervisor.

#### Article 63.- Duties of supervisors, etc.

1-Supervisors shall supervise the business of the juridical person.

2-Supervisors may demand that directors and employees of the juridical person report on the status of the business, or may investigate the status of the business and the property of the juridical person.

3-Supervisors shall examine the agenda and documents that the directors intend to submit to the general meeting of members or the board of directors. In this case, if the supervisors find any violation of laws or the articles of incorporation, or any extremely improper point, they must report it to the general meeting of members or the
```

#### Article 61.- Special representative

With respect to matters where the interests of a director and those of the juridical person conflict, the director shall not have the authority of representation. In cases where there is no representative for the juridical person due to this reason, a special representative shall be appointed in accordance with the procedures specified in Article 60 (Provisional director) of this Code.

#### Article 62.- Supervisors

1-A juridical person must have one or more supervisors. However, an unlimited liability juridical partnership may choose not to have a supervisor.

2-Supervisors shall be appointed in accordance with the articles of incorporation or by a resolution of the general meeting of members or the board of directors.

3-A director or an employee of the juridical person may not serve as a supervisor.

4-A juridical partnership may serve as a supervisor.

#### Article 63.- Duties of supervisors, etc.

1-Supervisors shall supervise the business of the juridical person.

2-Supervisors may demand that directors and employees of the juridical person report on the status of the business, or may investigate the status of the business and the property of the juridical person.

3-Supervisors shall examine the agenda and documents that the directors intend to submit to the general meeting of members or the board of directors. In this case, if the supervisors find any violation of laws or the articles of incorporation, or any extremely improper point, they must report it to the general meeting of members or the

board of directors.

4-Supervisors may express their opinions on the appointment or dismissal of supervisors or the remuneration of supervisors at the general meeting of members or the board of directors.

5-If supervisors find that a director has performed an act outside the scope of the purpose of the juridical person, or any other act that violates laws or the articles of incorporation, or if they fear that such an act is likely to be performed, they must report the fact to the general meeting of members or the board of directors. In this case, if necessary for making such report, the supervisors may convene a general meeting of members or a meeting of the board of directors. A supervisor of a juridical foundation must make such report to the competent ministry or authority at the latest at the same time as reporting to the board of directors.

6-In cases where a director has performed an act specified in paragraph 5 above or there is a fear that such an act is likely to be performed, and if such act is likely to cause serious damage to the juridical person, any member may demand that such director cease such act.

7-In cases where the juridical person brings an action against a director or vice versa, the supervisor shall represent the juridical person in such action.

## SUB-PART 7

## DISSOLUTION AND LIQUIDATION

#### Article 64.- Grounds for dissolution

1-A juridical person shall be dissolved for any of the following reasons:

a-Occurrence of a ground for dissolution specified in the articles of incorporation.

b-Completion of the business that was the purpose of the juridical person, or impossibility of such completion.

c-Bankruptcy.

d-Judgment ordering dissolution.

2-In addition to the grounds specified in paragraph 1 above, a juridical association shall also be dissolved for any of the following reasons:

a-Resolution of the general meeting of members, in the case of a limited liability juridical partnership, or the consent of all members, in the case of an unlimited liability juridical partnership.

b-When only one member remains.

3-The resolution of dissolution of a limited liability juridical partnership specified in point (a) of paragraph 2 above must be approved by members who represent at least one-half of the total number of members and at least three-fourths of the total voting rights of all members.

#### Article 6 Petitions for dissolution. Petition for dissolution

1-Members who hold at least one-tenth of the total voting rights of all members of a limited liability juridical partnership, any member of an unlimited liability juridical partnership, or any director of a juridical foundation, or the ministry or authority that has competence to supervise a juridical foundation, may petition the court for the dissolution of the juridical person.

2-In the cases specified in paragraph 1 above, the court may order the dissolution of the juridical person only if any of the following circumstances exist and there are unavoidable reasons:

a-The operations of the juridical person have reached a dead end, and irreparable damage has occurred or is likely to occur to the juridical person.

b-The management or administration of the property of the juridical person is extremely improper and endangers the existence of the juridical person.

3-Notwithstanding the provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2 above, in cases where any of the reasons specified below exists, the court may order the dissolution of the juridical person upon the application of the Minister of Justice, or a member, a creditor, or any other interested person, if it is found that the continuous existence of the juridical person cannot be permitted for the sake of the public interest:

a-The juridical person was formed for an illegal purpose.

b-The juridical person has not commenced its business within 1 (one) year after its formation, or has suspended its business for more than 1 (one) year, without any justifiable reason.

c-Despite having received a written warning from the Minister of Justice, the persons conducting the business of the juridical person continue to perform or repeat acts that exceed or abuse the authority specified in laws or the articles of incorporation, or acts that violate criminal laws.

4-In the cases specified in paragraph 3 above, the court may, even before ordering the dissolution, appoint a manager or take any other necessary measure to preserve the property of the juridical person, upon the application of the Minister of Justice, a member, a creditor, or any other interested person, or on its own motion.

#### Article 66.- Cases requiring liquidation and juridical person under liquidation

Except in cases of bankruptcy, when a juridical person is dissolved, it must be liquidated in accordance with the provisions of this Sub-part 7 (Dissolution and Liquidation). In such cases, the juridical person shall be deemed to continue to exist within the scope of the purpose of liquidation until the liquidation is completed.

#### Article 67.- Devolution of residual property

1-The devolution of residual property after the performance of all obligations upon the dissolution of a juridical person shall be governed by the articles of incorporation.

2-With respect to a juridical association, if the devolution of residual property cannot be determined in accordance with the provision of paragraph 1 above, such devolution shall be governed by a resolution of the general meeting of members in the case of a limited liability juridical partnership, or by the consent of all members in the case of an unlimited liability juridical partnership.

3-Residual property that cannot be devolved in accordance with the provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2 above shall belong to the National Treasury.

#### Article 68.- Tenure of liquidators

1-In cases where a juridical person is dissolved for any of the reasons specified in point (a) or (b) of paragraph 1 or point (a) of paragraph 2 of Article 64 (Grounds for dissolution) of this Code, the following persons shall become liquidators in the following order:

a-The person specified in the articles of incorporation.

b-The person appointed by a resolution of the general meeting

meeting of members in the case of a limited liability juridical partnership, or by the consent of a majority of the members of an unlimited liability juridical partnership.

c-Directors of a limited liability juridical partnership or of a juridical foundation, or members of an unlimited liability juridical partnership.

2-In the cases specified in paragraph 1 above, if no one becomes a liquidator in accordance with the provision of said paragraph 1, the court shall appoint a liquidator upon the application of any interested person.

3-In cases where a juridical person is dissolved for any of the reasons specified in point (d) of paragraph 1 or point (b) of paragraph 2 of Article 64 (Grounds for dissolution) of this Code, the court shall appoint a liquidator upon the application of an interested person or the Minister of Justice, or on its own motion.

#### Article 69.- Dismissal of liquidators

1-If there are material grounds, the court may dismiss a liquidator upon the application of any interested person.

2-Except for a liquidator appointed by the court, a liquidator of a juridical partnership may be dismissed by a resolution of the general meeting of members in the case of a limited liability juridical partnership, or by the consent of a majority of all members in the case of an unlimited liability juridical partnership.

#### Article 70.- Registration of liquidators and dissolution

Except in cases of bankruptcy, a liquidator must register their name and

domicile, as well as the reason and date of dissolution, at the place of the principal office within 2 (two) weeks and at the place of other offices within 3 (three) weeks after the dissolution.

#### Article 71.- Functions and authority of liquidators

1-Liquidators shall perform the following functions:

a-Concluding current business of the juridical person.

b-Collecting claims and performing obligations.

c-Devolving residual property.

2-In cases where there are multiple liquidators, the business of the juridical person shall be decided by a majority of the liquidators.

3-The provisions of Article 58 (Authority of representation of directors) of this Code shall apply to liquidators. However, if the court appoints multiple liquidators, the court may appoint one or more of them as representatives, or provide for joint representation.

#### Article 72.- Duty to report on investigation of property of juridical person

1-Upon assuming office, a liquidator must investigate the current status of the property of the juridical person, prepare an inventory of property and a balance sheet, and submit them to the court without delay.

2-With respect to a limited liability juridical partnership, the documents specified in paragraph 1 above must be approved by a general meeting of members before being submitted to the court.

3-With respect to an unlimited liability juridical partnership, the documents specified
```

#### Article 71.- Functions and authority of liquidators

1-Liquidators shall perform the following functions:

a-Concluding current business of the juridical person.

b-Collecting claims and performing obligations.

c-Devolving residual property.

2-In cases where there are multiple liquidators, the business of the juridical person shall be decided by a majority of the liquidators.

3-The provisions of Article 58 (Authority of representation of directors) of this Code shall apply to liquidators. However, if the court appoints multiple liquidators, the court may appoint one or more of them as representatives, or provide for joint representation.

#### Article 72.- Duty to report on investigation of property of juridical person

1-Upon assuming office, a liquidator must investigate the current status of the property of the juridical person, prepare an inventory of property and a balance sheet, and submit them to the court without delay.

2-With respect to a limited liability juridical partnership, the documents specified in paragraph 1 above must be approved by a general meeting of members before being submitted to the court.

3-With respect to an unlimited liability juridical partnership, the documents specified

specified in paragraph 1 above must be approved by all members before being submitted to the court.

4-With respect to a juridical foundation, the documents specified in paragraph 1 above must be approved by the competent ministry or authority before being submitted to the court.

#### Article 73.- Public notice to creditors

1-A liquidator must, at least 3 (three) times within 2 (two) months from the date of assuming office, give public notice in the official gazette of the Ministry of Justice to the creditors to present their claims within a specified period, which shall not be less than 2 (two) months.

2-The public notice specified in paragraph 1 above must also state that the creditors will be excluded from the liquidation if they do not present their claims within the specified period.

3-A liquidator must give individual notice to each known creditor to present their claim.

4-A liquidator may not exclude any known creditor from the liquidation.

#### Article 74.- Performance during period for presenting claims

1-A liquidator may not perform obligations to creditors during the period for presenting claims specified in Article 73 (Public notice to creditors) of this Code. However, the juridical person shall not be exempt from liability for any delay resulting therefrom.

2-Notwithstanding the provision of paragraph 1 above, a liquidator may, with the permission of the court, perform obligations relating to small claims, claims secured by security
```

#### Article 73.- Public notice to creditors

1-A liquidator must, at least 3 (three) times within 2 (two) months from the date of assuming office, give public notice in the official gazette of the Ministry of Justice to the creditors to present their claims within a specified period, which shall not be less than 2 (two) months.

2-The public notice specified in paragraph 1 above must also state that the creditors will be excluded from the liquidation if they do not present their claims within the specified period.

3-A liquidator must give individual notice to each known creditor to present their claim.

4-A liquidator may not exclude any known creditor from the liquidation.

#### Article 74.- Performance during period for presenting claims

1-A liquidator may not perform obligations to creditors during the period for presenting claims specified in Article 73 (Public notice to creditors) of this Code. However, the juridical person shall not be exempt from liability for any delay resulting therefrom.

2-Notwithstanding the provision of paragraph 1 above, a liquidator may, with the permission of the court, perform obligations relating to small claims, claims secured by security

interests, and other claims that are unlikely to cause damage to other creditors, with the permission of the court.

#### Article 75.- Performance to excluded creditors

Creditors who have been excluded from the liquidation may only demand performance from the residual property that has not yet been distributed.

#### Article 76.- Performance of claims

1-A juridical person may perform its obligations even if they are not yet due.

2-In the cases specified in paragraph 1 above, with respect to conditional claims, claims with an uncertain duration, or other claims whose value is not certain, the juridical person must perform them based on the valuation of an appraiser appointed by the court.

#### Article 77.- Devolution of residual property

A liquidator may not devolve the property of the juridical person in accordance with Article 67 (Devolution of residual property) of this Code until after all obligations of the juridical person have been performed. However, with respect to disputed obligations, the liquidator may distribute the residual property after setting aside the property necessary for performing such obligations.

#### Article 78.- Bankruptcy during liquidation

1-If, during liquidation, it becomes clear that the property of the juridical person is insufficient to perform all of its obligations, the liquidator must immediately petition for a declaration of bankruptcy and give public notice of such fact in the official gazette of the
```

#### Article 75.- Performance to excluded creditors

Creditors who have been excluded from the liquidation may only demand performance from the residual property that has not yet been distributed.

#### Article 76.- Performance of claims

1-A juridical person may perform its obligations even if they are not yet due.

2-In the cases specified in paragraph 1 above, with respect to conditional claims, claims with an uncertain duration, or other claims whose value is not certain, the juridical person must perform them based on the valuation of an appraiser appointed by the court.

#### Article 77.- Devolution of residual property

A liquidator may not devolve the property of the juridical person in accordance with Article 67 (Devolution of residual property) of this Code until after all obligations of the juridical person have been performed. However, with respect to disputed obligations, the liquidator may distribute the residual property after setting aside the property necessary for performing such obligations.

#### Article 78.- Bankruptcy during liquidation

1-If, during liquidation, it becomes clear that the property of the juridical person is insufficient to perform all of its obligations, the liquidator must immediately petition for a declaration of bankruptcy and give public notice of such fact in the official gazette of the

Justice.

2-The duties of a liquidator shall be deemed to have ended when the liquidator has transferred the business to a bankruptcy trustee.

3-In the cases specified in this Article, any property already performed to creditors or devolved to persons entitled to receive it may be recovered by the bankruptcy trustee.

#### Article 79.- Completion of liquidation

1-When the liquidation business is completed, the liquidator must prepare a final account report without delay.

2-With respect to a limited liability juridical partnership, the liquidator must submit the final account report specified in paragraph 1 above to the general meeting of members without delay and obtain its approval.

3-With respect to an unlimited liability juridical partnership, the liquidator must submit the final account report specified in paragraph 1 above to each member without delay and obtain the approval of each member.

4-With respect to a juridical foundation, the liquidator must submit the final account report specified in paragraph 1 above to the competent ministry or authority without delay and obtain its approval.

5-A liquidator appointed by the court must submit the final account report specified in paragraph 1 above to the court without delay.

#### Article 80.- Registration of completion of liquidation

When the procedures specified in Article 79 (Completion of liquidation) of this Code are completed, the liquidator must register the completion of liquidation at the place of the principal office within 2 (two) weeks and at the place of other offices within 3 (three) weeks.

#### Article 81.- Preservation of documents

The books of account and other important documents of the juridical person relating to the business and the liquidation must be preserved for 10 (ten) years from the date of registration of the completion of liquidation at the place of the principal office. The person who shall preserve such documents shall be appointed by the court upon the application of a liquidator or any other interested person.

## SECTION 2

## JURIDICAL PARTNERSHIP

## SUB-PART 1

## LIMITED LIABILITY JURIDICAL PARTNERSHIP

#### Article 82.- Formation and articles of incorporation

1-In order to form a limited liability juridical partnership, persons who intend to become its members must jointly prepare the articles of incorporation and each of them must sign it.

2-The articles of incorporation specified in paragraph 1 above must state all of the following:

a-Purpose.

b-Name.

c-Location of the principal office.

d-Provisions concerning the total amount of underlying property guaranteed in the name of the juridical person and the assets. In cases where there are contributions in kind other than money, the name or title of the person making such contributions, the name and value of such property, or in cases where any person has promised to make a donation of property after the formation of the juridical person, the value of such property and the name or title of the donor, or the amount of expenses incurred for the formation of the juridical person that the juridical person shall bear.

e-Business year.

f-Provisions concerning directors, supervisors, and other officers.

g-Provisions concerning the acquisition and loss of membership.

h-Provisions concerning accounting.

i-Provisions concerning dissolution.

j-Provisions concerning amendment of the articles of incorporation.

k-Method of public notice.

3-The articles of incorporation shall not be effective unless they are certified by a notary.

4-The articles of incorporation must be kept at the principal office and each branch office.

#### Article 83.- Minimum amount of underlying property

A limited liability juridical person must have underlying property worth not less than 20,000,000 (twenty million) Riels.

#### Article 84.- Appointment of directors and supervisors

1-If the directors or supervisors are not specified in the articles of incorporation, a limited liability juridical partnership that is in the process of formation must appoint
```

#### Article 83.- Minimum amount of underlying property

A limited liability juridical person must have underlying property worth not less than 20,000,000 (twenty million) Riels.

#### Article 84.- Appointment of directors and supervisors

1-If the directors or supervisors are not specified in the articles of incorporation, a limited liability juridical partnership that is in the process of formation must appoint

directors or supervisors by a resolution of the general meeting of members before the formation of the juridical person.

2-Any member may convene the general meeting specified in paragraph 1 above.

#### Article 85.- Investigation of underlying property, etc.

1-Directors must notify the persons who are to make contributions of the underlying property of the amount to be contributed by each such person, and demand that they make such contributions in order to complete the underlying property.

2-In cases where any of the matters specified in the second sentence of point (d) of paragraph 2 of Article 82 (Formation and articles of incorporation) of this Code are stated in the articles of incorporation, the directors must apply to the court for the appointment of an inspector to investigate such matters without delay.

3-The inspector specified in paragraph 2 above must report the results of the investigation to the court.

4-If the court finds that the matters stated in the articles of incorporation are improper based on the results of the investigation, the court must issue an order to amend such matters. In this case, the court must notify the members and directors of the order, and if there is an amendment concerning a contribution in kind other than money, it must also notify the person making such contribution.

5-The person making a contribution in kind other than money who has received the notice specified in paragraph 4 above may cancel the contribution in kind within 1 (one) week after the order becomes final and binding. In this case, the limited liability juridical partnership in the process of formation may proceed with the formation procedures by amending the articles of incorporation.

#### Article 86.- Investigation of formation procedures

1-The directors and supervisors must investigate whether the persons who are to make contributions of the total amount of underlying property have been determined for certain, and whether the performance of property contributed in kind other than money has been completed.

2-If, based on the results of the investigation specified in paragraph 1 above, the directors or supervisors find any violation of laws or regulations or the articles of incorporation, or any improper point, they must report it to the members.

#### Article 87.- Items to be registered and period of registration

1-In addition to the items specified in Article 50 (Items to be registered) of this Code, a limited liability juridical partnership must register all of the following:

a-Total amount of underlying property.

b-Provisions concerning the rights of persons making contributions of underlying property.

c-Procedures for returning underlying property.

d-Method of public notice.

2-The registration of formation of a limited liability juridical partnership must be performed at the place of the principal office within 2 (two) weeks after the completion of the procedures specified in Article 85 (Investigation of underlying property, etc.) and Article 86 (Investigation of formation procedures) of this Code.

3-At the place of each branch office, the registration must be performed within 2 (two) weeks after the registration specified in paragraph 2 above.

#### Article 88.- Responsibility to guarantee contributions of underlying property

1-At the time of formation of a limited liability juridical partnership, if any part of the

determined for certain at the time of formation, the directors and members at the time of formation shall be deemed to have jointly subscribed for such part. This provision shall also apply where the subscription of underlying property by a subscriber is cancelled after the formation.

2-At the time of formation of a limited liability juridical partnership, if any part of the underlying property has not been paid or if the performance of property contributed in kind other than money has not been completed, the directors and members at the time of formation shall be jointly and severally liable to pay the amount that has not been paid or the value of the property contributed in kind whose performance has not been completed.

3-At the time of formation of a limited liability juridical partnership, if the value of the property specified in the second sentence of point (d) of Article 82 (Formation and articles of incorporation) of this Code is significantly less than the value stated in the articles of incorporation, the directors and members at the time of formation shall be jointly and severally liable to pay the deficiency to the juridical person. However, in cases where an investigation by an inspector has been conducted in accordance with the provision of Article 85 (Investigation of underlying property, etc.) of this Code, such directors and members shall be exempt from liability, unless they themselves are the persons making the contribution in kind or the donors of such property.

#### Article 89.- Rights and duties of members

1-Members have the duty to pay the expenses of the juridical person.

2-Members may exercise voting rights at the general meeting of members. However, a member
```

#### Article 89.- Rights and duties of members

1-Members have the duty to pay the expenses of the juridical person.

2-Members may exercise voting rights at the general meeting of members. However, a member

may not exercise voting rights in cases where the resolution concerns the relationship between that member and the juridical person.

3-Each member shall have one vote. However, it may be provided otherwise in the articles of incorporation in consideration of the amount of contributions.

4-A member who does not attend the general meeting of members may vote in writing or by proxy. However, if there are different provisions in the articles of incorporation, such provisions shall govern.

#### Article 90.- Loss of membership

1-A member may withdraw at any time. However, if the articles of incorporation require a member who intends to withdraw to give advance notice to the juridical person, the member must give such notice, except in unavoidable circumstances.

2-The period of advance notice specified in the first sentence of paragraph 1 above shall not exceed 1 (one) year.

3-In addition to the cases specified in paragraph 1 above, a member shall lose membership for any of the following reasons:

a-Occurrence of a ground specified in the articles of incorporation.

b-Consent of all members.

c-Death or dissolution.

d-Expulsion.

#### Article 91.- Expulsion

1-Expulsion of a member may be effected by a resolution of the general meeting of members only for a justifiable reason. In this case, the juridical person must notify the member of the reason at least 1 (one) week before the general meeting and provide the member with an
```

#### Article 90.- Loss of membership

1-A member may withdraw at any time. However, if the articles of incorporation require a member who intends to withdraw to give advance notice to the juridical person, the member must give such notice, except in unavoidable circumstances.

2-The period of advance notice specified in the first sentence of paragraph 1 above shall not exceed 1 (one) year.

3-In addition to the cases specified in paragraph 1 above, a member shall lose membership for any of the following reasons:

a-Occurrence of a ground specified in the articles of incorporation.

b-Consent of all members.

c-Death or dissolution.

d-Expulsion.

#### Article 91.- Expulsion

1-Expulsion of a member may be effected by a resolution of the general meeting of members only for a justifiable reason. In this case, the juridical person must notify the member of the reason at least 1 (one) week before the general meeting and provide the member with an

opportunity to provide an explanation to the general meeting of members.

2-The resolution for expulsion specified in paragraph 1 above must be approved by members who represent at least one-half of the total number of members and at least three-fourths of the total voting rights of all members.

3-An expulsion shall not be effective until after the expelled member has been notified of such fact.

#### Article 92.- Ordinary general meeting

Directors must convene an ordinary general meeting of members at least once a year.

#### Article 93.- Extraordinary general meeting

1-Directors may convene an extraordinary general meeting of members at any time if they deem it necessary.

2-The directors must convene an extraordinary general meeting of members if members who hold at least one-tenth of the total voting rights demand such meeting by stating the purpose for the meeting. However, if the articles of incorporation provide for a different quorum, such provision shall govern.

3-Notwithstanding the provision of paragraph 2 above, if the directors fail to convene a general meeting without delay upon such demand, the members who made the demand may themselves convene the general meeting with the permission of the court.

#### Article 94.- Convening of general meeting

1-In order to convene a general meeting of members, notice of the general meeting must be given
```

#### Article 92.- Ordinary general meeting

Directors must convene an ordinary general meeting of members at least once a year.

#### Article 93.- Extraordinary general meeting

1-Directors may convene an extraordinary general meeting of members at any time if they deem it necessary.

2-The directors must convene an extraordinary general meeting of members if members who hold at least one-tenth of the total voting rights demand such meeting by stating the purpose for the meeting. However, if the articles of incorporation provide for a different quorum, such provision shall govern.

3-Notwithstanding the provision of paragraph 2 above, if the directors fail to convene a general meeting without delay upon such demand, the members who made the demand may themselves convene the general meeting with the permission of the court.

#### Article 94.- Convening of general meeting

1-In order to convene a general meeting of members, notice of the general meeting must be given

to each member at least 1 (one) week before the date of the meeting. However, this period may be shortened by the articles of incorporation.

2-If all members consent, a general meeting of members may be held without the procedures for convening.

#### Article 95.- Authority of general meeting

1-A general meeting of members may resolve only the matters provided in this Code or the articles of incorporation.

2-The directors and supervisors must provide explanations on matters demanded by members at the general meeting of members. However, this provision shall not apply where such matter is not related to the purpose of the meeting, or where giving such explanation would significantly damage the common interest of the members, or where such explanation requires investigation, or where there are other justifiable reasons.

3-In the case specified in paragraph 2 above, if a member has given notice of the matter to be explained at the general meeting in writing within a reasonable time before the date of the general meeting, the directors and supervisors may not refuse to provide the explanation on the ground that it requires investigation.

#### Article 96.- Matters that may be resolved by general meeting

A general meeting of members may resolve only the matters for which notice was given in accordance with the provision of Article 94 (Convening of general meeting) of this Code. However, this provision shall not apply where the articles of incorporation provide otherwise.

#### Article 97.- Amendment of articles of incorporation

1-The articles of incorporation of a limited liability juridical partnership may be amended only by a resolution of members who represent at least three-fourths of the total voting rights of all members. However, if the articles of incorporation provide otherwise, such provision shall govern.

2-An amendment of the articles of incorporation shall not be effective unless it is certified by a notary.

#### Article 98.- Preparation and approval of calculation documents, etc.

1-Each business year, the directors must prepare the following documents and the annexed specifications stating the important reasons to explain the contents of such documents:

a-Balance sheet.

b-Profit and loss statement.

c-Business report.

d-Proposed resolution concerning the distribution of surplus or the treatment of losses.

2-The directors must submit the documents specified in each point of paragraph 1 above to the ordinary general meeting; provided that the directors shall report the contents of the document specified in point (c), and shall obtain the approval of the general meeting for the documents specified in points (a), (b), and (d).

#### Article 99.- Audit of calculation documents, etc.

1-The directors must have the documents specified in each point of paragraph 1 of Article 98 (Preparation and approval of calculation documents, etc.) of this Code audited by the supervisors.

2-The audit specified in paragraph 1 above must be conducted before the ordinary general meeting of members.

3-The directors must submit the documents specified in each point of paragraph 1 of Article 98 (Preparation and approval of calculation documents, etc.) of this Code to the supervisors at least 5 (five) weeks before the general meeting, and the annexed specifications at least 3 (three) weeks before the commencement of the audit by the supervisors.

4-The supervisors must submit an audit report to the directors within 4 (four) weeks from the date of receiving the documents specified in paragraph 3 above, excluding the annexed specifications.

#### Article 100.- Public disclosure of calculation documents, etc.

1-A limited liability juridical partnership must keep the documents specified in each point of paragraph 1 of Article 98 (Preparation and approval of calculation documents, etc.) of this Code and the audit reports for 5 (five) years from the date the audit report was submitted to the directors at the principal office, and keep copies thereof for 3 (three) years at each branch office.

2-The members and creditors of the juridical person may request to inspect the documents specified in paragraph 1 above, or request a certified copy or an extract of such documents from the juridical person during its business hours. However, in the case of requesting a certified copy or an extract, the person making the request must pay the fees determined by the juridical person.

## SUB-PART 2

## UNLIMITED LIABILITY JURIDICAL PARTNERSHIP

#### Article 101.- Formation and articles of incorporation

1-In order to form an unlimited liability juridical partnership, persons who intend to become its members must jointly prepare the articles of incorporation and each of them must sign it.

2-The articles of incorporation specified in paragraph 1 above must state all of the following:

a-Purpose.

b-Name.

c-Names and residences of the members.

d-Location of the principal office and each branch office.

3-The articles of incorporation shall not be effective unless they are certified by a notary.

4-The articles of incorporation must be kept at the principal office and each branch office.

#### Article 102.- Items to be registered and period of registration

1-In addition to the items specified in Article 50 (Items to be registered) of this Code, an unlimited liability juridical partnership must register all of the following at the place of the principal office:

a-Names of the members who represent the juridical person, in cases where some members do not represent the juridical person.

b-Provisions concerning representation, in cases where multiple members represent the juridical person.

2-At the place of each branch office, the registration specified in paragraph 1 above must be performed within 2 (two) weeks after the registration
```

#### Article 101.- Formation and articles of incorporation

1-In order to form an unlimited liability juridical partnership, persons who intend to become its members must jointly prepare the articles of incorporation and each of them must sign it.

2-The articles of incorporation specified in paragraph 1 above must state all of the following:

a-Purpose.

b-Name.

c-Names and residences of the members.

d-Location of the principal office and each branch office.

3-The articles of incorporation shall not be effective unless they are certified by a notary.

4-The articles of incorporation must be kept at the principal office and each branch office.

#### Article 102.- Items to be registered and period of registration

1-In addition to the items specified in Article 50 (Items to be registered) of this Code, an unlimited liability juridical partnership must register all of the following at the place of the principal office:

a-Names of the members who represent the juridical person, in cases where some members do not represent the juridical person.

b-Provisions concerning representation, in cases where multiple members represent the juridical person.

2-At the place of each branch office, the registration specified in paragraph 1 above must be performed within 2 (two) weeks after the registration

in accordance with the provision of paragraph 1 above.

#### Article 103.- Liability of members, etc.

1-If an unlimited liability juridical partnership is unable to perform its obligations in full with its property, its members shall be jointly and severally liable for such performance.

2-The provision of paragraph 1 above shall also apply where compulsory execution against the property of the unlimited liability juridical partnership has proved ineffective.

3-The provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2 above shall not apply if a member proves that the unlimited liability juridical partnership has sufficient resources and that compulsory execution can be easily effected against it.

4-Members may assert against the creditors of the unlimited liability juridical partnership any defense that the unlimited liability juridical partnership has against such creditors.

5-If the unlimited liability juridical partnership has a right of set-off, a right of rescission, or a right of termination against its creditors, its members may refuse to perform the obligations to such creditors.

6-A member who joins an unlimited liability juridical partnership after its formation shall also be liable for the obligations of the juridical person that arose before such member joined.

7-A member who has withdrawn shall be liable for the obligations of the juridical person that arose before the registration of the withdrawal at the place of the principal office of the juridical person.

8-If 2 (two) years have elapsed after the registration specified
```

#### Article 103.- Liability of members, etc.

1-If an unlimited liability juridical partnership is unable to perform its obligations in full with its property, its members shall be jointly and severally liable for such performance.

2-The provision of paragraph 1 above shall also apply where compulsory execution against the property of the unlimited liability juridical partnership has proved ineffective.

3-The provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2 above shall not apply if a member proves that the unlimited liability juridical partnership has sufficient resources and that compulsory execution can be easily effected against it.

4-Members may assert against the creditors of the unlimited liability juridical partnership any defense that the unlimited liability juridical partnership has against such creditors.

5-If the unlimited liability juridical partnership has a right of set-off, a right of rescission, or a right of termination against its creditors, its members may refuse to perform the obligations to such creditors.

6-A member who joins an unlimited liability juridical partnership after its formation shall also be liable for the obligations of the juridical person that arose before such member joined.

7-A member who has withdrawn shall be liable for the obligations of the juridical person that arose before the registration of the withdrawal at the place of the principal office of the juridical person.

8-If 2 (two) years have elapsed after the registration specified

in paragraph 7 above, the liability specified in paragraph 7 shall be extinguished as against creditors of the juridical person who fail to make a demand or give notice of a demand within 2 (two) years after such registration.

9-Members shall bear the expenses of the juridical person in accordance with the provisions of the articles of incorporation.

#### Article 104.- Loss of membership

1-A member may withdraw at any time, unless otherwise provided in the articles of incorporation.

2-Notwithstanding the provision of paragraph 1 above, a member may withdraw at any time if there is an unavoidable reason.

3-In addition to the cases specified in paragraphs 1 and 2 above, a member shall lose membership for any of the following reasons:

a-Occurrence of a ground specified in the articles of incorporation.

b-Consent of all members.

c-Expulsion.

d-Death.

e-Bankruptcy.

f-Declaration of commencement of general guardianship.

#### Article 105.- Expulsion

Expulsion of a member may be effected by the unanimous consent of all other members only for a justifiable reason. However, the expulsion shall not be effective until after the expelled member has been notified of such fact.

#### Article 106.- Conduct of business

1-Members shall conduct the business of the unlimited liability juridical partnership.

2-The business of an unlimited liability juridical partnership shall be decided by a majority of the members, unless otherwise provided in the articles of incorporation.

3-If the articles of incorporation specify the members who are to conduct the business of the unlimited liability juridical partnership, such members shall conduct the business of the unlimited liability juridical partnership.

4-If there are multiple members specified in paragraph 3 above, the business of the unlimited liability juridical partnership shall be decided by a majority of such members, unless otherwise provided in the articles of incorporation.

5-Notwithstanding the provisions of paragraphs 2 and 4 above, the ordinary business of an unlimited liability juridical partnership may be conducted by any member. In the case specified in paragraph 3 above, only the members specified in that paragraph may conduct the ordinary business. However, this shall not apply if another member objects to such conduct of ordinary business before it is completed.

#### Article 107.- Representation of juridical person

1-Members shall represent the unlimited liability juridical partnership. However, in cases where the members who are to conduct the business are specified in paragraph 3 of Article 106 (Conduct of business) of this Code, only such members shall represent the juridical person.

2-If there are multiple members who represent the unlimited liability juridical partnership,
```

#### Article 106.- Conduct of business

1-Members shall conduct the business of the unlimited liability juridical partnership.

2-The business of an unlimited liability juridical partnership shall be decided by a majority of the members, unless otherwise provided in the articles of incorporation.

3-If the articles of incorporation specify the members who are to conduct the business of the unlimited liability juridical partnership, such members shall conduct the business of the unlimited liability juridical partnership.

4-If there are multiple members specified in paragraph 3 above, the business of the unlimited liability juridical partnership shall be decided by a majority of such members, unless otherwise provided in the articles of incorporation.

5-Notwithstanding the provisions of paragraphs 2 and 4 above, the ordinary business of an unlimited liability juridical partnership may be conducted by any member. In the case specified in paragraph 3 above, only the members specified in that paragraph may conduct the ordinary business. However, this shall not apply if another member objects to such conduct of ordinary business before it is completed.

#### Article 107.- Representation of juridical person

1-Members shall represent the unlimited liability juridical partnership. However, in cases where the members who are to conduct the business are specified in paragraph 3 of Article 106 (Conduct of business) of this Code, only such members shall represent the juridical person.

2-If there are multiple members who represent the unlimited liability juridical partnership,

in accordance with the provision of paragraph 1 above, each member shall represent the juridical person. However, an unlimited liability juridical partnership may specifically designate the members who are to represent the juridical person from among such members, by the articles of incorporation or with the consent of all members.

3-The provisions concerning directors shall apply to the members who represent an unlimited liability juridical partnership.

#### Article 108.- Reporting and investigation

1-A member may demand from other members a report on the status of the conduct of business, or may investigate the status of the business and property of the unlimited liability juridical partnership. However, in the case specified in paragraph 3 of Article 106 (Conduct of business) of this Code, a member may demand such report or conduct such investigation only from or against the members specified in that paragraph.

2-If another member performs an act outside the scope of the purpose of the juridical person or an act that violates any law or regulation or the articles of incorporation, or if there is a concern that such member might perform such act, and if there is a concern that such act might cause significant damage to the juridical person, a member may demand that such other member cease such act.

3-In cases where there are supervisors, the tasks specified in paragraphs 1 and 2 above shall be performed by the supervisors.

#### Article 109.- Amendment of articles of incorporation

1-An amendment of the articles of incorporation requires the consent of all members.

2-Notwithstanding the provision of paragraph 1 above, if the articles of incorporation provide
```
