#### Article 107.- Representation of juridical person

1-Members shall represent the unlimited liability juridical partnership. However, in cases where the members who are to conduct the business are specified in paragraph 3 of Article 106 (Conduct of business) of this Code, only such members shall represent the juridical person.

2-If there are multiple members who represent the unlimited liability juridical partnership, each member shall represent the juridical person. However, an unlimited liability juridical partnership may specifically designate the members who are to represent the juridical person from among such members, by the articles of incorporation or with the consent of all members.

3-The provisions concerning directors shall apply to the members who represent an unlimited liability juridical partnership.

#### Article 108.- Reporting and investigation

1-A member may demand from other members a report on the status of the conduct of business, or may investigate the status of the business and property of the unlimited liability juridical partnership. However, in the case specified in paragraph 3 of Article 106 (Conduct of business) of this Code, a member may demand such report or conduct such investigation only from or against the members specified in that paragraph.

2-If another member performs an act outside the scope of the purpose of the juridical person or an act that violates any law or regulation or the articles of incorporation, or if there is a concern that such member might perform such act, and if there is a concern that such act might cause significant damage to the juridical person, a member may demand that such other member cease such act.

3-In cases where there are supervisors, the tasks specified in paragraphs 1 and 2 above shall be performed by the supervisors.

#### Article 109.- Amendment of articles of incorporation

1-An amendment of the articles of incorporation requires the consent of all members.

2-Notwithstanding the provision of paragraph 1 above, if the articles of incorporation provide

that the articles of incorporation may be amended with the consent of a certain ratio of all members, such provision shall govern.

3-An amendment of the articles of incorporation shall not be effective unless it is certified by a notary.

## SECTION 3

## JURIDICAL FOUNDATION

#### Article 110.- Formation and articles of incorporation

1-A juridical foundation may be formed by preparing the articles of incorporation and obtaining the permission of the competent supervisory authority, provided that the foundation is formed for the public interest.

2-The articles of incorporation specified in paragraph 1 above must state the following:

a-Purpose.

b-Name.

c-Location of the principal office.

d-Provisions concerning the total amount of the underlying property and assets. In cases where contributions are made in kind other than money, the name and value of such property must be stated, or where a person has promised to donate certain property after the formation of the juridical person, the value of such property and the name of the donor must be stated, or the amount of formation expenses to be borne by the juridical person must be stated.

e-Business year.

f-Matters concerning directors, supervisors, and other officers.

g-Matters concerning accounting.

h-Matters concerning dissolution.

i-Matters concerning amendment of the articles of incorporation.

j-Method of public notice.

3-The articles of incorporation shall not be effective unless they are certified by a notary.

4-The articles of incorporation must be kept at the principal office and each branch office.

#### Article 111.- Minimum amount of underlying property

A juridical foundation must have underlying property with a value of at least 200,000,000 (two hundred million) Riels.

#### Article 112.- Completion of articles of incorporation

If the founder of a juridical foundation dies without determining its name, its office, or the method of appointment and dismissal of directors, the court may determine such matters upon the request of an interested person or a public prosecutor.

#### Article 113.- Mutatis mutandis application of provisions concerning gifts and testamentary gifts

1-The provisions of Chapter 3 (Gifts) of Book 5 shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where property is contributed to form a juridical foundation by an act inter vivos.

2-The provisions of Section 6 (Testamentary gifts) of Chapter 3 of Book 8 shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where property is contributed to form a juridical foundation by a will.

#### Article 114.- Time of acquisition of rights to property contributed

1-In cases where property is contributed to form a juridical foundation
```

#### Article 111.- Minimum amount of underlying property

A juridical foundation must have underlying property with a value of at least 200,000,000 (two hundred million) Riels.

#### Article 112.- Completion of articles of incorporation

If the founder of a juridical foundation dies without determining its name, its office, or the method of appointment and dismissal of directors, the court may determine such matters upon the request of an interested person or a public prosecutor.

#### Article 113.- Mutatis mutandis application of provisions concerning gifts and testamentary gifts

1-The provisions of Chapter 3 (Gifts) of Book 5 shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where property is contributed to form a juridical foundation by an act inter vivos.

2-The provisions of Section 6 (Testamentary gifts) of Chapter 3 of Book 8 shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where property is contributed to form a juridical foundation by a will.

#### Article 114.- Time of acquisition of rights to property contributed

1-In cases where property is contributed to form a juridical foundation

by an act inter vivos, the property contributed shall become the property of the juridical person from the time that the registration specified in Article 49 (Registration of formation and time of formation of juridical person) of this Code is performed.

2-In cases where property is contributed to form a juridical foundation by a will, the property contributed shall be deemed to have become the property of the juridical person from the time the will becomes effective.

#### Article 115.- Items to be registered and period of registration

1-In addition to the items specified in Article 50 (Items to be registered) of this Code, a juridical foundation must register all of the following at the place of the principal office:

a-Total value of the underlying property.

b-Method of public notice.

c-Date on which permission was obtained from the competent supervisory authority.

2-The registration of formation of a juridical foundation must be performed at the place of the principal office within 2 (two) weeks after the date on which permission was obtained from the competent supervisory authority.

3-At the place of each branch office, the registration must be performed within 2 (two) weeks after the registration specified in paragraph 2 above.

#### Article 116.- Preparation and approval of calculation documents, etc.

1-Each business year, the directors must prepare the following documents and the annexed specifications stating the important reasons to explain the contents of such documents:

a-Balance sheet.

b-Profit and loss statement.

c-Business report.

d-Proposed resolution concerning the distribution of surplus or the treatment of losses.

2-The directors must submit the documents specified in each point of paragraph 1 above to the competent supervisory authority and obtain its approval.

#### Article 117.- Audit of calculation documents, etc.

1-The directors must have the documents specified in each point of paragraph 1 of Article 116 (Preparation and approval of calculation documents, etc.) of this Code audited by the supervisors.

2-The audit specified in paragraph 1 above must be conducted before submitting the documents to the competent supervisory authority.

3-The directors must submit the documents specified in each point of paragraph 1 of Article 116 (Preparation and approval of calculation documents, etc.) of this Code to the supervisors at least 5 (five) weeks before, and the annexed specifications at least 3 (three) weeks before the commencement of the audit by the supervisors.

4-The supervisors must submit an audit report to the directors within 4 (four) weeks from the date of receiving the documents specified in paragraph 3 above, excluding the annexed specifications.

#### Article 118.- Public disclosure of calculation documents, etc.

1-A juridical foundation must keep the documents specified in each point of paragraph 1 of Article 116 (Preparation and approval of calculation documents, etc.) of

Article 116 (Preparation and approval of calculation documents, etc.) of this Code and the audit reports for 5 (five) years from the date the audit report was submitted to the directors at the principal office, and keep copies thereof for 3 (three) years at each branch office.

2-The creditors of the juridical foundation may request to inspect the documents specified in paragraph 1 above, or request a certified copy or an extract of such documents from the juridical foundation during its business hours. However, in the case of requesting a certified copy or an extract, the person making the request must pay the fees determined by the juridical foundation.

## BOOK 3

## RIGHTS OVER PROPERTY

### CHAPTER 1

### GENERAL PROVISIONS

#### SECTION 1

#### THINGS

#### Article 119.- Definition of things

In this Code, "things" refer to corporeal objects, including gas, liquid, and solid substances.

#### Article 120.- Movables and immovables

1-Things are divided into movables and immovables.

2-Immovables refer to land and things attached to land that cannot be moved, such as buildings, structures, crops, and plants.

3-Movables refer to things other than immovables.

4-Provisions concerning movables shall also apply to incorporeal property that can be controlled, unless otherwise provided by special laws.

#### Article 121.- Component parts of things

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code)

A component part of a thing that cannot be separated from such thing without destroying it or changing its essential nature cannot be the object of independent rights.

#### Article 122.- Component parts of land: General principles

Things attached to land or things that become a part of land, especially buildings or structures constructed on land that cannot be moved, or seeds sown on land, cultivated crops, and plants living on land are component parts of the land until they are separated from the land and cannot be the object of independent rights, unless otherwise provided.

#### Article 123.- Component parts of land: Exceptions

Buildings or other structures constructed on land or plants or crops planted on land by a person who has rights over another person's land in the exercise of such rights shall not become component parts of the land. This provision shall also apply to things attached to land for a temporary purpose.

#### Article 124.- Buildings, etc. constructed based on rights over another person's land

In the case specified in Article 123 (Component parts of land: Exceptions) of this Code, buildings or other structures constructed on land or plants or crops planted on land by a person who has rights over another person's land are deemed to be component parts of the rights over another person's land.

#### Article 125.- Component parts of buildings

Materials that are component parts of a building, building fixtures, built-in furniture, decorations, etc., which cannot be separated without destroying the building or changing its essential nature, are component parts of the building and cannot be the object of independent rights.

#### Article 126.- Principal things and accessory things

1-Things that are not component parts of a principal thing and which the owner of the principal thing

connected to the principal thing for the constant economic use of the principal thing are called accessory things.

2-The creation and transfer of rights over a principal thing shall also extend to its accessory things, unless otherwise agreed.

#### Article 127.- Definitions of principal things and fruits

1-The profits derived from a thing are called "fruits." The thing that produces fruits is called the "principal thing."

2-Things that are produced from a thing and acquired through the ordinary use of such thing are called "natural fruits."

3-Money, such as rent, etc., or other things received as consideration for the use of a thing are called "civil fruits."

#### Article 128.- Right to extract fruits

1-Natural fruits belong to the person who has the right to extract them at the time they are separated from the principal thing.

2-Civil fruits are acquired in proportion to the number of days during the period the right to acquire them exists.

#### Article 129.- Right to demand reimbursement of expenses by person obligated to return fruits

A person who is obligated to return fruits may demand from the person to whom they are returned reimbursement for the ordinary expenses incurred in producing such fruits. However, the amount of such reimbursement may not exceed the value of the fruits returned.

### SECTION 2

### REAL RIGHTS

#### Article 130.- Definition of real rights

A real right is a right to directly control a thing, which can be asserted against any other person.

#### Article 131.- Creation of real rights

Real rights may only be created in accordance with the types and contents recognized by this Code or by special laws. A real right recognized by customary law shall be effective under this Code to the extent that it does not conflict with this Code or special laws.

#### Article 132.- Types of real rights

The real rights provided for in this Code are as follows:

a-Ownership.

b-Possession.

c-Usufructuary real rights:

c1) Perpetual lease

c2) Usufruct

c3) Right of use and right of residence

c4) Easement.

d-Security real rights:

d1) Right of retention

d2) Statutory lien

d3) Pledge

d4) Hypothec

d5) Security by transfer of title.

### SECTION 3

### PRINCIPLES OF CREATION, TRANSFER, AND AMENDMENT OF REAL RIGHTS

#### Article 133.- Creation, transfer, and amendment of real rights by agreement

The creation, transfer, and amendment of real rights shall take effect by agreement between the parties.

#### Article 134.- Requirement for opposability of creation, transfer, and amendment of real rights

1-The creation, transfer, and amendment of real rights over immovables, other than possession, the right of retention, the right of use, and the right of residence, may not be asserted against a third party unless the right is registered in accordance with the provisions of laws or regulations concerning registration.

2-The transfer of a real right over a movable may not be asserted against a third party unless the movable has been delivered.

#### Article 135.- Requirement for effectiveness of transfer of ownership of immovables by agreement

Notwithstanding the provisions of Article 133 (Creation, transfer, and amendment of real rights by agreement) and Article 134 (Requirement for opposability of creation, transfer, and amendment of real rights) of this Code, the transfer of ownership of immovables by agreement shall not take effect unless it is registered in accordance with the provisions of laws or regulations concerning registration.

#### Article 136.- Confusion of rights

1-If ownership of a thing and another real right over the same thing belong to the same person, such other real right shall be extinguished. However, this shall not apply if such thing or such other real right is the object of a right of a third party.

2-If a real right other than ownership and another right which has such real right as its object belong to the same person, such other right shall be extinguished. In this case, the second sentence of paragraph 1 shall apply mutatis mutandis.

3-The provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2 above shall not apply to possession.

#### Article 137.- Presumptive effect of registration

1-If a right is registered in the land registry, it shall be presumed that such right belongs to the person in whose name it is registered.

2-If a right that was registered in the land registry has been cancelled, it shall be presumed that such right has been extinguished.

### CHAPTER 2

### OWNERSHIP

#### SECTION 1

#### CONTENT AND LIMITS OF OWNERSHIP

#### Article 138.- Definition of ownership

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code)

Ownership refers to the right of an owner to use, extract fruits from, and

dispose of the thing owned by him/her freely, within the limits prescribed by laws and regulations.

#### Article 139.- Limits of land ownership

1-Ownership of land shall extend to the area above and below the surface of the land, within the limits prescribed by laws and regulations, and to the extent that it is beneficial to the owner.

2-The owner of land may not use his/her land solely for the purpose of malicious intent against others or for the purpose of disturbing the living conditions of others.

#### Article 140.- Cessation of activities that disturb living conditions

1-In cases where disturbances to living conditions caused by smoke, steam, odors, soot, heat, noise, vibrations, etc., originating from another person's land, are within the scope of ordinary use and are not prohibited by regulations, the owner of the land may not demand that such other person cease the activities that cause such effects on him/her.

2-In the cases specified in paragraph 1 above, a land owner who suffers severe adverse effects may, depending on the circumstances, demand reasonable compensation from the person who disturbs his/her living conditions. Furthermore, this provision shall not prevent the land owner from claiming damages based on a tort.

#### Article 141.- Excavation of cultural heritage and minerals in the ground

1-The owner of land may not assert ownership over any statues, bas-reliefs, antiquities, or other cultural heritage found in his/her land. Such things are the property of the State, and the owner of the land where such things are found is obligated to deliver them to the Ministry of Culture and Fine Arts.

2-The owner of land may not assert ownership over minerals in the ground as specified in other laws. Such things are the property of the State, and persons who are granted mining rights by the State in accordance with other laws have the right to mine and acquire such things.

#### Article 142.- Right to cut branches and roots of trees

When branches or roots of trees on adjacent land extend onto his/her land, the owner of the land may acquire the fruits from such branches or roots, or cut off such branches or roots.

### SECTION 2

### RELATIONSHIP BETWEEN ADJACENT LANDS

#### Article 143.- Right to use adjacent land

The owner of land may demand the use of adjacent land to the extent necessary for constructing or repairing fences or structures on or near the boundary. If the neighbor suffers damage due to such construction or repair, the owner of the land shall pay compensation.

#### Article 144.- Right to pass through land to access public roads

1-Land that is surrounded by land belonging to other persons and has no access to a public road, or where the existing access is insufficient and presents great obstacles to the use of the land for agricultural or industrial purposes, is called "enclosed land."

2-The owner of enclosed land has the right to request passage through adjacent land by paying compensation for any damage caused by such passage.

3-As a rule, this right of passage must be exercised in the direction or way that has the shortest distance

points that are the shortest distance from the enclosed land to the public road. Notwithstanding the provision of the first sentence of this paragraph, the point of passage must be chosen so as to cause the least damage to the owner of the burdened land.

4-If a land becomes enclosed as a result of a division of land due to sale, exchange, division of estate, or any other contract, the right of passage may be claimed only over the land remaining after such division. In this case, the owner of the enclosed land is not required to pay compensation. However, if there is no sufficient access, the provisions of paragraphs 2 and 3 above shall also apply.

#### Article 145.- Obligation to receive natural flow of water

1-The owner of lower land is obligated to receive water flowing naturally from higher land. The owner of the lower land may not construct any embankment, dike, or structure that blocks such flow. The owner of the higher land may not do anything that increases the burden on the owner of the lower land.

2-If the natural flow of water on lower land is blocked due to force majeure for which neither party is responsible, the owner of the higher land may construct a channel for the flow of water at his/her own expense.

#### Article 146.- Right to use rainwater, etc.

Subject to the third sentence of paragraph 1 of Article 145 (Obligation to receive natural flow of water) of this Code, the owner of higher land has the right to use water originating from a source on his/her land and rainwater falling on his/her land, and has the right to drain such water.

#### Article 147.- Obligation to maintain water flow

The owner of land adjacent to a watercourse is obligated to allow the water to flow

allow the water to flow onto the neighbor's land as required for agriculture, and the owner of the adjacent land is also obligated to do the same for land further away.

#### Article 148.- Right to construct water channels for irrigation

An owner who wishes to use water that he/she has the right to use for irrigation of his/her land may construct and maintain a water channel through land located between the water source and his/her land, by paying compensation for any damage caused to the owner of the burdened land.

#### Article 149.- Right to drain irrigation water

The owner of land may drain water used for irrigation of his/her land through lower land by paying compensation to the owner of the lower land.

#### Article 150.- Right to drain water by owner of flooded land

An owner whose land is entirely or partially flooded may drain the water that causes damage from his/her land, provided that he/she complies with the provisions of laws or regulations concerning sanitation.

#### Article 151.- Right to construct and use dikes, etc.

1-An owner whose land is adjacent to a river, stream, or canal, and who wishes to use the water of such river, stream, or canal for irrigation of his/her land, may construct necessary structures for taking water on land on the opposite bank by paying compensation to the owner of the land on the opposite bank.

2-The owner of the land who permits the construction of structures on his/her land may demand to jointly use the dikes, etc. from the person who constructed such structures, by bearing half of the expenses for construction and maintenance. In this case, the person who constructed the structures is not obligated to pay the compensation provided in paragraph 1 above and may demand the return of the compensation already paid.

#### Article 152.- Activities that disturb living conditions

An owner who intends to perform on his/her land activities that may disturb the living conditions of neighbors, such as drilling wells, digging drainage ditches, excavating, or storing dangerous, offensive, or unsanitary materials, must comply with special regulations regarding the distances to be observed and the activities to be performed.

#### Article 153.- Obligation to place screens on windows, etc.

A person who constructs a window or similar structure that allows a direct view onto a neighbor's residential land at a distance of less than 2 (two) meters from the boundary must place a screen.

#### Article 154.- Trees near the boundary

A land owner may not plant trees, shrubs, or bushes exceeding 2 (two) meters in height within 2 (two) meters from the boundary of a neighbor's land. In case of violation of this provision, the owner is obligated to remove them upon the request of the adjacent land owner.

### SECTION 3

### RIGHTS TO DEMAND BASED ON OWNERSHIP

#### Article 155.- Right to demand return based on ownership

An owner may demand the return of his/her thing from a person who possesses it. However, this shall not apply if such person has a legal right of possession as against the owner.

#### Article 156.- Possessor and fruits

1-A good faith possessor may acquire the fruits derived from the possessed thing.

2-If a good faith possessor is defeated in an action on the legal right of possession, such possessor shall be deemed a bad faith possessor from the time the action was filed.

3-If a possessor returns the possessed thing to the owner, a bad faith possessor at the time the fruits were produced is obligated to return the fruits received by him/her and is obligated to compensate for the value of fruits that were damaged or failed to be collected due to his/her fault or negligence.

#### Article 157.- Liability for loss or damage of possessed thing

If the possessed thing is lost or damaged, or if its return becomes impossible due to a fault of the possessor, a bad faith possessor at the time of such loss, damage, or impossibility of return is obligated to compensate for all damages, while a good faith possessor is obligated to compensate for damages only to the extent of the benefits he/she continues to receive. However, a possessor who does not have the intention to own the thing is obligated to compensate for all damages even if the possession was in good faith.

#### Article 158.- Right of possessor to demand reimbursement of expenses

1-If a possessor returns the possessed thing to the owner, the possessor may demand from the owner reimbursement of the necessary expenses incurred for the management and maintenance of the thing. If the possessor has acquired fruits from the possessed thing, the possessor shall bear the ordinary necessary expenses for management and maintenance.

2-If a possessor has incurred expenses for improving the possessed thing or other

to increase the value of the thing, the owner must, at his/her option, reimburse the expenses incurred by the possessor or pay the amount of increase in the value of the thing, provided that such increase in value remains. However, for a bad faith possessor, the court may grant the owner a reasonable period for such reimbursement.

3-If a possessor returns land to the owner, the owner must reimburse the expenses incurred by a good faith possessor or pay the increase in value that remains, for buildings constructed or crops planted by such possessor that have not yet been harvested or trees that have not yet been cut. Notwithstanding the provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2 above, for a bad faith possessor, the owner has the option to require the possessor to remove at his/her own expense the buildings constructed or crops planted that have not yet been harvested or trees not yet cut, or to acquire them. If the owner chooses to require removal, the possessor must remove the buildings, crops, and trees at no cost to the owner. If the owner chooses to acquire the buildings, crops, and trees, the owner must reimburse the expenses incurred by the possessor or pay the increase in value, including the value of the things acquired, regardless of the area of adjacent land. In this case, the court may grant the owner a reasonable period for such reimbursement.

#### Article 159.- Right to demand cessation and prevention of disturbance based on ownership

1-If the exercise of ownership is being disturbed, the owner may demand that the person who is disturbing it cease such disturbance.

2-If there is a real concern that the exercise of ownership will be disturbed, the owner may demand that the person who may cause such disturbance prevent it.

### SECTION 4

### ACQUISITION OF OWNERSHIP

### SUB-SECTION 1

### ACQUISITION OF OWNERSHIP OF IMMOVABLES

#### Article 160.- Acquisition of ownership of immovables

Ownership of immovables may be acquired by contract, succession, and other grounds specified in this Section 4 (Acquisition of ownership), as well as by other provisions of this Code or other laws.

#### Article 161.- Ownerless immovables

Immovables that have no owner shall belong to the State.

#### Article 162.- Prescription for acquisition of ownership of immovables

1-A person who peacefully and publicly possesses an immovable with the intention to own it for a period of 20 (twenty) years shall acquire ownership of such immovable.

2-A person who peacefully and publicly possesses an immovable with the intention to own it for a period of 10 (ten) years shall acquire ownership of such immovable if the possession was in good faith and without fault at the time it commenced.

3-The provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2 above shall not apply to immovables that are State property, regardless of their type.

#### Article 163.- Retroactive effect of prescriptive acquisition

The effect of prescriptive acquisition provided for in Article 162 (Prescription for acquisition

of ownership of immovables) of this Code shall be retroactive to the date on which the prescription started. Fruits produced after that date shall belong to the person who acquires ownership by prescription.

#### Article 164.- Invocation of prescriptive acquisition

1-The court may not render a judgment based on prescriptive acquisition unless a party invokes it.

2-Prescriptive acquisition may be invoked only by a person who is to acquire ownership by prescription, a person who has received a perpetual lease, usufruct, right of use, right of residence, easement, lease, hypothec, or pledge from such person, or any other person who has a legitimate interest in invoking it.

3-If a person who is to acquire ownership by prescription invokes it, third parties shall also benefit therefrom. If a person entitled to invoke it, other than the person who is to acquire ownership by prescription, invokes it, the effect of such invocation shall exist only as between the person who invokes it and the original owner.

#### Article 165.- Waiver of benefits of prescriptive acquisition

Benefits of prescriptive acquisition may not be waived in advance. Only the benefits of a prescription that has been completed may be waived.

#### Article 166.- Scope of persons affected by waiver of benefits of prescriptive acquisition

A waiver of the benefits of prescriptive acquisition shall be effective only as between the original owner and the person entitled to invoke it who made the waiver.

#### Article 167.- Grounds for interruption of prescriptive acquisition

Prescriptive acquisition shall be interrupted on any of the following grounds:

a-Loss of possession with the intention to own.

b-Legal claim or exercise of a right having similar effect.

c-Compulsory execution or protective measures.

d-Acknowledgment.

#### Article 168.- Scope of persons affected by interruption of prescriptive acquisition

If a ground for interruption of prescriptive acquisition occurs against a person who is to acquire ownership by prescription, other persons may not deny the effect of such interruption. If a ground for interruption of prescriptive acquisition occurs in relation to a person entitled to invoke it, other than the person who is to acquire ownership by prescription, the effect of such interruption shall exist only as between the original owner and such person entitled to invoke it.

#### Article 169.- Loss of possession with intention to own

If a person who possesses with the intention to own loses possession against his/her will, and regains possession within 1 (one) year or by means of an action

filed within 1 (one) year, the prescriptive acquisition shall be deemed not to have been interrupted.

#### Article 170.- Legal claim

A legal claim shall not have the effect of interrupting prescriptive acquisition if the action is dismissed or withdrawn.

#### Article 171.- Annulment of compulsory execution or protective measures

If compulsory execution or protective measures are annulled upon the request of the person entitled to such measures or for failure to comply with legal requirements, the interruption of prescriptive acquisition caused by such measures shall be deemed never to have occurred.

#### Article 172.- Running of prescriptive acquisition after interruption

1-Prescriptive acquisition that has been interrupted shall commence to run anew from the time the ground for interruption ceases to exist.

2-Prescriptive acquisition that has been interrupted by a legal claim shall commence to run anew from the time the judgment becomes final and binding.

#### Article 173.- Suspension of completion of prescription by claim

1-If the original owner makes a claim at any time within 6 (six) months before the expiration of the period of prescriptive acquisition, the prescription shall not be completed against the other party to the claim for 6 (six) months after the claim is made. However, even if the original owner makes another claim, the completion of the prescription may not be delayed by such further claim.

2-Even if a legal claim does not have the effect of interrupting the prescription because the action is dismissed or withdrawn, the claimant shall be deemed to have continuously made the claim from the time the complaint was sent to the other party until the time the action was dismissed or withdrawn. In this case, the prescriptive acquisition shall not be completed against the other party for 6 (six) months after the action was dismissed or withdrawn.

3-If the original owner asserts his/her right as a defendant in an action, such owner shall be deemed to have continuously made the claim during the pendency of the action from the time he/she asserted the right. In this case, the prescriptive acquisition shall not be completed against the claimant for 6 (six) months after the judgment in the action becomes final and binding.

#### Article 174.- Suspension of completion of prescriptive acquisition in relation to minors or persons under general guardianship

If a minor or a person under general guardianship is without a legal representative at any time within 6 (six) months before the expiration of the period of prescriptive acquisition, the prescription shall not be completed against such person for 6 (six) months after the time he/she becomes a person of full capacity or a legal representative is appointed.

#### Article 175.- Suspension of completion of prescriptive acquisition between minors or persons under general guardianship and their legal representatives

In cases where a legal representative of a minor or a person under general guardianship is to acquire by prescription the ownership of an immovable belonging to such minor or person under general guardianship, the prescriptive acquisition

the prescription shall not be completed for 6 (six) months after the time he/she becomes a person of full capacity or a new legal representative is appointed.

#### Article 176.- Suspension of completion of prescriptive acquisition between spouses

If one spouse is to acquire by prescription the ownership of an immovable belonging to the other spouse, the prescriptive acquisition shall not be completed for 6 (six) months after the marriage is dissolved.

#### Article 177.- Suspension of completion of prescriptive acquisition due to natural disaster

If the original owner cannot interrupt the prescription due to a natural disaster or other force majeure at any time within 6 (six) months before the expiration of the period of prescriptive acquisition, the prescription shall not be completed for 6 (six) months after such force majeure has ceased to exist.

#### Article 178.- Prescription for acquisition of rights related to immovables

1-A person who peacefully and publicly exercises a right related to an immovable, such as perpetual lease, usufruct, right of use, right of residence, lease, or pledge, with the intention to exercise it for him/herself, shall acquire such right after the expiration of a period of 20 (twenty) or 10 (ten) years in accordance with the classifications provided in Article 162 (Prescription for acquisition of ownership of immovables) of this Code. However, an easement may be acquired by prescription only if the requirements provided in Article 300 (Prescriptive acquisition of easement) of this Code are met.

as well.

2-The provisions from Article 163 (Retroactive effect of prescriptive acquisition) to Article 177 (Suspension of completion of prescriptive acquisition due to natural disaster) shall apply mutatis mutandis to the prescription for acquisition of rights provided in paragraph 1 above.

3-The provisions of paragraph 1 above shall not apply to immovables that are State property, regardless of their type.

#### Article 179.- Ownership of alluvion

Accretions of land that are formed successively and imperceptibly by the natural action of water on land adjacent to a river, stream, or canal shall belong to the owner of the land adjacent to such river, stream, or canal, regardless of whether boats or rafts can navigate or float on such river, stream, or canal. For rivers, streams, or canals where boats or rafts can navigate or float, the owner of the adjacent land must leave a path for towing or pushing boats or rafts in accordance with law or regulations.

#### Article 180.- Ownership of alluvion

If land increases due to the water current carrying alluvion from one bank and depositing it on the other bank progressively and naturally, the owner of the land to which the alluvion is attached shall benefit from such accretion. The owner on the other bank who loses land may not demand the return of the lost land.

#### Article 181.- Right to demand return of land washed away and attached to other land

Regardless of whether boats or rafts can navigate or float on a river, stream, or canal, if a large and identifiable piece of land is carried away by a sudden force of flood water from one bank and attached to
```

land downstream or on the other bank, the owner of the washed-away land may demand its return. This right of return must be exercised within 1 (one) year. However, this shall not apply if the owner to whose land the piece is attached has not yet taken possession of it.

#### Article 182.- Ownership of islands and alluvion

Islands and alluvion formed in the beds of rivers, streams, or canals where boats or rafts can navigate or float shall belong to the State.

#### Article 183.- Ownership of islands and alluvion

With regard to islands and alluvion formed in the beds of rivers, streams, or canals where boats or rafts cannot navigate or float, they shall belong to the owners of the banks on the side where the island or alluvion is formed. If the island or alluvion is not formed on one side, it shall belong to the owners of both banks, with the center line of the river, stream, or canal as the boundary.

#### Article 184.- Ownership of islands

If a river, stream, or canal forms a new course and cuts off land belonging to a riparian owner, thereby forming an island, such owner shall not lose ownership of the land even if the island is formed in a river, stream, or canal where boats or rafts can navigate or float.

#### Article 185.- Ownership of old riverbeds or streambeds due to creation of new watercourses

(Amended by the Law on Implementation of the Civil Code)

When a river, stream, or canal where boats or rafts can navigate or float forms a new course by abandoning its old bed, the riparian owners

may acquire ownership of the old bed, and the area of each person's ownership shall extend to the center line of the river, stream, or canal. The riparian owners must pay a price determined by an appraiser. The value of the old bed shall be determined by an appraiser appointed by the competent court upon the request of the municipal or provincial authority or interested parties. If a riparian owner does not express an intention to acquire ownership of the old bed, such land shall be sold at public auction by the municipal or provincial authority. The money paid by the riparian owners or the proceeds from the sale shall be distributed to the owners who lost land due to the creation of the new watercourse, in proportion to the area of land lost.

#### Article 186.- Attachment of movables to immovables

If a movable thing becomes a component part of an immovable by being attached to it, the ownership of the immovable shall extend to such movable thing, unless otherwise provided by law or special agreement. In this case, the person who lost ownership of the movable may demand compensation according to the provisions on unjust enrichment, but may not demand restitution.

### SUB-SECTION 2

### ACQUISITION OF OWNERSHIP OF MOVABLES

#### Article 187.- Acquisition of ownership of movables

Ownership of movables may be acquired by contract, succession, and other grounds specified in this Section 4 (Acquisition of ownership), as well as by other provisions of this Code or other laws.

#### Article 188.- Ownership of ownerless movables

A person who first possesses a ownerless movable with the intention to own it shall acquire ownership of such movable. However, this shall not apply if there are special provisions in laws or regulations concerning the protection of wildlife.

#### Article 189.- Ownership of stray animals, etc.

A person who in good faith possesses an animal or bird that is not a domestic animal or bird, and which was kept by another person, shall acquire ownership of such animal or bird unless a claim is made by the owner within 1 (one) month from the time the animal or bird strayed.

#### Article 190.- Ownership of fish in lakes or ponds

Fish living in a private lake or pond shall belong to the owner of such lake or pond.

#### Article 191.- Ownership of lost property

1-A person who finds lost property must return it to the owner if the owner is known. If the owner is not known, the finder must deliver the property to the chief of the police post within 7 (seven) days from the time it was found.

2-The chief of the police post must keep the found property and make a public announcement. If the owner is not found within 6 (six) months, the finder shall acquire ownership of the property. However, if the finder fails to deliver the property to the chief of the police post within 7 (seven) days from the time it was found, the finder may not acquire ownership of the property, and the property shall belong to the State. This provision shall also apply if the finder does not

take the found property within 2 (two) months after acquiring ownership of it.

3-If the found property is of a perishable nature, the chief of the police post may sell it and keep the proceeds. The proceeds shall be dealt with in the same manner as the found property.

4-If the found property is returned to the owner, the owner must pay the finder a reward of between 5 (five) and 20 (twenty) percent of the value of the property. The finder shall lose the right to demand the reward if he/she fails to make the demand within 1 (one) month after the property is returned to the owner.

#### Article 192.- Ownership of treasure trove

The provisions of Article 191 (Ownership of lost property) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to treasure troves, except for cases provided in Article 141 (Excavation of cultural heritage and minerals in the ground) of this Code. However, if a treasure trove whose owner is not known is discovered in a thing belonging to another person, the finder and the owner of the thing shall acquire ownership of the treasure trove in equal shares.

#### Article 193.- Bona fide acquisition of ownership of movables

A person who has received the transfer of possession of a movable under a valid contract to transfer ownership shall acquire ownership of such movable if he/she acted in good faith and without fault, even if the transferor did not have ownership of the movable. However, this shall not apply if the transferor continues to possess the thing through a possessory representative.

#### Article 194.- Transfer of stolen or lost property

1-In cases provided in Article 193 (Bona fide acquisition of ownership of movables) of this Code, if the thing transferred is stolen or lost property, the victim or the person who lost the property may demand from the transferee the return of the property within 2 (two) years from the time it was stolen or lost.

2-If the transferee bought the stolen or lost property in good faith at a public auction, in a public market, or from a merchant or dealer who sells things of the same kind, the victim or the person who lost the property may not demand the return of the property without reimbursing the transferee for the price he/she paid.

#### Article 195.- Prescription for acquisition of ownership of movables

1-A person who peacefully and publicly possesses a movable with the intention to own it for a period of 10 (ten) years shall acquire ownership of such movable.

2-A person who peacefully and publicly possesses a movable with the intention to own it for a period of 5 (five) years shall acquire ownership of such movable if the possession was in good faith and without fault at the time it commenced.

#### Article 196.- Prescription for acquisition of rights related to movables and other property rights

A person who peacefully and publicly exercises a right related to a movable, such as a pledge, lease, or any other property right, with the intention to exercise it for him/herself, shall acquire such right after the expiration of a period of 10 (ten) or 5 (five) years in accordance with the classifications provided in Article 195 (Prescription for

of ownership of movables) of this Code.

#### Article 197.- Mutatis mutandis application of provisions on prescription for acquisition of ownership of immovables

The provisions from Article 163 (Retroactive effect of prescriptive acquisition) to Article 177 (Suspension of completion of prescriptive acquisition due to natural disaster) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to Article 195 (Prescription for acquisition of ownership of movables) and Article 196 (Prescription for acquisition of rights related to movables and other property rights) of this Code.

#### Article 198.- Attachment and commingling of movables

1-If several movable things are attached to each other such that they cannot be separated without destroying them, the ownership of the composite thing shall belong to the owner of the principal movable thing. This provision shall also apply if the separation would involve excessive expense.

2-If a principal thing and an accessory thing cannot be distinguished among the attached movables, the owners of the respective movables shall own the composite thing in common in proportion to the value of each movable at the time they were attached.

3-The provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2 above shall apply mutatis mutandis if several movable things are commingled such that they cannot be separated.

#### Article 199.- Processing of movables

1-A person who creates a new movable thing by processing or modifying raw materials belonging to another person shall acquire ownership of the processed thing. However, if the increase in value caused by the processing or modification is significantly less than the value of the raw materials, the ownership of the processed

the processed thing shall belong to the owner of the raw materials.

2-In cases where the person who processed the thing provided a portion of the raw materials, such person shall acquire ownership of the processed thing only if the value of his/her raw materials plus the increase in value caused by the processing or modification exceeds the value of the raw materials belonging to the other person.

#### Article 200.- Effect of extinction of rights on movables

1-If ownership of a thing is extinguished due to attachment, commingling, or processing, any other rights existing over such thing shall also be extinguished.

2-Rights of third parties over a thing that belonged to a person who has become the owner of a composite thing, commingled thing, or processed thing shall continue to exist over the thing newly acquired. In this case, if the person becomes a co-owner, such rights shall continue to exist over his/her share.

#### Article 201.- Extinction of rights and right to demand compensation

A person who has lost a right due to attachment, commingling, or processing of movables may demand compensation from the person who has acquired ownership and benefited therefrom, in accordance with the provisions on unjust enrichment. However, such person may not demand restitution.

### SECTION 5

### OWNERSHIP IN COMMON

#### Article 202.- Definition of ownership in common

A situation where several persons own a single property in proportion to their respective shares is called ownership in common.

#### Article 203.- Equality of shares

The shares of the co-owners are presumed to be equal.

#### Article 204.- Disposition of shares

Each co-owner may transfer his/her share or provide it as security. A creditor of a co-owner may attach such share.

#### Article 205.- Use of property in common

Each co-owner may use the entire property in common in proportion to his/her share.

#### Article 206.- Preservation of property in common

Each co-owner may individually perform acts of preservation on the property in common.

#### Article 207.- Change in function or use of property in common

A co-owner may not dispose of or make substantial changes to the property in common without the consent of the other co-owners.

#### Article 208.- Management of property in common

Matters relating to the management of the property in common shall be decided by a majority of the co-owners in accordance with the value of their respective shares, except for cases provided in Article 206 (Preservation of property in common) and Article 207 (Change in function or use of property in common) of this Code.

#### Article 209.- Burden of property in common

1-Each co-owner shall bear the burden of management expenses, taxes,

duties, etc., in proportion to their respective shares.

2-If a co-owner pays management expenses or other burdens in excess of his/her own share, such co-owner may demand reimbursement from the other co-owners in proportion to their respective shares.

3-The right to demand reimbursement provided in paragraph 2 above may also be exercised against a person who has acquired the share of another co-owner.

#### Article 210.- Renunciation of share, etc.

If a co-owner renounces his/her share or dies without an heir, such share shall belong to the other co-owners.

#### Article 211.- Right to demand partition of property in common

1-Each co-owner may demand the partition of the property in common at any time. However, the co-owners may enter into an agreement not to partition the property for a period not exceeding 5 (five) years.

2-The agreement provided in paragraph 1 above may be renewed. However, the period of the renewed agreement may not exceed 5 (five) years.

#### Article 212.- Method of partition of property in common

If the co-owners cannot reach an agreement on the partition of the property, any co-owner may apply to the court for partition. In this case, the court may order the partition of the property in kind, or if

if there is a concern that partition in kind would cause a significant decrease in value, or if there is any other reasonable ground, the court may order the sale of the property at auction and the distribution of the proceeds in proportion to the shares, or order the transfer of one co-owner's share to another co-owner upon payment of compensation.

#### Article 213.- Claims related to property in common

1-If a co-owner has a claim against another co-owner in relation to the preservation, management, or other burdens on the property in common, such co-owner may satisfy the claim from the share of the debtor in the property in common at the time of partition.

2-If it is necessary to sell the debtor's share in the property in common in order to satisfy the claim provided in paragraph 1 above, the creditor may apply to the court for such sale.

#### Article 214.- Quasi-co-ownership

The provisions on co-ownership shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where several persons share property rights other than ownership. However, this shall not apply if otherwise provided by law or regulations.

### SECTION 6

### OWNERSHIP IN COMMON OF WALLS, ETC.

#### Article 215.- Definition of ownership in common of walls, etc.

A situation where the owners of adjacent lands have co-ownership of a wall or other structure such as a ditch, dike, or fence located on the boundary of the lands is called ownership in common of walls, etc. (adj-sithipheap).

buildings on the land is called ownership in common of walls, etc.

#### Article 216.- Common walls

A wall separating two buildings of different heights and a fence between gardens shall be presumed to be held in common to the extent of the lower building or the height of the fence.

#### Article 217.- Repair and reconstruction of common walls

The costs of repair and reconstruction of a common wall shall be borne by the co-owners of the wall in proportion to their respective shares. Any co-owner may be exempted from the burden of repair or reconstruction by renouncing his/her co-ownership interest. However, this shall not apply if the common wall is a part of a building.

#### Article 218.- Use of common walls

Each co-owner may insert beams or joists up to half the thickness of a common wall for the purpose of carrying out various works using the wall.

#### Article 219.- Work on common walls

1-A co-owner may not perform any work on or against a common wall that would cause damage to the common wall without the consent of the other co-owner.

2-In the case provided in paragraph 1 above, if the other co-owner refuses to give consent without a justifiable ground, the co-owner may apply to the court for a judgment in lieu of such consent.

3-In the case provided in paragraph 2 above, the court may issue a judgment in lieu of consent subject to the provision of security.

appropriate security.

#### Article 220.- Converting a wall that is not held in common into a common wall

An owner of land adjacent to a wall may make the wall a common wall by paying half the value of the wall and half the value of the land on which the wall is built.

#### Article 221.- Increasing the height of a common wall

A co-owner may increase the height of a common wall. However, the costs of such increase and the costs of maintenance of the part of the wall exceeding the original height shall be borne solely by the co-owner who increased the height.

#### Article 222.- Reconstruction of common walls, etc.

1-If a common wall cannot support the weight of an increased height, the person who wishes to increase the height must reconstruct the entire wall at his/her own expense. However, if the thickness of the wall is increased, the additional thickness must be on his/her own side.

2-A neighbor who did not contribute to the increase in height may acquire a co-ownership interest in the added part by paying half the cost of the addition and half the value of the land required for the increased thickness, if any.

#### Article 223.- Common fences

Fences located on the boundary of immovables shall be presumed to be held in common by the owners of the respective immovables.

#### Article 224.- Maintenance costs of fences

1-Common fences other than common walls shall be maintained at the common expense of the co-owners of the common fence.

2-A co-owner of a common fence may be exempted from the burden of costs by renouncing his/her co-ownership interest.

3-A co-owner of a ditch in which water flows may not renounce his/her co-ownership interest according to the provisions of paragraph 2 above.

#### Article 225.- Fences not held in common

An owner of an immovable adjacent to a fence other than a wall that is not held in common may not demand that the owner of such fence make it a common fence.

#### Article 226.- Common fences, etc.

1-A co-owner of a common hedge may remove the hedge up to the boundary of his/her own property. However, such co-owner shall be obligated to build a wall on the boundary.

2-The provisions of paragraph 1 above shall also apply to common ditches and dikes which serve only as fences.

# CHAPTER 3

# POSSESSORY RIGHTS

### SECTION 1

### GENERAL PROVISIONS

#### Article 227.- Definition of possession

1-Possession means the holding of a thing.

2-Holding means the factual control over a physical object, whether direct or indirect.

#### Article 228.- Direct and indirect possession

1-Possession may be exercised indirectly through another person.

2-In the case provided in paragraph 1 above, the person who holds the thing directly is called a direct possessor, while the person who holds the thing through another person is called an indirect possessor.

#### Article 229.- Transfer of possession

1-Possession is transferred by delivery of the thing possessed. This form of transfer of possession is called actual delivery (kar-pro-kol-daoy-chak-sdaeng).

2-Possession may be transferred by agreement of the parties alone, without actual delivery. In this case, the transferee of possession shall acquire indirect possession through the transferor. This form of transfer of possession is called possessory agreement (kar-p-dor-leak-kha-na-nei-kar-kan-kab).

3-If the person to receive the transfer of possession is already holding the thing directly, possession may be transferred by agreement of the parties alone. By this means, the transferor of possession loses the indirect possession he/she previously had through the person holding the thing. This form of transfer of possession is called summary delivery (kar-pro-kol-daoy-sang-kheb).

4-An indirect possessor who possesses through another person may transfer his/her possession to a third party by agreement with the third party and by notifying the direct possessor of such fact. This form of transfer of possession is called transfer of possession by instruction (kar-p-te-kar-kan-kab-daoy-ban-chea).

#### Article 230.- Extinction of possession

Possession is extinguished when the possessor loses the holding of the thing possessed. However, if the possessor is deprived of possession, and if he/she recovers the thing or files a lawsuit for recovery of the thing within 1 (one) year, his/her possession shall not be deemed to have been extinguished.

#### Article 231.- Extinction of indirect possession

1-Indirect possession shall be extinguished in any of the following cases:

(a) If the legal ground for holding for the indirect possessor or the obligation of the direct possessor to hold for the indirect possessor is extinguished;

(b) If an event occurs which is recognized as signifying that the direct possessor no longer intends to hold for the indirect possessor;

(c) If the direct possessor loses the holding of the thing.

2-In the case of deprivation of possession as provided in subparagraph (c) of paragraph 1 above, if the direct possessor or the indirect possessor recovers the thing or files a lawsuit for recovery of the thing within 1 (one) year, his/her possession shall not be deemed to have been extinguished.

#### Article 232.- Possession with the intention to own and possession without the intention to own

1-Possession is classified into possession with the intention to own (kar-kan-kab-daoy-mean-chhan-teak-yo-chea-kam-sa-thit) and possession without the intention to own (kar-kan-kab-daoy-kmean-chhan-teak-yo-chea-kam-sa-thit). Whether possession is with or without the intention to own shall be determined by the objective nature of the ground for acquisition of possession.

2-If the ground for acquisition of possession objectively indicates that the possessor does not have the intention to own, the possession shall not be changed to possession with the intention to own, unless the possessor expresses an intention to own to the person who entrusted the possession, or unless the possessor commences possession with the intention to own based on a new ground of acquisition.

#### Article 233.- Defective possession

(As amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code.)

1-Possession where the possessor knows that he/she has no right of possession is called bad faith possession (kar-kan-kab-daoy-tor-chor-rit), while possession where the possessor does not know that he/she has no right of possession is called good faith possession (kar-kan-kab-daoy-so-chor-rit). If the lack of knowledge is due to fault, it is called possession with fault (kar-kan-kab-daoy-mean-kom-hos).

2-Peaceful possession means possession without the use of violence. However, even if a person who commenced possession peacefully uses violence to defend his/her possession against unlawful infringement by a third party, the possession shall still be considered peaceful.

3-Public possession means possession that is not concealed, so that any person having a right over the thing possessed could know or see the possession.

4-Bad faith possession, good faith possession with fault, non-peaceful possession, and non-public possession are called defective possession (kar-kan-kab-daoy-mean-vi-kea-reak).

#### Article 234.- Presumptions

1-A possessor is presumed to possess with the intention to own.

2-A possessor is presumed to possess in good faith, peacefully, and

publicly.

3-If there is proof of possession at two different times, possession is presumed to have been continuous throughout the intermediate period.

4-A possessor is presumed to lawfully have the right that he/she exercises over the thing possessed.

#### Article 235.- Succession of possession

1-A successor in possession may at his/her option assert either his/her own possession only or his/her own possession together with that of his/her predecessor.

2-If the successor asserts the possession of his/her predecessor together with his/her own, he/she shall also succeed to any defects in the predecessor's possession. Consequently, if the predecessor's possession was defective, the successor's possession shall also be deemed defective even if his/her own possession was not, provided that both possessions are asserted together.

### SECTION 2

### RIGHTS TO DEMAND PROTECTION OF POSSESSION

#### Article 236.- Rights to demand protection of possession

A possessor may demand the recovery of the thing of which he/she has been deprived, or the cessation or prevention of disturbance of his/her possession, in accordance with the provisions of Article 237 (Right to demand recovery of thing possessed) to Article 241 (Relationship with right having legal ground) of this Code, regardless of whether the possession is direct or indirect.

#### Article 237.- Right to demand recovery of thing possessed

1-If a possessor is deprived of his/her possession, he/she may demand the recovery of the thing.

2-The right to demand recovery of the thing possessed may not be exercised against a person who has acquired the thing from the person who deprived the possessor of possession, a person who has been granted a pledge, or any other particular successor. However, if such successor knew or could have known of the fact of deprivation of possession, the possessor may demand the recovery of the thing from such successor.

3-A lawsuit for recovery of the thing possessed must be filed within 1 (one) year from the time of deprivation of possession.

#### Article 238.- Right to demand cessation of disturbance of possession

1-If a possessor's possession is disturbed, he/she may demand the cessation of such disturbance.

2-A lawsuit for cessation of disturbance must be filed during the disturbance or within 1 (one) year after the disturbance has ceased. However, if the thing possessed has suffered damage due to construction work, the lawsuit may not be filed if 1 (one) year has elapsed since the commencement of the work or if the work has been completed.

#### Article 239.- Compensation for damages

1-The provisions of Article 237 (Right to demand recovery of thing possessed) and Article 238 (Right to demand cessation of disturbance of possession) of this Code shall not preclude a claim for damages based on tort.

2-A lawsuit for compensation for damages arising from deprivation of possession must be filed within 1 (one) year from the time when the possession

possession was deprived.

3-A lawsuit for compensation for damages arising from disturbance of possession must be filed during the disturbance or within 1 (one) year after the disturbance has ceased. However, if the thing possessed has suffered damage due to construction work, the lawsuit may not be filed if 1 (one) year has elapsed since the commencement of the work or if the work has been completed.

#### Article 240.- Right to demand prevention of disturbance of possession

1-If there is a concern that a disturbance of possession may occur, the possessor may demand the prevention of such disturbance. However, the court may, in lieu of preventing the disturbance, require the other party to provide appropriate security.

2-A lawsuit for prevention of disturbance may be filed as long as the concern of disturbance exists. However, if there is a concern that the thing possessed may suffer damage due to construction work, the lawsuit may not be filed if 1 (one) year has elapsed since the commencement of the work or if the work has been completed.

#### Article 241.- Relationship with right having legal ground

1-A right that legally justifies the holding of a thing, such as ownership, perpetual lease, usufruct, pledge, or lease, is called a right having legal ground (sithipheap-nea-dor-nam-oy-mean-muol-het-ka-teak-yoat).

2-In an action for protection of possession, the other party may not assert a defense based on a right having legal ground.

3-An action based on possession and an action based on a right having legal

ground shall not preclude each other. In an action based on possession, the other party may file a counter-claim based on a right having legal ground.

4-An action based on possession shall not be decided based on grounds relating to the right having legal ground.

### SECTION 3

### SPECIAL PROTECTION FOR POSSESSORS OF IMMOVABLES

#### Article 242.- Protection of possessors of immovables with holding certificates

(As amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code.)

1-A possessor who continuously uses and enjoys the fruits of an immovable and who has received a certificate of holding of immovable property or a certificate of holding of use of land, but whose title has not yet been registered as required for full ownership due to the lack of a cadastral map system and land register, shall be deemed to be an owner for the purposes of exercising actions based on real rights.

2-In the case provided in paragraph 1 above where the possessor allows a third party to use and enjoy the fruits of the immovable property, such possessor shall still be deemed to be an owner for the purposes of exercising actions based on real rights.

3-A person who has received the transfer of possession along with a certificate of holding of immovable property or a certificate of holding of use of land from the possessor provided in paragraph 1 above shall also be deemed to be an owner for the purposes of exercising actions based on real rights.

#### Article 243.- Protection of possessors of immovables from before the enactment of the Land Law

1-A possessor who has continuously, peacefully, and without contestation possessed an immovable that may be legally owned by a private individual for at least 5 (five) years prior to the date of enactment of the Land Law, but who failed to register his/her possession as required under the Land Law, may exercise an action for protection of possession against any infringement by a third party.

2-A possessor who commenced possession of an immovable prior to the enactment of the Land Law and who has received permission from the competent authority to continue possession for the purpose of acquiring ownership may exercise an action for protection of possession against any infringement by a third party.

3-For the purpose of an action for protection of possession under paragraphs 1 and 2 above, the period of 1 (one) year provided in Article 237 (Right to demand recovery of thing possessed) to Article 240 (Right to demand prevention of disturbance of possession) of this Code shall be interpreted as 3 (three) years.

# CHAPTER 4

# PERPETUAL LEASES

#### Article 244.- Definition of perpetual lease

A perpetual lease is a long-term lease of an immovable for a term of 15 (fifteen) years or more.

#### Article 245.- Creation of perpetual lease

1-A perpetual lease shall not be effective unless it is created in writing.

2-A perpetual lease that was created without a written document

title shall be deemed a lease without a fixed term and may be terminated by either party at any time in accordance with the provisions of Article 615 (Request for rescission of lease without a fixed term) of this Code.

#### Article 246.- Requirements for perfection of perpetual lease

1-A perpetual leaseholder may not assert the perpetual lease against a third party unless it is registered.

2-A registered perpetual lease may be asserted against a transferee of ownership of the land which is the subject of the perpetual lease, even if the ownership of the land has been transferred.

3-With respect to an unregistered perpetual lease, the provisions of Article 598 (Requirements for perfection of lease of immovable) of this Code shall apply during the period of 15 (fifteen) years.

#### Article 247.- Term of perpetual lease

1-The term of a perpetual lease may not exceed 50 (fifty) years. If a perpetual lease is created with a term exceeding 50 (fifty) years, it shall be shortened to 50 (fifty) years.

2-A perpetual lease may be renewed. However, the renewed term may not exceed 50 (fifty) years from the date of renewal.

#### Article 248.- Rent

1-A perpetual leaseholder shall pay rent to the lessor at the time specified in the contract.

2-If there is no specified time for payment of rent, the perpetual leaseholder shall pay

#### Article 249.- Right to demand increase or decrease of rent

If the rent is no longer appropriate due to changes in circumstances, either party may petition the court to increase or decrease the rent to an appropriate amount.

#### Article 250.- Right of termination by perpetual lessor

If a perpetual leaseholder fails to pay rent for 3 (three) years, the lessor may terminate the perpetual lease.

#### Article 251.- Right of termination by perpetual leaseholder

If the perpetual leaseholder fails to receive any profit from the immovable property for 3 (three) years due to unforeseen circumstances or force majeure, or if the annual profit expected from the immovable property in the future cannot exceed the rent for one year due to partial damage to the immovable property, the perpetual leaseholder may terminate the perpetual lease.

#### Article 252.- Assignment, etc. of perpetual lease

1-A perpetual lease may be assigned or otherwise disposed of, for or without consideration.

2-A perpetual leaseholder may sublease the subject of the perpetual lease.

3-A perpetual lease may be inherited.

#### Article 253.- Real right claim of perpetual leaseholder

A perpetual leaseholder may exercise the same rights to demand recovery of a thing, to demand cessation of disturbance, and to demand prevention of disturbance against an infringement of the perpetual lease as an owner.

#### Article 254.- Termination of perpetual lease

1-Upon termination of a perpetual lease, the lessor may not demand that the perpetual leaseholder restore the immovable property to its original condition, unless the perpetual leaseholder has destroyed the immovable property or fundamentally changed its nature.

2-Upon termination of a perpetual lease, the lessor shall acquire ownership of any improvements or structures made by the perpetual leaseholder on the immovable property without having to pay compensation to the perpetual leaseholder.

3-The parties may make special agreements that differ from the provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2 above. However, such agreements may not be asserted against a third party unless they are registered.

#### Article 255.- Mutatis mutandis application of provisions on lease

The provisions relating to lease shall apply mutatis mutandis to any matters relating to perpetual leases that are not provided for in this Chapter 4 (Perpetual Leases).

# CHAPTER 5

# USUFRUCT

#### Article 256.- Definition of usufruct

1-Usufruct (phal-lo-pa-phok) means the right to use and enjoy the fruits of an immovable property owned by another person for a specified period of time, which may not exceed the life of the usufructuary.

2-A usufructuary has the right to use the immovable property that is the subject of the usufruct according to its purpose, and has the right to enjoy the natural fruits and civil fruits arising from such immovable property.

#### Article 257.- Usufruct created by agreement and usufruct created by law

1-A usufruct shall be created by agreement of the parties or by operation of law.

2-The provisions of this Chapter 5 (Usufruct) shall also apply to usufructs created by operation of law, unless otherwise provided by law.

3-A usufruct created by operation of law shall prevail over a usufruct created by agreement, unless otherwise provided by law.

#### Article 258.- Creation of usufruct

1-A usufruct may be created with or without a written document.

2-The owner may at any time request the termination of a usufruct that was not created in writing. If the request for termination does not specify the time of termination, or if the period from the time of the request for termination to the time of termination specified in the request is shorter than the period specified below, the usufruct shall be terminated when the following period has elapsed from the time of the request for termination:

(a) 3 (three) months for a building;

(b) 1 (one) year for land.

3-In the case of a usufruct on land with a harvest season, the request for termination must be made before the usufructuary begins agricultural work for the season following the previous season.

#### Article 259.- Requirements for perfection of usufruct

1-A usufruct may not be asserted against a third party unless it is registered.

2-A registered usufruct may be asserted against an assignee of the immovable property which is the subject of the usufruct, even if the ownership of such immovable property has been assigned.

#### Article 260.- Period of existence of usufruct

1-A usufruct may exist for a specified period or until the occurrence of a specified event.

2-If the period of existence of a usufruct is not specified, the usufruct shall be deemed to exist until the death of the usufructuary.

#### Article 261.- Right to natural fruits

1-Natural fruits existing on the land at the time of the creation of the usufruct shall belong to the usufructuary.

2-Natural fruits existing on the land at the time of the extinction of the usufruct shall belong to the owner of the land.

3-In the cases specified in paragraphs 1 and 2 above, no relationship of unjust enrichment shall arise in respect of labor, etc., used to produce such natural fruits.

4-Notwithstanding the provisions of paragraph 1 above, in case there is a leasehold right that can be asserted against the land which is the subject of the usufruct, the provisions of paragraph

of paragraph 1 above, this shall not affect the right of such lessee to collect natural fruits.

5-Notwithstanding the provisions of paragraph 2 above, in respect of natural fruits which have been nurtured by the lessee until the extinction of the usufruct, the lessee who has leased the land from the usufructuary shall have the right to collect such natural fruits.

#### Article 262.- Right to civil fruits

Civil fruits shall belong to the usufructuary in proportion to the period of existence of the usufruct.

#### Article 263.- Assignment, etc. of usufruct

The usufructuary may assign the usufruct or otherwise dispose thereof, with or without consideration.

#### Article 264.- Lease of immovable property which is the subject of usufruct

1-The usufructuary may make the immovable property that is the subject of the usufruct the subject of a lease for a term not exceeding 3 (three) years.

2-The term of the lease specified in paragraph 1 above may be renewed. However, such term may not exceed 3 (three) years.

3-If the usufruct is extinguished, a lease of the immovable property that is the subject of the usufruct may not be asserted against the owner of the immovable property.

#### Article 265.- Right to demand based on real right of usufructuary

A usufructuary may exercise the right to demand restitution, the right to demand removal of disturbance, and the right to demand prevention of disturbance against an infringement of the usufruct, in the same manner as the owner.

#### Article 266.- Duty of usufructuary to notify

1-If a third party asserts a right over the immovable property which is the subject of the usufruct, the usufructuary shall notify the owner of the immovable property of such fact without delay. However, this provision shall not apply in cases where the owner is already aware of such fact.

2-The usufructuary shall bear the duty to compensate for damages incurred by the owner due to the usufructuary's negligence in giving such notification.

#### Article 267.- Demand for extinction of usufruct

1-In the event that the usufructuary commits an act contrary to the nature of the usufruct, such as causing damage to or failing to properly maintain the immovable property which is the subject of the usufruct, the owner of such immovable property may demand the court to order the extinction of the usufruct.

2-The demand for extinction of the usufruct specified in paragraph 1 above shall not preclude a claim for damages based on tort against the usufructuary.

#### Article 268.- Extinction of usufruct by death of usufructuary or expiration of term

A usufruct shall be extinguished upon the death of the usufructuary or upon the expiration of the term specified in the contract creating the usufruct.

#### Article 269.- Effect of extinction of usufruct

1-If a usufruct is extinguished, the owner of the immovable property may not demand that the usufructuary restore it to its original condition, except in cases where the usufructuary has destroyed the immovable property or substantially changed its character.

2-If the usufruct is extinguished, the owner of the immovable property may acquire ownership of improvements or structures, etc., that the usufructuary has made to the immovable property without having to pay compensation to the usufructuary or his/her successors.

3-The parties may make special agreements different from paragraphs 1 and 2 above. However, such agreements may not be asserted against third parties unless they are registered.

#### Article 270.- Destruction of object of usufruct
