1-If the object of a usufruct is only a building on land and such building is destroyed, the usufructuary may not exercise the usufruct over the land.

2-If the subject of a usufruct is both land and building and only the building is destroyed, the usufruct over the land shall remain in effect.

#### Article 271.- Allocation of expenses

1-The expenses for general maintenance and repair of the immovable property which is the subject of the usufruct shall be borne by the usufructuary, while the expenses for major repairs shall be borne by the owner. However, in the event that the necessity for major repairs has arisen due to the usufructuary's failure to perform maintenance and repair, such expenses shall be borne by the usufructuary.

2-In this Article, "major repairs" refers to the complete replacement of the roof, roof beams, and foundations, and the reconstruction of the roof, while other repairs are considered maintenance and repair.

3-In the event that the immovable property which is the subject of the usufruct is damaged or destroyed by force majeure, neither the owner nor the usufructuary shall be responsible for its reconstruction or have any other liability in respect of such damage or destruction.

#### Article 272.- Responsibility for taxes and duties and insurance premiums

During the term of the usufruct, the usufructuary shall bear the duty to pay taxes and duties on the immovable property which is the subject of the usufruct, and the insurance premiums for such immovable property under insurance contracts concluded by the owner before the creation of the usufruct.

#### Article 273.- Mutatis mutandis application of provisions on perpetual leases

The provisions of Article 248 (Rent) and Article 249 (Right to demand increase or decrease of rent) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where the usufructuary is obligated to pay rent periodically.

### Chapter 6
### Right of Use and Right of Residence

#### Article 274.- Definitions of right of use and right of residence

1-The "right of use" refers to the right to personally collect fruits of an immovable property to the extent of the needs of the holder of the right of use and his/her family.

2-The "right of residence" refers to the right to occupy a portion of a building to the extent of the housing needs of the holder of the right of residence and his/her family.

#### Article 275.- Right of use and right of residence created by contract and right of use and right of residence created by law

1-A right of use and a right of residence shall be created by agreement between the parties or by the provisions of law.

2-The provisions of Chapter 6 (Right of Use and Right of Residence) shall also apply to a right of use and a right of residence created by the provisions of law.

except as otherwise provided by law.

3-A right of use and a right of residence created by law shall have priority over a right of use and a right of residence created by agreement, except as otherwise provided by law.

#### Article 276.- Creation of right of use and right of residence

1-A right of use and a right of residence may be created with or without a document in writing.

2-The owner may demand the extinction of a right of use or a right of residence that has not been created in writing at any time. In the event that the demand for extinction does not specify the time of extinction, or if the period from the time of the demand for extinction until the time of extinction specified in such demand is less than 3 (three) months, the right of use or the right of residence shall be extinguished when 3 (three) months have elapsed from the time of the demand for extinction.

#### Article 277.- Requirements for perfection of right of use and right of residence

1-A holder of a right of use or a right of residence may not assert his/her right against a third party unless he/she actually uses or resides in the property.

2-A right of use or a right of residence may be asserted against an assignee even if ownership of the immovable property which is the subject of such right has been assigned, provided that the holder of such right actually uses or resides in the property.

#### Article 278.- Period of existence of right of use and right of residence

1-A right of use or a right of residence may exist for a specified period or until the occurrence of a specified event.

2-If no period of existence is specified for the right of use or the right of residence, the usufruct shall be deemed to exist

the right of use or the right of residence shall be deemed to exist until the death of the holder of such right.

#### Article 279.- Inalienability of right of use and right of residence

A right of use or a right of residence may not be assigned or made the subject of any other real right.

#### Article 280.- Liability for damages caused by holder of right

A holder of a right of use or a right of residence shall be liable for damages caused to the owner if such holder causes damage to the immovable property which is the subject of such right through his/her negligence.

#### Article 281.- Duty to pay taxes, duties and insurance premiums

If the holder of a right of use or a right of residence uses the entire immovable property or occupies the entire building, such holder shall bear the responsibility for taxes, duties, and insurance premiums in the same manner as a usufructuary. If such holder uses only a portion of the immovable property or occupies only a portion of the building, he/she shall bear such responsibility in proportion to the portion used or occupied.

#### Article 282.- Mutatis mutandis application of provisions on usufruct

The provisions concerning usufruct shall apply mutatis mutandis to the right of use and the right of residence, except as otherwise provided in this Chapter 6 (Right of Use and Right of Residence).

### Chapter 7
### Perpetual Lease

specified in paragraph 1 above shall not preclude a claim for damages based on tort against the holder of the right of use or the right of residence.

#### Article 283.- Extinction of right of use and right of residence by death of holder or expiration of term specified in contract creating right of use or right of residence

A right of use or a right of residence shall be extinguished upon the death of the holder of the right of use or the right of residence, or upon the satisfaction of the conditions specified in the contract creating the right of use or the right of residence.

#### Article 284.- Allocation of expenses

1-If a holder of a right of use or a right of residence collects all the fruits from the land or occupies the entire building, such holder shall have the duty to bear the expenses for repairs and maintenance, taxes and duties, and insurance premiums, in the same manner as a usufructuary.

2-If a holder of a right of use or a right of residence collects only a portion of the fruits or occupies only a portion of the building, such holder shall have the duty to bear the expenses specified in paragraph 1 above in proportion to such portion.

### Chapter 7
### Easements

#### Section 1
#### General Provisions

#### Article 285.- Definition of easement

1-An "easement" refers to the right to use the land of another for the benefit of one's own land in accordance with the purposes specified in the contract creating it. However, an easement with contents contrary to public order may not be created.

2-The land of another that provides the benefit is called the "servient land," while the land that receives the increased benefit is called the "dominant land."

3-A perpetual lessee of land or a usufructuary may create an easement by making such land the dominant land.

#### Article 286.- Creation of easement

1-An easement may be created with or without a document in writing.

2-The owner of the servient land may demand the extinction of an easement that was not created in writing at any time. In the event that the demand for extinction does not specify the time of extinction, or if the period from the time of the demand for extinction until the time of extinction specified in the demand is less than 1 (one) month, the easement shall be extinguished when 1 (one) month has elapsed from the time of the demand for extinction.

#### Article 287.- Requirements for perfection of easement

1-An easement may not be asserted against a third party unless it is registered.

2-A registered easement may be asserted against an assignee of the servient land.

#### Article 288.- Scope of persons entitled to benefit from easement

In addition to the owner of the dominant land, a lessee, a perpetual lessee, a usufructuary, or a holder of a right of use or a right of residence on the dominant land for whom the easement was created may also benefit from the easement. However, this provision shall not apply if otherwise provided in the contract creating the easement.

#### Article 289.- Appurtenance of easement

1-An easement shall be assigned along with the assignment of the ownership of the dominant land. However, this provision shall not apply if otherwise provided in the contract creating the easement.

2-An easement may not be assigned or made the subject of other rights separately from the dominant land.

#### Article 290.- Obligations of owner of servient land

1-The owner of the servient land may not obstruct the exercise of the easement or commit any act that diminishes the use of such easement.

2-The owner of the servient land may not substitute another piece of land for the servient land originally agreed upon without the consent of the holder of the easement. However, if maintaining the original servient land causes serious prejudice to the owner of the servient land due to changes in circumstances after

the creation of the easement, the owner of the servient land may substitute another piece of land for the original servient land provided that such land provides the same utility to the dominant land. In cases where the holder of the easement does not consent, such person may demand the court for a judgment or order to substitute for the consent.

#### Article 291.- Obligations of holder of easement

(As amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code.)

1-The holder of an easement may not use the servient land beyond the extent specified in the contract creating it. An easement to take water created at a source includes the right of way over the land containing the source.

2-The holder of an easement may not make any changes to the servient land or the dominant land which may have a serious impact on the condition of the servient land.

3-If the holder of an easement acts in violation of the first sentence of paragraph 1 and paragraph 2 above, the owner of the servient land may demand the court for the extinction of the easement.

4-The demand for extinction specified in paragraph 3 above shall not preclude a claim for damages based on tort against the holder of the easement.

5-In the event that a royalty is specified for the easement, if the holder of the easement fails to pay the specified royalty, the owner of the servient land may demand the court for the extinction of the easement.

#### Article 292.- Right of holder of easement to construct works

1-A person entitled to an easement may construct works necessary for the exercise of the easement on the servient land. The holder of the easement shall have the duty to remove such works and restore the servient land to its original condition when the easement is extinguished.

2-The owner of the servient land may use the works constructed on such land for the exercise of the easement, provided that such use does not obstruct the exercise of the easement.

3-In the case specified in paragraph 2 above, the owner of the servient land shall bear the expenses for the construction and maintenance of such works in proportion to the benefit received by such owner.

#### Article 293.- Allocation of expenses for construction of works

1-The owner of the dominant land shall bear the expenses for construction and maintenance of works necessary for the exercise of the easement, unless otherwise provided.

2-Where there is a special agreement providing that the owner of the servient land shall bear the expenses for construction and maintenance of works, if such special agreement is registered, an assignee of the ownership of the servient land shall also bear such obligation.

#### Article 294.- Right to demand based on real right of holder of easement

A holder of an easement may exercise the right to demand restitution, the right to demand removal of disturbance, and the right to demand prevention of disturbance against an infringement of the easement, in the same manner as the owner.

#### Article 295.- Relinquishment and transfer of ownership of servient land to holder of easement

1-Where there is a special agreement providing that the owner of the servient land shall bear the expenses specified in Article 293 (Allocation of expenses for construction of works) of this Code, the owner of the servient land may be released from such obligation by relinquishing and transferring the ownership of the portion of land necessary for the easement to the holder of the easement at any time.

2-The relinquishment and transfer shall be a unilateral declaration of intention to abandon the ownership of the servient land

of the owner of the servient land and place such ownership under the holder of the easement.

#### Article 296.- Specification of period of existence of easement

1-If a period of existence is specified in the contract creating the easement, the easement shall be extinguished upon the expiration of such period.

2-If no period of existence is specified in the contract creating the easement, the owner of the servient land may demand the court for the extinction of the easement. The court shall decide whether to grant or deny the demand for extinction by considering all circumstances, such as the circumstances of creation, the period that has elapsed, and the existence or non-existence of a royalty, etc.

#### Article 297.- Extinction of easement by total destruction of dominant land

An easement shall be extinguished if the dominant land is totally destroyed.

#### Article 298.- Extinction of easement by co-owner

1-A co-owner of the dominant land may not cause the easement to be extinguished in respect of his/her share.

2-A co-owner of the servient land may not cause the easement to be extinguished in respect of his/her share.

#### Article 299.- Partition of land or assignment of a part and the easement

1-If the dominant land is partitioned or a part thereof is assigned, the easement shall continue to exist for each part. However, if according to the nature of the easement it only relates to one part of the dominant land, the easement shall not continue to exist for the other parts.

2-If the servient land is partitioned or a part thereof is assigned, the easement shall continue to exist over each part. However, if the easement

relates to only one part of the servient land, the easement shall not continue to exist over the other parts.

#### Section 2
#### Easements and Prescription

#### Article 300.- Acquisitive prescription of easement

1-Only easements that are continuous and apparent may be acquired by acquisitive prescription.

2-A "continuous easement" refers to an easement that exists due to the location of the place without requiring any human act, providing a benefit to the dominant land and imposing a burden on the servient land without interruption.

3-An "apparent easement" refers to an easement that exists in a state where it is manifested through structures or other external signs.

#### Article 301.- Acquisitive prescription of easement by a co-owner of dominant land

1-If one of the co-owners of the dominant land acquires an easement by prescription, the other co-owners shall also acquire such easement.

2-Interruption of prescription against co-owners shall not be effective unless it is made against each co-owner who exercises the easement.

3-In the event that there are several co-owners exercising the easement, even if there is a cause for suspension of prescription against one of them, the prescription shall continue to run in favor of each of the other co-owners.

#### Article 302.- Commencement of extinctive prescription of easement

The period of extinctive prescription specified in Article 500 (Extinctive prescription of property rights other than claims or ownership) of this Code shall, in the case of a non-continuous easement, commence from the time that the easement was last exercised, and in the case of a continuous easement, from the time that an event obstructing the exercise of the easement has occurred.

#### Article 303.- Interruption or suspension of extinctive prescription of easement in case of co-ownership of dominant land

If the dominant land is co-owned by several persons, an interruption or suspension of prescription in favor of one of them shall also be effective in favor of the other co-owners.

#### Article 304.- Extinction of a part of easement by prescription

If the holder of an easement fails to exercise a part of his/her right, such part shall be extinguished by extinctive prescription.

#### Article 305.- Acquisitive prescription by possessor of servient land and effect on easement

1-If a possessor of the servient land satisfies the requirements for acquisitive prescription of ownership, the easement shall be extinguished.

2-If the holder of the easement exercises his/her right during the period of possession required for acquisitive prescription of ownership, the possessor shall acquire the ownership of the land subject to the easement.

### Chapter 8
### Ownership and Other Real Rights of the State, Buddhist Monasteries, Indigenous Communities and Other Communities

#### Article 306.- Ownership and other real rights of the State, Buddhist monasteries, indigenous communities and other communities

The provisions of the Civil Code shall apply to ownership and other real rights of the State, Buddhist monasteries, indigenous communities and other communities, except as otherwise provided by special laws or customs.

### Chapter 9
### Rights Created by Land Concession

#### Article 307.- Rights created by concession

The provisions concerning perpetual leases in the Civil Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to rights over land created by concession, to the extent of the conditions of the concession, except where otherwise provided by special laws.

## Book 5
## Obligations

### Chapter 1
### General Provisions

#### Section 1
#### Sources of Obligation and Definitions of Terms

#### Article 308.- Definition of obligation

1-The term "obligation" refers to a legal relationship that binds a specific person to perform a specific duty to another specific person.

2-A specific person who owes the duty is called a "debtor," and a specific person who is entitled to the benefit of the duty is called a "creditor."

3-A creditor shall have the right to demand performance of the duty from the debtor.

#### Article 309.- Sources of obligation

1-An obligation may arise from a contract, a unilateral legal act, management of affairs without mandate, unjust enrichment, a tort, or the provisions of law.

2-An obligation that arises from a contract or a unilateral legal act is an obligation created by the intention of a party.

3-An obligation that arises from management of affairs without mandate, unjust enrichment, a tort, or the provisions of law is an obligation created by law. The provisions from Chapter 3 (Performance of Contract) through Chapter 7 (Extinction of Obligation) shall apply mutatis mutandis to obligations created by law.

by law.

#### Article 310.- Definition of declaration of intention

1-A "declaration of intention" refers to a declaration made by a party with the intent to create a legal effect.

2-A declaration of intention shall become effective from the time that notice thereof reaches the other party.

#### Article 311.- Definition of contract

A "contract" refers to the agreement of intentions of two or more persons to create, modify, or extinguish an obligation.

#### Article 312.- Definition of unilateral legal act

1-A "unilateral legal act" refers to an act that creates, modifies, or extinguishes an obligation by a declaration of intention of one party to dispose of property or by exercising a right acquired under a contract or the provisions of law.

2-The provisions of Section 2 (Defects in Declaration of Intention and Validity of Contract) and Section 4 (Agency) of Chapter 2 shall apply mutatis mutandis to unilateral legal acts.

#### Section 2
#### Types and Modalities of Obligations

#### Article 313.- Types of obligations

In addition to the purpose of transferring ownership or a right of possession over a thing or money, an obligation may have the purpose of performing or not performing a specific act.

#### Article 314.- Obligation to deliver specific thing

With respect to an obligation to deliver a specific thing, the debtor shall have the duty to preserve such thing with the care of a good manager until it is delivered.

#### Article 315.- Obligation to deliver thing specified by kind

1-If the subject of an obligation is a thing specified by kind and the things to be delivered have several different qualities, the debtor shall deliver a thing of the quality specified by the parties. If the parties have not specified the quality, the debtor shall deliver a thing of average quality.

2-With respect to an obligation to deliver things specified by kind, once the debtor has identified the things to be delivered and has completed the acts necessary for delivery, the debtor shall be obligated to deliver only those things.

#### Article 316.- Monetary obligation

1-If the subject of an obligation is money, the debtor may make payment in any currency of his/her choice. However, the creditor and the debtor may agree on payment in a specific currency.

2-If the specific currency specified as the subject of the obligation is no longer legal tender at the time of performance of the obligation, the debtor may make payment in another currency.

3-The provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2 above shall apply mutatis mutandis where the delivery of a foreign currency is the subject of the obligation.

#### Article 317.- Obligation in foreign currency

1-If the amount of an obligation is specified in a foreign currency, the debtor may make payment in the currency of the Kingdom of Cambodia calculated at the foreign exchange rate at the place of performance at the time of performance. However, if there is a special agreement between the creditor and the debtor to the contrary, such agreement shall be followed.

2-If the debtor is in default in making payment in the currency of the Kingdom of Cambodia, the creditor may demand payment from the debtor calculated at the foreign exchange rate at the time of performance or at the time of actual payment. However, if there is a special agreement between the creditor and the debtor to the contrary, such agreement shall be followed.

#### Article 318.- Legal interest rate

With respect to an obligation that bears interest, if there is no agreement to the contrary, the interest rate shall be 5% (five percent) per annum.

#### Article 319.- Compound interest

If the payment of interest is in default for 1 (one) year or more, and if the debtor fails to pay such interest despite a demand by the creditor, the creditor may add such interest to the principal.

#### Article 320.- Alternative obligation

If the subject of an obligation is to be determined by a choice from among several types of performance, the right to choose shall belong to the debtor. However, the parties to a contract may agree to grant the right to choose to the creditor or a third party.

#### Article 321.- Exercise of right of choice

1-A right of choice shall be exercised by notice to the other party. In cases where the debtor has the right of choice, the delivery of the thing chosen by the debtor shall be deemed as the exercise of such right of choice. In cases where a third party has the right of choice, such right shall be exercised by notice to both the debtor and the creditor.

2-The choice made shall have retroactive effect from the time the obligation arose.

#### Article 322.- Transfer of right of choice

1-In cases where the obligation has become due, even if the debtor or the creditor who has the right of choice has received a demand to exercise such right within a reasonable period determined by the other party but fails to do so within that period, the right of choice shall be transferred to the other party.

2-In cases where a third party has the right of choice, even if such third party has received a demand to exercise such right within a reasonable period determined by the debtor or the creditor but fails to do so within that period, the right of choice shall be transferred to the debtor.

#### Article 323.- Determination of alternative obligation by impossibility

1-If the performance of any of the several acts of performance that are the subject of an alternative obligation becomes impossible from the time the contract was formed or subsequent to the formation of the contract without any fault of the parties, the subject of the obligation shall be the remaining acts of performance. The provisions of paragraph 2 of Article 321 (Exercise of right of choice) shall also apply to such cases.

2-If a party who has the right of choice is at fault for the impossibility of

performance, the right of choice shall be transferred to the other party.

3-If the debtor who does not have the right of choice is at fault for the impossibility of performance, the party who has the right of choice may choose the performance that has become impossible and may demand compensation for damages from such debtor.

4-If the creditor who does not have the right of choice is at fault for the impossibility of performance, the debtor who has the right of choice may choose the performance that has become impossible and may be released from the obligation.

#### Article 324.- Claims and obligations with multiple parties

1-In cases where there are several creditors for a single obligation, in the absence of any other manifestation of intention, each creditor shall have a right in equal shares.

2-In cases where the performance of an obligation is indivisible by its nature or by the manifestation of intention of the parties, if there are several creditors, each creditor may demand performance for the benefit of all the creditors, and the debtor may perform for any creditor for the benefit of all the creditors.

3-With respect to an obligation provided in paragraph 2 above, no act of any indivisible creditor or any event occurring with respect to any indivisible creditor shall have effect on other indivisible creditors. In cases where the debtor has entered into a settlement with or has received a release from any indivisible creditor, if the debtor performs the whole or a part of the obligation based on the demand of another indivisible creditor, the debtor may demand reimbursement of the benefit that the first-mentioned indivisible creditor would have received if such creditor had not lost his/her claim.

4-Joint and several obligations and other cases where there are several debtors shall be governed by the provisions from Article 921 (Meaning of joint and several obligation) to Article 937 (Indivisible obligation and obligation with characteristics similar to joint and several obligation and indivisible obligation).

### Section 3
### Conditions, Time of Maturity, and Periods

#### Part 1
#### Conditions

#### Article 325.- Meaning of condition

1-Parties to a contract may attach a condition to the formation or the termination of the effect of such contract. With respect to a unilateral act, the party may attach a condition only if it does not improperly prejudice the other party.

2-A condition is an event that occurs in the future and is uncertain.

3-In cases where a suspensive condition is attached to an obligation or a right arising from a contract or a unilateral act, the effect of such obligation or right shall arise from the time the suspensive condition is satisfied. In cases where a resolutory condition is attached to an obligation or a right arising from a contract or a unilateral act, the effect of such obligation or right shall terminate at the time the resolutory condition is satisfied.

#### Article 326.- Disposition of right subject to condition

A right subject to a condition may be the subject of succession or disposition...

...disposed of. Obligations subject to a condition shall be succeeded to in accordance with the provisions on succession and other provisions.

#### Article 327.- Infringement of right subject to condition

1-While it is not yet certain whether a condition has been satisfied or not, neither party to an obligation subject to a condition shall infringe upon the benefits that the other party would receive upon the satisfaction of such condition.

2-In cases where a third party infringes upon the benefits provided in paragraph 1 above, if such act constitutes a tort, such third party shall be liable for damages.

#### Article 328.- Prevention of satisfaction of condition

If a party for whom the satisfaction of a condition would be disadvantageous intentionally prevents the satisfaction of such condition, the other party may deem such condition as having been satisfied.

#### Part 2
#### Time of Maturity

#### Article 329.- Meaning of time of maturity

1-In cases where a time of commencement is attached to the effect of a contract, the effect shall not arise until such time has arrived. In cases where a time of commencement is attached to the performance of a contract, the creditor may not demand performance of the obligation by the debtor before such time has arrived.

2-In cases where a time of termination is attached to the effect of a contract, the effect...

...the effect shall terminate when such time has arrived.

3-With respect to a unilateral act, a party may attach a time of commencement within the meaning of paragraph 1 above or a time of termination within the meaning of paragraph 2 above only if it does not improperly prejudice the other party.

#### Article 330.- Benefit of time of maturity

1-A time of maturity shall be presumed to be for the benefit of the debtor.

2-The benefit of a time of maturity may be waived; provided, however, that this may be done only if it does not improperly prejudice the other party. If such waiver causes damage to the other party, the party who made the waiver shall be liable for such damage.

#### Article 331.- Forfeiture of benefit of time of maturity

The debtor shall forfeit the benefit of the time of maturity in the following cases:

(a) The debtor is declared bankrupt;

(b) The debtor has damaged the security;

(c) The debtor has failed to furnish security that he/she was bound to furnish;

(d) An event that the parties agreed upon has occurred.

#### Part 3
#### Periods

#### Article 332.- Method of determining period

A period may be determined by hours, minutes, seconds, days, weeks, months, or years.

#### Article 333.- Calculation of period by hours, minutes, or seconds

If a period is determined by hours, minutes, or seconds, the period shall be calculated from the time it begins until the time it ends.

#### Article 334.- Calculation of period by days, weeks, months, or years

1-If a period is determined by days, weeks, months, or years, the first day of the period shall not be included in the calculation; provided, however, that this shall not apply if the period begins at midnight.

2-In cases provided in paragraph 1 above, the period shall expire at the end of the last day of such period.

3-If the last day of the period is a public holiday, Sunday, or any other holiday determined by law or regulation, the period shall expire at the end of the first working day following such holiday.

#### Article 335.- Calculation of period according to solar calendar

1-In cases where a period is calculated by weeks, months, or years, it shall be calculated according to the solar calendar.

2-If a period is not calculated from the beginning of a week, month, or year, the period shall expire on the day of the last week, month, or year that precedes the day corresponding to the day on which the period began to run. However, if a period is calculated by months or years and there is no corresponding day in the last month, the period shall expire on the last day of that month.

# Chapter 2
# Manifestation of Intention and Contract

### Section 1
### Formation of Contract

#### Article 336.- Formation of contract by offer and acceptance

1-A contract is formed by the agreement of an offer and an acceptance.

2-Notwithstanding the provisions of paragraph 1 above, a contract in which a party assumes an obligation to transfer the ownership of an immovable property or to create ownership of an immovable property shall be effective only if it is made by an authentic act.

#### Article 337.- Definitions of offer and acceptance

1-An offer is a proposal to enter into a contract made with the intention to be bound and a legal intent in the event that the other party accepts such proposal.

2-An offer shall become effective at the time it reaches the other party. However, an offer shall not become effective if a notice of revocation of the offer reaches the other party simultaneously with or prior to the time the offer reaches the other party.

3-An acceptance is a manifestation of intention by the person to whom the offer was made to agree to such offer.

4-An acceptance shall become effective at the time it reaches the offeror. However, an acceptance shall not become effective if a notice of revocation of the acceptance reaches the offeror simultaneously with or prior to the time the acceptance reaches the offeror.

#### Article 338.- Offer with fixed period for acceptance and revocation thereof

1-An offer may be made by fixing a period for acceptance. The period...

...for acceptance shall be calculated from the day the offer is notified, unless the offeror clearly specifies otherwise.

2-An offer that is made by fixing a period for acceptance may not be revoked.

3-If the offeror does not receive a notice of acceptance within the period provided in paragraph 1 above, the offer shall automatically lapse upon the expiration of such period. If a notice of rejection of the offer reaches the offeror within the period for acceptance, the offer shall also lapse.

#### Article 339.- Offer without fixed period for acceptance and revocation thereof

1-With respect to an offer made without fixing a period for acceptance to a party in direct communication in a situation where an immediate answer can be given, if the party receiving the offer does not accept it immediately, the offer shall lapse.

2-With respect to an offer made to a party not in direct communication without fixing a period for acceptance, the offeror may not revoke such offer within a reasonable period.

3-A revocation of an offer shall be effective only if notice of such revocation reaches the other party before the other party dispatches a notice of acceptance.

#### Article 340.- Time of formation of contract: Principle of receipt of acceptance

A contract shall be formed at the time a notice of acceptance reaches the offeror.

#### Article 341.- Delayed reach of acceptance

1-In cases where a notice of acceptance for an offer with a fixed period...

...fixed for acceptance reaches the other party after the expiration of the period for acceptance, if the offeror is in a position to know that the other party sent the notice of acceptance at such time that it would normally have reached the offeror within the period for acceptance, the offeror shall dispatch notice of the delay to the acceptor without delay. In cases where the offeror neglects to dispatch notice of the delay, the delayed acceptance shall be valid.

2-The offeror may treat an acceptance that reached him/her after the expiration of the period for acceptance as a new offer.

#### Article 342.- Acceptance with modification

1-An acceptance that is conditional or has modifications to any material terms of the offer shall not be a valid acceptance but shall be a new offer. A contract shall be formed when the original offeror accepts such new offer.

2-If the conditions or modifications attached to the acceptance do not modify material terms of the offer, the acceptance shall be valid unless the offeror objects immediately. The meaning of the contract formed by such acceptance shall be in accordance with the meaning of the acceptance, unless the offeror who received the acceptance objects immediately.

#### Article 343.- Formation of contract by act recognized as acceptance

In cases where no notice of acceptance is necessary by reason of the offeror's manifestation of intention or commercial usage, a contract shall be formed at the time of an act that can be recognized as an acceptance.

#### Article 344.- Offer to unspecified persons

An offer made to unspecified persons by means of public advertisement...

...or by any other means, may be revoked only by the same means used for the offer.

### Section 2
### Defective Manifestation of Intention and Validity of Contract

#### Article 345.- Defective manifestation of intention

A person who made a manifestation of intention may rescind the contract on the ground that the manifestation of intention is defective, in accordance with the provisions of this Section 2 (Defective Manifestation of Intention and Validity of Contract) and Section 3 (Nullity and Rescission) in any of the following cases:

(a) The manifestation of intention was made under a mistake;

(b) The manifestation of intention was made under fraud, duress, or misrepresentation by the other party;

(c) The manifestation of intention was made through an act of exploitation with the intent to obtain excessive gain.

#### Article 346.- Mistake

1-In cases where a party made a mistake as to an essential point of the contract, if the other party was in a position to know of such mistake, the party who made the mistake may rescind the contract on the ground that the manifestation of intention is defective.

2-In cases where a party to a contract made a mistake as to a point of the contract that he/she considered essential or a point that he/she considered essential for the formation of the contract, if the other party was in a position to know of the importance of such point and of the mistake of the person making the manifestation of intention, the party who made the mistake may rescind the contract on the ground that the manifestation of intention is defective.

3-In cases where both parties made a mistake as to an essential point of the contract, or a point of the contract that both parties considered essential, or a point that both parties considered essential for the formation of the contract, even if one party was not in a position to know of the mistake of the other party, a party who made the mistake may rescind the contract on the ground that the manifestation of intention is defective.

4-Rescission of a contract on the ground of mistake may be asserted against a third party other than the other party to the contract. However, such rescission may not be asserted against a third party who has acted in good faith and without negligence regarding the mistake.

#### Article 347.- Fraud

1-A person who made a manifestation of intention because of the fraud of the other party may rescind the contract on the ground that the manifestation of intention is defective.

2-In cases where a manifestation of intention was made because of the fraud of a third party, the person making the manifestation of intention may rescind the contract on the ground that the manifestation of intention is defective only if the other party to such manifestation of intention knew or was in a position to know of such fraud.

3-Rescission of a contract on the ground of fraud may be asserted against a third party other than the other party to the contract. However, such rescission may not be asserted against a third party who has acted in good faith and without negligence regarding the fraud.

#### Article 348.- Misrepresentation

1-At the time a contract is formed, if a fact represented by one party is false, and the other party made a manifestation of intention believing such fact to be true, and if such party would not have made the manifestation of intention if he/she had known that the fact was false, such party may rescind the contract on the ground that the manifestation of intention is defective.

...defective.

2-The provisions of paragraph 1 above shall not preclude a party who made a manifestation of intention from demanding compensation for damages from a person who made a misrepresentation, if such person was at fault.

3-Rescission of a contract on the ground of misrepresentation may be asserted against a third party other than the other party to the contract. However, such rescission may not be asserted against a third party who has acted in good faith and without negligence regarding the misrepresentation.

#### Article 349.- Abuse of situation
(As amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code)

1-At the time a contract is formed, if a party to the contract has improperly used his/her superior economic or social position or has improperly used any other situation in which the other party is unable to resist, the other party may rescind the contract on the ground that the manifestation of intention is defective.

2-Rescission of a contract based on the provisions of paragraph 1 above may not be asserted against a third party who has acted in good faith and without negligence regarding the abuse of situation.

#### Article 350.- Duress

A person who made a manifestation of intention because of duress by the other party or a third party may rescind the contract on the ground that the manifestation of intention is defective.

#### Article 351.- Exploitation

In cases where a party to a contract has exploited the other party's economic difficulties, ignorance, or lack of experience, and has obtained an excessive gain from the contract, the other party may rescind the contract on the ground that the manifestation of intention is defective.

#### Article 352.- Mental reservation

The validity of a manifestation of intention shall not be affected by the fact that the person making the manifestation of intention knew that it was different from his/her true intention. However, if the other party knew that the manifestation of intention was different from the true intention of the person making the manifestation of intention, such person may refuse to perform the contract.

#### Article 353.- Fictitious manifestation of intention

1-A contract based on a fictitious manifestation of intention made in collusion with the other party shall be void.

2-The nullity of a contract provided in paragraph 1 above may not be asserted against a third party who has entered into a legal relationship based on such contract. However, this shall not apply if the third party acted in bad faith or was grossly negligent in not knowing of the fictitious manifestation of intention.

#### Article 354.- Illegality or violation of public order and good customs in the meaning of contract

1-Even if the parties to a contract made manifestations of intention without any defect, the contract shall be void in the following cases:

(a) The meaning of the contract violates any mandatory provision of law;

(b) The meaning of the contract violates public order or good customs.

2-If a part of the meaning of a contract violates a mandatory provision of law or is against public order or good customs, but the remaining parts would not frustrate the reasonable expectations of the parties, only the parts that violate the mandatory provision or are against public order or good customs shall be void.

#### Article 355.- Initial impossibility

1-Even if the performance of the contract was impossible at the time the contract was formed, the contract shall not be void on the ground of such impossibility. This, however, shall not preclude a party from rescinding the contract on the ground of mistake, provided the conditions for mistake are satisfied.

2-In cases where a party who knew or should have known that the performance of the contract was impossible at the time the contract was formed causes damage to the other party by forming the contract, such party shall be liable for damages in accordance with the provisions of Article 398 (Conditions for liability for damages) of this Code.

### Section 3
### Nullity and Rescission

#### Article 356.- Definition of "act"

The term "act" in this Section 3 (Nullity and Rescission) refers to...

...a contract or a unilateral act.

#### Article 357.- Meaning of nullity

If the meaning of an act violates a mandatory provision of law or is against public order or good customs, any person may assert the nullity of such act. In cases where an act is void because it fails to comply with the formalities required by this Code or other laws, any person may also assert the nullity of such act.

#### Article 358.- Voidable acts

1-A voidable act shall be valid until it is rescinded by a person who has the right to rescind it.

2-If a person who has the right to rescind a voidable act rescinds it, the act shall be deemed void ab initio.

3-If a person who has the right to rescind a voidable act ratifies the act, the act shall become valid and non-rescindable.

#### Article 359.- Persons having right to rescind

1-With respect to an act that is voidable on the ground that the manifestation of intention is defective as provided from Article 346 (Mistake) to Article 351 (Exploitation) of this Code, the person who made the defective manifestation of intention or his/her legal representative may rescind the act. A successor of the person who made the manifestation of intention or a person who succeeds to the status in the contract from such person may also exercise the right to rescind.

2-With respect to rescission on the ground of incapacity to make a manifestation of intention, minority, or other limited capacity, the person him/herself, his/her legal representative...

...legal representative or curator may rescind the act. A successor or universal legatee of the person him/herself may also exercise the right to rescind.

#### Article 360.- Method of rescission or ratification

In cases where the other party to a voidable act is identified, the rescission or ratification of the act shall be made by notice to such other party. The rescission or ratification shall be effective at the time the notice reaches the other party.

#### Article 361.- Persons having right to ratify and conditions for ratification

1-A person who has the right to rescind may ratify a voidable act. However, in cases where an act is voidable on the ground of a defective manifestation of intention, a person who has the right to rescind may ratify the act only after he/she becomes aware of the ground for rescission. With respect to an act performed by a person with limited capacity, the person who has the right to rescind may ratify the act only after the ground for rescission has ceased to exist.

2-The provisions of the third sentence of paragraph 1 above shall not apply in cases where the legal representative or curator of a person with limited capacity ratifies the act.

#### Article 362.- Constructive ratification

If any of the following events occurs after the time when ratification could be made under the provisions of Article 361 (Persons having right to ratify and conditions for ratification) above, the voidable act shall be deemed to have been ratified:

(a) Total or partial performance of an obligation arising...

...from a voidable act or the provision of security for such obligation;

(b) The exercise of a right acquired from a voidable act or a demand for performance made to the other party;

(c) The assignment or other disposition of all or part of a right acquired from a voidable act.

However, this provision shall not apply if a reservation was made that ratification was not being granted at the time any of the events specified in the three items above occurred.

#### Article 363.- Prescription of right of rescission

1-If a person who has the right to rescind does not exercise such right within three (3) years from the time that he/she becomes able to ratify, the right of rescission and the right to demand return for enrichment without cause arising from such rescission shall be extinguished by prescription. The provisions regarding suspension of prescription shall not apply to this period.

2-Notwithstanding the provisions of paragraph 1 above, the right of rescission shall also be extinguished if ten (10) years have elapsed from the date of the act.

3-In cases where multiple persons have the right to rescind the same act, the provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2 above shall apply to the right of rescission of each such person.

### Section 4
### Agency

#### Article 364.- Definition of agency

"Agency" refers to a legal relationship in which the effects of a contract shall be attributed directly to the principal in cases where the agent enters into a contract with another party by indicating that the contract is being made on behalf of the principal within the scope of the agent's authority.

#### Article 365.- Creation of agency authority

Agency authority is created by a contract between the principal and the agent, or by operation of law.

#### Article 366.- Scope of agency authority

1-The scope of agency authority shall be determined by the contract that grants such authority to the agent. In cases where agency authority is created by operation of law, its scope shall also be determined by such law.

2-In cases where the scope of agency authority is not clearly defined, the agent shall have authority to perform acts of preservation and acts of improvement. However, this provision shall not apply to acts of improvement that change the nature of the object or right that is the subject of the agency.

#### Article 367.- Limitations on agency authority

1-Even if an act is within the scope of agency authority, the agent shall have no authority to perform such act if there is a conflict of interest between the agent and the principal. However, this provision shall not apply in cases where the principal has given consent.

2-In transactions between the principal and the agent, it shall be presumed that a conflict of interest exists between the principal and the agent.

3-The provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2 above shall also apply to cases where an agent acts as representative of both the principal and the other party, and there is a conflict of interest between the principal and such other party.

#### Article 368.- Grounds for termination of agency authority

1-Agency authority granted by the principal by contract shall terminate on the following grounds:
(a) Death, bankruptcy, or dissolution of the principal;
(b) Death, bankruptcy, or limitation of capacity of the agent;
(c) Termination of the mandate, employment contract, or other legal relationship that was the basis for the grant of agency authority.

2-Agency authority created by operation of law shall terminate on the grounds provided by such law.

#### Article 369.- Unauthorized agency

1-An act performed by a person as the agent of another person without agency authority shall not be effective as to the principal. However, this provision shall not apply in cases where the principal ratifies such act.

2-The other party to an act performed by an unauthorized agent may not assert that the effects of the act should be attributed to the unauthorized agent. However, this provision shall not preclude the other party from demanding that the unauthorized agent be held liable in accordance with the provisions of Article 371 (Liability of unauthorized agent) of this Code.

#### Article 370.- Ratification of unauthorized act and right of rescission of the other party

1-The principal may not assert the effects of a ratification against the other party unless the principal has given notice of the ratification to the other party.

2-A ratification of an unauthorized act shall have retroactive effect From the time the act was performed. However, such ratification shall not prejudice the interests of a third party that arose during the period from the performance of the unauthorized act until the time of ratification.

3-The other party to an unauthorized act may notify the principal to respond as to whether he/she ratifies the act within a reasonable period fixed by the other party. If the principal fails to respond within such period, he/she shall be deemed to have refused to ratify the act.

4-The other party may rescind a contract entered into by an unauthorized agent before the principal ratifies it. After the other party has rescinded the contract, the principal may not ratify the unauthorized act.

#### Article 371.- Liability of unauthorized agent

If a person who entered into a contract as an agent for another person is unable to prove his/her agency authority or to obtain ratification from the principal, such person shall be liable for performance of the contract or for damages, at the choice of the other party. However, if the other party knew of the absence of agency authority, the unauthorized agent shall be released from such liability. An unauthorized agent who was without fault in performing the unauthorized act may also be released from the liability provided in this Article, even if the other party did not...

...know of the absence of agency authority due to his/her negligence.

#### Article 372.- Apparent agency

1-In cases where an agent enters into a contract exceeding the scope of his/her agency authority, if the other party believed that the agent had authority to enter into such contract and was not negligent in so believing, the principal shall be liable for the performance of the contract to the other party.

2-If, after the termination of agency authority, the other party enters into a contract with a person who represents himself/herself as an agent, the principal shall be liable for the performance of the contract to the other party. However, this provision shall not apply in cases where the other party knew or was negligent in not knowing that the agency authority had terminated.

3-In cases where the principal has not granted agency authority to another person, but the principal has acted in such a manner as to suggest that he/she has granted agency authority to such person, or has permitted such person to represent himself/herself as an agent, the principal shall be liable for the performance of the contract to the other party. However, this provision shall not apply in cases where the other party knew or was negligent in not knowing of the absence of agency authority.

#### Article 373.- Undisclosed agency

1-In cases where an agent intends to act for a principal but enters into a contract without disclosing that he/she is acting for the principal, the other party may assert that the contract was formed with the agent. However, if the other party knew at the time of the formation of the contract that the agent was acting for the principal, the other party may assert only that the contract was formed with the principal.

2-If the other party later discovers the existence of the principal after entering into a contract with an agent who did not disclose that he/she was acting for the principal, the other party may assert that the contract was formed with the principal.

#### Article 374.- Defects in agency acts

1-In cases where the effects of a contract formed by a manifestation of intention are affected by mistake, fraud, duress, or by knowledge or negligent lack of knowledge of a certain fact, the existence or non-existence of such grounds shall be determined with respect to the agent.

2-In the application of the provisions of paragraph 1 above, if the principal has instructed the agent to enter into a specific contract, the principal may not assert that the agent was unaware of a fact of which the principal was aware. This provision shall also apply to facts of which the principal was unaware due to his/her negligence.

#### Article 375.- Capacity of agent

The principal may grant agency authority to a person with limited capacity. In such case, the principal may not rescind a contract entered into by an agent with limited capacity with the other party on the ground of the agent's limited capacity.

#### Article 376.- Sub-agency

An agent appointed by contract with the principal may not appoint a sub-agent. However, this provision shall not apply in cases where the principal has given consent or there is an unavoidable reason.

#### Article 377.- Liability of agent who appointed sub-agent

1-When an agent appoints a sub-agent in accordance with the provisions of paragraph 2 of Article 376 (Sub-agency) above, the agent shall be liable to the principal for the selection and supervision of the sub-agent.

2-In cases where an agent appoints a sub-agent as designated by the principal, the agent shall not be liable for the acts of the sub-agent unless the agent knew of the sub-agent's unsuitability or untrustworthiness and failed to notify the principal thereof or to remove the sub-agent.

3-If an agent appoints a sub-agent without complying with the provisions of paragraph 2 of Article 376 (Sub-agency) above, the acts of the sub-agent shall not be binding on the principal. However, the other party who did not know that the sub-agent lacked authority may hold the agent liable for performance or for damages if the other party was not negligent in not knowing such fact.

#### Article 378.- Status of sub-agent

1-Acts performed by a sub-agent within the scope of his/her authority by indicating that they are performed on behalf of the principal shall be binding on the principal.

2-A sub-agent shall have the same rights and duties toward the principal as those of an agent.

3-The provisions of this Section 4 shall also apply to the relationship between the principal and the sub-agent, by treating the sub-agent as an agent.

### Section 5
### Contract for the Benefit of a Third Party

#### Article 379.- Definition

1-The parties to a contract may agree that a third party shall acquire a right arising from the contract. In such case, the party who is to perform for the third party is called the "promisor," and the other party is called the "promisee." The third party who is to receive the performance is called the "beneficiary."

2-The beneficiary may directly demand performance from the promisor.

#### Article 380.- Beneficiary

At the time of making a promise for the benefit of a third party, the beneficiary need not exist if the conditions for identifying the beneficiary are clear. However, if no beneficiary exists at the time the promisor is to perform, the promise shall lapse, and the promisor may be released From liability by performing for the promisee.

#### Article 381.- Manifestation of intention to receive benefit

1-Until the beneficiary has manifested his/her intention to receive the benefit to the promisor or the promisee, the promisee may revoke the promise to grant the right to the beneficiary at any time by notifying both the promisor and the beneficiary.

2-After the beneficiary has manifested his/her intention to receive the benefit, the parties...

...of the contract may not change or extinguish the right of the beneficiary.

#### Article 382.- Promisor's defenses

The promisor may assert against the beneficiary any defenses arising from the contract.

#### Article 383.- Nullity, rescission and termination of contract

1-In cases where there are grounds for nullity or rescission of the contract between the promisee and the promisor, the parties to the contract may assert such nullity or rescission in accordance with the provisions regarding nullity and rescission in Section 3 (Nullity and Rescission) of Chapter 2, notwithstanding that a promise has been made for the benefit of a third party.

2-The promisor may terminate the contract on the ground of a breach of contract by the promisee. In cases where the promisee seeks to terminate the contract on the ground of a breach of contract by the promisor, if there is a beneficiary who has manifested his/her intention to receive the benefit, the promisee may terminate the contract only after obtaining the consent of such third party.

## Chapter 3
## Performance of Contract

#### Article 384.- Debtor's duty to perform obligation

1-A debtor shall perform his/her obligation in accordance with the terms of the contract and the principle of good faith and fair dealing.

2-Performance in accordance with the provisions of paragraph 1 above shall be deemed a payment that extinguishes the obligation.

#### Article 385.- Creditor's right to demand performance of obligation

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code.)

1-A creditor may demand the debtor to perform the obligation either out-of-court or through judicial proceedings.

2-Notwithstanding the provisions of paragraph 1 above, a creditor may not demand performance of an obligation through judicial proceedings in cases where there is an agreement between the parties to the contract not to seek judicial performance.

3-Notwithstanding the limitation on the right to demand judicial performance in accordance with paragraph 2 above, if the debtor has performed the obligation voluntarily, the creditor may receive and retain such performance as a payment.

#### Article 386.- Defense of simultaneous performance

Each party to a bilateral contract may refuse to perform its own obligation until the other party performs its obligation. However, this provision shall not apply in cases where the obligation of the other party is not yet due.

#### Article 387.- Defense of insecurity

A party who is obligated to perform first in a bilateral contract may refuse to perform its own obligation if there is a significant concern that the other party will not perform the obligation in accordance with its terms. However, this provision shall not apply in cases where the other party provides security or takes other measures to extinguish such concern.

#### Article 388.- Application of provisions regarding payment

In addition to the provisions of this Chapter 3 (Performance of Contract), the provisions regarding payment set forth in Section 1 (Payment) of Chapter 7 shall also apply to the performance of contracts.

## Chapter 4
## Remedies for Breach of Contract

### Section 1
### General Provisions Regarding Non-performance

#### Article 389.- Definition and forms of non-performance

"Non-performance of an obligation" refers to cases where a debtor fails to perform an obligation arising From a contract. The forms of non-performance are as follows:
(a) The debtor fails to perform the obligation by the time it is due (delay in performance);
(b) Impossibility of performance;
(c) The debtor fails to perform the obligation completely in accordance with its terms;
(d) Any other cases where there is no performance in accordance with the terms of the obligation.

#### Article 390.- Remedies for non-performance

In cases where there is non-performance of an obligation by a debtor, the creditor may demand compulsory performance, damages, or termination of the contract, in accordance with the provisions of Sections 2 through 4 of this Chapter 4.

#### Article 391.- Delay in performance

A debtor shall be deemed to be in delay of performance in cases where...

...he/she fails to perform the obligation at the time specified below:
(a) If a fixed time for performance of the obligation is specified, at the arrival of such time;
(b) If a non-fixed time for performance of the obligation is specified, at the time the debtor becomes aware of the arrival of such time;
(c) If no time for performance of the obligation is specified, at the time the debtor receives a demand for performance.

#### Article 392.- Impossibility of performance

1-Performance of an obligation shall be considered impossible if performance is no longer possible. Such impossibility shall include cases where performance is considered impossible according to social or economic standards.

2-If it is certain that performance will be impossible at the time it is due, performance shall be considered impossible at such time.

#### Article 393.- Incomplete performance

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code.)

"Incomplete performance" refers to cases where a debtor has performed the obligation, but the performance is incomplete, or cases where only a part of the obligation has been performed, or any other cases where performance has not been completely carried out in accordance with the terms of the obligation.

#### Article 394.- Other cases of non-performance

In addition to the provisions of Article 391 (Delay in performance), Article 392 (Impossibility of performance) and Article 393 (Incomplete performance)...

...of this Code, cases where there is a breach of any other duty arising From a contract shall also be treated as non-performance of an obligation.

#### Article 395.- Multiple remedies

In cases where multiple remedies are available to a creditor, the creditor may choose one or more of such remedies, provided that such remedies are not inconsistent with each other.

### Section 2
### Compulsory Performance

#### Article 396.- Judicial order for performance

If a debtor fails to perform an obligation voluntarily, the creditor may demand compulsory performance, except in cases where the nature of the obligation does not permit compulsory performance. The procedure for compulsory performance shall be in accordance with the Code of Civil Procedure.

#### Article 397.- Relationship between demand for performance and other remedies

In cases where a debtor fails to perform an obligation, the creditor may demand damages in lieu of performance, or in addition to performance, to the extent that such demands are not inconsistent.

### Section 3
### Damages

#### Article 398.- Conditions for liability for damages

1-In cases where a debtor fails to perform an obligation, the creditor may demand...

...damages arising From such non-performance. However, if the debtor proves that he/she was not at fault for the non-performance, the debtor shall be released from liability for damages.

2-In cases where a debtor uses a third party as a performing assistant, the debtor shall not be released From liability for damages unless the debtor proves that he/she was not at fault in the selection and supervision of the assistant and that the assistant was also not at fault.

#### Article 399.- Special provisions for monetary obligations

1-With respect to an obligation for the payment of money, even if the debtor proves that the delay in performance was due to force majeure, the debtor shall not be released From the liability to pay interest for the period of delay. The interest for the period of delay shall be calculated at the legal interest rate. If the interest rate agreed upon in the contract is higher than the legal interest rate, the interest shall be calculated at the contract rate.

2-With respect to damages exceeding the amount of interest for the period of delay, if the debtor proves that he/she was not at fault for the non-performance, the debtor shall be released From liability for such damages.

#### Article 400.- Scope of damages

1-A creditor may demand damages which include the benefits of performance that the creditor would have received under the contract, and in addition, the creditor may also demand compensation for useless expenses incurred due to the non-performance and for any expenses or burdens increased due to the non-performance, to the extent that...

...overlap with compensation for the benefit of performance.

2-A court may also order a debtor to pay compensation for mental suffering based on a demand by the creditor. The amount of compensation for mental suffering shall be determined by the court, taking into account all surrounding circumstances.

#### Article 401.- Scope of damages

A debtor shall compensate for damages as follows:
(a) Ordinary damages that would normally arise From non-performance of the obligation;
(b) Special damages arising From special circumstances, if the parties to the contract could have foreseen such damages at the time of the formation of the contract. However, this provision shall not apply if the parties to the contract did not take such special damages into account at the time of the formation of the contract.
(c) In cases where the debtor fails to perform the obligation with malicious intent or by an act of extreme bad faith, the court may order the debtor to pay to the creditor the profits that the debtor acquired From the act constituting the non-performance, notwithstanding the provisions of items (a) and (b) above.

#### Article 402.- Grounds for reduction of damages

1-If the non-performance of an obligation or the damages are partially caused by the fault of the creditor, the court may reduce the amount of damages to be paid by the debtor according to the degree of such fault.

2-If damages have occurred because the creditor was negligence in mitigating the damages, the court may reduce the amount of damages...

...reduction in the amount of damages equivalent to the amount that the creditor could have mitigated.

#### Article 403.- Liquidated damages and other related matters

1-The creditor and the debtor may agree in advance on the conditions for and the amount of damages.

2-A special agreement that releases the debtor in advance From liability for non-performance arising From his/her intentional act or gross negligence shall be void.

3-If the parties have agreed in advance on the amount of damages, the court may not increase or decrease such amount. However, if the amount agreed upon in advance is excessively high or excessively low compared to the damages determined in accordance with the provisions of Article 401 (Scope of damages) above, the court may increase or decrease the agreed amount.

4-An agreement on liquidated damages shall not preclude a demand for performance or for termination of the contract. In cases where the creditor terminates the contract and demands damages in lieu of the subject matter of the contract, the amount of liquidated damages agreed upon in advance for damages arising From delay in performance shall not be binding on the parties.

5-A penalty for breach of contract shall be presumed to be liquidated damages.

#### Article 404.- Principle of monetary compensation

A debtor shall pay damages for non-performance of an obligation in money. However, this provision shall not apply in cases where...

...unless the parties have agreed otherwise by special agreement.

#### Article 405.- Subrogation by compensation

If a creditor has received damages representing the full value of the object or right which was the subject of the claim, the debtor shall be subrogated to the position of the creditor with respect to such object or right by operation of law.

#### Article 406.- Prescription of right to demand damages

The right to demand damages arising From non-performance of an obligation shall be extinguished by prescription if not exercised within five (5) years From the time such damages occurred.

### Section 4
### Termination of Contract

#### Article 407.- Termination on ground of non-performance

A party to a bilateral contract may immediately terminate the contract if the other party commits a serious breach of contract.

#### Article 408.- Serious breach of contract

1-A "serious breach of contract" refers to a case where a party cannot achieve the purpose of the contract due to the other party's breach. A breach of contract shall be deemed to be a serious breach in the following cases:
(a) A party has notified the other party who failed to perform the obligation by its due date to perform within a reasonable fixed period, and the other party fails to perform within such period;
(b) A party fails to perform the obligation by the time specified in the contract, in cases where the purpose of the contract cannot be achieved unless the obligation is performed at such time...

...time.
(c) A party is unable to perform its primary obligation;
(d) The breach of contract is clear, causing a loss of trust between the parties and making it impossible to believe that the obligation will be performed in the future.

2-A party who has breached the contract may not prevent termination based on the provisions of paragraph 1 above by claiming that he/she was not at fault for the non-performance.

#### Article 409.- Exercise of right of termination

1-A party who has a right of termination may terminate the contract by manifesting his/her intention to the other party. Such manifestation of intention may be made out-of-court.

2-A manifestation of intention to terminate may not be revoked.

3-A manifestation of intention to terminate may be subject to a suspensive condition.

#### Article 410.- Termination where one party consists of multiple persons

1-In cases where one party consists of multiple persons, termination of the contract shall be made by all such persons against the other party, or by the other party against all such persons.

2-In the case set forth in paragraph 1 above, if the right of termination is extinguished with respect to one of the persons comprising the party, it shall also be extinguished with respect to the others.

#### Article 411.- Effects of termination

1-Termination of a contract shall release the parties From their obligations under the contract, except for the obligation to pay damages.

2-A party who has received performance under the contract in whole or in part shall have the duty to return such performance to the other party as restitution upon termination. In cases where both parties have the duty of restitution, such duties shall be performed simultaneously.

3-A party who has the duty to return money upon termination of a contract shall pay interest From the date of receipt of the principal. A party who has the duty to return an object or other performance received From the other party upon termination shall also return any fruits received From such object or performance.

4-Termination of a contract shall not prejudice the legitimate interests of third parties.

#### Article 412.- Right of termination where subject matter of contract is damaged or lost

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code.)

1-The right of termination shall be extinguished if the party having the right of termination intentionally causes damage to or loss of the object which is the subject matter of the contract, or intentionally makes it impossible to return the object, or intentionally changes the object into one of a different kind Through processing or transformation.

2-If a party has exercised the right of termination, such party shall pay in money the value of the object which is the subject matter of the contract, up to the amount of the contract price.

3-In cases where the other party is at fault or is otherwise responsible for the damage to, loss of, or inability to return the object which is the subject matter of the contract, the party having the right of termination shall not be under the duty to pay in money the value of the object as set forth in paragraph 2 above.

#### Article 413.- Prescription of right of termination

The right to terminate a contract on the ground of non-performance and the right to demand restitution shall be extinguished by prescription if not exercised within five (5) years From the time of the non-performance.

#### Article 414.- Contractual right of termination and termination by agreement

1-The parties to a contract may agree that one or both parties shall have a right of termination. The provisions of Articles 409 (Exercise of right of termination) through 412 (Right of termination where subject matter of contract is damaged or lost) regarding statutory termination shall apply mutatis mutandis to the method and effects of exercising a contractual right of termination, unless otherwise agreed.

2-Even if no right of termination is specified in the contract, the parties may terminate the contract by mutual agreement. However, such termination shall not prejudice the interests of third parties.

## Chapter 5
## Burden of Risk

#### Article 415.- Extinguishment of obligation by impossibility

If performance of an obligation becomes impossible and the debtor is not at fault for such impossibility, the obligation shall be extinguished, and the creditor may no longer demand performance.

#### Article 416.- Burden of risk in contracts for transfer of ownership of specified objects

1-In bilateral contracts for the transfer of ownership of a specified object, if the object is lost or damaged without the fault of either party, the debtor shall bear the risk and may not demand counter-performance From the creditor.

2-Unless otherwise provided in the contract, the risk of loss of or damage to the object shall be transferred to the creditor at any of the following times:
(a) When the object is delivered to the creditor, or when the transfer of ownership of immovable property is registered, or when it is otherwise deemed that actual control over the object has been transferred to the creditor;
(b) When the debtor has made a proper tender of performance;
(c) When the creditor has refused to receive the debtor's performance without a justifiable reason.

#### Article 417.- Burden of risk for other rights

The provisions of Article 416 (Burden of risk in contracts for transfer of ownership of specified objects) shall apply mutatis mutandis to the creation or transfer of real rights other than ownership, claims, or other rights.

#### Article 418.- Cases where the object is unspecified

1-In bilateral contracts for the transfer of ownership of an unspecified object, if the object to be delivered has not yet been specified, the obligation shall not be extinguished even if the object prepared by the debtor for performance is lost without the debtor's fault, and the debtor remains under the duty to perform.

2-After the object to be delivered has been specified, the provisions of Article 416 (Burden of risk in contracts for transfer of ownership of specified objects) shall apply mutatis mutandis.

#### Article 419.- Cases where contract is subject to suspensive condition

In bilateral contracts subject to a suspensive condition, if the object is lost or damaged before the condition is fulfilled without the fault of the debtor, the debtor shall bear the risk. After the condition is fulfilled, the provisions of Article 416 (Burden of risk in contracts for transfer of ownership of specified objects) shall apply mutatis mutandis.

#### Article 420.- Burden of risk in bilateral contracts for obligation to do or not to do

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code.)

In bilateral contracts for an obligation to do or not to do, if the performance of the obligation becomes impossible without the fault of either party, the obligation shall be extinguished. In such case, the debtor may not demand counter-performance From the creditor.
