#### Article 421.- Impossibility of performance due to creditor's fault

In cases where the performance of an obligation becomes impossible without the fault of the debtor, but such impossibility is caused by the fault of the creditor, the debtor shall not lose the right to demand counter-performance From the creditor. However, if the debtor obtains any benefit from being released From his/her own obligation, the debtor shall return such benefit to the creditor.

## Chapter 6
## Effect of Claims against Third Parties

### Section 1
### Creditor's Right of Subrogation

#### Article 422.- Creditor's right of subrogation

1-In cases where it is necessary to preserve his/her own claim, a creditor may exercise a right belonging to the debtor on behalf of the debtor. However, this shall not apply to rights which are strictly personal to the debtor, rights which the debtor may exercise at his/her absolute discretion, or rights which are exempt From attachment.

2-In cases where it is necessary to obtain satisfaction of his/her own claim, a creditor may exercise a claim belonging to the debtor which is closely related to the creditor's claim by way of subrogation.

#### Article 423.- Conditions for exercising right of subrogation

1-In order to exercise the right set forth in Article 422 (Creditor's right of subrogation) above, the debtor must have failed to exercise the right himself/herself.

2-Until the creditor's own claim becomes due, the creditor may not exercise the debtor's right by way of subrogation unless the court's permission is obtained. However, this shall not apply to acts of preservation.

#### Article 424.- Defenses of third debtor

A third debtor against whom the creditor exercises the debtor's right by way of subrogation may assert against the creditor any defenses which the third debtor...

...has against his/her own creditor.

#### Article 425.- Relationship between subrogating creditor and debtor

After a creditor has given notice to the debtor that the creditor is exercising the debtor's right by way of subrogation, the debtor may not dispose of the right against the third debtor. However, this shall not prevent the debtor from receiving payment From the third debtor.

#### Article 426.- Effects of creditor's right of subrogation

1-In cases where a creditor exercises the debtor's right by way of subrogation, the effects of such exercise shall accrue directly to the debtor.

2-In cases where the debtor cannot receive performance From the third debtor, the creditor who exercises the right of subrogation may demand the third debtor to perform directly to the creditor.

#### Article 427.- Effects of exercise of subrogation and other creditors

A creditor who has exercised the right of subrogation shall have the duty to hold the performance received From the third debtor for the benefit of all creditors until such performance is delivered to the debtor. However, this shall not apply to the case set forth in paragraph 2 of Article 422 (Creditor's right of subrogation).

### Section 2
### Right to Revoke Acts Prejudicial to Creditor

#### Article 428.- Conditions for revocation of acts prejudicial to creditor

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code.)

1-A creditor may apply to the court for the revocation of an act performed by a debtor with the knowledge that it would prejudice the creditor, and may demand the beneficiary of such act or any subsequent transferee to return the object received or its value.

2-If the beneficiary of the act or the subsequent transferee did not know at the time of the act or at the time of the transfer that the act would prejudice the creditor, the revocation set forth in paragraph 1 above may not be granted. However, in the case of a beneficiary or subsequent transferee who received the object without consideration, the creditor may seek revocation even if such person did not know that the act would prejudice the creditor.

3-The provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2 above shall not apply to an act the subject of which is not a property right.

#### Article 429.- Preserved rights

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code.)

The provisions of Article 428 (Conditions for revocation of acts prejudicial to creditor) above shall also apply to a creditor whose claim...

...is not a monetary claim. However, at the conclusion of oral arguments relating to the judgment or ruling to revoke the act prejudicial to the creditor, such claim shall be converted into a monetary claim.

#### Article 430.- Method of revocation of act prejudicial to creditor

If there is no other appropriate way for the beneficiary or subsequent transferee to make restitution, the creditor who exercises the right of revocation may demand the beneficiary or subsequent transferee to deposit the object received.

#### Article 431.- Effects of revocation of act prejudicial to creditor

A revocation made under the provisions of Article 428 (Conditions for revocation of acts prejudicial to creditor) shall take effect for the benefit of all creditors.

#### Article 432.- Period for exercising right of revocation of act prejudicial to creditor

1-The right of revocation of an act prejudicial to the creditor set forth in Article 428 (Conditions for revocation of acts prejudicial to creditor) shall be extinguished if not exercised within one (1) year From the time the creditor becomes aware of the ground for revocation. It shall also be extinguished if three (3) years have elapsed From the time the act was performed.

2-The provisions concerning the suspension of prescription shall not apply to the period for the extinguishment of the right of revocation set forth in paragraph 1 above.

## Chapter 7
## Extinguishment of Obligations

#### Article 433.- Grounds for extinguishment of obligations

An obligation shall be extinguished by any of the following grounds:
(a) Performance, set-off, release, novation, or confusion as provided for in Section 1 (Performance) through Section 5 (Confusion) of this Chapter 7;
(b) Impossibility of performance without the fault of the debtor, as provided for in Chapter 5 (Burden of Risk) of this Book;
(c) Fulfillment of a condition for termination as provided for in Section 3 (Conditions and Time Periods) of Chapter 1, or termination of a contract as provided for in Section 4 (Termination of Contract) of Chapter 4 of this Book;
(d) Expiration of the prescriptive period as provided for in Chapter 8 (Prescription of Claims) of this Book;
(e) Exercise of a right of revocation under the provisions of Section 3 (Nullity and Revocation) of Chapter 2 of this Book.

### Section 1
### Performance

#### Sub-section 1
#### General Principles Concerning Performance

#### Article 434.- Person performing obligation

1-Performance may be made by the debtor or by a third party.

2-If the purpose of the obligation cannot be achieved by performance by a third party, such third party may not perform the obligation unless the creditor consents...

...From the creditor.

3-The creditor and the debtor may agree that performance by a third party shall not be recognized.

#### Article 435.- Capacity of person performing obligation to dispose

1-If the person performing the obligation is not the owner of the object given as performance or does not have the capacity to dispose of such object, the performance shall not be valid.

2-In cases where the person performing the obligation is not the owner of the object given as performance, such person may not recover the object unless he/she makes a new valid performance. However, the owner of such object may recover the object From the person who received the performance.

3-In cases where the person performing the obligation does not have the capacity to dispose of the object given as performance, such person may revoke the act of performance. In such case, the provisions of paragraph 2 above shall apply mutatis mutandis to the recovery of the object.

#### Article 436.- Consumption or transfer by creditor and good faith acquisition

1-In the cases set forth in paragraphs 2 and 3 of Article 435 (Capacity of person performing obligation to dispose) above, if the object given as performance is a movable and the creditor has consumed or transferred the object in good faith, the performance shall be deemed valid, and the person performing the obligation may not demand the return of the object.

2-In the case set forth in paragraph 1 above, the owner of the object given as performance may demand compensation for damages From the person who received the performance, unless the person who received the performance has acquired ownership of the object...

...performance in accordance with the provisions of Article 193 (Bona fide acquisition of ownership of movables) of this Code. However, a creditor who has paid damages to the owner of the object given as performance may demand the debtor to reimburse the amount paid.

#### Article 437.- Person entitled to receive performance

1-Performance shall be valid only if made to the creditor or another person who is authorized to receive performance.

2-If the debtor makes performance to a person who is not authorized to receive performance, the debtor shall perform again to the creditor upon the creditor's demand. In such case, the person who performed may recover the object From the person who received the unauthorized performance.

#### Article 438.- Restriction on right to receive performance due to attachment

1-If a claim has been attached by an order of the court, the creditor shall not have the right to receive performance From the debtor, and the debtor may not make performance to such creditor.

2-The provisions of paragraph 2 of Article 437 (Person entitled to receive performance) above shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where a debtor who is prohibited From making performance nonetheless makes performance to his/her creditor.

#### Article 439.- Performance to quasi-possessor of claim

1-Performance made to a person who, although not the creditor, appears to be the creditor, shall be valid if the person performing the obligation acted in good faith believing that such person was the true creditor, unless such belief was the result of gross negligence.

2-In the case set forth in paragraph 1 above, the true creditor may not demand the debtor to make performance again. However, the true creditor may demand the person who received the performance From the debtor to return the object received.

#### Article 440.- Method of performance

The debtor shall perform the obligation in accordance with its tenor and the principles of good faith and honesty.

#### Article 441.- Partial performance

A creditor is not under the duty to receive partial performance of an obligation. However, if the creditor receives partial performance knowing it is such, the performance shall be valid to the extent performed.

#### Article 442.- Performance in lieu of performance

1-If a debtor, with the consent of the creditor, makes a performance different From the original performance, such performance shall have the same effects as the original performance.

2-The creditor and the debtor may agree on a different performance in lieu of the original performance. In such case, the debtor may choose to perform the original obligation or the alternative performance, unless the right of choice belongs to the creditor.

#### Article 443.- Time of performance; performance before due date

1-A debtor shall perform the obligation by the time for performance set forth in the items of Article 391 (Delay in performance) of this Code.

2-A debtor may perform the obligation before the due date, in accordance with the provisions of paragraph...

...2 of Article 330 (Benefit of suspension) of this Code.

#### Article 444.- Time of day for performance

A debtor shall perform the obligation during normal business hours in accordance with custom and the principles of good faith and honesty.

#### Article 445.- Place of performance

Unless otherwise agreed, the delivery of a specified object shall be made at the place where the object was located at the time the claim arose. Other performances shall be made at the current residence of the creditor.

#### Article 446.- Expenses of performance

Unless otherwise agreed, the expenses of performance shall be borne by the debtor. However, if such expenses increase due to a change of residence or any other act of the creditor, the creditor shall bear the amount of such increase.

#### Article 447.- Issuance of receipt

A person who makes performance may demand the person receiving the performance to issue a receipt.

#### Article 448.- Return of document evidencing claim

If there is a document evidencing the claim, the person who made performance in full may demand the return of such document.

#### Sub-section 2
#### Appropriation of Performance

#### Article 449.- Appropriation by designation

1-If a debtor owes several obligations of the same kind to a single creditor, and the performance given is insufficient to discharge all the obligations, the person performing may, at the time of performance, designate the obligation to which the performance shall be appropriated.

2-If the person performing does not make the designation set forth in paragraph 1 above, the person receiving the performance may, at the time of receipt, designate the obligation to which the performance shall be appropriated. However, if the person performing objects immediately to such appropriation, the appropriation shall be made in accordance with the law.

3-The appropriation set forth in paragraphs 1 and 2 above shall be made by giving notice to the other party.

#### Article 450.- Statutory appropriation

If neither the person performing nor the person receiving the performance makes a designation of appropriation, the performance shall be appropriated in accordance with the following provisions:
(a) If some obligations are due and others are not, the due obligations shall be performed first;
(b) If all obligations are due or all are not due, the obligation which is most burdensome for the debtor shall be performed first;

(c) If all obligations carry equal benefit for the debtor, the one which becomes due first or will become due first shall be performed first;
(d) If all obligations carry equal benefit for the debtor and become due at the same time, the performance shall be appropriated in proportion to the amount of each obligation.

#### Article 451.- Order of appropriation: expenses, interest, and principal

If a debtor is obligated to pay expenses and interest in addition to the principal of one or more obligations, and the performance given is insufficient to discharge the entire obligation, it shall be appropriated in the following order: expenses, interest, and principal. However, if there is an agreement between the creditor and the debtor to the contrary, such agreement shall prevail.

#### Article 452.- Appropriation in cases involving multiple creditors

The provisions of Article 449 (Appropriation by designation), Article 450 (Statutory appropriation), and Article 451 (Order of appropriation: expenses, interest, and principal) above shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where a debtor owes several obligations to several creditors and it is not clear to which obligation the performance should be appropriated.

#### Sub-section 3
#### Tender of Performance and Deposit

#### Article 453.- Definition and basic effects of tender of performance

1- "Tender of performance" refers to the debtor's act of completing preparations for performance and demanding the creditor to receive it.

2-If a debtor makes a tender of performance but the creditor fails to perform acts necessary to receive the performance, the debtor shall not be liable for non-performance.

3-If a debtor who is obligated to pay money and interest makes a tender of performance but the creditor fails to receive it, the debtor shall not be liable for interest accruing after such tender.

#### Article 454.- Method of tender of performance

A tender of performance shall be made by actually offering the performance in accordance with the tenor of the obligation. However, if the creditor has refused in advance to receive the performance, or if an act of the creditor is necessary for the performance of the obligation, it shall be sufficient for the debtor to give notice to the creditor that preparations for performance have been completed and to urge the creditor to receive it.

#### Article 455.- Effects of tender of performance

If a creditor fails to receive a tender of performance, in addition to the effects set forth in Article 453 (Definition and basic effects of tender of performance) of this Code, the following effects shall also occur:
(a) In a bilateral contract, the risk of loss or damage shall be transferred to the creditor;
(b) In a bilateral contract, the creditor's defense of simultaneous performance shall be extinguished;
(c) The debtor may be released From the obligation by making a deposit.

#### Article 456.- Delay in receipt

1-If a creditor refuses in advance to receive performance, or if a debtor makes a tender of performance but the creditor fails to receive it due to the creditor's fault, the debtor may demand compensation for damages resulting From such failure to receive performance.

2-If the creditor's failure to receive a tender of performance constitutes a serious breach of duty on the part of the creditor, the debtor may terminate the contract.

#### Article 457.- Deposit

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code.)

1-A person obligated to perform may be released From the obligation by depositing the subject-matter of the performance with a depository office in any of the following cases:
(a) If the person who offered the performance is refused or cannot receive the performance;
(b) If the person obligated to perform cannot identify the creditor through no fault of his/her own.

2-Until the creditor accepts the deposit or the judgment or ruling declaring the deposit valid becomes final and binding, the person who made the deposit may withdraw the deposited object. In such case, it shall be deemed that no deposit was made. However, this shall not apply to cases where a pledge or hypothec has been extinguished by virtue of the deposit.

3-Details concerning deposits shall be determined by the Law on Deposit.

#### Article 458.- Right to sell by oneself

If the subject-matter of the performance is not suitable for deposit, or if there is a risk...

...deterioration or loss, the person performing may, with the permission of the court, sell the object by auction and deposit the proceeds. This provision shall also apply to cases where the preservation of the object would involve excessive expense.

#### Sub-section 4
#### Subrogation by Performance

#### Article 459.- Subrogation by performance

1-A person who has been released From liability by making performance or by incurring other expenses of his/her own and who has a right of recourse against the debtor may succeed to the claim and other rights held by the creditor by way of subrogation.

2-The provisions of Article 503 (Requirements for assertion of assignment of nominative claim) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to the case set forth in paragraph 1 above.

3-The notice set forth in Article 913 (Conditions for demand for reimbursement) and Article 933 (Notice as condition for demand for reimbursement) of this Code may be substituted for the notice set forth in paragraph 2 above. However, if a document with a certified date is required under the provisions applied mutatis mutandis from paragraph 2 above, the notice set forth in this paragraph shall also be made by a document with a certified date.

#### Article 460.- Extent of subrogation and relationship between subrogees

1-A person who is subrogated to the rights of the creditor under Article 459 (Subrogation by performance) above may exercise any and all rights that were held by the creditor as part of or as security for the claim to...

...the creditor. However, this shall not apply to rights arising From the creditor's status under a contract.

2-A subrogee shall comply with the following provisions:
(a) Unless a personal guarantor or real guarantor has obtained an additional entry of subrogation in the registration of the preferential right, pledge of immovable, or hypothec created over the debtor's property, such guarantor or real guarantor may not succeed to the creditor's rights by way of subrogation against a third party who has acquired the immovable;
(b) A third party who has acquired the subject-matter of the security From the debtor shall not succeed to the creditor's rights by way of subrogation against a guarantor;
(c) One of several third parties who have acquired the subject-matter of the security From the debtor shall succeed to the creditor's rights by way of subrogation against the other third parties in proportion to the value of the respective subject-matter acquired;
(d) The provision of item (c) above shall apply mutatis mutandis to the relationship between real guarantors as set forth in Article 766 (Definition of real guarantor and third party acquirer) of this Code;
(e) As between a guarantor and a real guarantor, subrogation shall take place based on the total number of such persons. However, if there are several real guarantors, they shall be subrogated to the creditor's rights with respect to the remaining amount after deducting the guarantor's share, in proportion to the value of the respective subject-matter of the security.

#### Article 461.- Partial subrogation

1-If a subrogee has made partial performance of the claim...

...the subrogee may exercise the claim concurrently with the creditor in proportion to the amount of performance made. However, if the subrogee exercises the claim, he/she shall obtain the creditor's consent, or if the creditor so demands, the subrogee shall transfer his/her rights or priority to the creditor to the extent of the remaining claim without charge.

2-In the case set forth in paragraph 1 above, only the creditor may terminate the contract on the ground of non-performance. However, in such case, the creditor shall reimburse the subrogee for the performance made plus interest.

#### Article 462.- Delivery of documents relating to claim and security

1-A creditor who has received full performance From a subrogee shall deliver to the subrogee documents relating to the claim and the security interest held by the creditor.

2-If a subrogee has made partial performance, the creditor shall note the subrogation on the documents relating to the claim and shall allow the subrogee to oversee the preservation of the security interest held by the creditor.

#### Article 463.- Duty to preserve security for subrogee

If a person who can be subrogated under the provisions of Article 459 (Subrogation by performance) of this Code exists, and if the creditor has intentionally or through negligence lost or diminished the security, the person who would have succeeded to the creditor's rights by way of subrogation shall be released From liability or may claim compensation for damages to the extent that such person has become unable to obtain reimbursement due to such loss or diminution.

### Section 2
### Set-off

#### Article 464.- Conditions for statutory set-off and set-off by contract

1-If two persons owe each other obligations of the same kind, and if both obligations are due, either party may extinguish the mutual obligations to the extent of the corresponding amount by a unilateral declaration of intention.

2-The parties may enter into a contract for set-off to extinguish mutual obligations even if the obligations are not of the same kind. However, the parties may not thereby prejudice the rights of a third party.

#### Article 465.- Method of set-off

1-A set-off shall be effected by a declaration of intention From one party to the other.

2-A declaration of intention for set-off may be subject to a condition precedent, but may not be subject to a condition subsequent or a time period.

#### Article 466.- Time when set-off takes effect

If the conditions for set-off are met, and a declaration of intention for set-off is made, the effect of extinguishment of the obligations by set-off shall relate back to the time when both obligations became suitable for set-off.

#### Article 467.- Restrictions on set-off

1-If the nature of either or both obligations does not permit set-off, the parties may not effect a set-off.

2-If the parties have made a special agreement not to set-off, neither party may effect a set-off. However, such agreement may not be asserted against a third party in good faith.

#### Article 468.- Set-off involving claim subject to defense

1-If the claim of the party declaring the intention for set-off is subject to a defense of the other party, such party may not effect a set-off.

2-If a claim which has been extinguished by prescription or by the expiration of the period for exercise of the right was suitable for set-off against the claim of the other party prior to such extinguishment, the creditor may still effect a set-off.

#### Article 469.- Set-off against claim arising from tort

A debtor who owes an obligation arising From a tort may not assert the extinguishment of such obligation by set-off.

#### Article 470.- Claim exempt from attachment

If a claim is exempt From attachment, the debtor may not assert the extinguishment of the obligation by set-off.

#### Article 471.- Claim which has been attached

1-If a claim has been attached by a third party, the debtor may set off his/her obligation by using a claim against the other party which the debtor acquired before the attachment, provided that such claim has reached maturity. However, this shall not apply if the maturity date of the debtor's claim used for set-off is later than...

...the maturity date of the claim of the other party.

2-The second sentence of paragraph 1 above shall not apply if the parties have made a special agreement that their respective obligations may be set off regardless of the sequence of their maturity dates.

#### Article 472.- Appropriation of set-off

If either or both parties owe several obligations suitable for set-off, the party claiming a set-off may designate the obligations on his/her part and on the other party's part to be extinguished. If no such designation is made, the provisions concerning the appropriation of performance shall apply mutatis mutandis.

### Section 3
### Release

#### Article 473.- Release of obligation

If the creditor declares an intention to release the debtor From the obligation, the obligation shall be extinguished.

#### Article 474.- Partial release

A creditor may release a part of an obligation.

#### Article 475.- Restrictions on release

If a claim has been attached by a third party, or if the creditor's right to dispose of the claim is otherwise restricted, the creditor may not release the obligation.

### Section 4
### Novation

#### Article 476.- Definition of novation

1-A "novation" refers to a contract between a creditor and a debtor whereby the original obligation is extinguished and a new obligation is created to replace it.

2-When a novation contract as set forth in paragraph 1 above takes effect, the original obligation shall be extinguished.

#### Article 477.- Non-extinguishment of original obligation

If the new obligation created by novation does not take effect due to reasons that the debtor of the old obligation could not have anticipated, or if the new obligation is originally impossible to perform, the original obligation shall not be extinguished.

#### Article 478.- Transfer of security

1-A pledge, hypothec, or other security interest created to secure the original obligation may be transferred to secure the new obligation, within the scope of the original obligation, by agreement between the parties to the novation contract. However, a security interest provided by a third party may not be transferred without the consent of such third party.

2-In an irregular deposit, an agreement as set forth in the first sentence of paragraph 1 above shall be presumed to exist.

### Section 5
### Merger

#### Article 479.- Merger of claim and obligation

1-If a claim and an obligation are vested in the same person, the claim and the obligation shall be extinguished by merger.

2-If the claim mentioned in paragraph 1 above is the subject-matter of a third party's right, such claim shall not be extinguished by merger.

## Chapter 8
## Extinctive Prescription of Claims

#### Article 480.- Definition of extinctive prescription of claims

"Extinctive prescription of a claim" refers to the extinguishment of a claim based on the creditor's failure to exercise the right during a specified period of time.

#### Article 481.- Commencement of extinctive prescription period for claims

Extinctive prescription of a claim shall commence From the time when the right can be exercised.

#### Article 482.- General extinctive prescription period for claims

The extinctive prescription period for a claim shall be five (5) years, unless otherwise provided in this Code or other laws or regulations.

#### Article 483.- Short-term extinctive prescription period

Claims for the price of goods sold by manufacturers or merchants to...

who are not merchants, and service fees provided by manufacturers or merchants to those who are not merchants, the extinctive prescription period for the loss of this right is 2 (two) years.

#### Article 484._ Extinctive prescription period for claims that have become final and binding

1- For claims that have become final and binding by way of a final and binding judgment, or by way of judicial settlement or any other method having the same effect as a final and binding judgment, even if there is a provision specifying an extinctive prescription period of less than 5 (five) years, the extinctive prescription period for such claims shall be 5 (five) years starting from the time the claim becomes final and binding.

2- The provision of paragraph 1 above shall not apply to cases where the time for performance has not yet arrived at the time the claim becomes final and binding.

#### Article 485._ Retroactive effect of extinctive prescription of claims

The extinctive prescription of a claim shall have retroactive effect from the day it began to run.

#### Article 486._ Invocation of extinctive prescription of claims

1- A court cannot render a judgment based on the extinctive prescription of a claim unless a party invokes such prescription.

2- In addition to the debtor, only joint and several debtors, guarantors, real security providers, and third party acquirers as defined in Article 766 (Definition of real security provider and third party acquirer) of this Code, and other persons who have a legally legitimate interest in invoking the prescription, may invoke the extinctive prescription of a claim.

3- If the debtor invokes the extinctive prescription of a claim, the third party acquirer shall also receive the benefit of such prescription. If a person who is not the debtor but has the right to invoke the prescription invokes the extinctive prescription of the claim, the effect of such invocation shall occur only between the creditor and the person who invokes the extinctive prescription.

#### Article 487._ Waiver of benefit of extinctive prescription of claims

The benefit of the extinctive prescription of a claim cannot be waived in advance. Only the benefit of an extinctive prescription that has already been completed may be waived.

#### Article 488._ Scope of persons affected by waiver of benefit of extinctive prescription of claims

A waiver of the benefit of the extinctive prescription of a claim shall be effective only between the creditor and the person entitled to invoke the prescription who has waived such benefit.

#### Article 489._ Grounds for interruption of extinctive prescription of claims

The extinctive prescription of a claim shall be interrupted in the following cases:

a- A judicial claim, participation in bankruptcy proceedings, or any other act having similar effect.

b- An act of compulsory execution or an act of provisional attachment or provisional disposition.

c- An acknowledgment of the obligation by way of partial payment, payment of interest, providing security, or other methods.

#### Article 490._ Scope of persons affected by interruption of extinctive prescription of claims

If a ground for interruption of the extinctive prescription of a claim exists against a debtor, other persons may not deny the effect of such interruption. If a ground for interruption of the extinctive prescription of a claim occurs against a person entitled to invoke the prescription other than the debtor, the effect of such interruption shall occur only between the creditor and the person entitled to invoke the prescription.

#### Article 491._ Judicial claims

A judicial claim shall not have the effect of interrupting the extinctive prescription of a claim in cases where the lawsuit is dismissed or withdrawn.

#### Article 492._ Annulment of compulsory execution or provisional measures

In cases where compulsory execution or provisional measures are annulled upon the application of a party or for failure to comply with legal requirements, the interruption of the extinctive prescription of a claim resulting from such compulsory execution or provisional measures shall be deemed never to have occurred.

#### Article 493._ Compulsory execution or provisional measures carried out against persons other than the debtor

Compulsory execution or provisional measures carried out against a person other than the debtor shall not have the effect of interrupting the extinctive prescription of a claim unless the debtor has been notified of such acts.

#### Article 494._ Running of extinctive prescription of claims after interruption

1- An extinctive prescription of a claim that has been interrupted shall begin to run anew from the time the ground for interruption ceases to exist.

2- An extinctive prescription of a claim that has been interrupted by a judicial claim shall begin to run anew from the time the judgment becomes final and binding.

#### Article 495._ Suspension of completion of extinctive prescription by demand and by judicial demand

1- If a person entitled to a right makes a demand at any time within 6 (six) months before the expiration of the extinctive prescription period for a claim, the extinctive prescription for that claim shall not be completed against that person during the period of 6 (six) months following the demand. However, even if the person entitled to the right makes another demand, the completion of the extinctive prescription for the claim cannot be further delayed by that subsequent demand.

2- Even if a judicial claim does not have the effect of interrupting the extinctive prescription because the lawsuit is dismissed or withdrawn, the plaintiff shall be deemed to have made a continuous demand from the time the complaint was served on the other party until the time the lawsuit was dismissed or withdrawn. In such cases, the extinctive prescription for the claim shall not be completed against that person during the period of 6 (six) months following the dismissal or withdrawal of the lawsuit.

3- If a person entitled to a right asserts his/her right as a defendant in a lawsuit, that person shall be deemed to have made a continuous demand while the lawsuit is pending from the time the person asserts his/her right. In such cases, the extinctive prescription for the claim shall not be completed against that person during the period of 6 (six

one) months after the judgment or ruling on the lawsuit becomes final and binding.

#### Article 496._ Suspension of completion of extinctive prescription of claims for minors or persons under general guardianship

If a minor or a person under general guardianship lacks a legal representative at any time within 6 (six) months before the expiration of the extinctive prescription period for a claim, the extinctive prescription for that claim shall not be completed against that person during the period of 6 (six) months following the time the person attains capacity or the time a legal representative is appointed.

#### Article 497._ Suspension of completion of extinctive prescription of claims between husband and wife

With regard to claims that one spouse has against the other, the extinctive prescription for the claim shall not be completed during the period of 6 (six) months following the dissolution of the marriage.

#### Article 498._ Suspension of completion of extinctive prescription of claims due to natural disaster

If a person entitled to a right is unable to interrupt the extinctive prescription of a claim due to a natural disaster or other force majeure at any time within 6 (six) months before the expiration of the extinctive prescription period for the claim, the extinctive prescription for that claim shall not be completed during the period of 6 (six) months following the time the force majeure ceases to exist.

#### Article 499._ Performance to satisfy a claim for which the extinctive prescription period has expired

With regard to performance provided to satisfy a claim for which the extinctive prescription period has expired, even if the person provided such performance without knowing that the extinctive prescription period had expired, such person may not demand the return of such performance.

#### Article 500._ Extinctive prescription of property rights other than claims or ownership

The extinctive prescription period for property rights other than claims or ownership shall be 10 (ten) years, unless otherwise provided in this Code or other laws or regulations. The provisions concerning the extinctive prescription of claims shall apply mutatis mutandis to the extinctive prescription of property rights other than claims or ownership.

### Chapter 9
### Assignment of Claims and Assumption of Obligations

#### Section 1
#### Assignment of Claims

#### Article 501._ Assignability of claims and special agreements prohibiting assignment

1- A claim may be assigned to another person, unless the nature of the claim does not permit assignment. In such cases, the assignee shall become the new creditor.

2- Even if the nature of a claim permits assignment, the parties may prohibit the assignment of the claim by an expression of intention.

3- The expression of intention referred to in paragraph 2 above cannot be asserted against a third party who is unaware of such expression of intention. However, this provision shall not apply if the third party is grossly negligent.

#### Article 502._ Formation of assignment of claims

An assignment of a claim shall be effective only by the agreement between the creditor who intends to assign the claim and the assignee. However, in order to assert the assignment of a claim against the debtor or a third party, the requirements for assertion provided in Article 503 (Requirements for assertion of assignment of nominated claims) below must be satisfied.

#### Article 503._ Requirements for assertion of assignment of nominated claims

1- An assignment of a nominated claim cannot be asserted against the debtor or any other third party unless the assignor gives notice to the debtor or the debtor consents to the assignor or the assignee.

2- The notice or consent referred to in paragraph 1 above cannot be asserted against a third party other than the debtor unless it is made by an instrument bearing a fixed date.

#### Article 504._ Priority of multiple assignments of claims

1- In cases where the same claim is assigned multiple times, the priority of the assignments shall be determined by the order of time when the notice by an instrument bearing a fixed date reaches the debtor or when the debtor consents by an instrument bearing a fixed date. In cases

the debtor must perform the obligation to the assignee with priority.

2- In the cases provided in paragraph 1 above, if the order of time when the notices reached the debtor or when the debtor gave consent cannot be determined, all notices or consents shall be deemed to have reached the debtor or been given at the same time. In such cases, each assignee may demand the debtor to perform the entire obligation assigned to him/her. When the debtor performs the obligation to any one of the assignees, the obligation shall be extinguished.

3- In the cases provided in paragraph 2 above, the debtor may be released from the obligation by depositing the subject matter of the obligation.

#### Article 505._ Effect of notice and consent

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code)

1- If the debtor gives consent as provided in Article 503 (Requirements for assertion of assignment of nominated claims) of this Code without objection, even if there is a ground that could have been asserted against the assignor, the debtor cannot assert such ground against the assignee. However, this provision shall not prevent the debtor from recovering any subject matter delivered to the assignor in order to extinguish the obligation, or from considering that the obligation has not arisen if the debtor assumed an obligation to the assignor in order to extinguish the obligation.

2- If the assignor has only given notice of the assignment, the debtor may assert against the assignee any ground that has arisen against the assignor up to the time the debtor received the notice.

#### Article 506._ Assignment of claims evidenced by securities

The provisions of special laws shall apply to the assignment of claims evidenced by

order permissions or other claims evidenced by securities.

#### Section 2
#### Assumption of Obligations

#### Article 507._ Assumability of obligations

1- An obligation that can be performed by a third party other than the debtor may be assumed by a third party.

2- The parties may prohibit the assumption of an obligation by a third party by an expression of intention.

#### Article 508._ Formation of assumption of obligations

An assumption of an obligation shall be effective by an agreement between the debtor and the person assuming the obligation, or by an agreement between the creditor and the person assuming the obligation.

#### Article 509._ Rights of the creditor

1- Notwithstanding the assumption of an obligation, the creditor shall not lose his/her rights against the debtor. In such cases, the debtor and the person assuming the obligation shall be jointly and severally liable to the creditor.

2- The person assuming the obligation may release the debtor from the obligation with the consent of the creditor.

#### Article 510._ Objections in assumption of obligations

In cases where an obligation has been assumed, the person assuming the obligation may assert against the creditor any ground that the debtor could have asserted up to the time the assumption of the obligation was formed.

#### Article 511._ Performance of obligations

If the person assuming the obligation does not assume the obligation itself but only assumes the performance of the obligation

only, by agreement between the debtor and the person assuming the performance, the creditor may not demand the person assuming the performance to perform the obligation.

#### Section 3
#### Assignment of Contractual Status

#### Article 512._ Assignment of contractual status

1- A person who has entered into a contract with another person may assign his/her status in the contract to a third party. However, this provision shall not apply to cases where the assignment of such status is not permitted by the nature of the contract. Furthermore, the parties to the contract may prohibit the assignment of the contractual status by agreement. In such cases, the provisions of paragraph 3 of Article 501 (Assignability of claims and special agreements prohibiting assignment) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis.

2- In cases where the assignment of contractual status would cause a serious disadvantage to the other party to the contract, the person intending to assign the contractual status must obtain the consent of the other party to the contract for such assignment.

#### Article 513._ Formation of assignment of contractual status

An assignment of contractual status shall be effective only by the agreement between the party to the contract who intends to assign the status and the assignee. However, in order to assert the assignment against the other party to the contract or a third party, the requirements for assertion provided in Article 503 (Requirements for assertion of assignment of nominated claims) of this Code must be satisfied.

#### Article 514._ Effect of assignment of contractual status

In cases where an assignment of contractual status has been made, all rights and obligations of the assignor shall be transferred to the assignee, unless specifically expressed otherwise. In such cases, the provisions of Article 505 (Effect of notice and consent) concerning the assignment of claims and Article 510 (Objections in assumption of obligations) concerning the assumption of obligations of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis.

### Book 5
### Particular Types of Contracts and Torts

### Chapter 1
### Sale

#### Section 1
#### Nature of Sale and Formation of Sales Contracts

#### Article 515._ Definition of sale

A sale is a contract whereby one party, called the seller, has an obligation to transfer ownership or other rights over property to the other party, called the buyer, and the buyer has an obligation to pay the price in money to the seller.

#### Article 516._ Formation of sales contracts

A sales contract shall be formed only by the agreement between both parties, unless otherwise provided by special provisions of law. However, the parties may stipulate the execution of an authentic instrument or a private instrument as a condition for the formation of the contract.

#### Article 517._ Unilateral promise to sell or buy

1- In cases where only one party makes a promise to sell or buy in advance, the sale shall become effective from the time the other party expresses his/her intention to complete the purchase or sale to the person who made the promise.

2- If the period for the expression of intention referred to in paragraph 1 above is not specified, the promisor may specify a reasonable period and

may demand the other party to express his/her intention as to whether or not to complete the purchase or sale within such period. If the other party fails to express his/her intention within the specified period, the promise shall cease to be effective.

#### Article 518._ Earnest money

If the buyer has paid earnest money to the seller, the buyer may rescind the sales contract by forfeiting the earnest money, and the seller may rescind the sales contract by returning twice the amount of the earnest money. However, neither party may rescind the contract after the other party has begun to perform the contract.

#### Article 519._ Sale on approval (tasting)

1- A sale of goods for which social custom requires tasting before purchase shall become effective when the buyer gives consent after tasting.

2- If the period for the buyer to give consent is not specified, the seller may demand the buyer to give a definite answer as to whether or not he/she consents within a reasonable period. If the buyer fails to give a definite answer within such period, the buyer shall be deemed to have refused to enter into the contract.

#### Article 520._ Sale on trial

1- In a sale on trial, the sales contract shall become effective when the buyer gives consent within the trial period, or when the trial period expires without the buyer expressing his/her intention. If the buyer refuses to accept the subject matter, the sales contract shall be deemed not to have been formed.

2- If the trial period is not specified in the contract, the seller may demand the buyer to give a definite answer as to whether or not he/she consents by specifying

...a reasonable period. If the buyer fails to give a definite answer within such period and continues to use the subject matter of the contract, the buyer shall be deemed to have given consent.

#### Article 521._ Purchase price

1- The parties to a contract shall specify the amount of the purchase price or the method of determining such amount in the contract.

2- The amount of the purchase price may be determined by reference to the market price of certain goods at the present or in the future, or based on an appraisal made by a third party selected according to the method determined by the parties. In cases where the third party's appraisal is made by mistake or where the appraisal is clearly contrary to equity, a party to the contract whose interests are prejudiced by such appraisal may object to the appraisal.

3- The parties to a contract may specify the amount of the purchase price by indicating the actual cost or by having the buyer pay a perpetual annuity or a life annuity. However, in cases where a third party makes an appraisal according to the provision of paragraph 2 above, the purchase price shall be determined by indicating the actual cost unless the parties have given the third party the authority to do so in secrecy.

#### Article 522._ Expenses of sales contract

The expenses of executing a contract or other expenses incurred for a sales contract shall be borne equally by both parties, unless otherwise agreed by the parties.

#### Article 523._ Mutatis mutandis application to other contracts for consideration

The provisions of this Chapter 1 (Sale) shall apply mutatis mutandis to contracts for consideration other than sales contracts.

#### Section 2
#### Parties to Sales Contracts and Subject Matter of Sales Contracts

#### Article 524._ Parties to sales contracts

Natural persons or legal persons may sell or buy, unless prohibited by law.

#### Article 525._ Persons who cannot become buyers (1)

1- A person who manages property by law, by court order, or by contract cannot become the buyer or the purchaser at a compulsory sale of the property that he/she is tasked to sell, whether directly or through an intermediary.

2- The provision of paragraph 1 above shall apply mutatis mutandis to public officials who are in charge of managing compulsory sales or in charge of supervising compulsory sales.

3- With regard to a sale that violates paragraph 1 or 2 above, only the owner of the property before the sale or his/her successor or representative may rescind the contract.

#### Article 526._ Persons who cannot become buyers (2)

1- Judges, prosecutors, clerks, or other court officials cannot become the buyer or the purchaser at a compulsory sale, whether directly or through an intermediary, of property or rights that are the subject matter of a dispute pending before the court where they serve.

...can perform the duty.

2- The provision of paragraph 1 above shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where a lawyer or a notary becomes the buyer or the purchaser at a compulsory sale of property or rights related to a lawsuit that they are handling.

3- With regard to a sale that violates paragraph 1 or 2 above, only the seller or the other party to the dispute concerning the right or their successor or representative may rescind the contract.

#### Article 527._ Property that can be sold

Any property or rights, even those to come into existence in the future, may be the subject matter of a sales contract, unless the property or rights are non-assignable by nature or prohibited by law from being assigned.

#### Section 3
#### Effect of Sales Contracts

#### Article 528._ Transfer of ownership

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code)

1- With regard to the transfer of ownership of the subject matter of a sales contract, the general principles provided in Article 133 (Creation, transfer and modification of real rights by agreement) through Article 135 (Requirements for creation of effect of transfer of ownership of immovable property by agreement) of this Code shall apply.

2- If the subject matter of a sales contract is a property right other than ownership, the transfer of such rights shall be governed by the general principles relating to the transfer of each such right.

#### Part 1

#### Obligations of the Seller

#### Article 529.- Seller's duty to explain

A seller has a duty to clearly explain the contents of the obligations for which the seller is responsible, the legal relationship concerning the object or right which is the subject matter of the sale, and especially in the case of a sale of immovable property, the seller must clearly explain to the buyer the contents of the rights, encumbrances, and the boundaries of the immovable property, among other things.

#### Article 530.- General obligations of the seller

A seller has an obligation to transfer the rights to the property sold, to deliver the property sold, to preserve the property sold until delivery, and to deliver various documents necessary for certifying the rights to the buyer, in accordance with the contract and the provisions of this Code.

#### Article 531.- Obligation to transfer rights and liability for warranty of the seller

1- A seller has an obligation to transfer the rights to the property which is the subject matter of the sale to the buyer.

2- If the subject matter of the sale is a right belonging to another person, the seller has an obligation to acquire that right and transfer it to the buyer.

3- If the seller is unable to transfer the rights which are the subject matter of the sale to the buyer, the buyer may rescind the contract. In this case, if the buyer was unaware at the time of the contract that the rights did not belong to the seller, the buyer may claim for compensation for damages.

#### Article 532.- Seller's right to rescind the contract in case of sale of another's rights

1- If a seller was unaware at the time of the contract that the rights which are the subject matter of the sale did not belong to the seller, and the seller is unable to acquire and transfer such rights to the buyer, the seller may rescind the contract, but shall compensate for damages.

2- In the case of paragraph 1 above, if the buyer was aware at the time of the contract that the rights which were the subject matter of the sale did not belong to the seller, the seller may rescind the contract without having to compensate for damages.

#### Article 533.- Liability for warranty in case where part of the rights belongs to another person

1- In the case where part of the rights among the rights that are the subject matter of the sale belongs to another person, and the seller is unable to acquire and transfer that part of the rights to the buyer, the buyer may demand a reduction in the price in proportion to that part.

2- In the case provided for in paragraph 1 above, if the buyer was unaware at the time of the contract that such part did not belong to the seller, and if it is recognized that the buyer would not have purchased only the remaining part, the buyer may rescind the contract.

3- In the case provided for in paragraph 1 or 2 above, if the buyer was unaware at the time of the contract that such part of the rights did not belong to the seller, the buyer may also demand compensation for damages.

4- The rights provided for in paragraphs 1, 2, and 3 above must be exercised within 1 (one) year from the time the buyer becomes aware of the facts, in the case where the buyer was unaware at the time of the contract that part of the rights among the rights which are the subject matter of the sale did not belong to the seller, or from the time of the contract in the case where the buyer was aware that part of the rights among the rights which are the subject matter of the

the subject matter of the sale do not belong to the seller at the time of the contract.

#### Article 534.- Liability for warranty in case of real rights such as usufruct, etc.

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code.)

1- In the case where the subject matter of the sale is encumbered with a perpetual lease, usufruct, right of use, right of residence, servitude, lease, right of retention, or pledge for a third party, and if the buyer is unable to use or obtain benefits from all or part of the subject matter due to the existence of such rights, the buyer who was unaware of the existence of such rights at the time of the contract may demand compensation for damages from the seller.

2- In the case provided for in paragraph 1 above, if the purpose of the contract cannot be achieved due to the existence of such rights, the buyer who was unaware of the existence of such rights at the time of the contract may also rescind the contract.

3- The provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2 above shall also apply to cases where a servitude that was supposed to exist for the immovable property which is the subject matter of the sale does not actually exist.

4- In the cases provided for in paragraph 1, 2, or 3 above, the rescission of the contract or the demand for compensation for damages must be made within 1 (one) year from the time the buyer becomes aware of the facts.

#### Article 535.- Liability for warranty in case of real security rights

1- In the case where there is a preferential right or a right of pledge that provides that the creditor shall not use and take profit, or a mortgage, encumbering the immovable property which is the subject matter of the sale, and the buyer has lost ownership of that immovable property because such real security rights have been enforced, the buyer may rescind the contract.

2- In the case where the buyer has made expenditures to preserve the ownership, the buyer may demand the seller to reimburse such expenditures.

3- In any of the cases provided for in paragraph 1 or 2 above, if the buyer has suffered damages, the buyer may demand compensation for such damages.

4- If the buyer has purchased immovable property encumbered with real security rights as provided for in paragraph 1 above, at a price from which the amount of the secured debt has already been deducted, the buyer shall not have the rights provided for in paragraphs 1, 2, and 3 above.

#### Article 536.- Liability for warranty in compulsory sale

1- A person who has purchased property through a compulsory sale may rescind the contract with the debtor or demand a reduction in the price, in accordance with the provisions from Article 531 (Obligation to transfer rights and liability for warranty of the seller) to Article 535 (Liability for warranty in case of real security rights) of this Code.

2- In the case provided for in paragraph 1 above, if the debtor is insolvent, the buyer may demand the creditors who have received a distribution of the proceeds of the sale to return all or part of such proceeds.

3- In the cases provided for in paragraph 1 or 2 above, if the debtor knew of the existence of the circumstances that would lead to liability for warranty and failed to notify the institution implementing the compulsory sale, or if a creditor knew of such circumstances but nevertheless demanded the compulsory sale, the buyer may demand compensation for damages from such persons.

#### Article 537.- Liability for warranty of a seller of a claim

1- A seller of a claim shall be responsible for the existence of the claim.

2- In the case where the seller of a claim has guaranteed the solvency of the debtor, it shall be presumed that the solvency was guaranteed at the time of the contract.

3- In the case where the seller of a claim that has not yet reached its due date has guaranteed the future solvency of the debtor, it shall be presumed that the solvency was guaranteed at the time the claim becomes due for payment.

#### Article 538.- Agreement for exemption from or limitation of liability for warranty

Even if there is an agreement for exemption from or limitation of the liability for warranty that the seller must bear according to the provisions from Article 531 (Obligation to transfer rights and liability for warranty of the seller) to Article 537 (Liability for warranty of a seller of a claim) of this Code, the seller cannot be released from the liability for facts that the seller knew but failed to disclose, or for rights that the seller created for a third party or transferred to a third party.

#### Article 539.- Obligation to deliver property without defects

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code.)

1- The seller has an obligation to deliver property without defects to the buyer.

2- Unless otherwise agreed by the parties, property delivered to the buyer that has any of the following characteristics is deemed to have a defect:

(a) The property does not conform to the quantity, quality, and characteristics specified in the contract.

(b) The property differs from the sample that the seller showed to the buyer in terms of quantity, quality, and characteristics.

(c) The property does not conform to the usage that the seller indicated, either explicitly or tacitly, at the time of the contract.

(d) The property does not conform to the ordinary usage of property of the same kind.

(e) The property is not packaged in the ordinary manner for property of the same kind or in a reasonable manner to preserve the property.

#### Article 540.- Liability for warranty for defects

1- When the risk is transferred to the buyer, if the property that is the subject matter of the sale has a defect, the seller must be responsible for the delivery of replacement property, repair of the defect, rescission of the contract, or a reduction in price, in accordance with the contract and the provisions of this Code, even if the defect is clearly identified after the risk has been transferred.

2- Even if a defect arises after the time specified in paragraph 1 above, if it arises due to the seller breaching their own obligations, the seller must also be responsible for the liability for warranty for the defect, as provided in the provisions of paragraph 1 above.

3- In the case where the seller guarantees that the property that is the subject matter of the sale shall conform to an ordinary or specific purpose within a specified period after delivery, or guarantees that the property shall maintain its specific quality or characteristics within a specified period after delivery, and if that guarantee is breached, the seller must be responsible for the liability for warranty for the defect in the same manner.

4- In the case where the buyer was aware of or was unaware due to their own gross negligence of the defect in the property that is the subject matter of the sale at the time of the contract of sale, the seller shall not be responsible for the liability for warranty for that defect.

#### Article 541.- Seller's right to repair

1- In the case where the seller delivers property with a defect before the time set for delivery, the seller may repair the defect until such set time, unless it causes unreasonable damage to the interests of the buyer. However, the buyer is not prohibited from exercising the right to demand compensation for damages.

2- Even after the time set for delivery, the seller may repair all defects at their own expense, unless it causes unreasonable damage to the interests of the buyer. However, the buyer is not prohibited from exercising the right to rescind the contract and the right to demand compensation for damages.

#### Article 542.- Buyer's right to demand full performance

1- The buyer may demand the seller to perform their obligations. However, this provision shall not be applied to cases where the remedy is inappropriate to the demand for performance of those obligations.

2- In the case where the property that is the subject matter of the sale has a defect, the buyer may demand the seller to deliver substitute property without defects. However, this provision shall not be applied to cases where providing property of the same kind by the seller would create an unreasonable burden, taking into account all the surrounding circumstances.

3- In the case where the property that is the subject matter of the sale has a defect, the buyer may demand the seller to repair the property that is the subject matter. However, this provision shall not be applied to cases where repairing the property that is the subject matter by the seller would create an unreasonable burden, taking into account all the surrounding circumstances.

4- The buyer may set an additional reasonable period for the seller to perform their obligations. The buyer may not demand remedies for breach of contract during this period, unless the buyer receives notice from the seller that the seller will not perform their obligations.

according to their own within that period. However, the buyer must not lose the right to demand compensation for damages that arose from the delay in performing the obligations according to the original specified time.

#### Article 543.- Buyer's right to rescind the contract

In the case where the buyer cannot achieve the purpose of the contract of sale because the delivered property has defects, the buyer may rescind that contract.

#### Article 544.- Buyer's right to demand a reduction in the purchase price

In the case where the delivered property has defects, whether the purchase price has been paid or not, the buyer may also demand a reduction in that purchase price in proportion to the difference at the time of delivery between the value of property without defects and the value of the property that was delivered. However, in the case where the seller repairs those defects in accordance with Article 542 (Buyer's right to demand full performance) of this Code, or in the case where the buyer refuses to accept the seller's proposal to provide repairs without a valid reason, the buyer cannot demand a reduction in the purchase price.

#### Article 545.- Buyer's right to demand compensation for damages

The buyer is not prohibited from demanding compensation for damages in accordance with the provisions of Section 3 (Compensation for damages), Chapter 4 of Part 4, without having to exercise or while exercising concurrently the rights specified in the provisions of Article 542 (Buyer's right to demand full performance) to Article 544 (Buyer's right to demand a reduction in the purchase price) of this Code.

#### Article 546.- Special provisions regarding land with deficient or excessive area

1- For any specific land that has been defined, in the case where the sale must

was made by specifying the area and defining the unit price of the land, but in fact the actual land area is less than the specified area, the buyer may demand the seller to perform for the deficient part, or demand a reduction in the price of the land in proportion to the deficient part, or rescind the contract and demand compensation for damages according to the provisions from Article 542 (Buyer's right to demand full performance) to Article 545 (Buyer's right to demand compensation for damages) of this Code, unless otherwise agreed between the parties.

2- For any specific land that has been defined, in the case where the sale was made by specifying the area and defining the unit price of the land, but in fact the actual land area exceeds the specified area, the seller who was unaware of the excess area and was not at fault for not knowing it may demand the buyer to pay an additional price for that excess area, unless otherwise agreed between the parties.

3- For any specific land that has been defined, in the case where the sale was made by specifying the area but defining only the price for the whole land, even if the land area is in fact less than the specified area, the buyer may not demand the seller to perform for the deficient part, or demand a reduction in the price of the land in proportion to the deficient part, or rescind the contract, or demand compensation for damages. However, this provision shall not apply to cases where the seller knew about the deficient land area, or in cases where the seller guaranteed the land area, or in cases where the deficient area exceeds 1/20 (one-twentieth).

4- For any specific land that has been defined, in the case where the sale was made by specifying the area but defining only the price for the whole land, even if in fact the actual land area exceeds the specified area, the seller may not demand the buyer to pay an additional price for the land. However, this provision shall not apply to cases where the excess area exceeds 1/20 (one-twentieth), and the seller was not aware of the excess area and was not at fault for not knowing it.

5- In the case where the seller demands an additional price according to the provisions of paragraph 2 or paragraph 4 of this Article, the buyer may rescind the contract.

#### Article 547.- Period during which rights can be exercised

1- The rights specified in Article 542 (Buyer's right to demand full performance) to Article 544 (Buyer's right to demand a reduction in the purchase price) and Article 546 (Special provisions regarding land with deficient or excessive area) of this Code must be exercised within 1 (one) year. However, the right to demand compensation for damages as defined in paragraph 1 and the second sentence of paragraph 3 of Article 546 (Special provisions regarding land with deficient or excessive area) shall conform to the provisions of Article 482 (Period of extinctive prescription for general claims) of this Code.

2- The period defined in the first sentence of paragraph 1 above shall be calculated from the time the buyer knows or should have known of the defects and the damages, while for the seller, it shall be calculated from the time of the contract.

#### Article 548.- Special agreement for exemption from or limitation of liability for warranty for defects

Even if there is an agreement for exemption from or limitation of the liability for warranty for defects that the seller must bear according to the provisions of Article 540 (Liability for warranty for defects) of this Code, for defects that the seller knew about but did not disclose, the seller cannot use that agreement for exemption or limitation as a defense.

#### Article 549.- Obligation to deliver the property which is the subject matter

1- The seller has an obligation to deliver the property which is the subject matter of the sale to the buyer at the time and place specified in the contract.

2- In the case where only a time for the payment of the purchase price is specified, it shall be presumed that the time for delivery of the property which is the subject matter is also the time for the payment of the purchase price.

3- In the case where neither the date for delivery nor the date for the payment of the purchase price is specified, the seller has an obligation to deliver the property which is the subject matter to the buyer when the buyer demands delivery.

4- In the case where the place for delivery is not specified, for the sale of specific property, the seller must deliver the property which is the subject matter at the place where the property was located at the time the contract was made, and for cases other than this, the property which is the subject matter must be delivered at the residence of the buyer.

5- The seller must bear the costs of delivery, unless there is a special agreement. However, if the cost of delivery increases because the buyer changed their residence or due to any act of the buyer, the buyer must bear the responsibility for the increased amount.

#### Article 550.- Method of delivery

The delivery of property must be carried out through the actual delivery of the subject matter, summary delivery, delivery by instruction, or delivery by change of possessory status, in accordance with the provisions of Article 229 (Transfer of possession) of this Code. The delivery of immovable property can be carried out by delivering the keys to the building or delivering the documents certifying the rights.

#### Article 551.- Right to defense of simultaneous performance and right of refusal based on insecurity

1- The seller can refuse to transfer the rights that are the subject matter and refuse to deliver the property that is the subject matter to the buyer until the buyer has provided payment for the obligation to pay the purchase price. However, this provision shall not be applied to cases where the obligation of the buyer has not yet become due, or in cases where there is an agreement regarding the performance of the obligation to transfer the rights or the obligation to deliver in advance.

2- Even if the seller has granted the buyer a period to pay the price of the subject matter, if the buyer becomes bankrupt or becomes insolvent after the sale contract was made, or if the buyer concealed their insolvency before the contract was made, the seller may also refuse to transfer the rights and refuse to deliver that subject matter. However, this provision shall not be applied to cases where the buyer has provided security or arranged measures to eliminate that insecurity.

#### Article 552.- Obligation to preserve the property that is the subject matter

1- A seller of property that has been specifically defined must preserve that property with the care of a good manager until that property is delivered to the buyer. This provision shall also be applied in the same manner to non-specific property after that property has been specifically defined.

2- In the case where the seller causes the loss of or damage to the property that is the subject matter because the seller was negligent and did not respect the obligation to take care as defined in paragraph 1 above, the seller must be responsible to the buyer according to the contract and the provisions of Section 3 (Compensation for damages), Chapter 4 of Part 4 of this Code.

#### Article 553.- Right to receive fruits

In the case where the property that is the subject matter of the sale has produced natural fruits before being delivered to the buyer, those fruits shall belong to the seller, unless there is a special agreement between the parties. This provision shall also be applied in the same manner to cases where the property that is the subject matter of the sale produces civil fruits such as rent, among others.

### Section 2
### Obligations of the Buyer

#### Article 554.- General obligations of the buyer

The buyer must have the obligation to pay the promised price in money and the obligation to receive the property that he/she has purchased.

#### Article 555.- Obligation to pay the purchase price

1- The buyer has the obligation to pay the purchase price in the amount of money specified in the contract at the time and place specified in the contract.

2- In the case of a sale of movable property where only the time for delivery of the movable property which is the subject matter of the sale is specified, or in the case of a sale of immovable property where only the time for performing necessary acts for registration of the immovable property which is the subject matter of the sale is specified, it shall be presumed that the time specified for payment of the purchase price is the same as the time specified for such delivery or performance of necessary acts for registration.

3- In cases where neither the time for delivery of the movable property which is the subject matter nor the time for performing necessary acts for registration of the immovable property which is the subject matter is specified, and the time for payment of the price is also not specified, the buyer has the obligation to pay the price to the seller when the seller demands that the buyer pay.

4- In cases where the place for payment of the purchase price is not specified in the contract, if payment is to be made concurrently with the delivery of the movable property which is the subject matter or concurrently with the performance of necessary acts for registration of the immovable property which is the subject matter, the buyer must pay the price at the place where the seller is to deliver the movable property which is the subject matter or perform the necessary acts for registration of the immovable property which is the subject matter.

#### Article 556.- Interest on the purchase price and compensation for damages due to delay

The buyer is not required to pay interest on the purchase price or compensation for damages due to delay until the object that is the subject matter has been delivered.

#### Article 557.- Right to refuse payment of the purchase price in case a third party claims rights over the object that is the subject matter of the sale

1- In cases where a third party claims rights over the object that is the subject matter of the sale and there is a concern that the buyer will lose all or part of the rights purchased, the buyer may refuse to pay all or part of the purchase price according to the extent of such risk. However, this provision shall not be applied to cases where the seller has provided adequate security.

2- In the cases specified in paragraph 1 above, the seller may demand that the buyer deposit the purchase price.

#### Article 558.- Right to demand simultaneous performance and right to object due to concern

1- The buyer may refuse to pay the purchase price until the seller has performed his/her obligations. However, this provision shall not be applied to cases where the seller's obligation is not yet due or cases where there is an agreement on advance payment of the price.

2- Even if the buyer has granted the seller a period to perform the obligation, if there is a clear concern that the seller will not perform his/her obligation, the buyer may also refuse to pay the purchase price. However, this provision shall not be applied to cases where the seller has provided security or taken measures to extinguish such concern.

#### Article 559.- Delay in performing the obligation to receive delivery

1- In cases where the buyer is in delay in performing the obligation to receive delivery, the seller may demand compensation for damages or rescind the contract in accordance with the provisions of Section 3 (Compensation for damages) and Section 4 (Rescission of contract) of Chapter 4 of Book 4.

2- In the cases specified in paragraph 1 above, if the object that is the subject matter is lost or damaged without the fault of the seller, the buyer shall bear the risk.

3- In cases where the seller has offered performance but the buyer still refuses to receive the object that is the subject matter, the seller may deposit or sell the object that is the subject matter in accordance with the provisions of Article 457 (Deposit) and Article 458 (Right to sell by oneself) of this Code.

### Section 5
### Rescission of sale contract by exercise of right of redemption

#### Article 560.- Special agreement for redemption

The seller may rescind the sale contract by returning the price paid by the buyer and the expenses specified in Article 563 (Method of exercising right of redemption) of this Code based on a special agreement for the right of redemption clearly specified in the contract instrument.

#### Article 561.- Period for redemption

1- The period for redemption cannot exceed 5 (five) years for immovable property and cannot exceed 2 (two) years for movable property. If a longer period has been specified, it shall be automatically reduced to 5 (five) years for immovable property and 2 (two) years for movable property.
