2- In cases where the parties have already specified a period for redemption...

The parties cannot extend that period.

3- In cases where no period for redemption has been specified, the seller must exercise the right of redemption within the period specified in paragraph 1 above.

#### Article 562.- Setting up of special agreement for redemption, etc.

1- The buyer may exercise all rights in the capacity of the owner. However, in cases where the exercise of such rights is contrary to the purpose of the special agreement for redemption, the buyer shall be responsible for non-performance of the obligation toward the seller.

2- A special agreement for redemption shall be effective against third parties only if such special agreement is registered concurrently with the sale contract.

#### Article 563.- Method of exercising right of redemption

1- The seller must return the purchase price and the expenses for the contract within the specified period in order to redeem.

2- In cases where the buyer or a successor to the object from the buyer has incurred expenses for the object that is the subject matter, the seller must reimburse the necessary expenses incurred for the management and preservation of the object. As for useful expenses, the seller must reimburse the buyer for the expenses incurred by the buyer or the successor to the object from the buyer, or the increased value thereof, at the seller's option, provided that the increased value still exists.

#### Article 564.- Sale of an object in co-ownership with a special agreement for redemption

1- In cases where co-owners of an object have sold the entire co-owned object with a special agreement for redemption by a single contract, and one of all the co-owners demands to redeem his/her share of the object, or the object...

the buyer is not required to agree to such redemption.

2- If all co-owners have sold their respective shares to a single buyer by separate contracts with a special agreement for redemption, each co-owner may redeem his/her respective share.

#### Article 565.- Effect of failure to exercise the right of redemption

If the seller fails to exercise the right of redemption within the period specified in Article 561 (Period for redemption) of this Code, the right of redemption shall be extinguished and the buyer's ownership shall become final.

### Chapter 2
### Exchange

#### Article 566.- Meaning

An exchange shall be effective based on a mutual agreement by which both parties transfer to each other property rights other than money.

#### Article 567.- Application of provisions on sale

The provisions concerning sale shall also apply to exchange.

### Chapter 3
### Gift

#### Article 568.- Definition

A gift refers to a contract that shall be effective when one party expresses his/her intention to give property to another party without consideration, and the other party...

accepts it.

#### Article 569.- Transfer of ownership

The transfer of ownership of the object that is the subject matter in a gift contract shall be in accordance with the general principles specified in Article 133 (Creation, transfer, and modification of real rights by agreement), Article 134 (Requirement for setting up creation, transfer, and modification of real rights), Article 135 (Requirement for creation of effect of transfer of ownership over immovable property by agreement), Article 160 (Requirement for ownership over immovable property), and Article 187 (Requirement for ownership over movable property) of this Code.

#### Article 570.- Gift not made in writing

Even if a promise of gift has been made, if the gift is not made in writing, either party to the contract may rescind the contract by withdrawing the expression of intention to make the gift. However, for any part that has already been performed, the expression of intention to make the gift cannot be withdrawn.

#### Article 571.- Revocation of gift due to breach of trust, etc.

1- In cases where the donee has committed a serious breach of trust against the donor, the donor may revoke the gift.

2- For any part that has already been performed, the revocation of the gift as specified in paragraph 1 above may only be made within 5 (five) years from the time such serious breach of trust occurred.

#### Article 572.- Revocation of gift due to poverty of the donor

1- If the donor falls into poverty and cannot maintain his/her own livelihood and the persons for whom he/she has an obligation to support after expressing the intention to make the gift, the donor may revoke that gift.

2- For any part that has already been performed, the revocation of the gift as specified in paragraph 1 above may only be made within 5 (five) years from the time of performance.

#### Article 573.- Effect of revocation of gift

In cases where a gift is revoked in accordance with the provisions of Article 571 (Revocation of gift due to breach of trust, etc.) and Article 572 (Revocation of gift due to poverty of the donor) above, the donor may demand the return of the property given as a gift based on the provisions concerning the return of enrichment without cause.

#### Article 574.- Reduction of liability for warranty

The donor shall not be liable for any defects or deficiencies in the object or rights that are the subject matter of the gift. However, this provision shall not be applied if the donor was aware of the defects or deficiencies but failed to inform the donee.

#### Article 575.- Gift for periodic support

A gift made for the purpose of providing periodic support shall lose its effect upon the death of either the donor or the donee.

#### Article 576.- Encumbered gift

1- A gift in which the donee has an obligation to perform periodic acts of performance in order to receive the property that is the subject matter of the gift shall be called an "encumbered gift".

2- In an encumbered gift, the donor shall be liable for warranty in the same manner as a seller, but only to the extent of the encumbrance.

3- In cases where one party has performed...

encumbrance, such person may demand that the other party perform the act.

4- In an encumbered gift, unless there is a special agreement, the other party to the contract may refuse to perform his/her part until the party who is required to perform fails to do so.

5- The donor of an encumbered gift may rescind the encumbered gift contract in accordance with the provisions on rescission of contract in cases where the other party fails to perform the encumbrance.

#### Article 577.- Gift effective upon death

1- A gift that becomes effective upon the death of the donor shall be called a "gift effective upon death".

2- The provisions concerning wills shall also apply to the effect of a gift effective upon death.

### Chapter 4
### Loan for consumption

### Section 1
### Definition and formation of loan for consumption contract

#### Article 578.- Definition of loan for consumption

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code.)

A loan for consumption refers to a contract by which one party, called the lender, has an obligation to deliver for free use money, food, rice, or other fungible objects for a specified period to another party, called the borrower, and after such period has elapsed, the borrower has an obligation to return to the lender...

an object of the same type, quality, and quantity as the object received from the lender.

#### Article 579.- Formation of loan for consumption contract

A loan for consumption contract shall be formed solely by the agreement between the lender and the borrower.

#### Article 580.- Withdrawal from interest-free loan for consumption contract not made in writing

In the case of an interest-free loan for consumption contract that is not made in writing, either party to the contract may withdraw from the contract at any time. However, this provision shall not be applied to any part of the object that is the subject matter that has already been delivered by the lender.

#### Article 581.- Rescission due to deterioration of borrower's credit

1- During the period from the formation of the contract until the delivery of the object that is the subject matter, if the economic situation of the borrower deteriorates significantly such that it would hinder the performance of the obligation to return, the lender may rescind the loan for consumption contract.

2- If the lender rescinds the contract based on the provisions of paragraph 1 above, in cases where the lender has received interest, commissions, or other charges in advance, the lender must return them to the borrower.

#### Article 582.- Quasi-loan for consumption

1- If a person who has an obligation to provide money or other fungible objects for reasons other than a loan for consumption has agreed with the creditor to make such objects the subject matter of a loan for consumption, a loan for consumption shall be formed by such agreement.

2- In the cases specified in paragraph 1 above, security for the old obligation shall be presumed to have become security for the new obligation.

### Section 2
### Loan for consumption with interest

#### Article 583.- Interest claims

1- Interest refers to an amount of money or other objects provided in proportion to the period of use as consideration for the use of money or objects delivered to the borrower, which shall be calculated by multiplying the amount of money or objects borrowed by a specified rate.

2- In the provisions of paragraph 1 above, the object delivered to the borrower is called the "principal", and the specified portion added to the amount of the principal is called the "interest rate".

3- The parties to a loan for consumption contract may agree to make interest payments the subject matter of a new claim. However, an agreement to create a claim for interest shall be ineffective unless it is made in writing and signed by the borrower.

4- In an agreement to create a claim for interest without referring to a written instrument signed by the borrower, if the borrower has made payment knowing the application of the provision of the second sentence of paragraph 3 above, the payment shall be effective only to the extent of the amount to be paid.

#### Article 584.- Legal interest rate and contractual interest rate

1- The interest rate may be specified by law or by agreement of...

the parties.

2- In cases where interest is to be paid by agreement between the parties or by provision of law, if the parties have not specified the interest rate, the legal interest rate specified in this Code or special laws shall apply.

3- An interest rate specified by agreement of the parties that exceeds the legal interest rate shall be ineffective unless it is specified in a written instrument based on the formula specified in paragraph 3 of Article 583 (Interest claims) above.

#### Article 585.- Limitation of interest

1- The limited interest rate refers to the maximum interest rate that can be legally specified by agreement of the parties as specified by law and other legal norms.

2- The parties may specify an interest rate higher than the legal interest rate specified in paragraph 2 of Article 584 (Legal interest rate and contractual interest rate) above, but it cannot exceed the limited interest rate.

3- If the parties have specified an interest rate exceeding the limited interest rate, the portion of the contract exceeding such limited interest rate shall be void, and the borrower shall only be obligated to pay interest calculated at the limited interest rate.

4- In cases where interest exceeding the limited interest rate has already been paid, the portion exceeding such limit shall be deemed to have been applied to the principal. In cases where the principal has been fully paid off by such application, the lender must return to the borrower any remaining amount exceeding the limit...

together with compensation for damages calculated at the legal interest rate from the day such remainder was created until the day of such return.

5- For the purpose of applying this Article, in cases where the amount of principal in the contract differs from the amount of the object actually delivered by the lender, such as in the case of delivering the principal by deducting interest to be paid in the future, the amount of the object actually delivered to the borrower by the lender shall be deemed to be the principal amount.

6- For the purpose of applying this Article, money and other objects received by the lender other than the principal in connection with the loan for consumption contract, whether received in the name of a bonus, commission, investigation fee, or any other charge, shall be deemed to be interest. However, this provision shall not be applied to the expenses for making the contract and the expenses for performance.

#### Article 586.- Time for payment of interest and legal compound interest

1- Unless there is a special agreement, interest shall be paid every time a period of 1 (one) year has elapsed from the time of delivery of the object that is the subject matter. However, if the object that is the subject matter is to be returned before a period of 1 (one) year has elapsed, the interest shall be paid at the time of such return.

2- In cases where there is a delay in the payment of interest for a period of 1 (one) year or more, and the borrower still fails to pay the interest despite the lender's demand, the lender may incorporate such interest into the principal.

### Section 3
### Lending obligations of the lender

#### Article 587.- Obligation to lend the object that is the subject matter

1- The lender has an obligation to allow the borrower to use the object in accordance with the contract.

2- The borrower may freely use, enjoy the fruits of, and dispose of the object, and shall bear the risk of loss or damage to the object that is the subject matter occurring due to reasons not attributable to the lender, in the capacity of the owner, from the day the borrower receives delivery of the object that is the subject matter.

#### Article 588.- Liability for warranty of the lender in case of delivery of an object belonging to another person

1- In a loan for consumption contract with interest, a lender who has delivered an object that is not under his/her ownership has an obligation to acquire the ownership of such object and transfer it to the borrower, or to replace it with another object under his/her ownership. However, once the borrower has already consumed the object, the borrower cannot demand a replacement object.

2- If the lender cannot perform the obligation specified in paragraph 1 above, the borrower may rescind the contract.

3- In the cases specified in paragraphs 1 and 2 above, if the borrower received delivery without knowing that the lender had no right to the object, the borrower may demand compensation for damages from the lender.

4- In an interest-free loan for consumption contract, the provisions of paragraphs 1, 2, and 3 above shall apply only in cases where the lender knew that he/she had no right to dispose of the object that is the subject matter...

without informing the borrower.

5- Except in cases where a replacement object has been delivered in accordance with the provisions of paragraph 1 or 4 above, if the borrower of an object belonging to another person has returned the object provided or its value to the owner, the borrower shall be discharged from his/her obligation toward the lender.

#### Article 589.- Lender's right to rescind in case of lending an object belonging to another person

A lender who has delivered an object that is the subject matter without knowing that the object belongs to another person may rescind the contract if he/she cannot cause the borrower to acquire ownership of such object or a replacement object. However, if the borrower was in good faith and has a right to demand compensation for damages in accordance with the provisions of Article 588 (Liability for warranty of the lender in case of delivery of an object belonging to another person) above, the lender may rescind the contract only after compensating the borrower for such damages.

#### Article 590.- Liability for warranty of the lender for defects in the object

1- In a loan for consumption contract with interest, if there is a latent defect in the object delivered by the lender, a borrower who received delivery without knowing of such defect may demand the replacement of the object with one that has no defect and may demand compensation for damages.

2- In an interest-free loan for consumption contract, a borrower who received delivery of an object with a defect may return the value of the defective object. However, the provisions of paragraph 1 above shall also apply if the lender knew of the defect but failed to inform the borrower.

#### Section 4
#### Obligations of the Borrower to Return

**Article 591.- Obligations of the Borrower to Return the Principal and Pay Interest**

1-The borrower shall have an obligation to return to the lender items of the same type, quality and quantity as the items received at the time fixed for return.

2-The borrower under a loan for consumption with interest shall pay interest in accordance with the provisions from Article 584 (Legal interest rate and agreed interest rate) to Article 586 (Time for payment of interest and compound interest prescribed by law) of this Code.

**Article 592.- Return by Reference to Value**

1-If it is impossible to return based on the provisions of Article 591 (Obligations of the Borrower to Return the Principal and Pay Interest) above, the borrower shall have an obligation to pay an amount of money equivalent to the value of the subject matter at the place and time fixed for return.

2-In the case provided for in paragraph 1 above, if the value or place of return is not fixed, it shall be deemed that an amount of money equivalent to the value at the time and place where the contract was made shall be paid.

**Article 593.- Changes in Value of Currency, etc.**

Even if there is a change in the value of currency or the value of items before the time for return, except in cases where there is a special agreement, the borrower shall return an amount equal to the amount received at the time of delivery.

**Article 594.- Time for Return**

1-If the parties have fixed a date for return, the lender may not demand

return the subject matter until the time fixed for return in accordance with the date of the contract. However, this provision shall not apply to cases where any of the grounds set forth in Article 331 (Loss of benefit of time) of this Code has occurred.

2-If the parties have not fixed a date for return, the lender may demand return by fixing a reasonable period of time.

3-The borrower under a loan for consumption without interest may return the items at any time.

4-The borrower under a loan for consumption with interest may return the items before the time fixed for return. However, if the lender suffers damages due to such return, the borrower shall compensate for such damages in accordance with the provisions of paragraph 2 of Article 330 (Benefit of time) of this Code.

**Article 595.- Place of Return**

If there is no agreement between the parties regarding the place of return, the borrower shall return the subject matter to the residence of the lender.

#### Chapter 5
#### Lease

#### Section 1
#### General Provisions

**Article 596.- Definition of Lease**

1-A lease refers to a contract whereby one party allows the other party to use and receive profits from a certain thing for a fee.

2-The object of a lease may be either a movable or an immovable.

**Article 597.- Formation of Lease**

A lease shall be formed when one party promises to allow the other party to use and receive profits from a certain thing and the other party promises to pay rent for it.

**Article 598.- Perfection of Lease of Immovable**

1-By continuously possessing, using and receiving profits from the leased property, a lessee under a lease of an immovable may set up the lease against a person who has acquired a real right over the immovable after the lessee has taken possession.

2-A lessee who is in possession of the leased property may exercise the right to demand return, the right to demand cessation of interference and the right to demand prevention of interference against any infringement of the lease, in the same manner as an owner.

**Article 599.- Term of Lease**

1-A lease may be made with or without a fixed term.

2-A lease of an immovable that is not made in writing shall be deemed to be a lease without a fixed term.

3-A lease of an immovable with a fixed term of 15 (fifteen) years or more shall be governed by the provisions from Article 244 (Definition of perpetual lease) of this Code.

#### Section 2
#### Effect of Lease

**Article 600.- Rights and Obligations of Use and Receipt of Profits Based on Manner of Use by Lessee**

1-A lessee shall have a right and an obligation to use and receive profits from the leased property in a manner prescribed by the contract or according to the nature of the property.

2-If the lessee violates the obligation set forth in paragraph 1 above, the lessor may rescind the contract.

3-The lessor shall not interfere with the lessee's use and receipt of profits that are based on the prescribed manner of use.

**Article 601.- Obligation to Manage with Care of a Good Manager by Lessee**

1-A lessee shall have an obligation to manage the leased property with the care of a good manager.

2-If the lessee violates the obligation set forth in paragraph 1 above, the lessor may rescind the contract.

**Article 602.- Obligation of Lessor to Repair**

The lessor shall have an obligation to effect repairs necessary for the use and receipt of profits from the leased property.

**Article 603.- Preservation Activities by Lessor**

1-If the lessor intends to carry out activities necessary for the preservation of the leased property, the lessee cannot refuse.

2-In cases where the lessor intends to carry out activities necessary for the preservation of the leased property against the will of the lessee, if the lessee is thereby rendered unable to achieve the purpose of the lease, the lessee may demand a reduction in the rent or rescind the contract.

**Article 604.- Right to Demand Reimbursement of Expenses Incurred by Lessee**

1-If the lessee has incurred necessary expenses that should be borne by the lessor for the leased property, the lessee may immediately demand reimbursement for such expenses from the lessor.

2-If the lessee has incurred expenses for the improvement of the leased property or other expenses to increase its value, the lessee may demand from the lessor, only upon the termination of the lease and provided that the increase in value still exists, the reimbursement of the expenses incurred or the amount of the increase in value, at the option of the lessor. However, the court may, upon the application of the lessor, grant a reasonable period for reimbursement.

**Article 605.- Liability of Lessor for Defect in Leased Property**

(To be further amended by the Law on Implementation of the Civil Code.)

1-If the lessee failed to inspect the condition of the subject matter of the lease at the time of delivery to ensure it conforms to the condition specified in the contract, the lessee may not hold the lessor liable for any discrepancy from the condition specified in the contract that the lessee could have easily discovered upon inspection.

2-In cases where the leased property has a latent defect and the lessee was unaware of such defect, the lessee may demand repair of the defect or replacement with property without defect, and may also demand compensation for damages.

3-The lessor may repair the defect at his/her own expense, provided that it does not cause

unreasonable interference with the lessee's interest. However, the lessee shall not be prevented from exercising the right to demand compensation for damages.

4-Instead of the demand specified in paragraph 2 above, the lessee may demand a reduction in the rent commensurate with the defect, starting from the time of delivery.

5-If the purpose of the contract cannot be achieved due to the latent defect, the lessee may rescind the lease.

6-The demands for repair, replacement, reduction of rent and rescission must be exercised within 1 (one) year from the time the lessee became aware or should have become aware of the grounds.

**Article 606.- Right of Rescission and Right to Demand Reduction of Rent in Case of Decrease in Income**

1-If a lessee of land for the purpose of receiving profits from the land receives profits in an amount less than the rent due to force majeure, the lessee may demand a reduction in the rent to the amount of such profits.

2-In the case provided for in paragraph 1 above, if the profits received are less than the rent for more than 2 (two) consecutive years due to force majeure, the lessee may rescind the lease.

**Article 607.- Right of Rescission and Right to Demand Reduction of Rent Due to Partial Loss or Destruction of Leased Property**

1-If a part of the leased property is lost or destroyed through no fault of the lessee, the lessee may demand a reduction in the rent in proportion to the part lost or destroyed.

2-In the case provided for in paragraph 1 above, if the lessee cannot achieve the purpose

of the lease with the remaining part, the lessee may rescind the lease.

**Article 608.- Assignment of Lease and Sublease of Leased Property**

1-A lessee may not assign the lease or sublease the leased property without the consent of the lessor, except in the case of a perpetual lease.

2-If the lessee allows a third party to use or receive profits from the leased property in violation of the provision of paragraph 1 above, the lessor may rescind the contract.

**Article 609.- Sublease**

1-If the lessee has legally subleased the leased property, the sublessee shall be directly liable to the lessor. In respect of the obligation to pay rent under the sublease, the sublessee may not set up against the lessor a payment of rent made in advance to the lessee.

2-The provisions of paragraph 1 above shall not prevent the lessor from exercising his/her rights against the lessee.

**Article 610.- Obligation and Time for Payment of Rent**

1-The lessee shall have an obligation to pay rent to the lessor at the time fixed.

2-In cases where there is no special agreement in the contract regarding the time for payment of rent, for movables, houses or buildings, the rent shall be paid at the end of each month; for land, it shall be paid at the end of each year. However, for property with a harvest season, the rent shall be paid without delay after such season has passed.

**Article 611.- Obligation of Lessee to Give Notice**

If a need for repair of the leased property arises, or if a third party claims a right over the leased property, the lessee shall without delay give notice thereof to the lessor. However,

this provision shall not apply if the lessor was already aware of such fact.

#### Section 3
#### Termination of Lease

**Article 612.- Expiration of Fixed Term**

A lease with a fixed term shall terminate upon the expiration of such term.

**Article 613.- Refusal to Renew Lease of Immovable**

Under a lease of an immovable, if neither party manifests an intention to refuse renewal to the other party at least 3 (three) months before the expiration of the term for a house or building, or at least 1 (one) year before the expiration of the term for land, the lease shall be deemed to have been renewed. However, in this case, the renewed lease shall be a lease without a fixed term.

**Article 614.- Implicit Renewal**

Under a lease of a movable, in cases where the lessee continues to use or receive profits from the leased property after the expiration of the term of the lease, if the lessor was aware of such fact and did not object, the lease shall be presumed to have been renewed without a fixed term and on the same conditions as the previous lease, except for the term.

**Article 615.- Demand for Rescission of Lease without Fixed Term**

1-If the term of the lease is not fixed in the contract, either party may at any time demand the rescission of the contract. In cases where the demand for rescission does not specify the time of rescission, and in cases where the period from the time of the demand for rescission

is specified in the demand for rescission, but is less than the period set forth below, the lease shall terminate upon the expiration of the following periods from the time of the demand for rescission:
(a) 1 (one) day for movables;
(b) 3 (three) months for buildings;
(c) 1 (one) year for land.

2-In the case of a lease of land where there is a harvest season, the demand for rescission shall be made after the harvest season but before the commencement of cultivation for the next season.

**Article 616.- Reservation of Right to Rescind Contract**

Even in cases where the parties have fixed the term of the lease, the provisions of Article 615 (Demand for rescission of lease without fixed term) above shall also apply if one or both parties have reserved the right to rescind the contract during such term.

**Article 617.- Non-Retroactive Effect of Rescission of Contract**

In cases where a lease has been rescinded, the rescission of the contract shall be effective only for the future.

**Article 618.- Obligation to Return Leased Property**

1-When a lease is terminated, the lessee shall have an obligation to immediately return the leased property to the lessor.

2-Except for wear and tear resulting from normal use, the lessee shall repair any damage occurring for reasons for which he/she is responsible, or shall be liable to pay compensation for such damage.

**Article 619.- Lessee's Right to Remove Improvements**

Upon termination of the lease, the lessee may remove items that he/she has attached to the leased property.

**Article 620.- Successor's Right to Rescind Contract in Case of Death of Lessee**

If the successor of the lessee does not intend to continue the lease, he/she may rescind the contract.

**Article 621.- Period for Exercising Right to Demand Compensation for Damages and Demand for Reimbursement of Expenses**

Compensation for damages resulting from the lessee using or receiving profits from the leased property in a manner contrary to the nature of the contract, and the reimbursement of expenses incurred by the lessee, must be demanded within 1 (one) year from the time the lessor receives the returned property.

#### Section 4
#### Profit-Sharing Lease

**Article 622.- Definition of Profit-Sharing Lease**

A contract whereby an owner allows a lessee to borrow land or livestock for the purpose of receiving profits and sharing such profits between the lessor and the lessee is called a profit-sharing lease.

**Article 623.- Method of Sharing Profits**

If the method of sharing profits is not specified, the profits shall be shared equally.

**Article 624.- Right of Lessee to Dispose of Profits**

Only after the portion of the profits to be given to the lessor has been distributed may the lessee under a profit-sharing lease dispose of his/her own share of the profits.

#### Chapter 6
#### Loan for Use

**Article 625.- Definition of Loan for Use**

A loan for use refers to a contract whereby one party allows the other party to use and receive profits from a certain thing without charge.

**Article 626.- Formation of Loan for Use**

A loan for use shall be formed when one party receives a certain thing from the other party and promises to return the thing after having used and received profits from it without charge.

**Article 627.- Obligations of Borrower in Respect of Use and Receipt of Profits**

1-The borrower shall manage the borrowed property with the care of a good manager.

2-The borrower shall use and receive profits from the borrowed property in a manner prescribed by the contract or according to the nature of the property.

3-The borrower may not allow a third party to use or receive profits from the borrowed property without the consent of the lender.

4-If the borrower violates any of the provisions of paragraph 1, 2 or 3 above, the lender may rescind the contract.

**Article 628.- Burden of Expenses**

1-The borrower shall bear the ordinary necessary expenses for the borrowed property.

2-If the borrower has incurred extraordinary necessary expenses, such as expenses for major repairs, other than the ordinary necessary expenses, or has incurred expenses to improve or otherwise increase the value of the borrowed property, the borrower may, upon the termination of the loan for use, demand from the lender, in respect of such expenses, the reimbursement of the expenses incurred or the increase in value, at the option of the lender, to the extent that the increase in value still exists. However, the court may, upon the application of the lender, grant a reasonable period for reimbursement.

**Article 629.- Responsibility of Lender for Warranty**

The lender shall not be responsible for any warranty for defects in the property forming the subject matter of the loan for use or for any lack of title. However, this provision shall not apply if the lender was aware of the defect or lack of title but failed to give notice thereof to the borrower.

**Article 630.- Termination of Loan for Use Upon Expiration of Term or Completion of Use and Receipt of Profits**

1-A loan for use with a fixed term shall terminate upon the expiration of such term.

2-In cases where the parties have not fixed the term, if the purpose of the loan for use was specified, the loan for use shall terminate when the borrower has completed the use and receipt of profits from the borrowed property in accordance with such purpose. However, if the borrower has not completed the use and receipt of profits due to his/her negligence, the loan for use shall terminate upon the expiration of a period sufficient for such use and receipt of profits.

**Article 631.- Demand of Rescission by Lender**

1-If neither the term nor the purpose of the loan for use has been fixed, the lender may demand the rescission of the loan for use at any time.

2-Even before the expiration of the term or the completion of the use and receipt of profits, the lender may demand the rescission of the loan for use if an urgent and necessary need for the borrowed property arises which the lender could not have foreseen.

**Article 632.- Demand of Rescission by Borrower**

The borrower may demand the rescission of the loan for use at any time, whether or not the term has been fixed. However, if the lender has an interest in the term, the borrower shall be liable for any damages resulting from the demand for rescission.

**Article 633.- Termination of Loan for Use Upon Death of Borrower**

A loan for use shall terminate upon the death of the borrower.

**Article 634.- Obligation to Return Borrowed Property**

1-Upon termination of the loan for use, the borrower shall have an obligation to immediately return the borrowed property to the lender.

2-Except for wear and tear resulting from normal use, the borrower shall repair any damage occurring for reasons for which he/she is responsible, or shall be liable to pay compensation for such damage.

**Article 635.- Borrower's Right to Remove Improvements**

Upon termination of the loan for use, the borrower may remove items that he/she has attached to the borrowed property.

**Article 636.- Period for Exercising Right to Demand Compensation for Damages and Demand for Reimbursement of Expenses**

Compensation for damages resulting from the borrower using or receiving profits from the borrowed property in a manner contrary to the nature of the contract, and the reimbursement of expenses incurred by the borrower, must be demanded within 1 (one) year from the time the lender receives the returned property.

#### Chapter 7
#### Mandate

**Article 637.- Definition of Mandate**

A mandate refers to a contract whereby one party, called the mandator, grants the other party, called the mandatary, the power to manage affairs for the mandator.

**Article 638.- Principle of Gratuity of Mandate**

1-A mandate may be either for or without compensation. If no intention is shown to the effect that it is to be for compensation, the mandate shall be presumed to be without compensation.

2-The provisions of Article 644 (Right of mandatary to demand compensation) of this Code shall apply to compensation in the case of a mandate for compensation.

**Article 639.- Formality of Contract Not Required**

A contract of mandate shall be formed by the mere agreement of the parties.

**Article 640.- Mandatary's Obligation of Care**

1-A mandatary shall have an obligation to manage the mandated affairs with the care of a good manager, in accordance with the nature of the mandate. However, this provision

shall not prevent the reduction of the mandatary's obligation of care by agreement of the parties.

2-If the mandatary violates the obligation of care set forth in paragraph 1 above and causes damage to the mandator, the mandator may demand compensation for damages from the mandatary. In this case, if the mandate is without compensation, the court may reduce the amount of the compensation for damages.

**Article 641.- Mandatary's Obligation to Report**

The mandatary shall, at any time upon the demand of the mandator, report on the status of the management of the mandated affairs, and shall, without delay after the termination of the mandate, report on the results of such management.

**Article 642.- Mandatary's Obligation to Deliver**

1-The mandatary shall deliver to the mandator money and other things received in the course of the management of the mandated affairs. The mandatary shall also deliver any interest or profits collected.

2-Rights acquired by the mandatary in his/her own name for the mandator shall be transferred to the mandator.

**Article 643.- Mandatary's Obligation to Pay Interest and Compensate for Damages from Use of Money**

If the mandatary has used for his/her own benefit money that should have been delivered to the mandator or money to be used for the mandator's benefit, the mandatary shall pay interest from the day on which he/she used it. If there is any damage, the mandatary shall also be liable to compensate for such damage.

**Article 644.- Right of Mandatary to Demand Compensation**

1-The mandatary may not demand compensation from the mandator unless there is a special agreement in the contract.

2-In the case of a mandate for compensation, the mandatary may not demand compensation unless he/she has completed the performance of the obligations required by the mandate. However, if the compensation is fixed by period, the mandatary may demand it after the expiration of such period.

3-If the performance of the mandate has been terminated partway through for reasons not attributable to the mandatary, the mandatary may demand compensation in proportion to the part of the performance already completed.

**Article 645.- Mandator's Obligation to Pay Expenses in Advance**

If expenses are necessary for the management of the mandated affairs, the mandator shall, upon the demand of the mandatary, pay such expenses in advance.

**Article 646.- Mandatary's Right to Demand Reimbursement of Expenses, etc.**

1-If the mandatary has incurred expenses recognized as necessary for the management of the mandated affairs, the mandatary may demand from the mandator the reimbursement of such expenses and interest from the day on which they were incurred.

2-If the mandatary has assumed an obligation recognized as necessary for the management of the mandated affairs, the mandatary may require the mandator to perform such obligation in his/her stead or, if the obligation is not yet due, to provide appropriate security.

3-If the mandatary has suffered damage which was not caused by his/her fault in the course of the management of the mandated affairs, the mandatary may demand compensation for such damage from the mandator. However, if the damage was caused by the intent or fault of

a third party, the mandatary may demand compensation from the mandator only to the extent that he/she has not received compensation from such third party.

**Article 647.- Rescission of Mandate**

1-Either party may rescind the mandate at any time.

2-A party who rescinds the mandate at a time when it would be disadvantageous to the other party shall compensate the other party for any damages. However, this shall not apply if there are unavoidable reasons.

**Article 648.- Non-Retroactive Effect of Rescission of Mandate**

In cases where a mandate is rescinded, the rescission shall be effective only for the future. However, this shall not prevent a demand for compensation for damages resulting from such rescission if either party was at fault.

**Article 649.- Grounds for Termination of Mandate**

1-In addition to the rescission of the mandate under the provisions of Article 647 (Rescission of mandate) of this Code, a mandate shall terminate on the following grounds:
(a) The death of the mandator or the mandatary.
(b) The mandator or the mandatary being declared bankrupt.
(c) The mandator being the subject of an order for commencement of general guardianship or commencement of curatorship.
(d) The mandatary being the subject of an order for commencement of general guardianship or commencement of curatorship.
(e) The dissolution of a mandator or mandatary that is a legal entity.
(f) The merger of a mandator or mandatary that is a legal entity.

merger with another legal entity.
(g) The occurrence of any other ground fixed by agreement of the parties.

2-The parties may agree that the mandate shall not terminate even if any of the grounds fixed in items (a) through (f) of paragraph 1 above occurs. However, this shall not apply to the death of the mandatary, the commencement of general guardianship for the mandatary, or the dissolution or merger of a mandatary that is a legal entity.

**Article 650.- Mandatary's Obligation of Urgent Management After Termination of Mandate**

In cases where a mandate has terminated, if an urgent situation arises, the mandatary, his/her successor or legal representative shall take necessary measures until the mandator, his/her successor or legal representative is able to manage the mandated affairs.

**Article 651.- Conditions for Setting Up Termination of Mandate**

The grounds for termination of a mandate, whether occurring on the part of the mandator or the mandatary, may not be set up against the other party unless the other party has been notified thereof or was aware of such grounds.

#### Chapter 8
#### Contract for Work

**Article 652.- Definition of Contract for Work**

A contract for work refers to a contract whereby one party has an obligation to complete an agreed work and the other party has an obligation to pay compensation for the results of such work.

**Article 653.- Time for Payment of Compensation**

Compensation shall be paid simultaneously with the delivery of the object of the work. If delivery of an object is not required, the contractor may demand payment of the compensation after the work has been completed.

**Article 654.- Obligation to Complete Work Without Defects**

1-The contractor shall have an obligation to complete the work without defects for the principal.

2-If the work does not have the qualities agreed upon, it shall be deemed to have defects. In cases where the qualities were not agreed upon, if the work is not suitable for the use specified in the contract, or if no such use was specified, if the work is not suitable for ordinary use, it shall be deemed to have defects.

3-If the contractor has acted contrary to instructions or if the work performed is insufficient in quantity, it shall be deemed to have defects.

**Article 655.- Demand for Complete Performance**

1-If the work has defects, the principal may demand complete performance from the contractor within a reasonable period. In this case, the contractor may repair

the defects or redo the work at his/her option.

2-The contractor may refuse complete performance if it would require excessive expenses compared to the damage caused by the defect.

3-If the contractor has recommenced the work, he/she may demand that the principal return the defective work.

**Article 656.- Repair of Defects by Principal**

1-After the expiration of a reasonable period fixed for complete performance, the principal may repair the defect him/herself and demand from the contractor the necessary expenses incurred for such repair.

2-In cases where complete performance is not achieved, or in cases where it is estimated that complete performance by the contractor would cause undue disadvantage to the principal, the principal may repair the defect him/herself and demand from the contractor the necessary expenses for the repair.

3-In the cases set forth in paragraphs 1 and 2 above, the principal may demand from the contractor the advance payment of the necessary expenses for the repair of the defect.

4-The provisions of paragraphs 1, 2, and 3 above shall not apply if the contractor has refused complete performance in accordance with the provisions of paragraph 2 of Article 655 (Demand for complete performance) above.

**Article 657.- Principal's Right of Rescission**

1-The principal may rescind the contract on the ground of defects in the work in accordance with the provisions of Section 4 (Rescission of contract) of Chapter 4 of Part 4. This provision

shall also apply in cases where complete performance is not achieved, or in cases where it is estimated that complete performance by the contractor would cause undue disadvantage to the principal.

2-The provisions of paragraph 1 above shall not apply to buildings or other structures on land. However, the provisions of paragraph 1 above shall apply if the structure is of no use to the principal due to severe defects.

**Article 658.- Principal's Right to Demand Reduction of Compensation**

1-After the expiration of a reasonable period fixed for complete performance, the principal may demand a reduction in compensation by manifesting such intention to the contractor on the ground of the defects in the work. This provision shall also apply in cases where complete performance is not achieved, or in cases where it is estimated that complete performance by the contractor would cause undue disadvantage to the principal.

2-The compensation shall be reduced upon the demand for reduction in proportion to the value of the work with defects compared to the unit price of the work without defects.

**Article 659.- Principal's Right to Demand Compensation for Damages**

1-The principal may demand compensation for damages in accordance with the provisions of Section 3 (Compensation for damages) of Chapter 4 of Part 4, either in lieu of or together with the rights provided for in Articles 655 (Demand for complete performance) through 658 (Principal's right to demand reduction of compensation) of this Code. However, for complete performance in lieu of compensation for damages

after the expiration of a reasonable period fixed for complete performance, the principal may demand compensation for damages only in cases where complete performance is not achieved, or in cases where it is estimated that complete performance by the contractor would cause undue disadvantage to the principal.

2-If complete performance would require excessive expenses compared to the damage caused by the defect, the principal may not demand the amount of expenses necessary for the repair of the defect as compensation for damages pursuant to the provisions of paragraph 1 above.

**Article 660.- Cases Where Defect Arises from Materials Provided or Instructions Given by Principal**

1-The provisions of Articles 655 (Demand for complete performance) through 659 (Principal's right to demand compensation for damages) above shall not apply if the defect in the work arose from the nature of the materials provided by the principal or the instructions given by the principal. However, a mere expression of the principal's wishes shall not be deemed an instruction.

2-The provisions of paragraph 1 above shall not apply if the contractor, while aware of the unsuitability of the materials or the impropriety of the instructions, failed to give notice thereof.

**Article 661.- Period for Exercising Rights**

1-The rights provided for in Articles 655 (Demand for complete performance) through 658 (Principal's right to demand reduction of compensation) above

shall be exercised within 1 (one) year.

2-The period set forth in paragraph 1 above shall be calculated from the time when the principal knew or should have known of the existence of the defect.

**Article 662.- Special Agreement for Exemption from Warranty**

Even if there is a special agreement for the exemption from or limitation of liability under the provisions of Articles 655 (Demand for complete performance) through 659 (Principal's right to demand compensation for damages) above, the contractor may not be exempt from or limited in his/her liability in respect of any fact of which he/she was aware but failed to give notice.

**Article 663.- Principal's Right of Rescission Before Completion of Work**

Until the contractor has completed the work, the principal may at any time rescind the contract by paying compensation for damages.

#### Chapter 9
#### Employment Contract

**Article 664.- Employment Contract**

1-An employment contract shall be formed when one party promises to perform labor for the other party and the other party promises to pay wages for such labor.

2-The party who promises to perform labor is called the employee, and the other party is called the employer.

**Article 665.- Specification of Working Conditions**

1-Upon entry into an employment contract, the employer shall specify the wages, working hours, and other working conditions for the employee.

2-In cases where the working conditions specified in accordance with the provisions of paragraph 1 above differ from the facts, the employee may immediately rescind the employment contract.

**Article 666.- Obligation of Care for Safety**

The employer shall have an obligation to take care to protect the life, body, and health of the employee from danger at the place where the employee performs labor, in respect of the facilities, equipment, and other materials used.

**Article 667.- Personal Nature of Employment Contract**

1-The employer may not assign his/her rights to a third party without the consent of the employee.

2-The employee may not have a third party perform labor in his/her stead without the consent of the employer.

3-If either party to an employment contract violates the provisions of paragraph 1 or 2 above, the other party may rescind the employment contract.

**Article 668.- Application of Labor Law**

In respect of employment contracts, in addition to the provisions of this Code, the provisions of the Labor Law shall apply.

#### Chapter 10
#### Bailment

#### Section 1
#### General Provisions on Bailment

**Article 669.- Definition of Bailment**

1-A bailment refers to a contract whereby one party, the bailee, promises to receive a bailed property from the other party, the bailor, and keep it in custody for an agreed period, and to return such property to the bailor upon the expiration of such period.

2-Unless otherwise specially agreed, the bailor shall not be obligated to pay compensation to the bailee. However, if the bailee has entered into the contract with the bailor as part of his/her business, or if it is provided by law, the bailee may demand reasonable compensation from the bailor even if there is no special agreement for compensation.

**Article 670.- Necessity of Receiving Property for Formation of Bailment Contract**

1-A bailment contract shall be formed when the bailee receives the subject matter of the bailment by agreement.

2-In cases where the bailee already has possession of the subject matter of the bailment, the bailment contract shall be formed by an agreement to establish a bailment relationship. In respect of property held by a third party, a bailment contract shall be formed when the transfer of possession is made by instruction.

**Article 671.- Effect of Agreement to Establish Bailment Relationship**

If the parties have merely agreed to establish a bailment relationship in the future, either party may withdraw such agreement at any time until the subject matter of the bailment is delivered. However, in cases where there is a promise to establish a bailment for compensation, if the bailee causes damage to the other party by refusing to receive the baited property contrary to such promise, the bailee shall be obligated to compensate the other party for such damage, unless there is a justifiable reason for such refusal.

**Article 672.- Bailment Contract Entered Into by Non-Owner**

Even if the bailor does not have ownership of the baited property, the bailment contract shall still be effective. In this case, even if a lawsuit is filed by the owner demanding the return of the property from the bailee, the bailee may return the baited property to the bailor. However, this shall not apply if the owner's claim for the return of the property has been recognized.

**Article 673.- Bailee's Obligations**

1-The bailee shall have an obligation to keep the bailed property with the care of a good manager.

2-The bailee may not use the bailed property without the consent of the bailor.

**Article 674.- Bailee's Liability**

1-In cases where the bailed property is lost or damaged, the bailee shall compensate for such damage. However, this shall not apply if the bailee proves that the loss or

damage was not caused by his/her fault.

2-In cases where a bailment is formed upon the demand of the bailee, the bailee may not be exempt from liability for loss or damage unless he/she proves that such loss or damage was caused by force majeure.

3-At places where guests gather, such as hotels, guesthouses, restaurants, public baths, and other such facilities, if the manager of such facilities receives property from a guest for bailment, the manager may not be exempt from liability for loss or damage unless he/she proves that such loss or damage was caused by force majeure.

**Article 675.- Reduction of Bailee's Liability in Gratuitous Bailment**

In a gratuitous bailment, the court may reduce the bailee's liability for damages to the bailor, taking into account the financial status of both parties and other circumstances.

**Article 676.- Mitigation of Liability in Cases of Failure to Report High-Value Property**

In cases where the bailor has bailed money, negotiable instruments, or other high-value property, if the bailor failed to report the type and value thereof to the bailee, the court may reduce the bailee's liability for damages.

**Article 677.- Obligation to Preserve Property Personally**

1-The bailee may not have a third party keep the bailed property in custody without the consent of the bailor.

2-In cases where the bailee is permitted to have a third party keep the bailed property in custody, the bailee shall be liable for the selection and supervision of the sub-bailee.

**Article 678.- Sub-bailment**

1-The provisions of Chapter 10 (Bailment) shall also apply to the relationship between the bailee and the sub-bailee.

2-The sub-bailee shall have the same rights and obligations as the bailee toward the bailor.

3-In cases where the bailed property has been returned to the bailor, the sub-bailee shall be released from the obligation to return it to the bailee.

**Article 679.- Bailee's Obligation to Give Notice**

If a third party claiming a right to the bailed property has filed a lawsuit against the bailee or if the bailed property has been seized, the bailee shall give notice thereof to the bailor without delay.

**Article 680.- Bailor's Right to Demand Return**

Even if the parties have fixed a period for the return of the bailed property, the bailor may demand its return at any time.

**Article 681.- Return by Bailee**

1-If the parties have not fixed a period for the return of the bailed property, the bailee may return it at any time.

2-If a period for return is fixed, the bailee may not return the bailed property before the expiration of such period unless there is an unavoidable reason.

**Article 682.- Meaning of Obligation to Return**

1-The bailee shall have an obligation to return the same property that he/she received at the time of bailment.

2-In cases where the bailee is not liable for the loss or damage of the bailed property, if the bailee has received insurance money or other property in substitution for the lost or damaged bailed property, the bailee shall have an obligation to return such substituted property to the bailor.

**Article 683.- Disposal of Fruits, etc.**

In cases where fruits or income have arisen from the bailed property, the bailee shall deliver such fruits or income to the bailor.

**Article 684.- Bailment of Money**

1-If money has been bailed, the bailee shall keep it in the manner fixed in the contract. In this case, unless there is a special agreement, the bailee shall not be obligated to pay interest.

2-In cases where the bailee may keep the money by depositing it in a bank, the provisions of Article 683 (Disposal of fruits, etc.) above shall also apply to the interest thereof.

**Article 685.- Expenses for Return of Bailed Property**

In a gratuitous bailment, the necessary expenses for the return of the bailed property shall be borne by the bailor. However, in cases where the bailee has changed the place of bailment, the expenses for the return of the bailed property incurred due to such change of place shall be borne by the bailee.

**Article 686.- Bailor's Obligation to Reimburse Expenses**

1-The bailor shall pay the necessary expenses for the custody of the bailed property.

2-The bailee may demand compensation from the bailor for any damage caused by the bailed property, unless such damage was caused by the bailee's fault.

**Article 687.- Place of Return**

Unless otherwise fixed in the contract, the bailed property shall be returned at the place where it was kept. However, if the bailee has changed the place of custody for a justifiable reason, the property may be returned at the actual place of custody at the time of return.

**Article 688.- Bailee's Right of Retention**

The bailee may retain the bailed property until the bailor has paid the expenses and compensation for damages provided for in paragraphs 1 and 2 of Article 686 (Bailor's obligation to reimburse expenses) of this Code.

#### Section 2
#### Commingled Bailment

**Article 689.- Commingled Bailment**

If the bailee receives for bailment property of the same type from several bailors and may keep them commingled without separating each bailed property, each bailor shall have a right in proportion to the quantity of his/her bailed property in the whole of the property kept commingled.

#### Section 3
#### Consumption Bailment

**Article 690.- Consumption Bailment**

In a bailment contract, if it is agreed that the bailee may consume the bailed property, the bailee shall have an obligation to return property of the same type and quantity as the bailed property.

**Article 691.- Application of Provisions on Loan for Consumption**

The provisions on loan for consumption shall also apply to consumption bailment as provided in Article 690 (Consumption bailment) above. However, in this case, if no period for return is fixed in the contract, the bailor may demand return at any time.

#### Section 4
#### Sequestration

**Article 692.- Definition of Sequestration**

Sequestration refers to a contract whereby several persons who have a dispute over the right of possession or ownership of a property bail the property that is the subject of the dispute to a third party, called a sequester, until the person entitled to it is finally determined.

**Article 693.- Parties to Sequestration**

1-In cases where several persons claim rights to a property held by another person, the persons in dispute may enter into a contract to have a third party keep the property that is the subject of the dispute in custody with the consent of the possessor.

the sequestration contract shall become effective when the possessor of the property that is the subject of the dispute delivers such property to the third party.

2-In the cases set forth in paragraph 1 above, the persons in dispute may also enter into a bailment contract by appointing the possessor as the sequester.

**Article 694.- Conditions for Validity**

Unless all persons claiming rights to the property consent, the bailment of the property that is the subject of the dispute shall not be valid.

**Article 695.- Principle of Compensation**

In sequestration, the bailors shall have an obligation to pay compensation to the sequester. If the parties have not fixed the amount of compensation, the sequester may demand a reasonable amount of compensation from the bailors.

**Article 696.- Return of Property That Is Subject of Dispute**

The sequester shall have an obligation to return the property that was the subject of the dispute to the person who is determined to have the right to it after the dispute has ended.

**Article 697.- Rescission of Sequestration Contract**

The sequester may not return the property to the bailors before the dispute is settled, unless all the bailors consent thereto.

**Article 698.- Sequestration by Court Order**

In cases where the right of possession or ownership of a property is the subject of a dispute in court, the court may, even if there is no agreement between the parties, order a third party to keep the property that is the subject of the dispute in custody.

#### Chapter 11
#### Partnership Contract

**Article 699.- Definition of Partnership Contract**

A partnership contract refers to a contract whereby the parties agree to form a group that is not organized as a legal entity in order to carry on a business together by each party investing assets.

**Article 700.- Formation of Partnership Contract**

1-A partnership is a group formed by a contract whereby each party promises to invest assets and carry on a business together.

2-The assets to be invested by each party in a partnership contract may consist of labor in addition to property rights.

3-In cases where a party has promised to invest money and delayed in paying such money, in addition to paying interest for the delay, such party shall also compensate for any damage caused by the delay to the partnership.

4-In cases where the act of a party among the parties who have agreed to form a partnership to form such partnership becomes void or is rescinded in accordance with the provisions of Section 2 (Defects in manifestation of intention and validity of contract) of Chapter 2 of Part 4, the partnership shall be deemed to have been formed by the other parties. However, if the partnership cannot achieve its objectives due to the absence of such party, the partnership shall be deemed not to have been formed.

**Article 701.- Joint Ownership of Partnership Property**

1-The assets invested by each partner and other partnership property shall be under the joint ownership of all the partners.

2-A partner may not demand the partition of partnership property before the liquidation of the partnership. However, if all partners agree, the partition may be demanded even before liquidation.

3-The partition provided for in the second sentence of paragraph 2 above may not be set up against a third party who has performed a transaction with the partnership before such partition.

4-A partner may not dispose of his/her share of the partnership property.

**Article 702.- Method of Conducting Business**

1-In cases where no partners or third parties are appointed as managers in the partnership contract, each partner shall have the authority to conduct the business of the partnership. However, the conduct of the partnership's business shall be determined by a majority of the partners.

2-If several partners or third parties are appointed as managers in the partnership contract, the conduct of the partnership's business shall be determined by a majority of such managers.

3-Notwithstanding the provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2 above, each partner or each manager may perform the ordinary business of the partnership alone. However, this shall not apply if any other partner or manager objects before the completion of such business.

**Article 703.- Mutatis Mutandis Application of Provisions on Mandate**

The provisions from Article 640 (Duty of care of mandatary) to

Article 646 (Right to demand reimbursement of expenses by the mandatary, etc.) of this Code shall also apply to the partners who conduct the business of the partnership.

**Article 704.- Resignation or Removal of Managing Partner**

1-A partner who conducts the business of the partnership may not resign or be removed without a justifiable reason.

2-In order to remove a partner who conducts the business of the partnership for a justifiable reason, the consent of a majority of the other partners shall be required.

**Article 705.- Right to Inspect Business and Condition of Property**

Each partner may, even if he/she does not have the authority to conduct the business of the partnership, inspect the business and the condition of the property of the partnership.

**Article 706.- Right to Demand Distribution of Profits and Shared Distribution of Profit and Loss**

1-The partners shall calculate the profit and loss at regular intervals, and in cases where there is a profit, they may demand the distribution of such profit.

2-If the shares of the partners in the distribution of profit and loss are not fixed, such shares shall be in proportion to the value of the assets invested by each partner.

3-If only the share of either profit or loss is fixed, such share shall be presumed to be the same for both profit and loss.

**Article 707.- Share of Liability for Losses of Partners Toward Creditors**

1-The obligations incurred as a result of the business of the partnership shall be joint and several obligations. However, such obligations shall be performed first out of the partnership property, and if they cannot be fully performed out of the partnership property, they shall be performed out of the separate property of each partner.

2-The share of liability of each partner shall be in proportion to his/her share of investment, unless otherwise specially agreed.

**Article 708.- Prohibition of Set-off of Partnership Obligations**

A debtor of the partnership may not set off his/her obligation against a claim that he/she has against an individual partner.

**Article 709.- Voluntary Withdrawal**

1-If the duration of the partnership is not fixed in the partnership contract, or if it is fixed for the lifetime of a partner, each partner may withdraw at any time. However, a partner may not withdraw if the withdrawal would be disadvantageous to the partnership, unless there is an unavoidable reason.

2-Even if the duration of the partnership is fixed, each partner may withdraw in cases where there is an unavoidable reason.

3-A withdrawal shall be made by a manifestation of intention to all the other partners.

**Article 710.- Statutory Withdrawal**

In addition to the cases provided for in Article 709 (Voluntary withdrawal),

above, a partner shall withdraw for any of the following reasons:

(a) Death;
(b) Bankruptcy;
(c) Adjudication of commencement of general guardianship;
(d) Expulsion.

**Article 711.- Expulsion**

Expulsion of a partner may be carried out with the unanimous consent of the other partners, but only for a justifiable reason. However, if notice of the expulsion is not given to the expelled partner, it may not be set up against such partner.

**Article 712.- Return of Share to Withdrawing Partner**

1-The settlement between the withdrawing partner and the other partners shall be conducted based on the condition of the partnership property at the time of withdrawal.

2-The share of the withdrawing partner may be returned in money regardless of the type of his/her investment.

3-As for matters that have not yet been completed at the time of withdrawal, the settlement may be made after the completion thereof.

4-The return of the share to an expelled partner shall include interest at the legal rate from the time the notice of expulsion is given until the time of return.

**Article 713.- Dissolution of Partnership**

1-A partnership shall be dissolved for any of the following reasons:

(a) Completion of the business that was the object of the partnership, or the impossibility of such completion;
(b) Agreement of all the partners;
(c) When only one partner remains;
(d) Expiration of the duration of the partnership fixed in the partnership contract.

2-If there is an unavoidable reason, each partner may demand the dissolution of the partnership.

3-The demand mentioned in paragraph 2 above shall be made by a manifestation of intention to all the other partners.

**Article 714.- Non-Retroactivity of Dissolution**

The dissolution of a partnership shall not have retroactive effect.

**Article 715.- Liquidators**

1-If a partnership is dissolved, the liquidation shall be conducted jointly by all the partners or by a liquidator appointed by the partners.

2-The appointment of a liquidator mentioned in paragraph 1 above shall, unless otherwise specially provided for in the partnership contract, be determined by a majority of the partners.

**Article 716.- Conduct of Business by Liquidators**

The provisions of Article 702 (Method of conducting business) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to

in cases where there are several liquidators.

**Article 717.- Resignation or Removal of Liquidator**

The provisions of Article 704 (Resignation or removal of managing partner) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where all the partners have agreed to appoint a liquidator from among the partners.

**Article 718.- Duties and Authority of Liquidator and Method of Distribution of Residual Property**

1-The provisions of Article 71 (Duties and authority of liquidator) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to the duties and authority of liquidators.

2-The residual property shall be distributed in proportion to the value of the assets invested by each partner.

#### Chapter 12
#### Life Annuity Contract

**Article 719.- Definition of Life Annuity Contract**

1-A life annuity contract refers to a contract whereby one party, the annuity debtor, promises to pay money periodically to the other party, the annuity creditor, or a third party until his/her own death, the death of the other party, or the death of the third party, and the other party promises to pay the principal that is the consideration for such annuity.

2-A life annuity contract shall be void unless it is made in writing.

it shall not be effective.

3-The annuity creditor shall have a statutory lien on the movable or immovable property among the principal provided to the other party as consideration for the annuity. The statutory lien that the annuity creditor receives shall be deemed to be the statutory lien provided for in Article 797 (Statutory lien on sale of movables) or Article 802 (Statutory lien on sale of immovables), depending on the type of property that is the subject of the statutory lien.

**Article 720.- Calculation of Life Annuity**

A life annuity shall be calculated by the number of days.

**Article 721.- Non-Performance of Annuity Obligation**

1-If the annuity debtor neglects to pay the life annuity periodically or fails to perform any other obligation, the other party may rescind the contract and demand the return of the principal provided as consideration. However, in this case, the other party shall return to the annuity debtor the total amount of the annuity received until the rescission, minus the interest on such principal until the rescission.

2-The provisions of paragraph 1 above shall not preclude a claim for damages.

**Article 722.- Death Caused by Annuity Debtor**

If the death of the person who is the basis for the duration of the life annuity contract is caused by a reason for which the annuity debtor is responsible, the court may declare that the annuity claim shall continue

for a reasonable period upon the demand of the annuity creditor or his/her successor.

**Article 723.- Life Annuity by Gift**

The provisions of Chapter 12 (Life Annuity Contract) shall also apply to a gift subject to a life annuity charge.

#### Chapter 13
#### Compromise

**Article 724.- Definition**

A compromise refers to a contract whereby the parties agree to terminate a dispute existing between them by making mutual concessions.

**Article 725.- Subject Matter of Compromise**

1-In order to enter into a compromise, the parties must have the authority to dispose of the property that is the subject of the compromise.

2-A compromise entered into for the purpose of making effective a legal relationship that is void because it is contrary to public order or good morals, or to mandatory provisions, shall be void.

**Article 726.- Clause on Penalty for Breach of Contract**

A clause on penalty for breach of contract may be added to a compromise contract against a person who fails to perform the contract.

**Article 727.- Effect of Compromise**

In cases where it is recognized through a compromise that one party has a right or that the other party does not have a right over the subject matter of the dispute, if evidence is discovered

it is proven that such party did not have the right from the beginning, or if evidence is discovered from which it is concluded that the other party had the right, such right shall be deemed to have been transferred to such party by the compromise, or the right of the other party shall be deemed to have been extinguished.

**Article 728.- Mistake Concerning Right That Is Subject of Dispute, etc.**

Even in cases where a compromise is entered into based on a mistake by one party concerning the holder of the right or the facts that are the basis for the calculation of the value of the subject matter, if the compromise is made by mutual concessions concerning the holder of the right, or the existence or non-existence of the facts, or the valuation, etc., the party may not rescind the compromise on the ground of mistake concerning the holder of the right, or the existence or non-existence of the facts, or the valuation, etc.

#### Chapter 14
#### Management of Affairs Without Mandate

**Article 729.- Duty of Manager in Management of Affairs Without Mandate**

1-Management of affairs for another person without having authority to do so is called management of affairs without mandate. A person who manages affairs without mandate is called a manager.

2-A manager shall manage the affairs with the care of a good manager, in a manner most appropriate to the interest of the principal, according to the nature of such affairs.

3-If the manager knows the intention of the principal, or is able to know such intention, the manager shall manage the affairs in accordance with such intention.

**Article 730.- Management of Affairs Without Mandate in Urgency**

If a manager manages affairs without mandate in order to

avert danger to the life, body, honor, or property of the principal in an urgent situation, the manager shall not be liable for any damages arising therefrom unless the manager acted in bad faith or with gross negligence.

**Article 731.- Duty of Manager to Notify**

A manager shall notify the principal of the commencement of the management of affairs without mandate without delay. However, this shall not be required if the principal is already aware of the management.

**Article 732.- Duty of Manager to Continue Management**

A manager shall continue the management until the principal, the successor of the principal, or the principal's legal representative can take over the management. However, if it is clear that the continuation of the management would be contrary to the intention of the principal or would be disadvantageous to the principal, the manager shall terminate the management.

**Article 733.- Mutatis Mutandis Application of Provisions on Mandate**

The provisions from Article 641 (Duty of mandatary to report) to Article 643 (Duty of mandatary to pay damages for use of money) shall apply mutatis mutandis to the management of affairs without mandate.

**Article 734.- Right of Manager to Demand Reimbursement of Expenses**

1-If a manager has incurred necessary or useful expenses for the management and maintenance of the property, or for increasing the value thereof for the principal, the manager may demand reimbursement of such expenses from the principal.

2-The provisions of paragraph 2 of Article 646 (Right to demand reimbursement of expenses, etc.)

of Article 646 (Right to demand reimbursement of expenses, etc.) shall also apply mutatis mutandis if the manager has incurred an obligation that constitutes a useful expense for the principal.

3-The provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2 above shall apply only to the extent of the actual benefit received by the principal if the manager managed the affairs against the intention of the principal.

**Article 735.- Right of Manager to Demand Remuneration**

If the management of affairs without mandate falls within the scope of the manager's profession or business, the manager may demand the remuneration normally paid for such management with respect to the affairs performed after the principal became aware of the management.

#### Chapter 15
#### Unjust Enrichment

**Article 736.- Conditions for and Effect of Unjust Enrichment**

1-A person who has received a benefit from the property or labor of another person without any legal ground and thereby caused loss to another person shall have an obligation to return such benefit to the extent that it exists.

2-In cases where a person has received performance from another person based on a contract, if the contract is void or is extinguished, the person who received the performance shall have an obligation to return the benefit received from such performance to the person who provided it, in the same manner as provided in paragraph 1 above.

**Article 737.- Duty of Beneficiary in Bad Faith to Return**

1-A beneficiary who received a benefit as provided in Article 736 (Conditions for and effect of unjust enrichment) above in the knowledge that there was no legal ground or that the contract was void, shall have an obligation to return the benefit existing at the time he/she became aware thereof, together with interest from the time he/she became aware thereof until the time of return.

2-If a beneficiary has caused the loss of the effect of the contract intentionally or by negligence, such beneficiary shall have an obligation to return the benefit together with interest in the same manner as provided in paragraph 1 above.

3-If the person who suffered the loss of the benefit has sustained damages as provided in paragraphs 1 and 2 above, the beneficiary shall be liable to compensate such damages.

**Article 738.- Performance of Non-Existent Obligation**

A person who has performed an obligation that he/she did not owe may demand the return of the performance. However, if the person knew at the time of performance that no obligation existed, he/she may not demand the return of the performance.

**Article 739.- Performance Before Maturity**
