If an obligation is performed before it becomes due, the debtor may not demand the return of the performance. However, if the debtor performed the obligation without knowing that it was not yet due, the creditor shall have an obligation to return the benefit received from such performance before maturity.

**Article 740.- Performance of Another's Obligation**

1-In cases where a person other than the debtor has performed another's obligation by mistake as his/her own, such person may demand the return of the performance. However, if the creditor did not know that the performance was made by mistake and in good faith destroyed the instrument evidencing the existence of the claim or gave up a security, the person who made the performance may not demand the return of the performance.

2-The provisions of paragraph 1 above shall not preclude the person who made the performance from exercising the right to demand reimbursement from the debtor.

**Article 741.- Performance for Illegal Cause**

In this Chapter 15 (Unjust Enrichment), in cases where the claim of the person who suffered loss against the beneficiary is contrary to public order or good morals, or to laws relating to public order, such claim for return shall not be permitted.

#### Chapter 16
#### Torts

**Article 742.- Definition of Intentional and Negligent Fault**

In this Chapter 16 (Torts), an act with intentional or negligent fault refers to an act that falls under any of the following:

(a) Committing a tortious act while foreseeing the occurrence of the result and

accepting the occurrence of such result.

(b) Committing an act where a person in the same position or with the same experience as the actor could normally have foreseen the occurrence of the result, but the actor failed to foresee it due to negligence, and even if there was a duty to avoid such result, the actor acted in violation of such duty.

**Article 743.- Conditions for and Liability to Prove General Torts**

1-A person who intentionally or negligently violates the rights or interests of another person unlawfully shall be liable to compensate for the damages arising therefrom.

2-The provisions of paragraph 1 above shall also apply to cases where damages have occurred as a result of a failure to perform an act that the person was under a duty to perform.

3-The person claiming damages must prove the intentional or negligent fault of the tortfeasor, the causal relationship between the act of the tortfeasor and the occurrence of the damages, and the damages sustained by the victim, unless otherwise specially provided for in this Code or other laws.

**Article 744.- Damages for Non-Pecuniary Harm**

A person who is liable under Article 743 (Conditions for and liability to prove general torts) above shall also compensate for harm that is not to property.

**Article 745.- Liability Capacity**

1-A minor under the age of 14 (fourteen) shall not be liable for a tort.

2-A person who, at the time of committing a tortious act, lacked the capacity to understand and judge his/her own responsibility for the act due to a mental disorder or other cause shall not be liable for the tort. However, this shall not apply if the person has brought about such condition intentionally or negligently.

**Article 746.- Liability of Person with Duty to Supervise**

1-A person who has a legal duty to supervise a minor under the age of 14 (fourteen) or a person who lacks the capacity to understand and judge his/her own responsibility due to a mental disorder or other cause shall be liable to compensate for the damages caused by such minor or person.

2-A person who has a legal duty to supervise a minor of 14 (fourteen) years of age or older shall be jointly and severally liable with such minor for the damages caused by a tort committed by the minor.

3-A person with a duty to supervise who is liable for damages under paragraph 2 above may be exempted from liability for damages if he/she proves that he/she has performed his/her duty of supervision normally.

**Article 747.- Employer's Liability**

1-A person who employs an employee to perform a business shall be liable for damages caused to another person by the employee unlawfully through intentional or negligent fault in the course of the performance of such business.

2-A person who is in a position to supervise the employee on behalf of the employer shall also be liable in the same manner as the employer. However, this shall not apply if the person has performed the supervision properly.

3-The employer or the person who supervised the employee on behalf of the employer may demand reimbursement from the employee according to the degree of fault of the employee.

**Article 748.- Tort of Legal Entity**

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code)

1-A legal entity shall be liable for damages caused to another person by its directors or other representatives unlawfully through intentional or negligent fault in the course of the performance of their duties.

2-If a legal entity has compensated for damages in accordance with paragraph 1 above, it may demand reimbursement from the representatives who committed the tortious act.

**Article 749.- Tort of Public Official**

1-If a public official who is in charge of the exercise of the public power of the State or a public institution causes damage to another person unlawfully through intentional or negligent fault in the course of the performance of his/her duties, the State or the public institution shall be liable for such damage.

2-In the cases set forth in paragraph 1 above, if the failure of the public official to perform his/her duties to avoid the result is gross, the State or the public institution may demand reimbursement from such public official.

**Article 750.- Liability of Possessor of Animal**

A person who possesses an animal shall be liable for damages caused to another person by such animal.

**Article 751.- Product Liability**

1-If there is a defect in a produced movable property that makes it unreasonably dangerous and such defect causes damage to another person, the manufacturer of such movable property shall be liable for such damage. However, this shall not apply if the defect could not have been discovered according to the state of scientific and technical knowledge at the time the manufacturer delivered the product.

2-A person who produced a movable property that is a component or raw material of the product and that is defective shall also be liable for the damage as a manufacturer.

3-For the purposes of this Article, an importer of movable property shall be deemed to be its manufacturer.

4-For the purposes of this Article, a person who puts his/her name, trademark or other distinguishable mark on the movable property as its manufacturer or seller shall be deemed to be its manufacturer.

**Article 752.- Liability for Dangerous Objects**

The owner or person in control of an automobile, other transport machinery, explosives, radioactive substances, toxic chemicals, virulent microorganisms or other objects of a highly dangerous nature shall be liable for damages caused to another person by such dangerous objects. However, this shall not apply if the damage was caused by force majeure, or by the act of the victim or a third party without any defect in the control of the dangerous objects.

**Article 753.- Liability for Structures on Land**

1-If a defect in the construction or maintenance of a structure on land causes damage to another person, the person in possession and the owner of the structure shall be jointly and severally liable for such damage. However, if the person in possession proves that he/she has exercised proper care to prevent the occurrence of the damage, the owner shall be liable for the damage.

2-The allocation of the burden of compensation between the owner and the person in possession of the structure on land shall be determined according to the degree of their respective contribution to the defect in the construction or maintenance.

**Article 754.- Joint Tortfeasors**

1-If damage is caused by the acts of several persons jointly, each tortfeasor shall be jointly and severally liable for the damages.

2-In the cases set forth in paragraph 1 above, if each tortfeasor proves his/her degree of contribution to the occurrence of the damage, he/she shall be liable for the damages in proportion to his/her degree of contribution. However, this shall not apply if there was a conspiracy or close cooperation among the tortfeasors.

3-If one of the joint tortfeasors has compensated for the entire damages at his/her own expense, he/she may demand reimbursement from the other joint tortfeasors in proportion to their respective degrees of contribution.

**Article 755.- Definition of Self-Defense and Necessity**

1-Defensive act refers to an unavoidable act performed to protect the life, body or property of oneself or another person against an unlawful

unlawful act, provided that the time of the unlawful act and the defensive act are close and there is a balance between the method of defense and the degree of the unlawful act.

2-Act of necessity refers to an act performed to protect the life, body or property of oneself or another person against an imminent or current danger, provided that there is a balance between the method of protection and the degree of danger.

**Article 756.- Grounds for Exemption from Illegality**

1-A tortfeasor shall be exempted from liability if the victim consented to or assumed the risk of the harm. However, this shall not apply if such consent or assumption of risk is not recognized as reasonable by society.

2-A person who committed a defensive act or an act of necessity shall not be liable for the damages arising therefrom. In this case, the person who committed the unlawful act that was the cause of such defensive act or act of necessity shall be liable.

3-A tortfeasor shall not be liable for damages caused by an act that is recognized as reasonable by society.

**Article 757.- Principle of Monetary Compensation and Exceptions**

1-In principle, compensation for damages shall be made in money.

2-If adequate relief cannot be obtained by monetary compensation, the victim may demand restitution or an injunction against certain acts.

3-In the case of defamation, the victim may demand, in addition to monetary compensation, measures for restoring his/her reputation, such as a public apology.

may be ordered.

**Article 758.- Calculation of Amount of Damages**

1-In calculating the amount of pecuniary damages caused by a tort, the difference between the pecuniary condition as it would presumably have existed if the tortious act had not occurred and the actual pecuniary condition after the tortious act occurred shall be assessed using statistics and other documents.

2-In calculating the amount of non-pecuniary damages caused by a tort, the amount of solatium shall be assessed taking into account various circumstances such as the degree of intentional or negligent fault of the tortfeasor, the type and degree of harm, and the conduct of the tortfeasor after the harm occurred.

**Article 759.- Claim for Damages for Damage to or Destruction of Object**

If an object is damaged or destroyed by a tort, the victim may demand compensation such as the value of the damaged or destroyed object or the expenses for repair, etc.

**Article 760.- Claim for Damages for Violation of Life**

1-If a victim dies due to a tort, the victim may acquire a right to demand compensation for pecuniary and non-pecuniary damages sustained by the victim until his/her death. Pecuniary damages set forth in this paragraph 1 shall include medical expenses and other expenses paid or incurred by the victim from the time of the occurrence of the harm until the death, and loss of income from the time of the occurrence of the harm until the

death, etc.

2-If a victim who had a duty to provide support under law, custom or contract dies due to a tort, a person entitled to receive such support may demand compensation for pecuniary damages sustained by him/her due to the death of the victim. Pecuniary damages set forth in this paragraph 2 shall include the amount of support that the person entitled to receive support has lost due to the death of the victim, expenses paid on behalf of the victim, and funeral expenses, etc.

3-If a victim dies due to a tort, the spouse, relatives within the first degree of relationship, and relatives living together with the victim may demand compensation for non-pecuniary damages sustained by them due to the death of the victim.

**Article 761.- Claim for Damages for Violation of Body**

If the body is violated by a tort, the victim may demand compensation for pecuniary and non-pecuniary damages sustained by him/her. Pecuniary damages set forth in this Article shall include medical expenses already paid or estimated to be paid in the future, loss of income while receiving medical treatment, and loss of future income due to the after-effects of the violation, etc. Damages set forth in this Article shall also include mental suffering while receiving medical treatment and in the future, etc.

**Article 762.- Claim for Non-Pecuniary Damages for Defamation**

If defamation is caused by a tort, the victim may demand compensation for non-pecuniary damages sustained by him/her due to the loss of his/her social reputation.

**Article 763.- Set-off of Profits and Losses**

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code)

If a victim has received a benefit from a tort, the amount of such benefit shall be deducted in calculating the amount of compensation for damages.

**Article 764.- Comparative Fault**

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code)

If the fault of the victim or the fault of a person who has a duty to supervise the victim has contributed to the occurrence or enlargement of the damage, the court may take such contribution into account in calculating the amount of compensation for damages.

**Article 765.- Extinctive Prescription**

The right to demand compensation for damages caused by a tort shall be extinguished by prescription if it is not exercised within 3 (three) years from the time the victim or his/her legal representative became aware of the damage and the person who committed the tort, or within 10 (ten) years from the time the tortious act was committed.

### BOOK 6
### SECURING OF OBLIGATIONS

#### Chapter 1
#### General Provisions

**Article 766.- Definition of Real Security Provider and Third Party Purchaser**

1-A person who creates a real security right over his/her own property to secure the obligation of another person is called a real security provider.

2-A person who has acquired property that is the subject of a real security right created by a debtor to secure his/her own obligation is called a third party purchaser.

**Article 767.- Types of Real Security Rights**

1-The types of real security rights shall be limited to those provided in the Civil Code or special laws, and no other real security rights may be created.

2-The real security rights provided in this Civil Code are of 5 (five) types: right of retention, preferential right, pledge, hypothec and security right by way of transfer of title.

**Article 768.- Subject Matter of Real Security Right**

A real security right may not be created over an object or right that is not transferable. However, this shall not preclude the creation of a right of retention over an object that is not transferable.

**Article 769.- Appurtenance of Real Security Right**

1-A real security right is created to secure an obligation that...

already exists. However, even if the obligation is to arise in the future, if such obligation is identifiable, a real security right may be created to secure it.

2-If the secured obligation was not created due to the lack of a prerequisite, the real security right shall not be created.

3-If the secured obligation is void due to a defect in the declaration of intention or for any other reason, or if the secured obligation is rescinded, the real security right shall not be valid.

4-If the secured obligation is extinguished due to performance, extinctive prescription or any other reason, the real security right shall also be extinguished.

5-The provisions of paragraphs 1, 2, 3 and 4 above shall not apply to a floating hypothec created to secure a large number of obligations arising from a continuous contract.

**Article 770.- Appurtenance of Real Security Right**

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code)

1-If the secured obligation is transferred, unless otherwise intended, the real security right shall also be transferred to the transferee of the obligation. However, if the holder of the security right is required to possess the subject matter thereof, the transferee of the obligation must also receive possession of such subject matter.

2-The provisions of paragraph 1 above shall not apply to an obligation secured by a floating hypothec.

**Article 771.- Indivisibility of Real Security Right**

A real security right may be exercised over the whole of the object or right that is its subject matter until the secured obligation is fully performed.

**Article 772.- Pursuit of Real Security Right**

If the subject matter of a real security right is transferred to a third party, the holder of the security right may assert the security right against the third party, except as provided in Article 807 (Effect against third party purchaser) concerning preferential rights or Article 894 (Disposition of subject matter by creator) concerning security rights by way of transfer of title. However, the holder of the security right must satisfy the requirements for perfection.

**Article 773.- Enforcement of Real Security Right**

If the secured obligation is not performed, the holder of the security right may enforce the real security right in accordance with the procedures provided by law.

#### Chapter 2
#### Right of Retention

**Article 774.- Meaning of Right of Retention**

1-If a possessor of an object belonging to another person has an obligation in his/her favor that has arisen in connection with such object, he/she may retain the object until the obligation is performed. However, this shall not apply if the obligation is not yet due.

2-The provisions of paragraph 1 above shall not apply if...

possession began as a result of a tortious act.

**Article 775.- Right to Be Compensated from Fruits with Priority**

1-A holder of a right of retention may collect the fruits of the retained object and apply them to satisfy the obligation in priority to other creditors.

2-The fruits mentioned in paragraph 1 above shall first be applied to the interest on the obligation, and any remainder shall be applied to the principal.

**Article 776.- Duty of Holder of Right of Retention to Preserve the Retained Object**

1-A holder of a right of retention shall preserve the retained object with the care of a good manager.

2-A holder of a right of retention may not use, lease or give as security the retained object without the consent of the debtor. However, the holder of a right of retention may use the retained object only to the extent necessary for its preservation.

3-If the holder of a right of retention violates the provisions of paragraph 1 or 2 above, the debtor may demand the extinction of the right of retention.

**Article 777.- Claim for Reimbursement of Expenses by Holder of Right of Retention**

1-If the holder of a right of retention has incurred necessary expenses for the maintenance and preservation of the retained object, he/she may demand reimbursement from the owner of the object.

2-If the holder of a right of retention has incurred beneficial expenses that increased the value of the retained object, he/she may demand reimbursement from the owner of the object for such expenses...

it has incurred or the increased value of the object, at the option of the owner, provided that the increased value still exists. However, the court may allow a reasonable time for reimbursement upon the request of the owner.

**Article 778.- Extinctive Prescription of Secured Obligation**

The exercise of a right of retention shall not prevent the running of extinctive prescription of the secured obligation.

**Article 779.- Extinction of Right of Retention by Providing Security**

The debtor may demand the extinction of the right of retention by providing reasonable security.

**Article 780.- Extinction of Right of Retention by Loss of Possession**

1-A right of retention shall be extinguished if the holder of the right of retention loses possession of the retained object. However, this shall not apply if the holder of the right of retention has leased or pledged the retained object with the consent of the debtor in accordance with the provisions of paragraph 2 of Article 776 (Duty of holder of right of retention to preserve the retained object).

2-Even if the holder of a right of retention loses possession of the retained object, if he/she recovers possession in accordance with the provisions of Article 237 (Right to demand return of possession), the possession shall be deemed to have continued.

#### Chapter 3
#### Preferential Rights

##### Section 1
##### General Provisions

**Article 781.- Meaning of Preferential Rights**

1-A creditor of a preferential right has a right to receive performance of the obligation in his/her favor out of the property of the debtor in priority to other creditors.

2-A preferential right that exists over the whole of the property of the debtor is called a general preferential right.

3-A preferential right that exists over a specific property of the debtor is called a special preferential right. In this case, a preferential right over a specific movable is called a preferential right over movables, and a preferential right over a specific immovable is called a preferential right over immovables.

**Article 782.- Real Subrogation**

1-A preferential right may also be exercised against money or other objects that the debtor is entitled to receive as a result of the sale, lease, loss of or damage to the subject matter of the preferential right. However, the holder of the preferential right may not exercise it after such money has been paid or such other objects have been delivered to the debtor.

2-The provisions of paragraph 1 above shall also apply to the consideration for a real right created by the debtor over the subject matter of the preferential right.

##### Section 2
##### General Preferential Rights

**Article 783.- Meaning of General Preferential Rights**

A person who has an obligation in his/her favor arising from any of the following causes has a preferential right over the whole of the property of the debtor:

(a) Expenses for the common benefit;

(b) Employee's claims;

(c) Funeral expenses;

(d) Supply of daily necessities.

**Article 784.- Preferential Right for Expenses for Common Benefit**

1-A preferential right for expenses for the common benefit shall exist with respect to expenses incurred for the common benefit of all creditors in relation to the preservation, liquidation or distribution of the debtor's property.

2-With respect to expenses mentioned in paragraph 1 above that were incurred for the benefit of only some of the creditors, such preferential right shall exist only for such creditors who benefited from such expenses.

**Article 785.- Preferential Right for Employee's Claims**

A preferential right for employee's claims shall exist with respect to all claims that an employee has against the employer based on an employment contract.

**Article 786.- Preferential Right for Funeral Expenses**

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code)

1-A preferential right for funeral expenses shall exist with respect to expenses incurred for the funeral of the debtor according to his/her social status.

2-If a relative of the debtor for whom the debtor had a duty to provide support dies, the preferential right mentioned in paragraph 1 above shall also exist with respect to expenses incurred by the debtor for the funeral of such relative according to his/her social status.

**Article 787.- Preferential Right for Supply of Daily Necessities**

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code)

A preferential right for the supply of daily necessities shall exist with respect to the supply of food, drink and other daily necessities for the last 6 (six) months necessary for the living of the debtor, his/her relatives living with him/her for whom he/her has a duty to provide support, and his/her domestic servants.

##### Section 3
##### Preferential Rights over Movables

**Article 788.- Meaning of Preferential Rights over Movables**

A person who has an obligation in his/her favor arising from any of the following causes has a preferential right over specific movables of the debtor:

(a) Lease of immovable property;

(b) Carriage of passengers or goods;

(c) Preservation of movables.

(d) Sale of movables;

(e) Supply of seeds, plants or fertilizer, and supply of breeding stock, bird's nests or feed.

**Article 789.- Preferential Right for Lease of Immovable Property**

A preferential right for the lease of immovable property shall exist over the movables of the lessee with respect to the rent and other obligations of the lessee arising from the lease relationship.

**Article 790.- Scope of Subject Matter of Preferential Right for Lease of Immovable Property in Normal Case**

1-The preferential right of a lessor of land shall exist over movables placed on the leased land or in the buildings used for the enjoyment of the land, movables provided for the enjoyment of the land, and fruits of the land in the possession of the lessee.

2-The preferential right of a lessor of a building shall exist over movables placed in the building by the lessee.

**Article 791.- Scope of Subject Matter of Preferential Right for Lease of Immovable Property in Case of Assignment of Lease or Sublease**

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code)

In the case of an assignment of the lease or a sublease, the preferential right of the lessor shall extend to the movables of the assignee or sublessee. The same shall apply to money that the assignor or sublessor is to receive.

**Article 792.- Scope of Effectiveness of Preferential Right for Lease of Immovable Property**

In the case of a general liquidation of the lessee's property, the preferential right of the lessor shall exist with respect to the rent and other obligations for the previous term, the current term and the next term, and for damages that arose during the previous term and the current term.

**Article 793.- Preferential Right for Lease of Immovable Property in Case of Security Deposit**

If a lessor has received a security deposit, his/her preferential right shall exist only with respect to the portion of the obligation that is not covered by such security deposit.

**Article 794.- Preferential Right for Carriage**

A preferential right for carriage shall exist over the goods in the possession of the carrier with respect to the fare or freight for passengers or goods and other expenses incidental to the carriage.

**Article 795.- Bona Fide Acquisition**

The provisions of Article 193 (Bona fide acquisition of ownership of movables) concerning bona fide acquisition shall apply to the preferential rights mentioned in Articles 789 (Preferential right for lease of immovable property) through 794 (Preferential right for carriage).

**Article 796.- Preferential Right for Preservation of Movables**

1-A preferential right for the preservation of movables shall exist over the movables preserved with respect to the expenses for such preservation.

2-The preferential right mentioned in paragraph 1 above shall also exist with respect to necessary expenses incurred for the purpose of preserving, acknowledging or enforcing a right in relation to such movables.

**Article 797.- Preferential Right for Sale of Movables**

A preferential right for the sale of movables shall exist over the movables sold with respect to the purchase price and interest thereon.

**Article 798.- Preferential Right for Supply of Seeds, Plants or Fertilizer, and Breeding Stock or Feed**

1-A preferential right for the supply of seeds, plants or fertilizer shall exist over the fruits derived from the land for 1 (one) year after such seeds, plants or fertilizer were used, with respect to the price thereof and interest thereon.

2-A preferential right for the supply of breeding stock or feed shall exist over the livestock and fruits derived therefrom for 1 (one) year after such breeding stock or feed were used, with respect to the price thereof and interest thereon.

3-The preferential right mentioned in paragraph 2 above shall also exist over the products derived from silkworm eggs or mulberry leaves with respect to the price of such silkworm eggs or mulberry leaves used for raising silkworms.

##### Section 4
##### Preferential Rights over Immovables

**Article 799.- Preferential Rights over Immovables**

A person who has an obligation in his/her favor arising from any of the following causes has a preferential right over specific immovables of the debtor:

(a) Preservation of an immovable;

(b) Work on an immovable;

(c) Sale of an immovable.

**Article 800.- Preferential Right for Preservation of Immovable**

1-A preferential right for the preservation of an immovable shall exist over the immovable with respect to the expenses for the preservation of such immovable.

2-The preferential right mentioned in paragraph 1 above shall also...

also exist with respect to necessary expenses incurred for the purpose of preserving, acknowledging or enforcing a right in relation to such immovable.

**Article 801.- Preferential Right for Work on Immovable**

1-A preferential right for work on an immovable shall exist over the immovable of the debtor with respect to the expenses for work performed by artisans, technicians and contractors on such immovable.

2-The preferential right mentioned in paragraph 1 above shall exist only to the extent of the increase in value, provided that the increased value of the immovable caused by such work still exists.

**Article 802.- Preferential Right for Sale of Immovable**

A preferential right for the sale of an immovable shall exist over the immovable with respect to the purchase price and interest thereon.

##### Section 5
##### Priority of Preferential Rights

**Article 803.- Priority of General Preferential Rights**

1-If general preferential rights conflict, the priority thereof shall be in the order mentioned in Article 783 (Meaning of general preferential rights).

2-If a general preferential right conflicts with a special preferential right, the special preferential right shall take priority. However, a preferential right for expenses for the common benefit shall take priority over all creditors who benefited therefrom.

**Article 804.- Priority of Preferential Rights over Movables**

1-If special preferential rights conflict with respect to the same movable, the priority thereof shall be in the following order:

First: Preferential rights for the lease of immovable property and for carriage;

Second: Preferential rights for the preservation of movables. However, if there are two or more preservers, the later preserver shall take priority over the earlier one;

Third: Preferential rights for the sale of movables, and for the supply of seeds, plants or fertilizer and the supply of breeding stock or feed.

2-If a holder of a preferential right of the first rank knew at the time he/she acquired the obligation that there was a holder of a preferential right of the second or third rank, he/she may not exercise his/her right in priority to such person. The same shall apply with respect to a person who has preserved the object for the benefit of such holder of a preferential right of the first rank.

3-With respect to fruits, the first rank shall belong to the supplier of seeds, plants or fertilizer, and the second rank shall belong to the lessor of the land.

**Article 805.- Priority of Preferential Rights over Immovables**

1-If special preferential rights conflict with respect to the same immovable, the priority thereof shall be in the order mentioned in Article 799 (Preferential rights over immovables).

2-If successive sales of the same immovable have been made, the priority of the preferential rights among the sellers shall be in the order of the time of the sales.

**Article 806.- Preferential Rights of Same Rank**

If there are two or more holders of preferential rights of the same rank with respect to the same object, each holder of a preferential right shall receive performance in proportion to the amount of his/her obligation.

##### Section 6
##### Effectiveness of Preferential Rights

**Article 807.- Effect against Third Party Purchaser**

A holder of a preferential right may not exercise his/her preferential right against a movable after the debtor has delivered it to a third party who has acquired it.

**Article 808.- Relationship with Pledge of Movables**

If a preferential right conflicts with a pledge of movables, the pledgee of movables shall have the same rights as a holder of a preferential right of the first rank mentioned in Article 804 (Priority of preferential rights over movables).

**Article 809.- Effectiveness of General Preferential Right**

1-A holder of a general preferential right may not receive performance out of the immovable property of the debtor unless he/she has first received performance out of property other than immovables and a deficiency remains.

2-With respect to immovable property, a holder of a general preferential right shall first receive performance out of an immovable that is not the subject matter of a special security right.

3-If a holder of a general preferential right fails to participate in the distribution according to the provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2 above, he/she may not...

be exercised against a registered third party to the extent of the portion he/she would have received if he/she had participated in the distribution.

4-The provisions of paragraphs 1, 2 and 3 above shall not apply if the distribution of the proceeds of the immovable that is the subject matter of a special security right is made before the distribution of the proceeds of property other than immovables.

**Article 810.- Effectiveness of General Preferential Right against Third Party**

A holder of a general preferential right may assert such right against a creditor of a special security right even if the preferential right is not registered against the immovable. However, such general preferential right may not be asserted against a third party who has registered the acquisition of an interest in the immovable.

**Article 811.- Effectiveness against Third Party of Preferential Right for Preservation of Immovable**

1-A preferential right for the preservation of an immovable may not be asserted against a third party unless it is registered immediately after the preservation activity is completed.

2-The increase in value of the immovable caused by the preservation shall be evaluated by an appraiser appointed by the court at the time of participation in the distribution.

**Article 812.- Effectiveness against Third Party of Preferential Right for Work on Immovable**

1-A preferential right for work on an immovable may not be asserted against a third party unless it is registered immediately after the work is completed.

2-The increase in value of the immovable caused by the work shall be evaluated by an appraiser appointed by the court at the time of participation in the distribution.

**Article 813.- Relationship between Preferential Right for Preservation of Immovable, Preferential Right for Work on Immovable and Hypothec**

A preferential right registered in accordance with the provisions of Article 811 (Effectiveness against third party of preferential right for preservation of immovable) and Article 812 (Effectiveness against third party of preferential right for work on immovable) may be exercised in priority to a hypothec.

**Article 814.- Effectiveness against Third Party of Preferential Right for Sale of Immovable**

A preferential right for the sale of an immovable may not be asserted against a third party unless it is registered at the same time as the contract of sale, stating that the purchase price or interest thereon has not been paid.

**Article 815.- Application Mutatis Mutandis of Provisions on Hypothec**

In addition to the provisions of this Chapter 3, the provisions of Articles 846 (Scope of effectiveness of hypothec) through 848 (Effect of hypothec on fruits), Article 851 (Priority of hypothecs), Article 852 (Scope of secured obligation), Article 853 (Compulsory sale of hypothecated immovable), and Articles 859 (Sub-hypothec) through 863 (Priority of disposition of hypothec) shall apply mutatis mutandis to the effectiveness of preferential rights.

#### Chapter 4
#### Pledge

##### Section 1
##### General Provisions

**Article 816.- Meaning of Pledge**

A pledgee has a right to possess the object received from the debtor or a third party as security for his/her obligation and to receive performance of the obligation out of such object in priority to other creditors.

**Article 817.- Real Subrogation**

1-A pledge may also be exercised against money or other objects that the pledgor is entitled to receive as a result of the sale, loss of or damage to the subject matter of the pledge. However, the pledgee may not exercise it after such money has been paid or such other objects have been delivered to the pledgor.

2-The provisions of paragraph 1 above shall also apply to the consideration for a real right created by the pledgor over the subject matter of the pledge.

**Article 818.- Creation of Pledge and Delivery of Object**

1-A pledge is created by the delivery of the subject matter of the pledge by the debtor or a third party creator of the pledge to the pledgee.

2-The delivery mentioned in paragraph 1 above includes summary delivery provided in paragraph 3 of Article 229...

(Transfer of possession) of this Code.

**Article 819.- Prohibition of Direct Possession by Pledgor**

A pledgee may not allow the pledgor to directly possess the subject matter of the pledge.

**Article 820.- Scope of Secured Obligation**

A pledge shall secure the principal, interest, penalties, expenses for enforcement of the pledge, expenses for the preservation of the subject matter of the pledge, and damages arising from non-performance of the obligation or latent defects in the subject matter of the pledge. However, this shall not apply if otherwise provided in the act of creation of the pledge.

**Article 821.- Effect of Retention**

A pledgee may retain the subject matter of the pledge until the obligation mentioned in Article 820 (Scope of secured obligation) is performed. However, this right may not be asserted against a creditor who has priority over the pledgee.

**Article 822.- Right to Be Compensated from Fruits with Priority**

1-A pledgee may collect the fruits of the subject matter of the pledge and apply them to satisfy the obligation in priority to other creditors.

2-The fruits mentioned in paragraph 1 above shall first be applied to the interest on the obligation, and any remainder shall be applied to the principal.

**Article 823.- Duty of Pledgee to Preserve the Subject Matter of Pledge**

1-A pledgee shall preserve the subject matter of the pledge with the care of a good manager.

2-If the pledgee violates the provisions of paragraph 1 above, the debtor may demand the extinction of the pledge.

**Article 824.- Claim for Reimbursement of Expenses by Pledgee**

1-If a pledgee has incurred necessary expenses for the maintenance and preservation of the subject matter of the pledge, he/she may demand reimbursement from the owner of the object.

2-If a pledgee has incurred beneficial expenses that increased the value of the subject matter of the pledge, he/she may demand reimbursement from the owner of the object for the whole of the expenses he/she incurred or for the increased value, at his/her option, provided that the increased value still exists. However, the court may allow a reasonable time for reimbursement upon the request of the owner.

**Article 825.- Extinctive Prescription of Secured Obligation**

The exercise of a pledge shall not prevent the running of extinctive prescription of the secured obligation.

**Article 826.- Re-pledge**

1-A pledgee may re-pledge the subject matter of the pledge within the scope of his/her own right with the consent of the pledgor.

2-During the term of his/her own right, a pledgee may re-pledge the subject matter of the pledge on his/her own responsibility. In such case...

the pledgee shall also be responsible for loss or damage caused by force majeure that would not have occurred if the re-pledge had not been made.

3-Upon the sale of the subject matter of the pledge by compulsory sale, the sub-pledgee may receive performance of his/her obligation out of the amount to be distributed to the original pledgee. However, if the obligation in favor of the sub-pledgee has not yet become due, he/she may only demand that the amount to be distributed be officially deposited.

**Article 827.- Prohibition of Forfeiture Agreement**

In the absence of a special provision in this Code or other laws, the pledgor may not, by the act of creation of the pledge or by a contract entered into before the obligation becomes due, agree that the pledgee shall acquire ownership of the subject matter of the pledge or dispose of it in any manner other than as provided by law.

**Article 828.- Right to Indemnification of Real Surety**

If a person who created a pledge to secure the obligation of another has performed such obligation or lost ownership of the subject matter of the pledge as a result of the enforcement of the pledge, he/she shall have the right to be indemnified by the debtor in accordance with the provisions on suretyship.

##### Section 2
##### Pledge of Movables

**Article 829.- Requirements for Asserting Pledge against Third Party**

A pledgee of movables may not assert his/her pledge against a third party...

unless he/she continues to possess the subject matter of the pledge.

**Article 830.- Case of Deprivation of Possession**

If a pledgee of movables is deprived of his/her possession of the subject matter of the pledge, he/she may recover it only by means of a claim for recovery of possession.

**Article 831.- Use of and Collection of Fruits from Subject Matter of Pledge by Pledgee**

1-A pledgee may not use or lease the subject matter of the pledge without the consent of the debtor. However, the pledgee may use it to the extent necessary for its preservation.

2-If the pledgee violates the provisions of paragraph 1 above, the debtor may demand the extinction of the pledge.

**Article 832.- Summary Enforcement of Pledge**

1-If a pledgee of movables is not paid, he/she may apply to the court to have the subject matter of the pledge appropriated for the performance of the obligation at its value as determined by an appraiser, provided that there is a justifiable reason. In such case, the pledgee shall give prior notice of such application to the debtor.

2-In the case mentioned in paragraph 1 above, if the value determined by the appraiser exceeds the amount of the obligation to be paid to the pledgee of movables, the pledgee shall return the excess amount to the owner of the subject matter of the pledge.

**Article 833.- Priority of Pledges of Movables**

If two or more pledges are created with respect to the same movable, the priority of these pledges shall be determined by the order of their creation.

##### Section 3
##### Pledge of Immovables

**Article 834.- Right to Use and Collect Fruits by Pledgee**

1-A pledgee of an immovable may use and collect the fruits of the immovable that is the subject matter of the pledge in accordance with the ordinary method of use of the immovable.

2-With respect to land used for agriculture by a pledgee of an immovable created to secure an obligation without a fixed term, even if the obligation is extinguished by performance, if the harvest of crops sown or planted by the pledgee is to take place within 1 (one) year, the pledgee may continue to use the land until such harvest.

3-With respect to land used for agriculture by a pledgee of an immovable created to secure an obligation with a fixed term, even if the obligation is extinguished by performance before the due date, if the harvest of crops sown or planted by the pledgee is to take place within 1 (one) year, the pledgee may continue to use the land until such harvest. However, even if the harvest is to take place within 1 (one) year, if the harvest is to take place after the original due date of the obligation, the pledgee shall return the land immediately upon receiving performance.

**Article 835.- Responsibility for Management Expenses**

A pledgee of an immovable shall pay the management expenses and all other charges on the immovable.

**Article 836.- Interest on Secured Obligation**

A pledgee of an immovable may not claim interest on his/her obligation.

**Article 837.- Effectiveness of Special Agreement**

The provisions of Article 834 (Right to use and collect fruits by pledgee), Article 835 (Responsibility for management expenses) and Article 836 (Interest on secured obligation) shall not apply if otherwise provided in the act of creation of the pledge.

**Article 838.- Duration of Pledge**

1-The duration of a pledge of an immovable may not exceed 5 (five) years. If a pledge of an immovable is created for a longer period, it shall be reduced to 5 (five) years.

2-A pledge of an immovable may be renewed. However, its duration may not exceed 5 (five) years from the time of renewal.

**Article 839.- Application Mutatis Mutandis of Provisions on Hypothec**

In addition to the provisions of this Section 3, the provisions of Chapter 5 (Hypothec) shall apply mutatis mutandis to a pledge of an immovable.

##### Section 4
##### Pledge of Rights

**Article 840.- Subject Matter of Pledge of Rights**

1-A pledge may be created over a property right.

2-In addition to the provisions of this Section 4, the provisions of Section 2 (Pledge of movables) and Section 3 (Pledge of immovables) shall apply mutatis mutandis to the pledge mentioned in paragraph 1 above.

**Article 841.- Requirements for Asserting Pledge of Nominative Claim**

1-If a nominative claim is the subject matter of a pledge, the pledge may not be asserted against the third party debtor or any other third party unless notice of the creation of the pledge has been given to the third party debtor or the third party debtor has consented thereto.

2-The notice or consent mentioned in paragraph 1 above may not be asserted against a third party other than the third party debtor unless it is made by means of an instrument bearing a fixed date.

**Article 842.- Enforcement of Pledge by Collection of Claim**

1-A pledgee may directly collect the claim that is the subject matter of the pledge.

2-If the subject matter of the claim is money, the pledgee may collect only the portion that corresponds to the amount of his/her own claim.

3-In the case mentioned in paragraph 2 above, if the due date of the...

due date of the pledged claim arrives before the due date of the pledgee's own claim, the pledgee may require the third party debtor to officially deposit the amount of the payment. In such case, the pledge shall exist over the amount so deposited.

4-If the subject matter of the pledged claim is not money, the pledgee shall have a pledge over the object received as performance.

#### Chapter 5
#### Hypothec

##### Section 1
##### Meaning of Hypothec

**Article 843.- Meaning of Hypothec**

1-A hypothee has a right to receive performance of his/her claim in priority to other creditors out of the immovable property that the debtor or a third party has provided as security for the obligation without transferring possession thereof.

2-A perpetual lease and usufruct may also be the subject matter of a hypothec. In such case, the provisions of this Chapter 5 (Hypothec) shall apply mutatis mutandis.

3-If a special law allows other property to be the subject matter of a hypothec, the provisions of such special law shall apply.

##### Section 2
##### Creation of Hypothec

**Article 844.- Creation of Hypothec**

A hypothec is created by an agreement between the creditor and...

the debtor or a third party who provides his/her own immovable property as security.

**Article 845.- Assertion of Hypothec**

A hypothee may not assert the hypothec against a third party other than the creator of the hypothec unless the contract for the creation of the hypothec is made by means of an authentic document and is registered in the Land Register.

##### Section 3
##### Effectiveness of Hypothec

**Article 846.- Scope of Effectiveness of Hypothec**

1-A hypothec shall extend to all things that are attached to and form part of the land that is the subject matter of the hypothec at the time of its creation, including buildings thereon. It shall also extend to things that are attached to the land after the creation of the hypothec.

2-A different provision may not be made by the parties in the contract for the creation of the hypothec with respect to the provision of paragraph 1 above.

**Article 847.- Effect of Hypothec over Land on Building Owned by Third Party**

If a third party owns a building on the land that is the subject matter of a hypothec based on a perpetual lease, usufruct or lease, the hypothec shall not extend to such building.

**Article 848.- Effect of Hypothec on Fruits**

The provisions of Article 846 (Scope of effectiveness of hypothec) shall not apply to fruits. However, the provision of the first sentence of this paragraph shall not apply...

after the hypothecated immovable has been seized.

**Article 849.- Real Subrogation**

1-A hypothec may also be exercised against money or other objects that the creator of the hypothec is entitled to receive as a result of the sale, loss of or damage to the subject matter of the hypothec. However, the hypothee may not exercise it after such money has been paid or such other objects have been delivered to the creator.

2-A hypothec may also be exercised against fruits produced after the seizure of the hypothecated immovable.

**Article 850.- Right to Indemnification of Real Surety**

If a person who created a hypothec to secure the obligation of another has performed such obligation or lost ownership of the hypothecated immovable as a result of the enforcement of the hypothec, he/she shall have the right to be indemnified by the debtor in accordance with the provisions on suretyship.

**Article 851.- Priority of Hypothecs**

If two or more hypothecs are created with respect to the same immovable, the priority thereof shall be in the order of their registration.

**Article 852.- Scope of Secured Obligation**

1-If a hypothee has a right to claim interest or other periodical payments, he/she may exercise the hypothec only with respect to the portions that have become due during the last 2 (two) years. However, if a special registration is made with respect to prior periodical payments after they have become due, the hypothec may be exercised from the time of such registration.

2-The provisions of paragraph 1 above shall also apply to...

due for the last 2 (two) years, in cases where the hypothee has a right to claim damages resulting from non-performance of the obligation. However, the total amount of damages, interest and other periodical payments may not exceed the total amount due for the last 2 (two) years.

##### Section 4
##### Enforcement of Hypothec

**Article 853.- Compulsory Sale of Hypothecated Immovable**

In the event of non-performance of the obligation, the hypothee may apply to the court for a compulsory sale of the hypothecated immovable.

**Article 854.- Compulsory Sale of Building Owned by Third Party**

1-If the creator of the hypothec owned a building that he/she or a third party built on the land after the creation of the hypothec, the hypothee may sell such building together with the land. However, if the price of the land and building together is lower than the price of the land without the building, the hypothee may require the creator of the hypothec to remove the building before the compulsory sale.

2-If a third party owns a building on the hypothecated land based on a perpetual lease, usufruct or lease, and if such third party cannot assert his/her perpetual lease, usufruct or lease against the hypothee, the hypothee may sell such building together with the land.

**Article 855.- Purchase by Third Party Purchaser**

A third party who has purchased the hypothecated immovable may also be the purchaser at the compulsory sale.

**Article 856.- Right of Third Party Purchaser to Demand Reimbursement of Expenses**

If a third party who has purchased the hypothecated immovable has incurred necessary expenses for its management or preservation, or beneficial expenses that increased its value, he/she may receive reimbursement out of the proceeds of the compulsory sale of the immovable in priority to the hypotheee.

**Article 857.- Simultaneous Distribution and Asynchronous Distribution in Case of Joint Hypothec**

1-If a creditor has a hypothec over several immovables to secure one claim, and the proceeds of the compulsory sale are to be distributed simultaneously, the burden of the claim shall be divided in proportion to the value of each immovable.

2-If the proceeds of the compulsory sale of only one of the immovables are to be distributed, the hypothee may receive performance of his/her entire claim out of such proceeds. In such case, a hypothee with the next priority may exercise the hypothec of the prior hypothee by subrogation up to the amount that the prior hypothee would have received out of the other immovables in accordance with the provision of paragraph 1 above.

**Article 858.- Joint Hypothec - Registration of Subrogation**

A person who exercises a hypothec by subrogation in accordance with the provision...

of paragraph 2 of Article 857 (Simultaneous distribution and asynchronous distribution in case of joint hypothec) above must register such subrogation in the register where the hypothec is registered.

##### Section 5
##### Disposition of Hypothec

**Article 859.- Sub-hypothec**

1-A hypothee may provide his/her hypothec as security for his/her own obligation or that of a third party. This is called a sub-hypothec.

2-Upon the distribution of the proceeds of the compulsory sale of the hypothecated immovable, the sub-hypothee may receive performance of his/her entire claim out of the amount to be distributed to the original hypothee. However, if the obligation in favor of the sub-hypothee has not yet become due, he/she may only demand that the amount to be distributed be officially deposited.

**Article 860.- Transfer or Waiver of Hypothec**

1-A hypothee may transfer or waive his/her hypothec for the benefit of another creditor of the same debtor.

2-The transferee of a hypothec may exercise the hypothec he/she received as security for his/her own claim. In such case, the provisions of paragraph 2 of Article 859 (Sub-hypothec) shall apply mutatis mutandis.

3-A person who has waived his/her hypothec may not assert its effect against the person for whose benefit the waiver was made.

**Article 861.- Transfer, Waiver or Change of Priority of Hypothec**

(As amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code)

1-A hypothee may transfer or waive his/her priority for the benefit of another hypothee of the same debtor. Furthermore, a hypothee may also change his/her priority with the consent of another hypothee. However, if such change would affect the interests of a third party, the consent of such party must be obtained.

2-A hypothee who has received a transfer of priority may receive performance of his/her entire claim in priority to the hypothee who transferred his/her priority out of the total amount of the distribution to be made to both hypothees from the proceeds of the compulsory sale of the hypothecated immovable.

3-A hypothee who has received a waiver of priority may receive performance of his/her claim together with the hypothee who waived his/her priority in proportion to the amount of their respective claims out of the total amount of the distribution to be made to both hypothees from the proceeds of the compulsory sale of the hypothecated immovable.

4-A hypothee who has changed his/her priority may exercise the hypothec in accordance with the agreement.

**Article 862.- Effect of Disposition of Hypothec**

1-The dispositions of a hypothec provided in Article 859 (Sub-hypothec), Article 860 (Transfer or waiver of hypothec) and Article 861 (Transfer, waiver or change of priority of hypothec) above shall not be effective unless they are made by means of an authentic document and a supplemental registration is made in the register.

2-A disposition of a hypothec may not be asserted against the principal debtor, a surety, the creator of the hypothec or their successors unless notice of the disposition has been given to the principal debtor or the principal debtor has consented thereto.

3-If the principal debtor has received notice or given consent as provided in paragraph 2 above, any payment made to the disposer of the hypothec without the consent of the person who received the benefit of the disposition shall not be asserted against the person who received such benefit.

**Article 863.- Priority of Dispositions of Hypothec**

If a hypothee has made more than one disposition of his/her hypothec as provided in Article 859 (Sub-hypothec), Article 860 (Transfer or waiver of hypothec) and Article 861 (Transfer, waiver or change of priority of hypothec) above, the priority of the persons who received the benefit of such dispositions shall be determined in the order of the supplemental registrations in the register.

##### Section 6
##### Extinction of Hypothec

###### Subsection 1
##### Extinction by Performance to Hypothee

**Article 864.- Payment of Purchase Price**

If a third party who has purchased ownership of, a perpetual lease of or a usufruct in the hypothecated immovable pays the purchase price to the hypothee at...

the request of the hypothee, the hypothec shall be extinguished as against such third party.

###### Subsection 2
###### Extinction by Prescription

**Article 865.- Extincitve Prescription of Hypothec**

A hypothec shall not be extinguished by prescription as against the debtor and the creator of the hypothec unless the secured obligation is extinguished at the same time.

**Article 866.- Extinction of Hypothec by Acquisitive Prescription of Hypothecated Immovable**

If a person other than the debtor or the creator of the hypothec has possessed the hypothecated immovable in accordance with the requirements for acquisitive prescription, the hypothec shall be extinguished. However, this shall not apply if the third party purchaser knew of the existence of the hypothec at the time he/she commenced possession.

##### Section 7
##### Floating Hypothec

**Article 867.- Definition of Floating Hypothec**

1-A hypothec may be created to secure unspecified obligations of a certain type that may arise from a continuous business relationship between the creditor and the debtor, up to a maximum amount. Such a hypothec is called a floating hypothec.

2-The scope of unspecified obligations to be secured by a floating hypothec...

shall be specified in the contract for the creation of the floating hypothec.

**Article 868.- Scope of Secured Obligation**

A floating hypotheee may exercise his/her hypothec up to the maximum amount with respect to the principal fixed, interest and other periodical payments, and damages resulting from non-performance of the obligation.

**Article 869.- Alteration of Scope of Secured Obligation**

1-Before the principal is fixed, the parties to a contract for the creation of a floating hypothec may alter the scope of the obligations to be secured by the floating hypothec by agreement.

2-To make the alteration provided in paragraph 1 above, it is not necessary to obtain the consent of subordinate hypothees or other third parties.

3-The alteration provided in paragraph 1 above must be registered before the principal is fixed.

**Article 870.- Alteration of Maximum Amount**

1-The parties to a contract for the creation of a floating hypothec may alter the maximum amount of the floating hypothec by agreement.

2-To make the alteration provided in paragraph 1 above, the consent of interested parties must be obtained.

3-The alteration provided in paragraph 1 above may not be asserted against a third party other than those who have consented as provided in paragraph 2 above...

unless the alteration is registered.

**Article 871.- Date for Fixing Principal**

1-The date on which the principal secured by a floating hypothec is to be fixed may be determined, or such determined date may be changed.

2-The provisions of paragraph 2 of Article 869 (Alteration of scope of secured obligation) above shall apply mutatis mutandis to the case mentioned in paragraph 1 above.

3-The date mentioned in paragraph 1 above must be within 5 (five) years from the date on which it was determined or changed.

4-The change of the date mentioned in paragraph 1 above must be registered before such date.

**Article 872.- Transfer of Secured Obligation or Assumption of Debt**

1-A person who has acquired a claim from a floating hypotheee before the principal is fixed may not exercise the floating hypothec with respect to such claim. A person who has performed the obligation on behalf of the debtor before the principal is fixed may not exercise the floating hypothec by subrogation based on such performance.

2-If a debt has been assumed before the principal is fixed, the floating hypotheee may not exercise the floating hypothec with respect to the obligation of the person who assumed the debt.

**Article 873.- Inheritance of Floating Hypotheee or Debtor**

1-If inheritance of the floating hypotheee has commenced before the principal is fixed, the floating hypothec shall secure the claims existing at the time the inheritance commenced. Furthermore, the floating hypothec shall also secure claims acquired after the inheritance commenced by a successor determined by agreement between the successors and the creator of the floating hypothec.

2-If inheritance of the debtor has commenced before the principal is fixed, the floating hypothec shall secure the obligations existing at the time the inheritance commenced. Furthermore, the floating hypothec shall also secure obligations assumed after the inheritance commenced by a successor determined by agreement between the floating hypotheee and the creator of the floating hypothec.

3-The provisions of paragraph 2 of Article 869 (Alteration of scope of secured obligation) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to the agreement mentioned in paragraphs 1 and 2 above.

4-If the agreement mentioned in paragraphs 1 and 2 above is not registered within 6 (six) months after the inheritance commenced, the principal shall be deemed to have been fixed at the time the inheritance commenced.

**Article 874.- Merger of Floating Hypotheee or Debtor**

1-If a merger of the floating hypotheee has occurred...

before the principal was fixed, the floating hypothec shall secure the claims existing at the time of the merger. Furthermore, the floating hypothec shall also secure claims acquired after the merger by the juristic person that continues to exist after the merger or the juristic person that is established by the merger.

2-If a merger of the debtor has occurred before the principal was fixed, the floating hypothec shall secure the obligations existing at the time of the merger. Furthermore, the floating hypothec shall also secure obligations assumed after the merger by the juristic person that continues to exist after the merger or the juristic person that is established by the merger.

3-In the cases mentioned in paragraphs 1 and 2 above, the creator of the floating hypothec may demand that the principal be fixed. However, this shall not apply to the case mentioned in paragraph 2 above if the debtor is the creator of the floating hypothec.

4-If a demand mentioned in paragraph 3 above has been made, the principal shall be deemed to have been fixed at the time of the merger.

5-The demand mentioned in paragraph 3 above may not be made if 2 (two) weeks have elapsed since the creator of the floating hypothec became aware of the merger. The same shall apply if 1 (one) month has elapsed since the date of the merger.

**Article 875.- Division of Floating Hypotheee or Debtor**

1-If a division of the floating hypotheee juristic person has occurred before the principal is fixed, the floating hypothec shall secure the claims existing at the time of the division. Furthermore, the floating hypothec shall also secure claims acquired after the division by the juristic person that was divided or the juristic person that was established by the division, or the juristic person that was divided and the juristic person that succeeded to the business.

2-If a division of the debtor juristic person has occurred before the principal is fixed, the floating hypothec shall secure the obligations existing at the time of the division. Furthermore, the floating hypothec shall also secure obligations assumed after the division by the juristic person that was divided or the juristic person that was established by the division, or the juristic person that was divided and the juristic person that succeeded to the business.

3-The provisions of paragraphs 3, 4 and 5 of Article 874 (Merger of floating hypotheee or debtor) above shall apply mutatis mutandis to the cases mentioned in paragraphs 1 and 2 of this Article.

**Article 876.- Disposition of Floating Hypothec**

1-Before the principal is fixed, the floating hypotheee may not make any disposition of the hypothec as provided in paragraph 1 of Article 860 (Transfer or waiver of hypothec) or paragraph 1 of Article 861 (Transfer, waiver or change of priority of hypothec) above. However, this shall not prevent a disposition as provided in paragraph 1...

of Article 859 (Sub-hypothec) of this Code.

2-The provisions of paragraph 3 of Article 862 (Effect of disposition of hypothec) of this Code shall not apply to payments made before the principal is fixed in the case mentioned in the second sentence of paragraph 1 of this Article.

**Article 877.- Transfer of Floating Hypothec**

1-Before the principal is fixed, a floating hypotheee may transfer the floating hypothec with the consent of the creator of the floating hypothec.

2-A floating hypotheee may divide the floating hypothec into two and transfer either of them in accordance with the provision of paragraph 1 above. In such case, if the floating hypothec was use as security for a third party's right, such right shall be extinguished as against the part of the floating hypothec that was transferred.

3-To make the transfer provided in paragraph 2 above, the floating hypotheee must obtain the consent of the third party mentioned in the second sentence of paragraph 2 above.

**Article 878.- Partial Transfer of Floating Hypothec**

Before the principal is fixed, a floating hypotheee may partially transfer the floating hypothec with the consent of the creator of the floating hypothec and may hold the floating hypothec jointly with the transferee.

**Article 879.- Co-ownership of Floating Hypothec**

1-Co-owners of a floating hypothec shall receive performance in proportion to their respective claims. However, if they agreed on a different proportion or that one of them should receive performance in priority to the others before the principal is fixed, such agreement shall govern.

2-A co-owner of a floating hypothec may transfer his/her right in accordance with the provision of paragraph 1 of Article 877 (Transfer of floating hypothec) above with the consent of the other co-owners.

**Article 880.- Effect of Transfer of Floating Hypothec by Floating Hypotheee who has Received Transfer or Waiver of priority**

If a floating hypotheee who has received a transfer or waiver of priority has transferred his/her floating hypothec in whole or in part, the transferee shall receive the benefit of such transfer or waiver.

**Article 881.- Joint Floating Hypothec**

The provisions of Article 857 (Simultaneous distribution and asynchronous distribution in case of joint hypothec) and Article 858 (Joint hypothec - registration of subrogation) of this Code shall apply to a floating hypothec only where several floating hypothecs are created simultaneously with respect to several immovables to secure one claim and this fact is registered.

**Article 882.- Alteration, Transfer and Fixing of Principal of Joint Floating Hypothec**

1-An alteration of the scope of the obligations to be secured, a change of the debtor, an alteration of the maximum amount or a transfer of the floating hypothec with respect to a floating hypothec for which the registration mentioned in Article 881 (Joint floating hypothec) above was made shall not be effective unless such alteration, change or transfer is registered with respect to all of the immovables.

2-The principal secured by a floating hypothec for which the registration mentioned in Article 881 (Joint floating hypothec) above was made shall be fixed even where a ground for fixing the principal has occurred with respect to only one of the immovables.

**Article 883.- Accumulation of Floating Hypothecs**

A person who has floating hypothecs with respect to several immovables may receive performance in priority out of the proceeds of each immovable up to their respective maximum amounts, except for the case mentioned in Article 881 (Joint floating hypothec) above.

**Article 884.- Demand for Fixing Principal**

1-After 3 (three) years have elapsed from the creation of a floating hypothec, the creator of the floating hypothec may demand that the principal be fixed. However, this shall not apply where the date for fixing the principal has been determined.

2-If the principal to be secured is no longer expected to arise due to an alteration of the scope of the obligations to be secured, the termination of the business relationship or other grounds...

the creator of the floating hypothec may demand that the principal be fixed.

3-A floating hypotheee may demand that the principal be fixed. However, this shall not apply where the date for fixing the principal has been determined.

4-If a demand mentioned in paragraph 1, 2 or 3 above has been made, the principal shall be fixed upon the lapse of 2 (two) weeks from the time of such demand.

**Article 885.- Grounds for Fixing Principal**

1-Other than the cases where a demand for fixing the principal was made in accordance with the provisions of Article 884 (Demand for fixing principal) above, the principal secured by a floating hypothec shall be fixed in the following cases:

(a) If the floating hypotheee has applied for a compulsory sale with respect to the hypothecated immovable. However, this shall only apply where the compulsory sale procedure has commenced.

(b) If the floating hypotheee has executed an attachment against the hypothecated immovable for taxes.

(c) If 2 (two) weeks have elapsed since the floating hypotheee became aware of the commencement of a compulsory sale procedure or an attachment for taxes against the hypothecated immovable.

(d) If the debtor or the creator of the floating hypothec has received an adjudication of bankruptcy.

2-If the effect of the compulsory sale procedure or attachment mentioned in point (c) of paragraph 1 above or the effect of the adjudication of bankruptcy mentioned in point (d) has ceased, the fixing of the principal shall be deemed not to have occurred. However, this shall not apply as against a person who has acquired the floating hypothec or a right for which the floating hypothec is the subject matter based on the assumption that the principal was fixed.

**Article 886.- Demand for Reduction of Maximum Amount**

1-After the principal is fixed, the creator of the floating hypothec may demand a reduction of the maximum amount of the floating hypothec to the sum of the amount of the existing obligations plus the amount of the interest and other periodical payments and damages resulting from non-performance of the obligations that will accrue during the next 2 (two) years.

2-With respect to a reduction of the maximum amount of a floating hypothec for which the registration mentioned in Article 881 (Joint floating hypothec) of this Code was made, a demand mentioned in paragraph 1 above with respect to only one of the immovables shall be sufficient.

**Article 887.- Demand for Extinction of Floating Hypothec**

1-If the amount of the existing obligations after the principal is fixed exceeds the maximum amount of the floating hypothec, a person who created the floating hypothec to secure another's obligation or a third party who has acquired ownership, a perpetual lease or a lease that can be asserted against a third party with respect to the hypothecated immovable may demand the extinction of the floating hypothec...

by paying or depositing an amount equal to the maximum amount. In such case, such payment or deposit shall have the effect of performance.

2-A floating hypothec for which the registration mentioned in Article 881 (Joint floating hypothec) of this Code was made shall be extinguished if the demand mentioned in paragraph 1 above is made with respect to any of the immovables.

3-The principal debtor, a surety and their successors may not make the demand for extinction provided in paragraph 1 above.

4-A third party who has acquired ownership or another right mentioned in paragraph 1 above subject to a condition precedent may not make the demand for extinction provided in paragraph 1 above during the period in which the fulfillment of the condition is pending.

#### Chapter 6
#### Transfer of Title as Security

##### Section 1
##### Definition of Transfer of Title as Security

**Article 888.- Definition of Transfer of Title as Security**

1-Transfer of title as security means the transfer of the title of a specified movable owned by the debtor or a third party to the creditor for the purpose of securing an obligation. In such case, if the obligation is performed, the title of the subject matter shall be transferred back to the creator.

2-Several movables associated with each other by their types and locations may be the subject matter of a transfer of title as security...

treated as one single movable even where individual movables included therein change.

##### Section 2
##### Creation of Transfer of Title as Security

**Article 889.- Creation of Transfer of Title as Security**

A transfer of title as security shall be created by an agreement between the creditor and the debtor or a third party who provides a movable for the purpose of a transfer of title as security.

**Article 890.- Perfection of Transfer of Title as Security**

A holder of title of a movable for security may not assert the transfer of title as security against a third party other than the creator unless he/she has received the transfer of possession of the subject matter in accordance with the provisions of Article 229 (Transfer of possession) of this Code.

##### Section 3
##### Effect of Transfer of Title as Security

**Article 891.- Scope of Effect of Transfer of Title as Security**

1-A transfer of title as security shall extend to everything that is joined to the subject matter and forms a part thereof at the time of the creation of the transfer of title as security. It shall also extend to everything that is joined to the subject matter after the creation of the transfer of title as security.

2-The parties may agree otherwise in the agreement creating the transfer of title as security.

**Article 892.- Effect of Transfer of Title as Security on Fruits**

1-Where the creator or a third party authorized by the creator remains in direct possession of the subject matter, the provisions of Article 891 (Scope of effect of transfer of title as security) above shall not apply to the fruits thereof. However, this shall not apply after the holder of title of a movable for security has demanded the delivery of the subject matter in accordance with the provisions of paragraph 2 of Article 898 (Disposition by conversion into cash or final transfer of title) of this Code.

2-Where the holder of title of a movable for security or a third party authorized by such holder has direct possession of the subject matter, the provisions of Article 822 (Right to receive performance from fruits in priority) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to the fruits produced by such subject matter.

**Article 893.- Real Subrogation**

1-A holder of title of a movable for security may exercise his/her right against money or other things that the creator is entitled to receive as a result of the sale, loss or damage of the subject matter. However, the holder of title of a movable for security may not exercise his/her right after such money or other things have been paid or delivered to the creator.

2-Notwithstanding the provisions of paragraph 1 above, if the subject matter of the transfer of title as security is the group of movables mentioned in paragraph 2 of Article 888 (Definition of transfer of title as security) of this Code and the creator intends to sell the subject matter to another person, the transfer of title as security may not be exercised against the proceeds of such sale.

**Article 894.- Disposition of Subject Matter by Creator**

1-If the creator, who remains in direct possession of the subject matter of the transfer of title as security after its creation, transfers the subject matter to a third party or creates a real security right over it for a third party, the third party may assert his/her right against the holder of title of a movable for security if the third party satisfies the requirements provided in Article 193 (Bona fde acquisition of ownership of movable) of this Code.

2-With respect to the group of movables mentioned in paragraph 2 of Article 888 (Definition of transfer of title as security) of this Code which the creator intends to sell to another person, if the creator, who remains in direct possession of the subject matter after the creation of the transfer of title as security, has sold the subject matter to a third party, the holder of title of a movable for security may not assert the transfer of title as security against such third party, even if the third party does not satisfy the requirements provided in Article 193 (Bona fde acquisition of ownership of movable) of this Code.

3-The provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2 above shall apply mutatis mutandis where a person other than the creator authorized by the creator remains in direct possession of the subject matter of the transfer of title as security after its creation and the creator has transferred the subject matter to a third party or created a real security right over it for a third party.

**Article 895.- Scope of Secured Obligation**
