A transfer of title as security shall secure the principal, interest, penalty, costs of exercising the transfer of title as security, costs of preserving the subject matter, and damages...

due to non-performance of the obligations or hidden defects of the subject matter. However, this shall not apply where otherwise agreed at the time of the creation of the transfer of title as security.

**Article 896.- Special Provision for Cases where Holder of Title of Movable for Security is in Possession of Subject Matter**

The provisions of Article 821 (Effect of retention), Article 823 (Duty of pledgee to preserve pledged thing) and Article 824 (Pledgee's right to reimbursement of expenses) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis where the holder of title of a movable for security or a third party authorized by such holder has direct possession of the subject matter.

**Article 897.- Disposition of Subject Matter by Holder of Title of Movable for Security**

1-If the holder of title of a movable for security, who remains in direct possession of the subject matter, transfers the subject matter to a third party or creates a real security right over it for a third party, the third party may assert his/her right against the creator if the third party satisfies the requirements provided in Article 193 (Bona fde acquisition of ownership of movable) of this Code.

2-The provision of paragraph 1 above shall apply mutatis mutandis where a person other than the holder of title of a movable for security authorized by the holder remains in direct possession of the subject matter and the holder of title of a movable for security has transferred the subject matter to a third party or created a real security right over it for a third party.

##### Section 4
##### Enforcement of Transfer of Title as Security

**Article 898.- Disposition by Conversion into Cash or Final Transfer of Title**

1-If the obligation is not performed, the holder of title of a movable for security may dispose of the subject matter by converting it into cash or finally acquire the title thereof by giving notice to the creator.

2-In the case mentioned in paragraph 1 above, if the creator or a third party authorized by the creator has direct possession of the subject matter, the holder of title of a movable for security may demand the delivery of the subject matter.

3-In the case mentioned in paragraph 1 above, if the value of the subject matter exceeds the amount of the obligation to be performed, the holder of title of a movable for security shall pay the difference to the creator as liquidation money.

**Article 899.- Creator's Right to Get Back**

Even after the time for performing the obligation has passed, the creator may get back the subject matter by performing the obligation in full and showing such performance to the holder of title of a movable for security, at any time before liquidation money is provided in accordance with the provisions of paragraph 3 of Article 898 (Disposition by conversion into cash or final transfer of title) above. However, this shall not apply where the value of the subject matter of the transfer of title as security does not exceed the amount of the obligation to be performed and the holder of title of a movable for security has notified the creator of such fact.

#### Chapter 7
#### Guaranty

##### Section 1
##### Creation of Contract of Guaranty

**Article 900.- Creation of Contract of Guaranty**

1-A contract of guaranty shall be created by an agreement between the person who intends to become a guarantor and the creditor, whereby the guarantor promises to perform the obligation in whole or in part on behalf of the principal debtor if the principal debtor fails to perform his/her obligation, and the creditor accepts such promise.

2-The parties to a contract of guaranty may make a special promise to indemnify, whereby the guarantor assumes an obligation to indemnify that is independent of the principal obligation. However, an obligation based on a special promise to indemnify may arise only if the person who assumes the obligation based on the special promise to indemnify is in the business of indemnifying or guaranteeing.

3-Where the person who intends to become a guarantor is not in the business of guaranteeing, the creditor shall provide him/her with important information regarding his/her becoming the guarantor and shall give him/her a sufficient opportunity to deliberate the matter.

4-If the creditor has acted in violation of paragraph 3 above, the guarantor may rescind the contract of guaranty.

**Article 901.- Formal Requirements for Contract of Guaranty**

1-A guaranty without a written document may be revoked at any time...

be revoked. However, this shall not apply if the guarantor has voluntarily commenced the performance of the guarantee obligation.

2-The provision of paragraph 1 above shall also apply where the amount of the guarantee obligation is not written in the guarantor's own handwriting in the case of a guarantee of an obligation to pay money.

3-The written document of guaranty must clearly state the substance of the guarantee obligation.

**Article 902.- Floating Guaranty**

1-Notwithstanding the provisions of paragraphs 2 and 3 of Article 901 (Formal requirements for contract of guaranty) above, a contract of floating guaranty, which secures unspecified future obligations arising from a continuous legal relationship, shall be valid only if it clearly states the continuous legal relationship that is the basis for the creation of the principal obligations.

2-If the contract of floating guaranty does not provide for the period of the guaranty, the floating guarantor may terminate the contract of floating guaranty for the future after a reasonable period has elapsed from its creation.

3-In the case of a contract of floating guaranty, if the state of the business or assets of the principal debtor has significantly deteriorated since the creation of the contract of floating guaranty, the floating guarantor may immediately terminate the contract of floating guaranty for the future.

4-In the case of a contract of floating guaranty, if the floating guarantor has died, only the guarantee obligations with respect to the principal obligations that have arisen up to the time of the guarantor's death shall be inherited.

##### Section 2
##### Effect of Guaranty

**Article 903.- Scope of Guarantee Obligation**

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code.)

1-The guarantee obligation shall include interest, penalties, and damages in connection with the principal obligation, and everything else that is incidental to the principal obligation.

2-A contract of guaranty may provide for a penalty or liquidated damages for non-performance of the guarantee obligation.

**Article 904.- Nature of Guaranty**

1-A contract of guaranty may not exist without a principal obligation. However, a contract of guaranty may be created with respect to a future obligation or a conditional obligation.

2-If the guarantor's burden is more onerous than the principal obligation in terms of its subject matter or conditions, it shall be reduced to the extent of the principal obligation.

3-If the principal obligation is extinguished, the guarantee obligation shall also be extinguished.

4-Interruption of the period of extinctive prescription by a demand against the principal debtor or other grounds shall also be effective against the guarantor.

5-If the claim against the principal debtor has been transferred, the claim against the guarantor shall also be deemed to have been transferred unless otherwise agreed.

**Article 905.- Rights of Guarantor**

1-The guarantor may assert the defense of extinctive prescription of the principal obligation...

.

2-The principal debtor's waiver of the benefit of extinctive prescription shall not be effective against the guarantor.

3-The guarantor may assert a set-off with a claim that the principal debtor has against the creditor.

4-So long as the principal debtor has the right to rescind or terminate the principal obligation, the guarantor may refuse to perform the guarantee obligation.

5-If the principal debtor is in a position to effect a set-off with a claim that he/she has against the creditor, the guarantor may refuse to perform the guarantee obligation to the extent that the principal obligation would be extinguished by such set-off.

**Article 906.- Guaranty of Voidable Obligation**

If a guarantor who is in the business of indemnifying or guaranteeing has guaranteed a voidable obligation while being aware of the grounds for its voidability, the guarantor shall be presumed to have assumed an independent obligation of the same substance as the principal obligation.

**Article 907.- Qualifications of Guarantor**

1-Where the principal debtor is bound to provide a guarantor, the guarantor must be a person of full capacity and have sufficient means to perform the obligation.

2-If the guarantor ceases to fulfill the requirements set forth in paragraph 1 above, the creditor may demand that the principal debtor provide another guarantor meeting those requirements.

3-If the principal debtor cannot provide a guarantor who fulfills the requirements...

mentioned in paragraph 1 above, the creditor may demand that the debtor provide other security.

4-This article shall not apply where the creditor has designated the guarantor.

**Article 908.- Meaning and Principle of Joint Guaranty**

1-A person who has assumed a joint guarantee obligation with the principal debtor may not demand that the creditor first demand performance from the principal debtor or show that the principal debtor has sufficient means to perform and that execution would be easy.

2-A guarantor shall be a joint guarantor with the principal debtor unless otherwise agreed.

**Article 909.- Effect of Events Occurring with respect to Guarantor**

Events occurring with respect to the guarantor shall not be effective as against the principal debtor. However, this shall not apply to a demand or other grounds for interruption of extinctive prescription with respect to a guarantor who has been commissioned by the principal debtor.

**Article 910.- Co-guaranty**

1-Where there are multiple guarantors in one contract, each guarantor shall be liable for the full amount of the principal obligation.

2-The shares of the burden among co-guarantors who have not made a special agreement on the shares shall be presumed to be equal.

##### Section 3
##### Right to Indemnification

**Article 911.- Right to Indemnification of Commissioned Guarantor**

1-Where a guarantor who has been commissioned by the principal debtor has extinguished the principal obligation by performing it or by incurring expenses of his/her own at his/her expense in place of the principal debtor, the guarantor shall be entitled to be indemnified by the principal debtor.

2-The guarantor mentioned in paragraph 1 above may demand the amount of his/her expenses, interest thereon from the day he/she incurred such expenses, and any damages he/she has sustained.

3-A guarantor commissioned by the principal debtor may demand indemnification in advance in the following cases:

(a) where the guarantor, without any fault on his/her part, has had a judgment ordering him/her to perform the obligation to the creditor rendered against him/her, and such judgment has become final and binding;

(b) where the principal debtor has been adjudicated bankrupt and the creditor does not participate in the bankruptcy proceedings;

(c) where the principal obligation has become due;

(d) where the time for performing the principal obligation is uncertain and cannot be determined even by referring to the maximum possible period, and 5 (fve) years have elapsed from the creation of the contract of guaranty.

4-Where the creditor has not yet received full performance, the principal debtor from whom the commissioned guarantor has demanded indemnification...

may demand that the guarantor provide security or demand to be released from the obligation.

5-In the case mentioned in paragraph 4 above, the principal debtor may be released from the demand for indemnification by depositing money or providing security to the guarantor or by causing the guarantor to be released from the obligation.

**Article 912.- Right to Indemnification of Non-commissioned Guarantor**

If a person who has not been commissioned by the principal debtor has extinguished the principal obligation by performing it or by incurring expenses of his/her own, the principal debtor shall be bound to indemnify the guarantor only to the extent that the principal debtor was enriched at the time of performance. However, if the guaranty was provided against the will of the principal debtor, the principal debtor shall be bound to indemnify the guarantor only to the extent that the principal debtor is currently enriched.

**Article 913.- Requirements for Indemnification**

1-If a guarantor has extinguished the principal obligation by performing it or by incurring expenses of his/her own without notifying the principal debtor that he/she has received a demand from the creditor, and if the principal debtor had a defense against the creditor, the principal debtor may assert such defense against the guarantor. However, if such defense is a set-off, the guarantor may demand that the creditor perform the obligation that was supposed to be extinguished by the set-off.

2-If the principal debtor has performed the obligation to the creditor or otherwise extinguished the principal obligation for value in good faith because the guarantor...

negligently failed to notify the principal debtor that he/she had extinguished the obligation at his/her own expense, the principal debtor may treat his/her own act of performance or other act of extinction as valid.

**Article 914.- Right to Indemnification of Guarantor for One of Several Debtors**

A person who has guaranteed one of several joint and several debtors or debtors of an indivisible obligation may demand indemnification from the other debtors only in proportion to their respective shares of the burden.

**Article 915.- Right to Indemnification of Co-guarantors**

1-If one of several co-guarantors has extinguished the principal obligation by performing it or by incurring expenses of his/her own and has thereby released the other co-guarantors, the guarantor may demand indemnification from the other co-guarantors in proportion to their respective shares of the burden.

2-In the case mentioned in paragraph 1 above, the guarantor may demand the amount of his/her expenses, interest thereon and any damages he/she has sustained.

3-The provisions of Article 912 (Right to indemnification of non-commissioned guarantor), Article 913 (Requirements for indemnification) and Article 914 (Right to indemnification of guarantor for one of several debtors) above shall apply mutatis mutandis where several co-guarantors have made a special agreement on their respective shares of the burden and one of them has extinguished the principal obligation by performing it or by incurring expenses of his/her own in excess of his/her share.

##### Section 4
##### Subrogation

**Article 916.- Subrogation by Performance**

1-A guarantor who has extinguished the principal obligation by performing it or by incurring expenses of his/her own shall succeed to the claim held by the creditor against the principal debtor, and may exercise any security rights held by the creditor to secure such claim.

2-In the case mentioned in paragraph 1 above, a guarantor who has extinguished a part of the principal obligation may exercise his/her rights concurrently with the creditor in proportion to the part performed by him/her. In such case, only the creditor may terminate the contract. However, in such case, the creditor shall reimburse the subrogee for the part performed by him/her and interest thereon.

**Article 917.- Duty to Deliver Documents confirming Claim**

1-A creditor who has received full performance of the claim as a result of performance or expenses incurred by the guarantor shall deliver to the guarantor who has performed or incurred expenses the documents confirming the claim and any security in the creditor's possession.

2-A creditor who has received partial performance of the claim as a result of performance or expenses incurred by the guarantor shall record the subrogation in the documents confirming the claim and shall allow the guarantor to supervise the preservation of any security in the creditor's possession.

**Article 918.- Duty to Preserve Security**

A creditor who has received performance...

expenses shall have the duty to preserve security for the benefit of the guarantor who has performed or incurred expenses.

**Article 919.- Order of Subrogation**

1-A guarantor may not assert the subrogation against a third party who has acquired an immovable that is the subject of security unless he/she has registered the subrogation after his/her performance or payment of expenses.

2-As among personal guarantors and property guarantors, subrogation shall be effected in proportion to the number of such persons.

**Article 920.- Mutatis Mutandis Application of Provisions on Subrogation by Performance**

In addition to this Section 4 (Subrogation), the provisions of Part 4 (Subrogation by performance) of Section 1 of Chapter 7 of Part 4 shall apply mutatis mutandis to subrogation by performance or payment of expenses by a guarantor.

#### Chapter 8
#### Joint and Several Obligations

##### Section 1
##### Creation of Joint and Several Obligations

**Article 921.- Meaning of Joint and Several Obligations**

1-Where several persons assume a joint and several obligation, the creditor may demand full or partial performance from any one of the debtors, or from all of the debtors simultaneously or sequentially.

2-If all or any of the debtors have been adjudicated bankrupt, the creditor may participate in the distribution...

bankruptcy proceedings of each debtor for the full amount of his/her claim.

**Article 922.- Creation of Joint and Several Obligation**

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code.)

1-Except as otherwise provided by law, a joint and several obligation shall be created by an express or implied agreement between the creditor and several debtors whose mutual intent to be jointly and several liable is recognized.

2-The agreement mentioned in paragraph 1 above may be made between the creditor and each of the debtors simultaneously or sequentially.

3-The creditor may waive the joint and several liability with respect to any of the debtors.

4-After the waiver of joint and several liability, the debtor with respect to whom the waiver was made shall be liable only for his/her share of the burden as it existed before the waiver.

5-The shares of the burden of the joint and several debtors shall be presumed to be equal.

**Article 923.- Nullity and Rescission with respect to One Debtor**

The existence of grounds for nullity or rescission with respect to one of the joint and several debtors shall not affect the validity of the obligation of the other debtors.

##### Section 2
##### Effect of Events Occurring with respect to One Joint and Several Debtor

**Article 924.- Absolute Effect of Demand and Other Grounds for Interruption of Extinctive Prescription**

A demand for performance made against one joint and several debtor and other grounds for interruption of extinctive prescription occurring with respect to one joint and several debtor shall be effective against all other joint and several debtors.

**Article 925.- Absolute Effect of Performance, Etc.**

If one joint and several debtor performs the obligation, or otherwise extinguishes the obligation at his/her expense, such as by substitute performance or set-off, the extinction of the obligation shall be effective against all other joint and several debtors.

**Article 926.- Absolute Effect of Set-off**

1-If one joint and several debtor has a claim against the creditor and asserts a set-off, the claim shall be extinguished for the benefit of all joint and several debtors.

2-So long as the debtor who has a claim as mentioned in paragraph 1 above does not assert a set-off, any other joint and several debtor may refuse to perform his/her share of the burden.

**Article 927.- Absolute Effect of Novation**

If a novation is made between the creditor and one of the joint and several debtors, the claim shall be extinguished for the benefit of...

all debtors.

**Article 928.- Absolute Effect of Confusion**

If confusion of rights and obligations occurs between the creditor and one of the joint and several debtors, such debtor shall be deemed to have performed the obligation.

**Article 929.- Absolute Effect of Release**

1-A release from the entire obligation for one joint and several debtor shall be effective for the benefit of the other joint and several debtors only to the extent of the share of the burden of the debtor who was released.

2-A release from a part of the obligation for one joint and several debtor shall be effective for the benefit of the other joint and several debtors according to the proportion that such part bears to the total amount of the obligation for which all debtors are jointly and severally liable.

**Article 930.- Absolute Effect of Extinctive Prescription**

1-If one joint and several debtor asserts the defense of extinctive prescription that has been completed with respect to him/her, the other joint and several debtors shall also be released from the obligation to the extent of the share of the burden of such debtor.

2-In the case mentioned in paragraph 1 above, so long as the debtor with respect to whom extinctive prescription has been completed does not assert the defense of extinctive prescription, the other joint and several debtors may assert such defense to the extent of the share of the burden of such debtor.

**Article 931.- Principle of Relative Effect**

Except as provided in Articles 924 (Absolute effect of demand and other grounds for interruption of extinctive prescription) through 930 (Absolute effect of extinctive prescription) above, any event occurring with respect to one joint and several debtor shall not be effective against the other joint and several debtors.

##### Section 3
##### Right to Indemnification

**Article 932.- Right to Indemnification of Debtor who has Performed**

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code.)

1-If one joint and several debtor has extinguished the obligation by performing it or by incurring expenses of his/her own and has thereby released the other joint and several debtors, the debtor shall be entitled to be indemnified by the other joint and several debtors in proportion to their respective shares of the burden. If the performance or expenses of one joint and several debtor has extinguished only a part of the obligation and the other joint and several debtors have been released to that extent, such debtor may demand indemnification in proportion to their respective shares of the burden.

2-The demand for indemnification mentioned in paragraph 1 above may include legal interest from the day of the performance or other act of extinction, and any unavoidable expenses.

**Article 933.- Notification as Requirement for Indemnification**

1-If one joint and several debtor has extinguished the obligation by performing it or by incurring expenses of his/her own and has thereby released the other joint and several debtors, but failed to notify the other joint and several debtors that he/she had received a demand from the creditor, and if any other joint and several debtor had a defense against the creditor, such debtor may assert such defense against the debtor who has performed. However, if such defense is a set-off, the debtor who has performed may demand that the creditor perform the obligation that was supposed to be extinguished by the set-off.

2-If one joint and several debtor has extinguished the obligation by performing it or by incurring expenses of his/her own and has thereby released himself/herself and the other joint and several debtors, but failed to notify the other joint and several debtors of such fact, and if any other joint and several debtor has performed the obligation or otherwise extinguished it for value without knowledge of such fact, such other debtor may treat his/her own act of performance or other act of extinction as valid.

3-If performance or other act of extinction for value has been made by more than one joint and several debtor, and notification as mentioned in paragraphs 1 and 2 above has not been given, the performance or other act of extinction for value that was made first shall be treated as valid.

**Article 934.- Performance by Insolvent Debtor**

If one of the joint and several debtors is insolvent, the portion of the obligation that he/she is unable to perform shall be borne by the debtor seeking indemnification and the other solvent debtors in proportion to their respective shares of the burden. However, if the debtor seeking indemnification is at fault, he/she may not demand that the other debtors bear any part of such portion.

**Article 935.- Waiver of Joint and Several Liability and Share of Insolvent Debtor**

If one of the joint and several debtors has received a waiver of joint and several liability, and if there is an insolvent debtor among the other joint and several debtors, the creditor shall bear the portion of the insolvent debtor's share that the debtor who received the waiver would have been liable for.

##### Section 4
##### Subrogation by Performance

**Article 936.- Subrogation**

1-If one joint and several debtor has acquired a right to indemnification against the other joint and several debtors, the debtor seeking indemnification shall be subrogated to the creditor's rights against the other debtors and any security for such rights to the extent of the indemnification.

2-In addition to this Article, the provisions of Part 4 (Subrogation by performance)...

of Section 1 of Chapter 7 of Part 4 shall apply mutatis mutandis to subrogation by the debtor seeking indemnification.

##### Section 5
##### Other Forms of Obligations with Multiple Debtors

**Article 937.- Indivisible Obligations and Obligations Similiar to Joint and Several Obligations and Divisible Obligations**

1-The provisions of Article 921 (Meaning of joint and several obligations) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis where several persons assume an obligation with the same purpose toward one creditor, and their mutual intent to be jointly and severally liable is not recognized. However, if the performance of the obligation is divisible and the division would not be detrimental to the creditor's interest, each debtor shall be liable for the divided portion of the obligation in proportion to his/her share of the burden. If the shares of the burden are not certain, they shall be presumed to be equal.

2-In the case mentioned in the first sentence of paragraph 1 above, any event occurring with respect to one debtor shall not affect the other debtors, except as provided in Article 925 (Absolute effect of performance, etc.) and paragraph 1 of Article 926 (Absolute effect of set-off).

3-If one of the debtors mentioned in the first sentence of paragraph 1 above has extinguished the obligation by performing it or by incurring expenses of his/her own and has thereby released the other debtors, and if it is necessary to maintain equality among the debtors who have incurred expenses...

and the other debtors, the debtor who has incurred the expenses shall be entitled to be indemnified by the other debtors to the extent necessary to maintain such equality.

# BOOK 7
# RELATIVES

## Chapter 1
## General Provisions

**Article 938.- Scope of Relatives**

The following persons are relatives:

(a) Blood relatives within the sixth degree of consanguinity;

(b) Spouses;

(c) Relatives by marriage within the third degree of affinity.

**Article 939.- Calculation of Degree of Relationship**

1-The degree of relationship shall be determined by the number of generations between the relatives.

2-To determine the degree of relationship in the collateral line, the number of generations shall be counted from one person or his/her spouse up to the common ancestor, and then down from that ancestor to the other person.

**Article 940.- Creation of Relationship by Adoption**

The relationship between an adopted child and the adoptive parents and their blood relatives shall be the same as that of blood relatives from the date of adoption.

**Article 941.- Dissolution of Relationship by Affinity**

1-The relationship by affinity shall be dissolved by divorce.

2-The provision of paragraph 1 above shall also apply where one spouse has died and the surviving spouse has remarried...

remarried or has expressed its intention to dissolve the relationship by affinity.

**Article 942.- Dissolution of Relationship by Dissolution of Adoption**

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code.)

The relationship between an adopted child, his/her spouse, his/her lineal descendants and his/her spouse, and the adoptive parents and their blood relatives shall be dissolved by the dissolution of the adoption.

**Article 943.- Mutual Respect among Relatives and Prevention of Family Violence**

1-Each member of a family shall respect the rights and freedoms of the other members and shall support each other.

2-Each member of a family shall prevent family violence and ensure that no violation of human rights occurs.

## Chapter 2
## Engagement

**Article 944.- Formation of Engagement**

An engagement shall be formed by an agreement between a man and a woman that they will marry in the future and by performing a ceremony of engagement.

**Article 945.- Return of Engagement Gifts**

If an engagement is dissolved, a party who has given engagement gifts in expectation of marriage may demand the other party to return such gifts.

**Article 946.- Liability for Improper Rejection of Engagement**

If one party rejects an engagement without...

good reason, the other party may demand compensation for damages resulting from such rejection.

**Article 947.- Period for Filing Lawsuit**

A lawsuit related to Article 945 (Return of engagement gifts) or Article 946 (Liability for improper rejection of engagement) above must be filed within one year from the date the engagement is dissolved.

## Chapter 3
## Marriage

### Section 1
### Formation of Marriage

#### Part 1
#### Conditions for Marriage

**Article 948.- Marriageable Age**

A man and a woman who have not reached the age of majority may not marry. However, if one party has reached the age of majority and the other party is a minor at least 16 years of age, they may marry with the consent of the person exercising parental power or the guardian of such minor.

**Article 949.- Prohibition of Bigamy**

A person who has a spouse may not remarry.

**Article 950.- Period of Prohibition of Remarriage**

1-A woman may not remarry until 120 days have elapsed from the date of the dissolution or annulment of her previous marriage.

2-The provision of paragraph 1 above shall not apply if the woman was pregnant before the dissolution or annulment of the previous marriage and has given birth, or if she has a medical certificate from a physician stating that she is not pregnant.

**Article 951.- Prohibition of Marriage between Blood Relatives**

1-Marriage may not be contracted between direct blood relatives or between collateral blood relatives within the third degree of consanguinity.

2-The provision of paragraph 1 above shall also apply after the relationship has been dissolved by the provisions of Article 1014 (Termination of relationship with biological parents), Article 1016 (Effect of full adoption) or Article 1032 (Effect of dissolution of ordinary adoption) of this Code.

**Article 952.- Prohibition of Marriage between Relatives by Affinity**

1-Marriage may not be contracted between relatives by affinity in the direct line. This provision shall also apply after the relationship by affinity has been dissolved by the provisions of Article 941 (Dissolution of relationship by affinity) or Article 1016 (Effect of full adoption) of this Code.

2-Marriage may not be contracted between collateral relatives by affinity within the third degree of relationship. This provision shall also apply after the relationship by affinity has been dissolved by the provisions of Article 941 (Dissolution of relationship by affinity) above. However, if one spouse has died, the surviving spouse...

may marry a person who was a collateral relative by affinity within the third degree.

**Article 953.- Marriage of Minor**

1-If one of the parties wishing to marry is a minor, the consent of the person exercising parental power or the guardian of such minor must be obtained.

2-If one of the persons exercising parental power does not consent, the consent of the other person exercising parental power shall be sufficient.

3-If the person exercising parental power or the guardian refuses to consent without reasonable grounds, the minor wishing to marry may apply to the court for a judgment in lieu of such consent.

**Article 954.- Marriage of Person under General Guardianship**

A person under general guardianship may marry if he/she has the capacity to express his/her intent at the minimum level necessary for marriage. In this case, the consent of the general guardian is not required.

**Article 955.- Application for Marriage and Registration of Marriage**

1-A marriage shall become effective upon application for marriage, public notice, execution of the marriage contract and registration before the civil registrar.

2-The application for marriage, public notice, execution of the marriage contract and registration mentioned in paragraph 1 above shall be made in accordance with the procedures prescribed in the regulations on civil registration.

"general guardianship" - yes.
"civil registrar" - yes.
"public notice" - yes.
"marriage contract" - yes.
"registration" - yes.

Fixing.

**Article 956.- Acceptance of Application for Marriage**

An application for marriage may not be accepted if it is recognized that the marriage would contravene the provisions of Articles 948 (Marriageable age) through 954 (Marriage of person under general guardianship) above or other legal norms.

**Article 957.- Formalities of Marriage between Cambodian Citizens Living Abroad**

1-Cambodian citizens living abroad who wish to marry each other may apply for marriage, give public notice, execute the marriage contract and register the marriage before an Ambassador, Chargé d'Affaires or Consul of the Kingdom of Cambodia accredited to the country of their residence.

2-The public notice mentioned in paragraph 1 above shall be given by posting it on the bulletin board of the Embassy or Consulate of the Kingdom of Cambodia accredited to the country of residence.

#### Part 2
#### Nullity and Annulment of Marriage

**Article 958.- Nullity of Marriage**

A marriage shall be null and void only in the following cases:

(a) If there is no intent to marry between the parties due to mistake as to the identity of the other party, coercion or any other reason;

(b) If the parties have not applied for marriage, given public notice, executed the marriage contract or registered the marriage. However, if there is only a minor formal defect in the procedures, the validity of the marriage shall not be affected thereby.

**Article 959.- Annulment of Marriage**

A marriage may be annulled in accordance with the provisions of Articles 960 (Annulment of illegal marriage, etc.) through 963 (Annulment of marriage based on fraud or duress) below.

**Article 960.- Annulment of Illegal Marriage, Etc.**

1-In the case of a marriage contracted in contravention of the provisions of Articles 948 (Marriageable age) through 952 (Prohibition of marriage between relatives by affinity), either of the parties, their relatives or the public prosecutor may apply to the court for its annulment. However, the public prosecutor may not apply for annulment after the death of one of the parties.

2-In the case of a marriage contracted in contravention of Article 949 (Prohibition of bigamy) or Article 950 (Period of prohibition of remarriage), the spouse or former spouse of the parties may also apply for its annulment.

**Article 961.- Extinction of Right of Annulment of Marriage of Person under Marriageable Age**

1-A person reaching the marriageable age may no longer apply for the annulment of a marriage contracted in contravention of Article 948 (Marriageable age).

2-A party who was under the marriageable age at the time of the marriage may still apply for its annulment within 3 (three) months after reaching the marriageable age. However...

this provision shall not apply if such party has ratified the marriage after reaching the marriageable age.

**Article 962.- Annulment of Marriage Contracted during Period of Prohibition of Remarriage**

A marriage contracted in contravention of Article 950 (Period of prohibition of remarriage) may no longer be annulled if 120 days have elapsed from the date of the dissolution or annulment of the previous marriage, or if the woman has become pregnant after the remarriage.

**Article 963.- Annulment of Marriage based on Fraud or Duress**

1-A person who has been induced by fraud or duress to contract a marriage may apply to the court for its annulment.

2-The right of annulment mentioned in paragraph 1 above shall be extinguished if 3 (three) months have elapsed after the party has discovered the fraud or became free from the duress, or if the party has ratified the marriage.

**Article 964.- Effect of Annulment of Marriage**

1-The annulment of a marriage shall not have retroactive effect.

2-A party who was unaware of the grounds for annulment at the time of the marriage and who has acquired property by reason of the marriage shall return such property only to the extent that he/she still benefits therefrom.

3-A party who was aware of the grounds for annulment at the time of the marriage shall return all benefits acquired by reason of the marriage. If the other party acted in good faith, such party shall also be liable for damages.

4-The provisions of Article 988 (Presumption of paternity) shall apply mutatis mutandis to children born between the parties to a marriage that is annulled.

5-The provisions of Article 980 (Division of property) shall apply mutatis mutandis to the dissolution of a marriage by annulment.

##### Section 2
##### Effect of Marriage

**Article 965.- Surname of Husband and Wife**

As determined at the time of the marriage, the husband and wife may use the surname of either spouse or may use their respective original surnames held prior to the marriage.

**Article 966.- Duty to Live Together, Cooperate and Support Each Other**

Husband and wife shall live together, and shall cooperate with and support each other.

**Article 967.- Freedom to Choose Occupation and Right to Participate in Social Activities**

Husband and wife shall have the freedom to choose their occupation and may participate in political, cultural and social activities.

**Article 968.- Deemed reaching Age of Majority by Marriage**

If a minor marries, he/she shall be deemed to have reached the age of majority for the purposes of this Civil Code.
Check
Wait, Article 968 Khmer says: "ក្នុងការអនុវត្តក្រមរដ្ឋប្បវេណីនេះ" (in the implementation of this Civil Code).

Fixing.

##### Section 3
##### Matrimonial Property System

###### Part 1
###### Contractual Property System

**Article 969.- Matrimonial Property Contract and Statutory Property System**

1-Husband and wife may enter into a contract regarding their property relationship before or after the marriage. However, such contract may not contravene the provisions regarding the right to demand maintenance and the legally secured portion.

2-If the husband and wife have not entered into a special contract regarding their property, their property relationship shall be governed by the provisions of Part 2 (Statutory property system).

**Article 970.- Requirement of Writing for Matrimonial Property Contract and Conditions for Setting Up against Third Parties**

1-A matrimonial property contract must be in writing.

2-If the husband and wife have entered into a contract that differs from the statutory property system, it may not be set up against third parties unless it is registered.

###### Part 2
###### Statutory Property System

**Article 971.- Burden of Expenses of Marriage**

Husband and wife shall share the burden of the expenses arising from the marriage...

taking into account their property, income and all other circumstances.

**Article 972.- Separate Property**

The following property shall be separate property belonging to one of the spouses:

(a) Property held by a spouse before marriage;

(b) Property acquired by a spouse during marriage by gift, succession or legacy;

(c) Property acquired as consideration for the disposition of property mentioned in items (a) and (b) above.

**Article 973.- Common Property**

Common property is any property acquired by either or both spouses during the marriage, except for the property mentioned in items (b) and (c) of Article 972 (Separate property) above.

**Article 974.- Management and Use of Property**

1-Husband and wife shall have equal rights to use, enjoy benefits from and manage common property. Each spouse may use, enjoy benefits from and manage his/her separate property.

2-A spouse has the right to use common property to the extent necessary for daily life.

3-Husband and wife shall manage common property in cooperation with each other. One spouse may demand the other spouse to take necessary measures for the management or preservation of common property.

4-If a dispute arises regarding the management or use of common property...

is not achieved or if discussion is impossible, the other spouse may apply to the court for an order regarding the management or use of common property.

**Article 975.- Joint and Several Liability for Obligations**

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code.)

Husband and wife shall be jointly and severally liable for the following obligations:

(a) Expenses for maintaining the common life of the husband and wife, and expenses for education, medical treatment or care of children, etc.;

(b) Obligations and other duties agreed upon in writing between both spouses and the creditor during the marriage, or obligations and other duties incurred by one spouse with the written consent of the other spouse during the marriage;

(c) Expenses for the management and preservation of common property.

**Article 976.- Disposition of Common Property of Husband and Wife**

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code.)

1-Common property of husband and wife may not be sold or otherwise disposed of without the consent of both spouses.

2-Notwithstanding the provision of paragraph 1 above, if there are unavoidable circumstances for the maintenance of the common life in the marriage and to maintain the living standards, either spouse may sell or otherwise dispose of common property with the permission of the court.

3-In the case where one spouse has sold or otherwise disposed of common property without the consent of the other spouse and without the permission...

of the court, the spouse who did not consent may apply to the court for the annulment of such disposition within 2 (two) years from the time he/she became aware of such disposition if the common property disposed of is an immovable property used for the family residence, and within 1 (one) year from the time he/she became aware of such disposition if the common property disposed of is any other property.

**Article 977.- Use of Separate Property for Residence**

1-One spouse may reside in an immovable property which is the separate property of the other spouse if it is used for the family residence.

2-In the case where one spouse resides in the immovable property mentioned in paragraph 1 above, he/she may continue to reside there even if the other spouse has disposed of such immovable property.

##### Section 4
##### Divorce

###### Part 1
###### Grounds for Divorce

**Article 978.- Grounds for Divorce**

1-The husband or wife may file a petition for divorce only in the following cases:

(a) If the other spouse has committed an act of infidelity;

(b) If the other spouse has deserted the petitioner without good reason;

(c) If it has been unknown for more than 1 (one) year whether the other spouse is alive or dead;

(d) If the husband and wife have lived apart for more than 1 (one) year, contrary to the purpose of the marriage;

(e) Any other case where the marriage has broken down and there is no hope of reconciliation.

2-Notwithstanding the cases mentioned in paragraph 1 above, if the court finds that the divorce would cause extreme hardship to the life of the other spouse or the children, or would cause unbearable suffering, the court may reject the petition for divorce by taking into account all the circumstances.

3-In the cases mentioned in items (d) or (e) of paragraph 1 above, if the court finds that the petition is contrary to good faith because the spouse filing the petition has been seriously negligent in cooperating with and supporting the other spouse, the court may reject the petition for divorce by exercising its discretion.

**Article 979.- Divorce by Agreement**

The parties to a marriage may file a petition for divorce with the court if they have agreed to divorce. However, the court may grant the divorce only after it has determined that the parties truly intend to dissolve the marriage.

**Article 980.- Division of Property**

1-If the husband and wife divorce, the property shall be divided fairly based on the discussions between the parties.

2-If the husband and wife cannot reach an agreement through discussion, the property shall be divided based on the following rules:

(a) Each spouse shall receive the separate property determined...

in Article 972 (Separate property).

(b) In addition to the separate property, each spouse shall have the right to receive one-half of the common property. However, in special circumstances and upon the request of either spouse, the court may divide the common property by taking into account various factors such as the extent of contribution of each spouse to the acquisition, preservation or improvement of the property during the marriage, the living standards during the marriage, age, mental and physical condition, occupation, income, working capacity of each spouse and the interests of children, etc.

3-Housework shall be deemed to have the same value as work performed outside the home.

**Article 981.- Divorce and Surname**

A spouse who has changed his/her surname by reason of marriage may revert to his/her surname used before the marriage or may continue to use the surname used at the time of the divorce.

###### Part 2
###### Procedure for Divorce

**Article 982.- Petition for Divorce**

1-A petition for divorce may be filed with the competent court in accordance with the provisions of Article 10 (Special jurisdiction over divorce cases and cases concerning personal status) of the Code of Civil Procedure.

2-The parties may file an application for divorce with the Commune/Sangkat Council of their residence or domicile as provided in paragraphs 1 and 3 of Article 10 (Special jurisdiction over divorce cases and cases concerning personal status) of the Code of Civil Procedure...

In this case, the Commune/Sangkat Council may attempt reconciliation within 15 (fifteen) days after receiving the application. If reconciliation cannot be achieved, it shall be deemed that a petition for divorce has been filed, and the Commune/Sangkat Council shall immediately forward the petition to the court.

**Article 983.- Provisional Measures**

The court receiving a petition for divorce may, if necessary, issue an order for provisional measures regarding separation of residence, custody and care of children, management and preservation of property, maintenance of the spouse, burden of marriage expenses and child support, etc., upon application or of its own motion.

**Article 984.- Attempt at Reconciliation by Court**

1-Even if one party strongly demands a divorce, the court may attempt to reconcile the parties.

2-If reconciliation cannot be achieved, the court shall proceed with the judgment on the petition for divorce and pronounce a judgment.

## Chapter 4
## Parents and Children

### Section 1
### Paternity and Maternity

#### Part 1
#### General Provisions

**Article 985.- Duty to Register Birth**

1-The father or mother of a child shall apply for the registration of the birth of the child at the Commune/Sangkat office of the residence of the father or mother within 30 (thirty) days from the date of the child's birth. If the father or mother is unable to apply for registration, a relative of majority age living with the parents of the child shall apply for the registration of the birth as soon as possible.

2-If the parents fail to fulfill the duty mentioned in paragraph 1 above without good reason, they shall be subject to a civil fine not exceeding 10,000 (ten thousand) Riels.

**Article 986.- Method of Calculating Age**

1-Age shall be calculated from the date of birth.

2-The provisions of Article 335 (Calculation of period according to solar calendar) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to the calculation of age.

#### Part 2
#### Determination of Paternity and Maternity

**Article 987.- Maternity**

The woman who gave birth to a child shall be the mother of the child.

**Article 988.- Presumption of Paternity**

1-A child conceived by a wife during the marriage shall be presumed to be the child of the husband.

2-A child born after 180 (one hundred and eighty) days from the date of the formation of the marriage or within 300 (three hundred) days from the date of its dissolution or annulment shall be presumed to have been conceived during the marriage.

3-In the case where a woman remmaries in contravention of Article 950 (Period of prohibition of remarriage) and gives birth to a child, if the paternity of the child cannot be determined in accordance with the provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2 above, the child shall be presumed to be the child of the later husband.

**Article 989.- Denial of Paternity**

In the cases set forth in Article 988 (Presumption of paternity) above, the person presumed to be the father or the child may deny the paternity.

**Article 990.- Action for Denial of Paternity by Person Presumed to be Father**

1-The right of denial of the person presumed to be the father as provided in Article 988 (Presumption of paternity) above shall be exercised by an action against the child.

2-The person presumed to be the father shall file an action for denial of paternity within 6 (six) months from the time he became aware of the birth of the child.

3-If the person presumed to be the father is a person under general guardianship and the period mentioned in paragraph 2 above has expired, the period shall be calculated from the time of the rescission of the adjudication of commencement of general guardianship as provided in Article 27 (Rescission of adjudication of commencement of general guardianship) of this Code.

**Article 991.- Action for Denial of Paternity by Child**

1-The right of denial of a child as provided in Article 989 (Denial of paternity) of this Code shall be exercised by an action against the person presumed to be the father.

2-In the case where a child who is a minor exercises the right of denial, if the child has no mother exercising parental power or no guardian for the minor, the court shall appoint a special representative.

3-A child may not exercise the right of denial if 6 (six) months have elapsed from the time he/she has reached the age of majority.

4-If the child is a person under general guardianship and the period mentioned in paragraph 3 above has expired, the period shall be calculated from the time of the rescission of the adjudication of commencement of general guardianship as provided in Article 27 (Rescission of adjudication of commencement of general guardianship) of this Code.

**Article 992.- Retroactive Effect of Denial of Paternity**

The denial of paternity shall have retroactive effect from the time of the birth of the child.

#### Part 3
#### Acknowledgment

**Article 993.- Voluntary Acknowledgment**

The father of a child may acknowledge the child. However, if the child already has a legal father, he may not acknowledge the child.

**Article 994.- Capacity for Acknowledgment**

To acknowledge a child, even if the father is a minor or a person under general guardianship, the consent of his legal representative shall not be required.

**Article 995.- Acknowledgment by Father**

1-The father may acknowledge a minor child.

2-The father may acknowledge even an unborn child with the consent of the mother.

**Article 996.- Acknowledgment of Adult Child and Deceased Child**

1-A father wishing to acknowledge an adult child may not do so without the consent of the child.

2-A father may acknowledge a deceased child only if there is a lineal descendant of the child. In this case, if the descendant is an adult, his/her consent shall be obtained.

3-The provisions of paragraph 2 of Article 997 (Form of acknowledgment) below shall apply mutatis mutandis to the consent mentioned in paragraphs 1 and 2 of this Article.

**Article 997.- Form of Acknowledgment**

1-Acknowledgment shall be made by notification to the civil status registrar. Acknowledgment may also be made by will.

2-The consent of the mother mentioned in paragraph 2 of Article 995 (Acknowledgment by father) above may be given by signing the instrument of acknowledgment.

**Article 998.- Effect of Registration of Birth in Birth Register and Acknowledgment**

If a person applies for registration of birth of a child, registers him/herself as the father and signs or puts his/her thumbprint in the birth register, the person shall be deemed to have acknowledged the child at the time of the application for registration of birth.

**Article 999.- Retroactive Effect of Acknowledgment**

Acknowledgment of a child shall have retroactive effect from the time of the birth of the child. However, acknowledgment shall not prejudice any right already acquired by a third party.

**Article 1000.- Prohibition of Revocation of Acknowledgment**

A father who has acknowledged a child may not revoke the acknowledgment.

**Article 1001.- Action for Acknowledgment**

1-A child may file an action for acknowledgment. However, a child who has not consented as provided in paragraph 1 of Article 996 (Acknowledgment of adult child and deceased child) of this Code may not file an action for acknowledgment.

2-If the child has died, a lineal descendant of the child may file...

paternity acknowledgement action.

3-A child or his/her lineal descendant may file an action for acknowledgment within 1 (one) year from the time he/she became aware of the identity of the father.

4-In the case where the child or his/her lineal descendant is a minor or a person under general guardianship at the time he/she became aware of the identity of the father, the period mentioned in paragraph 3 above shall be calculated from the time the child or his/her lineal descendant reaches the age of majority or from the time of the rescission of the adjudication of commencement of general guardianship as provided in Article 27 (Rescission of adjudication of commencement of general guardianship) of this Code.

5-A child or his/her lineal descendant may file an action for acknowledgment within 1 (one) year from the date of the father's death, if he/she became aware of the identity of the father after the father's death.

6-With regard to the action mentioned in paragraphs 1 and 2 above, a legal representative of the child or the lineal descendant may file the action for acknowledgment on behalf of such person.

#### Part 4
#### Action for Clarification of Existence of Paternity or Maternity

**Article 1002.- Action for Clarification of Existence of Paternity or Maternity**

1-A child may file an action with the court for clarification of the existence of paternity or maternity against a person who is registered as the father in the birth register, even if there is no presumption of paternity, or against a person who is registered as the mother in the birth register, even if she did not give birth to the child.

4-The action mentioned in paragraphs 2 and 3 above may not be filed if the legal paternity or maternity was established through the intent or gross negligence of the person registered as the father or mother in the register, or if 20 (twenty) years have elapsed from the date of the child's birth or the date the effect of acknowledgement arose.

5-If the child is a minor, the father or mother who is not the other party to the action shall represent the child. If there is no father or mother, or if the father or mother is unable to represent the child, or if the action for clarification of the existence of paternity or maternity is filed against both parents as the other parties, the court shall appoint a special representative.

**Article 1003.- Retroactive Effect of Clarification of Existence of Paternity and Maternity**

Clarification of the existence of paternity or maternity shall have retroactive effect from the time of birth of the child. However, this shall not prejudice any right already acquired by a third party.

#### Action for Clarification of Existence of Maternity

**Article 1004.- Action for Clarification of Existence of Maternity by Child**

5-A child or his/her lineal descendant may file the action mentioned in paragraph 1 above within 1 (one) year from the date of the mother's death, if he/she became aware of the identity of the mother after the mother's death.

6-With regard to the action mentioned in paragraphs 1 and 2 above, a legal representative of the child or the lineal descendant may file the action for clarification of the existence of maternity on behalf of such person.

**Article 1005.- Action for Clarification of Existence of Maternity by Mother**

1-A mother may file an action for clarification of the existence of maternity against the child whom she gave birth.

2-A mother may file the action mentioned in paragraph 1 above against a deceased child only if there is a lineal descendant of the child.

5-If the mother is a person under general guardianship and the period mentioned in paragraphs 3 and 4 above has expired, the period mentioned in paragraphs 3 and 4 above shall be calculated from the time of the rescission of the adjudication of commencement of general guardianship as provided in Article 27 (Rescission of adjudication of commencement of general guardianship) of this Code.

Clarification of the existence of maternity shall have retroactive effect from the time of birth of the child. However, this shall not prejudice any right already acquired by a third party.

### Section 2
### Adoption

#### Part 1
#### Full Adoption

**Article 1007.- Formation of Full Adoption**

If the court finds that the conditions set forth in Articles 1008 (Joint adoption by spouses) through 1012 (Standard for judgment creating full adoption) below are met, the court may create a full adoption which terminates the relationship with the natural relatives of the parents, upon application of the persons who are to become the adoptive parents.

**Article 1008.- Joint Adoption by Spouses**

1-A person intended to become an adoptive parent must be a married person.

2-A husband or wife may not alone become an adoptive parent unless the other spouse also becomes an adoptive parent. However, this provision shall not apply if one spouse becomes the adoptive parent of a natural child of the other spouse.

**Article 1009.- Age of Adoptive Parents**

Adoptive parents must be at least 25 (twenty-five) years of age and must be at least 20 (twenty) years older than the child to be adopted.

**Article 1010.- Age of Child to be Adopted**

As a rule, the child to be adopted shall be less than 8 (eight) years of age.

**Article 1011.- Consent of Parents**

In order to create a full adoption, the consent of the natural parents or the guardian for a minor of the child to be adopted shall be required. However, this provision shall not apply if the parents are unable to express their intent, or if the parents have abused the child, or abandoned the child with malicious intent, or if there are other reasons that seriously violate the interests of the child to be adopted.

**Article 1012.- Standard for Judgment Creating Full Adoption**

A full adoption shall be created in cases where it is extremely difficult or inappropriate for the natural parents to care for the child to be adopted, or in other special circumstances, if it is found that there is a special need for the interests of the child.

**Article 1013.- Trial Period of Care**

In order to create a full adoption, the court shall consider the circumstances of the person who is to become the adoptive parent in caring for the child to be adopted for a period of, as a rule, at least 6 (six) months.

**Article 1014.- Termination of Relationship with Natural Parents**

The relationship between the adopted child and the natural parents, as well as the blood relatives of the natural parents, shall be terminated by full adoption. However, this provision shall not apply to the relationship with the other parent and the blood relatives of such parent as provided in the second sentence of paragraph 2 of Article 1008 (Joint adoption by spouses) of this Code.

**Article 1015.- Effect of Full Adoption**

1-By full adoption, the adopted child shall acquire the same status as a natural child of the adoptive parents, and shall have the same rights and duties as a natural child in relationship with the adoptive parents.

2-The adopted child may use the surname of the adoptive parents or the surname prior to the adoption.

3-The adopted child shall be subject to the parental power of the adoptive parents. However, in cases where one spouse becomes the adoptive father or mother of a natural child of the other spouse, the adopted child shall be subject to the joint parental power of the natural parent and the adoptive parent.

**Article 1016.- Dissolution of Full Adoption**

1-If the court finds that there is a special need for the interests of the adopted child, the court may dissolve the adoption upon application of the adopted child, the natural parents or a prosecutor, only in cases where all of the following points are met:

(a) If there is abuse by the adoptive parents, malicious abandonment of the adopted child, or other reasons that seriously violate the interests of the adopted child.

(b) If the natural parents are able to appropriately care for the child.

2-Dissolution of adoption may not be made except in accordance with the provisions of paragraph 1 above.

**Article 1017.- Effect of Dissolution of Full Adoption**

The relationship between the adopted child and the natural parents, as well as the blood-relatives of the natural parents, which was terminated by full adoption, shall be restored from the date of the dissolution.

**Article 1018.- Registration and Confidentiality of Full Adoption**

If a full adoption is created, a new birth record shall be prepared for the adopted child. However, no mention of the adoption shall be made in any birth certificate, transcript of birth certificate or extract from the birth register.

**Article 1019.- Right of Full Adopted Child to Know Origins**

An adopted child who has reached the age of majority may demand the court which maintains the adoption records to disclose information to the extent necessary. However, this provision shall not apply if the demand is made for an improper purpose. The maintenance of records and the procedure for demanding disclosure, etc. shall be prescribed by the rules of the court.

#### Part 2
#### Ordinary Adoption

**Article 1020.- Application for Ordinary Adoption**

1-A person who is at least 25 (twenty-five) years of age may, together with the person to be adopted, apply to the court for creation of an ordinary adoption. However, a person to be adopted may not be a lineal ascendant of the person who is to become the adoptive parent, nor may he/she be older than the person who is to become the adoptive parent.

2-In the case where the person to be adopted is a minor, the person exercising parental power or the guardian for the minor shall apply as provided in paragraph 1 above on behalf of such person.

**Article 1021.- Case where Person intended to become Adoptive Parent has Spouse**

If a person who has a spouse adopts a minor child, the person shall apply...

mentioned in Article 1020 (Application for ordinary adoption) above jointly with his/her spouse. However, this provision shall not apply if the person intended to become an adoptive parent adopts a child of his/her spouse or if the spouse is unable to express his/her intent.

**Article 1021.- Case where Person intended to become Adoptive Parent has Spouse**

A person who has a spouse shall obtain the consent of his/her spouse to apply for creation of an ordinary adoption. However, this provision shall not apply if the application is made jointly with the spouse as provided in Article 1020 (Application for ordinary adoption) above or if the spouse is unable to express his/her intent.

**Article 1023.- Standard for Judgment Creating Ordinary Adoption**

1-The court may create an adoption only in cases where the true intent of the parties to create a parent-child relationship can be confirmed.

2-The court shall not create an adoption if it finds that the adoption is being abused for the purpose of marriage between persons of the opposite sex or the same sex, or for any other improper purpose.

**Article 1024.- Case where Person to be Adopted is Minor**

1-If the person to be adopted is a minor, the court may create an adoption only if it finds that there is a special need for the interests of the minor.

2-In the case where the person to be adopted is a minor, if the natural parents of such person are divorced, the court shall hear the opinion of the father or mother who does not exercise parental power in order to judge on the point mentioned in paragraph 1 above.

**Article 1025.- Adoption Between Guardian and Ward**

In the case where a guardian for a minor applies for adoption of his/her ward, or a general guardian applies for adoption of his/her ward, the court may create the adoption only if the guardian has properly performed his/her duties of care for the ward and management of the ward's property. This provision shall apply even after the guardian's duties have terminated, but before the completion of accounting for the management of the property.

**Article 1026.- Effect of Ordinary Adoption on Paternity or Maternity**

1-Even after an ordinary adoption is created, the relationship between the adopted child and his/her natural parents shall continue to exist, and the adopted child and natural parents shall have the right to succeed each other.

2-The adopted child and the natural parents shall have rights and duties regarding support.

However, this provision shall not apply if one spouse is unable to express his/her intent.

1-Either party to an adoption may file an action for dissolution of adoption only in the following cases:

(c) If there are other grave reasons that make it difficult to continue the adoption.

**Article 1032.- Effect of Dissolution of Ordinary Adoption**

1-The effects of an ordinary adoption shall be terminated for the future by dissolution.

2-After the dissolution, the adopted child may resume the surname that the child used prior to the adoption, or may continue to use the surname of the adoptive parents.

**Article 1033.- Registration of Ordinary Adoption and Birth Record**

If an ordinary adoption is created, the fact of creation of the ordinary adoption shall be recorded in the original birth record of the adopted child.

### Chapter 5
### Parental Power

#### Section 1
#### General Provisions

**Article 1034.- Meaning of Parental Power**

A person exercising parental power shall have the right and duty to care for the person of a minor child and to manage the property of such child.

**Article 1035.- Parental Power over Adopted Child**

The persons exercising parental power over an adopted child are the adoptive parents.

**Article 1036.- Principle of Joint Exercise of Parental Power**

Parents who are married shall exercise parental power jointly. However, if either the father or the mother is unable to exercise parental power, it shall be exercised by the other parent.

#### Section 2
#### Determination of Person to Exercise Parental Power in Case of Divorce or Recognition

**Article 1037.- Determination of Person to Exercise Parental Power at Time of Divorce**

1-In the case of divorce, the parents shall determine by agreement which of them is to be the person exercising parental power.

2-In the case where the parents divorce before the birth of the child, the mother shall exercise parental power for the child born after the divorce. However, after the birth of the child, the father may be determined to be the person exercising parental power by agreement of the parents.

3-If the agreement mentioned in paragraph 1 or 2 above is not reached, or if the parents are unable to reach an agreement, the court shall determine the person to exercise parental power by considering the interests of the child.

**Article 1038.- Relationship between Recognition and Parental Power**

1-The person who exercises parental power for a child born to a mother who is not married is the mother.

2-The father may exercise parental power over a child whom he has recognized only in cases where the parents have determined by agreement that the father is to be the person exercising parental power.

3-If the agreement mentioned in paragraph 2 above is not reached, or if the parents are unable to reach an agreement, the court shall determine the person to exercise parental power...

parental power upon application of the father or the mother.

4-If the parents of a child who was recognized by the father marry each other, the parents shall exercise parental power jointly.

**Article 1039.- Change of Person to Exercise Parental Power**

In the cases provided in Article 1037 (Determination of person to exercise parental power at time of divorce) and Article 1038 (Relationship between recognition and parental power) above, the court may change the person to exercise parental power upon application of a relative of the child within the 4th (fourth) degree of relationship, if it finds it necessary for the interests of the child.

**Article 1040.- Rights and Duties of Parent Not Living with Child**

1-The father or mother who does not exercise parental power after divorce shall have the right to visit and communicate with the child, and shall have the duty to bear the expenses necessary for the care of the child.

2-Parents who intend to divorce shall determine by agreement the method of visiting and communicating with the child and the expenses necessary for the care of the child to be borne.

3-A father who does not live with a child whom he has recognized may determine by agreement with the mother the method of visiting and communicating with the child and the expenses necessary for the care of the child to be borne.

4-If the agreement mentioned in paragraph 2 or 3 above is not reached, or if the parents are unable to reach an agreement, the court shall determine the method of visiting and communicating with the child and the expenses necessary for the care of the child to be borne, upon application of the father or the mother.

5-If the court finds it necessary for the interests of the child, the court may change...

the method of visiting and communicating with the child and the expenses necessary for the care of the child to be borne, upon application of the father or the mother.

6-In making the decision provided in paragraphs 4 and 5 above, the court shall determine the method of visiting and communicating with the child by giving priority to the interests of the child, and shall determine the expenses necessary for the care of the child to be borne by considering the standard of living and the economic situation of the parents.

7-If the father or the mother is unable to reach an agreement or apply to the court, a relative of the child within the 4th (fourth) degree of relationship may make the application provided in paragraphs 4 and 5 above.

**Article 1041.- Right to Demand Report on Status of Child**

1-The father or mother who does not exercise parental power over a child may demand the person who exercises parental power to report on the status of the child.

2-The content and method of the report provided in paragraph 1 above shall be determined by agreement between the parents. However, if the agreement is not reached, or if the parents are unable to reach an agreement, the court may make a decision in lieu of the agreement, upon application of the father or the mother.

3-A relative of the child within the 4th (fourth) degree of relationship may demand the person who exercises parental power to report on the status of the child.

**Article 1042.- Duty to Discuss Important Matters Concerning Child**
(Amended by the Law on Implementation of the Civil Code.)

Except in cases of urgent necessity, the person exercising parental power shall discuss with the parent who does not exercise parental power about the following matters concerning the child:
Check
Wait, 1042 title: "Duty to Discuss Important Matters Concerning Child".

Fixing.

(a) Education of the child that goes beyond the compulsory education level prescribed by law.

(b) Change of the child's place of residence from one capital city or province to another.

(c) The child's profession or business.

(d) Medical treatment that affects the child's life.

(e) Disposition of the child's major property.

(f) Other important matters concerning the care of the child and the management of his/her property.

#### Section 3
#### Rights and Duties of Person Exercising Parental Power

**Article 1043.- Education of Child**

A person exercising parental power shall have the right and duty to educate the child.

**Article 1044.- Child's Place of Residence**

The child's place of residence shall be determined by the person exercising parental power.

**Article 1045.- Discipline of Child**

A person exercising parental power may personally discipline the child to the extent necessary.

**Article 1046.- Permission for Profession or Business**

1-A child may not engage in a profession unless he/she obtains permission from the person exercising parental power.

2-In the case provided in paragraph 2 of Article 20 (Minor who is permitted to...

**Article 1047.- Claim for Delivery of Child**

1-A person exercising parental power may demand a third party who unlawfully refuses to deliver the child to deliver the child.

2-A person exercising parental power may demand the parent who does not exercise parental power to deliver the child.

#### Section 4
#### Suspension and Divestment of Parental Power

**Article 1048.- Adjudication of Suspension or Divestment of Parental Power**

If a father or mother who exercises parental power abuses such power or is guilty of gross neglect in the exercise of such power, the court may adjudicate the suspension or divestment of the parental power upon application of any relative of the child within the 4th (fourth) degree of relationship, a commune chief or sangkat chief, the head of a public child welfare institution or a prosecutor.

**Article 1049.- Rescission of Adjudication of Suspension or Divestment of Parental Power**

If the causes provided in Article 1048 (Adjudication of suspension or divestment of parental power) above have ceased to exist, the court may rescind the adjudication of suspension or divestment of parental power upon application of the person who was subject to such adjudication or any relative within the 4th (fourth) degree of relationship of such person.

Fixing.

**Article 1050.- Resignation and Restoration of Parental Power**

1-If there are unavoidable reasons, the parent who exercises parental power may resign from the parental power upon obtaining the permission of the court.

2-If the reasons mentioned in paragraph 1 above have ceased to exist, the parent who resigned from the parental power may restore the parental power upon obtaining the permission of the court.

**Article 1051.- Change of Person to Exercise Parental Power**

1-In cases where only one parent exercises parental power and the child falls into a state where no one exercises parental power because such parent is subject to an adjudication of suspension or divestment of parental power or has resigned from the parental power, the court may determine that the parent who did not exercise parental power is to be the person exercising parental power for the child.

2-If it is necessary, a relative of the child within the 4th (fourth) degree of relationship may apply for the adjudication mentioned in paragraph 1 above.

3-At the time of divorce, if the parent who was determined to be the person exercising parental power by agreement of the parents dies, and if there is no person having parental power for the child, the other parent shall become the person exercising parental power.

**Article 1052.- Commencement of Guardianship for Minor**

1-If there is no person who exercises parental power over a child in accordance with the provisions of Article 1048 (Adjudication of suspension or divestment of parental power) or paragraph 1 of Article 1050 (Resignation and restoration of parental power), and if no person has been determined to be the person exercising parental power under Article 1051 (Change of person to exercise parental power...

power) above, a guardianship for a minor shall commence in respect of the child in accordance with the provisions of Article 1067 (Causes for commencement of guardianship for minor).

2-In the cases mentioned in paragraph 1 above, the court shall appoint a guardian for the minor in accordance with the provisions of paragraph 3 of Article 1068 (Designated guardian for minor, appointed guardian for minor and standard for appointment).

#### Section 5
#### Authority for Management of Child's Property

**Article 1053.- Property Management and Representation**

A person exercising parental power shall have the right and duty to manage the child's property and to represent the child in legal acts concerning the property. However, if the act of the person exercising parental power creates an obligation whose object is an act of the child, the consent of the child shall be obtained.

**Article 1054.- Act Performed by One Parent in Name of Both Parents**

1-If one of the parents who exercise joint parental power performs a legal act on behalf of the child in the name of both parents, the effect of the act shall not be prevented even if it is contrary to the intent of the other parent. However, this provision shall not apply if the other party to the act has acted with malicious intent.

2-The provisions of paragraph 1 above shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where one of the parents who exercise joint parental power gives consent...
