**Article 1055.- Duty of Care in Management of Property**

A person exercising parental power shall manage the child's property with the care of a good manager.

**Article 1056.- Accounting for Management of Property**

When the child reaches the age of majority, the person who has exercised parental power shall account for the management of the property without delay. However, the expenses for the care of the child and those for the management of the property shall be deemed to have been set off against the income from the child's property.

**Article 1057.- Special Provision**

The provision of the second sentence of Article 1056 (Accounting for management of property) above shall not apply to property given to the child without charge by a third party who has expressed a contrary intent.

**Article 1058.- Management of Property Given to Child by Third Party**

1-If a third party who gives property to a child without charge has expressed an intent that the property shall not be managed by either or both parents who exercise parental power, such property shall not be subject to the management of such parent or parents.

2-In the case where neither parent has the right to manage the property mentioned in paragraph 1 above, if the third party has not designated a person to manage the property, the court may appoint a manager...

property, upon application of the child, a relative within the 4th (fourth) degree of relationship of the child, or a prosecutor.

3-Even if the third party has designated a manager of the property, the provision of paragraph 2 above shall apply mutatis mutandis if the authority of such manager has been terminated or if it becomes necessary to change the manager, but the third party fails to designate another manager.

4-The provisions of Article 37 (Appointment of property manager by court) through Article 40 (Rights of property manager, etc.) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to the cases provided in paragraphs 2 and 3 above.

**Article 1059.- Mutatis Mutandis Application of Provisions on Mandate**

The provisions of Article 650 (Urgent management after termination of mandate) and Article 651 (Requirement for setting up termination of mandate) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where a person exercising parental power manages the child's property and to the cases provided in Article 1058 (Management of property given to child by third party) above.

**Article 1060.- Prescription of Claims Concerning Property Management between Parents and Child**

1-Financial claims between the person exercising parental power and the child arising from the management of the property shall be extinguished by prescription if not exercised within 3 (three) years from the time the authority of management is terminated.

#### Section 6
#### Suspension and Divestment of Authority to Manage Property

**Article 1061.- Adjudication of Suspension or Divestment of Authority to Manage Property**

If a person exercising parental power endangers the child's property by improper management, the court may adjudicate the suspension or divestment of the authority to manage the property upon application of any relative of the child within the 4th (fourth) degree of relationship, a commune chief or sangkat chief, the head of a public child welfare institution or a prosecutor.

**Article 1062.- Rescission of Adjudication of Suspension or Divestment of Authority to Manage Property**

If the causes provided in Article 1061 (Adjudication of suspension or divestment of authority to manage property) above have ceased to exist, the court may rescind the adjudication of suspension or divestment of authority to manage the property upon application of the person who was subject to such adjudication or any relative within the 4th (fourth) degree of relationship of such person.

**Article 1063.- Resignation and Restoration of Authority to Manage Property**

1-If there are unavoidable reasons, the person exercising parental power may resign from...

2-If the reasons provided in paragraph 1 above have ceased to exist, the person who resigned from the authority to manage the property may restore such authority upon obtaining the permission of the court.

**Article 1064.- Cases where No Person Exercises Authority to Manage Property**

If there is no person who exercises the authority to manage the property in accordance with the provisions of Article 1061 (Adjudication of suspension or divestment of authority to manage property) or paragraph 1 of Article 1063 (Resignation and restoration of authority to manage property) above, the court shall appoint a special representative for the child.

**Article 1065.- Conflict of Interest between Parent and Child**

1-In respect of acts where the interests of the person exercising parental power conflict with those of the child, the person exercising parental power shall apply to the court for the appointment of a special representative for the child.

2-In cases where a person exercising parental power exercises such power over two or more children, if the interests of one child conflict with those of another child in respect of a certain act, the person exercising parental power shall apply to the court for the appointment of a special representative for one of them.

#### Section 7
#### Mutatis Mutandis Application of this Chapter

**Article 1066.- Person who Exercises Parental Power in Place of Parent**

The person who exercises parental power over a minor shall exercise parental power in place of the minor over the minor's child. In such case, the provisions of Chapter 5 (Parental Power) shall apply mutatis mutandis to the person who exercises parental power in place of the parent.

### Chapter 6
### Guardianship

#### Section 1
#### Guardianship for Minor

#### Subsection 1
#### Commencement of Guardianship for Minor

**Article 1067.- Causes for Commencement of Guardianship for Minor**

If there is no person who exercises parental power over a minor, a guardianship for the minor shall commence.

#### Subsection 2
#### Guardian for Minor

**Article 1068.- Designated Guardian for Minor and Appointed Guardian for Minor and Standard for Appoint...**

Standard for Appointment

1-The person who last exercised parental power over a minor may designate a guardian for the minor by will.

2-If no guardian for a minor has been designated in accordance with the provisions of paragraph 1 above, the court shall appoint a guardian for the minor upon application of any relative of the minor within the 4th (fourth) degree of relationship, a commune chief or sangkat chief of the minor's place of residence, the head of a public child welfare institution or a prosecutor. This shall also apply to cases where a vacancy occurs in the office of guardian for minor.

3-In appointing a guardian for a minor, the court shall consider the physical and mental condition, the standard of living and the property of the minor, the profession and personal history of the person to be the guardian for the minor, as well as the existence or non-existence of a relationship centered on the interests of the child between the guardian and the minor, the opinion of the minor and all other circumstances.

**Article 1069.- Duty to Appoint Guardian for Minor**

If there occurs a vacancy in the office of an appointed guardian for a minor under paragraph 2 of Article 1052 (Commencement of guardianship for minor) of this Code, the court shall appoint a new guardian for the minor without delay.

**Article 1070.- Number of Guardians for Minor**

1-There shall be only one guardian for a minor. However, in respect of a minor under guardianship who is in a permanent state of lacking the capacity to understand and judge the legal consequences of his/her acts due to...
Check
Wait, 1069: paragraph 2 of Article 1052.

Fixing.

mental disability, if a general guardianship is commenced under Article 24 (Adjudication of commencement of general guardianship) of this Code, one general guardian may be appointed in addition to the guardian for the minor.

2-The provisions on general guardianship of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to a general guardian for a minor, except for Article 1120 (Cases where there are two or more general guardians).

**Article 1071.- Resignation of Guardian for Minor**

If there are justifiable reasons, a guardian for a minor may resign from the office upon obtaining the permission of the court.

**Article 1072.- Duty to Appoint Guardian for Minor**

If a guardian for a minor resigns, the court shall appoint a new guardian for the minor without delay.

**Article 1073.- Removal of Guardian for Minor**

If a guardian for a minor performs an improper act or is guilty of other conduct unfit for the office of guardian for minor, the court may remove such guardian upon application of the supervisor of guardian for minor, any relative of the minor within the 4th (fourth) degree of relationship, a commune chief or sangkat chief of the minor's place of residence, the head of a public child welfare institution or a prosecutor, or may remove the guardian by its own authority.

**Article 1074.- Persons Ineligible to be Guardian for Minor**

The persons listed in the following items may not be a guardian for a minor:

(a) Minors, persons under general guardianship and persons under curatorship.

(b) Legal representatives, curators and their supervisors who have been removed by a court.

(c) Bankrupts.

(d) Persons whose whereabouts are unknown.

#### Subsection 3
#### Supervisor of Guardian for Minor

**Article 1075.- Designated Supervisor of Guardian for Minor and Appointed Supervisor of Guardian for Minor**

1-The person who may designate a guardian for a minor may designate a supervisor of the guardian for the minor by will.

2-If the court finds it necessary, in cases where no supervisor of the guardian for a minor has been designated in accordance with the provisions of paragraph 1 above, the court may appoint a supervisor of the guardian for the minor upon application of the guardian for the minor, any relative of the minor within the 4th (fourth) degree of relationship, a commune chief or sangkat chief of the minor's place of residence, the head of a public child welfare institution or a prosecutor, or by its own authority. This shall also apply to cases where a vacancy occurs in the office of supervisor of the guardian for a minor.

**Article 1076.- Persons Ineligible to be Supervisor of Guardian for Minor**
(Amended by the Law on Implementation of the Civil Code.)

The spouse and any relative within the 4th (fourth) degree of relationship of the guardian for a minor may not be the supervisor of the guardian for the minor.

**Article 1077.- Duties of Supervisor of Guardian for Minor**

The duties of a supervisor of the guardian for a minor are as follows:

(a) To supervise the performance of the duties of the guardian for the minor.

(b) To apply to the court for the appointment of a new guardian for the minor without delay if a vacancy occurs in the office of guardian for the minor.

(c) To take necessary measures for the care of the minor under guardianship and the management of his/her property in cases of urgent necessity.

(d) To represent the minor under guardianship in respect of acts where the interests of the minor under guardianship conflict with those of the guardian for the minor.

**Article 1078.- Mutatis Mutandis Application**

The provisions of paragraph 3 of Article 1068 (Designated guardian for minor, appointed guardian for minor and standard for appointment), Article 1071 (Resignation of guardian for minor), Article 1073 (Removal of guardian for minor), Article 1074 (Persons ineligible to be guardian for minor), and paragraph 2 of Article 1087 (Estimate of expenses and remuneration for guardianship for...

#### Subsection 4
#### Duties of Guardian for Minor

**Article 1079.- Rights and Duties of Guardian for Minor**

1-A guardian for a minor shall have the same rights and duties as a person exercising parental power in respect of the matters provided in Section 3 (Rights and Duties of Person Exercising Parental Power) of Chapter 5 of this Book VII. However, in order to change the method of education or the place of residence determined by the person exercising parental power, to discipline the minor under guardianship, to permit the minor to conduct business, to revoke such permission or to limit such business, the guardian for the minor shall obtain the consent of the supervisor of the guardian for the minor, if any.

2-If there is a supervisor of the guardian for a minor, the guardian for the minor shall consult with the supervisor of the guardian for the minor in respect of the matters provided in Article 1042 (Duty to consult on important matters concerning child) of this Code. However, this shall not apply to cases of urgent necessity.

**Article 1080.- Property Management and Representation**

1-A guardian for a minor shall manage the property of the minor under guardianship and shall represent the minor under guardianship in respect of acts concerning his/her property.

2-The provision of the second sentence of Article 1053 (Property management and representation) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to the cases provided in paragraph 1 above.

**Article 1081.- Inventory of Property**

1-A guardian for a minor shall commence an inventory of the property of the minor under guardianship without delay upon assuming office, and shall complete the inventory within 3 (three) months. However, this period may be extended by the court.

2-If there is a supervisor of the guardian for a minor, the inventory of the property shall have no effect unless conducted in the presence of the supervisor of the guardian for the minor.

**Article 1082.- Authority Prior to Completion of Inventory**

Until the inventory of the property is completed, the guardian for a minor shall have authority only over acts of urgent necessity. However, this may not be set up against a third party acting in good faith.

**Article 1083.- Report on Claims and Obligations of Guardian for Minor against Minor under Guardianship**

1-If a guardian for a minor has any claim or obligation against the minor under guardianship, fixed at the time of assuming office, and if there is a supervisor of the guardian for a minor, the guardian shall report such claim or obligation to the supervisor before commencing the inventory of property.

2-If a guardian for a minor, knowing that he/she has a claim against the minor under guardianship, fails to report it, such claim shall be forfeited.

**Article 1084.- Mutatis Mutandis Application to Cases where Minor under Guardianship Acquires Property by Universal Title**

The provisions of Article 1081 (Inventory of property), Article 1082 (Authority prior to completion of inventory) and Article 1083 (Report on claims and obligations of guardian for minor against minor under guardianship) above shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where a minor under guardianship has acquired property by universal title after the guardian for the minor has assumed office.

**Article 1085.- Permission for Management of Property**

In order to sell, lease, cancel a lease, create a mortgage or otherwise dispose of an immovable used for the minor under guardianship's residence in place of the minor under guardianship, the guardian for the minor...

**Article 1086.- Conflict of Interest between Guardian for Minor and Minor under Guardianship**

The provisions of Article 1065 (Conflict of interest between parent and child) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to a guardian for a minor. However, this shall not apply to cases where there is a supervisor of the guardian for a minor.

**Article 1087.- Estimate of Expenses and Remuneration for Guardianship for Minor**

1-A guardian for a minor shall, upon assuming office, estimate the amount to be expended annually for the care of the minor under guardianship and the management of his/her property.

2-The necessary expenses for the performance of the duties by a guardian for a minor shall be paid out of the property of the minor under guardianship.

**Article 1088.- Remuneration for Guardian for Minor**

The court may grant reasonable remuneration to a guardian for a minor out of the property of the minor under guardianship, taking into consideration the financial capacity of the guardian and the minor under guardianship, and other circumstances.

**Article 1089.- Supervision of Performance of Duties of Guardian for Minor**

1-The supervisor of the guardian for a minor or the court may at any time demand...

**Article 1090.- Limitation of Authority of Representation and Authority to Give Consent**

If there is a supervisor of the guardian for a minor, the guardian for the minor shall obtain the consent of the supervisor in order to conduct business or perform any of the acts provided in Article 30 (Right to rescind act) of this Code in place of the minor under guardianship, or in order to give consent to the performance of such acts by the minor under guardianship. However, this shall not apply to the receipt of principal.

**Article 1091.- Effect of Acts Performed in Violation of Article 1090**

1-An act performed or consented to by a guardian for a minor in violation of the provisions of Article 1090 (Limitation of authority of representation and authority to give consent) above may be rescinded only by the minor under guardianship or the guardian for the minor. In such case, the provisions of Article 32 (Right to demand) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis.

2-The provisions of paragraph 1 above shall not preclude the application of the provisions of Article 358 (Voidable acts) and Article 360 (Method of rescission or ratification) through Article 363 (Prescription of right to rescind) of this Code.

**Article 1092.- Acquisition of Property, etc., from Minor under Guardianship**

1-If a guardian for a minor has acquired property of the minor under guardianship or a third party's right against the minor under guardianship, the minor under guardianship may rescind such acquisition. In such case, the provisions of Article 32 (Right to demand) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis.

2-The provisions of paragraph 1 above shall not preclude the application of the provisions of Article 358 (Voidable acts) and Article 360 (Method of rescission or ratification) through Article 363 (Prescription of right to rescind) of this Code.

**Article 1093.- Duty of Care of Guardian for Minor**

1-A guardian for a minor shall perform the duties of guardianship with the care of a good manager.

2-If a guardian for a minor causes damage to the minor under guardianship by violating the duty of care provided in paragraph 1 above, the minor under guardianship may claim compensation for such damage from the guardian for the minor.

**Article 1094.- Management of Property Given to Minor under Guardianship by Third Party**

1-If a third party who gives property to a minor under guardianship without charge has expressed an intent that the property shall not be managed by the guardian for the minor, such property shall not be subject to the management of such guardian.

2-In the case where the guardian for the minor has no right to manage the property mentioned in paragraph 1 above, if the third party has not designated a person to manage the property, the court may appoint a manager of the property upon application of the supervisor of the guardian for the minor, any relative of the minor within the 4th (fourth) degree of relationship, a commune chief or sangkat chief of the minor's place of residence, the head of a public child welfare institution or a prosecutor.

3-Even if the third party has designated a manager of the property, the provision of paragraph 2 above shall apply mutatis mutandis if the authority of such manager has terminated or if it becomes necessary to change the manager, but the third party fails to designate another manager.

4-The provisions of Article 37 (Appointment of property manager by court) through Article 40 (Rights of property manager, etc.) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to the cases provided in paragraphs 2 and 3 above.

#### Section 5
#### Termination of Guardianship for Minors

**Article 1095.- Conditions for termination of guardianship for minors**

1-In cases where a minor under guardianship who has reached the age of 16 (sixteen) years is able to support themselves independently, the court may declare the termination of guardianship for the minor upon the request of the minor under guardianship, if it is deemed that such termination is consistent with the interests of the minor under guardianship. In this case, the court shall seek the opinion of the guardian for the minor. If there is a supervisor of the guardian for the minor, the court shall also seek the opinion of such supervisor of the guardian.

2-A minor under guardianship who has entered into marriage shall be released from guardianship for the minor without any declaration by the court.

3-In the case provided for in the preceding paragraph 2, even if the parties subsequently divorce, the effect of the termination of guardianship for the minor shall not be extinguished.

**Article 1096.- Effect of termination of guardianship for minors**

A minor under guardianship who has been released from guardianship for the minor shall be deemed to have attained majority.

**Article 1097.- Accounting of income and expenses in property management; Presence of supervisor of guardian for minor**

1-If the duties of the guardian for the minor have terminated, the guardian for the minor or their successor shall account for the income and expenses of the property management within 3 (three) months from

the date on which such duties terminated. However, this period may be extended by the court.

2-If there is a supervisor of the guardian for the minor, the accounting provided for in the preceding paragraph 1 shall be conducted in the presence of such supervisor of the guardian for the minor.

**Article 1098.- Rescission of contract between minor under guardianship and guardian for minor**

1-A minor under guardianship may rescind a contract entered into with the guardian for the minor or their successor after the minor has attained majority and before the accounting of the income and expenses of the guardianship has been completed. This provision shall apply mutatis mutandis to unilateral acts performed by the minor towards the guardian for the minor or their successor.

2-The provisions of Article 358 (Voidable acts) and the provisions from Article 360 (Method of rescission or ratification) to Article 363 (Statute of limitations for extinction of right of rescission) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to the cases provided for in the preceding paragraph 1.

**Article 1099.- Obligation to pay interest by guardian for minor and minor under guardianship**

1-The amount of money to be returned by the guardian for the minor to the minor under guardianship, and the amount of money to be returned by the minor under guardianship to the guardian for the minor, shall bear interest from the time the accounting of the income and expenses of the guardianship for the minor has been completed.

2-If the guardian for the minor has used the minor's money for their own benefit, they shall pay interest from the time such money was used. If any damage occurs, the guardian for the minor shall be liable for compensation for such damage.

**Article 1100.- Urgent management duties after termination of guardianship for minor**

1-If there is an urgent situation after the termination of guardianship for the minor, the guardian for the minor, their successor, or the legal representative of the guardian for the minor shall take necessary measures until the minor under guardianship, the minor's successor, or the legal representative of the minor under guardianship is able to manage the affairs.

2-The provisions of the preceding paragraph 1 shall apply mutatis mutandis to a person who manages property that has been specified or selected in accordance with the provisions of Article 1094 (Management of property provided to minor under guardianship by third party).

**Article 1101.- Conditions for setting up termination of guardianship for minor against third party**

1-The cause of termination of guardianship for the minor, whether it occurred on the side of the minor under guardianship or the guardian for the minor, cannot be set up against the other party unless the other party has been notified or the other party was aware of the cause

thereof.

2-The provisions of the preceding paragraph 1 shall apply mutatis mutandis to the management of property conducted in accordance with the provisions of Article 1094 (Management of property provided to minor under guardianship by third party) of this Code.

**Article 1102.- Statute of limitations for extinction of claims relating to guardianship for minor**

1-The provisions of Article 1060 (Statute of limitations for extinction of claims relating to property management between parents and children) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to claims arising in relation to guardianship for a minor between the guardian for the minor or the supervisor of the guardian for the minor and the minor under guardianship.

2-In cases where an act has been rescinded in accordance with the provisions of Article 1098 (Rescission of contract between minor under guardianship and guardian for minor) of this Code, the statute of limitations provided for in the preceding paragraph 1 shall be calculated from the time of such rescission.

#### Section 6
#### Mutatis Mutandis Application to Children of Minor under Guardianship

**Article 1103.- Exercise of parental power on behalf of minor under guardianship**

1-A guardian for a minor shall exercise parental power over a child of a minor under guardianship on behalf of such minor under guardianship.

2-The provisions from Article 1079 (Rights and duties of guardian for minor) to Article 1102 (Statute of limitations for extinction of claims relating to guardianship for minor) above shall apply mutatis mutandis to

mutatis mutandis to the cases provided for in the preceding paragraph 1.

### Part 2
### General Guardianship

#### Section 1
#### Commencement of General Guardianship

**Article 1104.- Commencement of general guardianship**

General guardianship shall commence in accordance with the provisions of Article 24 (Declaration of commencement of general guardianship) of this Code.

#### Section 2
#### General Guardian

**Article 1105.- Selection of general guardian**

1-Upon a declaration of commencement of general guardianship, the court shall select a general guardian ex officio.

2-If there is a vacancy in the position of general guardian, the court shall select a general guardian upon the request of the supervisor of the general guardian, the ward under general guardianship, the spouse of the ward under general guardianship, a relative within the 4th (fourth) degree of the ward under general guardianship, the chief of the commune or sangkat where the ward under general guardianship resides, the prosecutor, or any other interested person, or ex officio.

3-Even if a general guardian has been selected, if the court deems it necessary, it may select additional general guardians upon the request of the persons provided for in the preceding paragraph 2 or the general guardian

general guardian, or ex officio.

4-In selecting a general guardian, the court shall take into account the physical and mental condition, the living and property situation of the ward under general guardianship; the profession and personal history of the person to be the general guardian; the existence or non-existence of conflict of interest between the general guardian and the ward under general guardianship; the opinion of the ward under general guardianship; and all other circumstances. In cases where the general guardian is a legal person, the court shall also take into account the type and content of the business of the legal person, and the existence or non-existence of conflict of interest between the legal person or its representative and the ward under general guardianship.

5-In cases where a general guardian is selected for a minor, the court may select a person who has parental power or a guardian for the minor as the general guardian. In cases where the court selects a person other than the person who has parental power over the minor or the guardian for the minor as the general guardian, the court shall specify the division of duties between the person who has parental power or the guardian for the minor and the general guardian.

**Article 1106.- Resignation of general guardian**

If there is a justifiable reason, a general guardian may resign with the permission of the court.

**Article 1107.- Duty to select general guardian**

If a general guardian has resigned, the court shall select a new general guardian promptly.

**Article 1108.- Dismissal of general guardian**

If a general guardian has committed an inappropriate act or there is any other reason that is unsuitable for the duties of a general guardian, the court may dismiss such general guardian upon the request of the supervisor of the general guardian, the ward under general guardianship, the spouse of the ward under general guardianship, a relative within the 4th (fourth) degree of the ward under general guardianship, the chief of the commune or sangkat where the ward under general guardianship resides, the prosecutor, or any other interested person, or ex officio.

**Article 1109.- Persons ineligible to be general guardian**

The persons specified in the following items may not be a general guardian:

a-A minor, a person under general guardianship, or a person under curatorship.

b-A legal representative, curator, or their supervisor who has been removed by the court.

c-A bankrupt person.

d-A person whose whereabouts are unknown.

#### Section 3
#### Supervisor of General Guardian

**Article 1110.- Supervisor of general guardian**

If the court deems it necessary, it may select a supervisor of the general guardian upon the request of the general guardian, the ward under general guardianship, the spouse of the ward under general guardianship, a relative within the 4th (fourth) degree of the ward under general guardianship, the chief of the commune or sangkat where

the ward under general guardianship resides, the prosecutor, or any other interested person, or ex officio. This provision shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where there is a vacancy in the position of supervisor of the general guardian.

**Article 1111.- Persons ineligible to be supervisor of general guardian**

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code)

A spouse or a relative within the 4th (fourth) degree of the general guardian may not be a supervisor of the general guardian.

**Article 1112.- Duties of supervisor of general guardian**

A supervisor of the general guardian shall have the following duties:

a-To supervise the performance of duties by the general guardian.

b-To request the court to select a new general guardian without delay if there is a vacancy in the position of general guardian.

c-To take necessary measures regarding the living conditions, medical care, and property management of the ward under general guardianship in urgent situations.

d-To represent the ward under general guardianship in cases where there is a conflict of interest between the general guardian or the person represented by the general guardian and the ward under general guardianship.

**Article 1113.- Mutatis mutandis application**

The provisions of paragraph 4 of Article 1105 (Selection of general guardian), Article 1106 (Resignation of general guardian), Article 1108 (Dismissal of general guardian), and Article 1109 (Persons ineligible to be general guardian)

Article 1109 (Persons ineligible to be general guardian), Article 1120 (Case where there are multiple general guardians), Article 1121 (Permission to manage property), paragraph 2 of Article 1123 (Plan for amount of expenses and costs of general guardianship), Article 1124 (Remuneration for general guardian), Article 1129 (Duty of care of general guardian), Article 1133 (Urgent management duties after termination of general guardianship), and Article 1134 (Conditions for setting up termination of general guardianship) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to the supervisor of the general guardian.

#### Section 4
#### Duties of General Guardian

**Article 1114.- Property management and representation**

1-A general guardian shall manage the property of the ward under general guardianship and shall represent the ward under general guardianship in acts relating to the property of the ward under general guardianship.

2-The provisions of the second sentence of Article 1093 (Property management and representation) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to the cases provided for in the preceding paragraph 1.

**Article 1115.- Investigation of property and preparation of inventory**

1-A general guardian shall commence an investigation of the property of the ward under general guardianship without delay after assuming office, and shall complete such investigation and prepare an inventory of the property within 3 (three) months. However, this period may be extended by the court.

2-If there is a supervisor of the general guardian, the property investigation

and the preparation of the inventory of such property shall be void unless it is conducted in the presence of the supervisor of the general guardian.

**Article 1116.- Authority before completion of inventory**

Until the completion of the inventory, the general guardian shall have authority only over acts of urgent necessity. However, such acts cannot be set up against a third party in good faith.

**Article 1117.- Reporting of claims and obligations of general guardian against ward under general guardianship**

1-In cases where a general guardian has a claim or an obligation against the ward under general guardianship, if there is a supervisor of the general guardian, the general guardian shall report such claim or obligation to the supervisor of the general guardian before commencing the investigation of property.

2-If the general guardian was aware of a claim against the ward under general guardianship but failed to report it, the general guardian shall lose such claim.

**Article 1118.- Mutatis mutandis application to cases where ward under general guardianship has succeeded to universal property**

The provisions of Article 1115 (Investigation of property and preparation of inventory), Article 1116 (Authority before completion of inventory), and Article 1117 (Reporting of claims and obligations of general guardian against ward under general guardianship) above shall apply mutatis mutandis

mutatis mutandis to cases where the ward under general guardianship has succeeded to universal property after the general guardian assumed office.

**Article 1119.- Respect for will of ward under general guardianship**

1-In performing duties relating to the living conditions, medical care, and property management of the ward under general guardianship, the general guardian shall respect the will of the ward under general guardianship and take into account the physical and mental condition and the living situation of such ward.

2-The general guardian shall provide medical care and support to the ward under general guardianship in accordance with the property of the ward under general guardianship and the general guardian, and the relationship between them.

**Article 1120.- Cases where there are multiple general guardians**

1-If there are multiple general guardians, the court may ex officio specify that the multiple general guardians shall exercise their authority jointly or divide their duties.

2-The court may ex officio rescind the specification provided for in the preceding paragraph 1.

3-If there are multiple general guardians, it is sufficient for a third party to manifest their will to any one of the general guardians.

**Article 1121.- Permission to manage property**

In order to sell, lease, terminate a lease, create a mortgage, or perform any other similar act on behalf of the ward under general guardianship with respect to a building used as the residence of the ward under general guardianship or the land on which such building is situated, the general guardian must obtain permission from the court.

**Article 1122.- Conflict of interest between general guardian and ward under general guardianship**

The provisions of Article 1065 (Case where there is a conflict of interest between person exercising parental power and child) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to a general guardian. However, this provision shall not apply to cases where there is a supervisor of the general guardian.

**Article 1123.- Plan for amount of expenses and costs of general guardianship**

1-A general guardian shall prepare a plan for the amount of money to be spent annually for the living conditions, medical care, and property management of the ward under general guardianship at the beginning of the guardianship.

2-Necessary costs for the general guardian to perform their duties shall be paid from the property of the ward under general guardianship.

**Article 1124.- Remuneration for general guardian**

The court may grant reasonable remuneration from the property of the ward under general guardianship to the general guardian, taking into account the property situation of the general guardian and the ward under general guardianship, as well as other circumstances.

**Article 1125.- Supervision of duties of general guardian**

1-A supervisor of the general guardian or the court may at any time demand the general guardian to report on their duties or submit an inventory, or may investigate the living conditions, medical care, or property situation of the ward under general guardianship.

2-The court may order the general guardian to take necessary measures regarding the living conditions, medical care, or property management of the ward under general guardianship, or any other necessary measures relating to the duties of the general guardian, upon the request of the supervisor of the general guardian, the ward under general guardianship, the spouse of the ward under general guardianship, a relative within the 4th (fourth) degree of the ward under general guardianship, the chief of the commune or sangkat where the ward under general guardianship resides, the prosecutor, or any other interested person, or ex officio.

**Article 1126.- Restriction on legal representation and right of consent**

If there is a supervisor of the general guardian, the general guardian must obtain the consent of such supervisor in order to conduct business or perform any of the acts provided for in Article 30 (Right to rescind acts) of this Code on behalf of the ward under general guardianship, or in order to consent to the ward under general guardianship conducting business or performing such acts. However, this provision shall not apply to the receipt of principal.

**Article 1127.- Effect of acts performed in violation of Article 1126**

1-Acts performed or consented to by the general guardian in violation of the provisions of Article 1126 (Restriction on legal representation and right of consent) above may be rescinded only by the ward under general guardianship or the general guardian. In this case, the provisions of Article 32 (Right of inquiry) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis.

2-The provisions of the preceding paragraph 1 shall not preclude the application of the provisions of Article 358 (Voidable acts) and the provisions from Article 360 (Method of rescission or ratification) to Article 363 (Statute of limitations for extinction of right of rescission)

rescission) of this Code.

**Article 1128.- Receipt of assignment of property, etc. from ward under general guardianship**

1-If a general guardian has received an assignment of property from the ward under general guardianship or a right of a third party against the ward under general guardianship, the ward under general guardianship may rescind such assignment. In this case, the provisions of Article 32 (Right of inquiry) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis.

2-The provisions of the preceding paragraph 1 shall not preclude the application of the provisions of Article 358 (Voidable acts) and the provisions from Article 360 (Method of rescission or ratification) to Article 363 (Statute of limitations for extinction of right of rescission) of this Code.

**Article 1129.- Duty of care of general guardian**

1-A general guardian shall have the duty to perform the duties of general guardianship with the care of a good manager.

2-In cases where a general guardian has caused damage to the ward under general guardianship by acting in violation of the duty of care provided for in the preceding paragraph 1, the ward under general guardianship may demand compensation for such damage from the general guardian.

**Article 1130.- Management of property provided by third party to ward under general guardianship**

1-If a third party who has provided property to the ward under general guardianship for no consideration has manifested their will not to let the general guardian manage

such property, such property shall not be under the management of the general guardian.

2-In cases where the general guardian does not manage the property provided for in the preceding paragraph 1, if the third party has not designated a person to manage the property, the court may select a manager of the property upon the request of the supervisor of the general guardian, the ward under general guardianship, the spouse of the ward under general guardianship, a relative within the 4th (fourth) degree of the ward under general guardianship, the chief of the commune or sangkat where the ward under general guardianship resides, or the prosecutor.

3-Even if the third party has designated a manager of the property, the provisions of the preceding paragraph 2 shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where the authority of such manager has terminated or it is necessary to change such manager, but the third party has not designated another manager.

4-The provisions from Article 37 (Selection of property manager by court) to Article 40 (Rights of property manager, etc.) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to the cases provided for in the preceding paragraphs 2 and 3.

#### Section 5
#### Termination of General Guardianship

**Article 1131.- Accounting for property management and presence of supervisor of general guardian**

1-If the duties of a general guardian have terminated, the general

guardian or their successor shall account for the property management within 3 (three) months from the date of termination of duties. However, this period may be extended by the court.

2-If there is a supervisor of the general guardian, the accounting provided for in the preceding paragraph 1 shall be conducted in the presence of such supervisor.

**Article 1132.- Duty to pay interest by general guardian and ward under general guardianship**

1-Interest shall be paid on the amount of money to be returned by the general guardian to the ward under general guardianship or vice versa from the time of completion of the accounting for the general guardianship affairs.

2-If the general guardian has used the money of the ward under general guardianship for their own benefit, interest shall be paid from the time of such use. If there is damage, the general guardian shall be liable for compensation for such damage.

**Article 1133.- Urgent management duties after termination of general guardianship**

1-If there are urgent circumstances in cases where the general guardianship has terminated, the general guardian, their successor, or their legal representative shall take necessary measures until the ward under general guardianship, their successor, or their legal representative is able to manage the affairs.

2-The provisions of the preceding paragraph 1 shall apply mutatis mutandis to a property manager designated or selected pursuant to the provisions of Article 1130 (Management of property provided by third party to ward under general guardianship).

**Article 1134.- Conditions for setting up termination of general guardianship, etc.**

1-The grounds for termination of general guardianship, whether they arise on the part of the ward under general guardianship or the general guardian, may not be set up against the other party unless the other party has been notified of such grounds or the other party was aware of them.

2-The provisions of the preceding paragraph 1 shall apply mutatis mutandis to property management pursuant to the provisions of Article 1130 (Management of property provided by third party to ward under general guardianship).

**Article 1135.- Statute of limitations for extinction of claims relating to general guardianship**

The provisions of Article 1060 (Statute of limitations for extinction of claims relating to property management between parents and children) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to claims arising in connection with general guardianship between the general guardian or the supervisor of the general guardian and the ward under general guardianship.

#### Chapter 7
#### Curatorship

**Article 1136.- Commencement of curatorship**

Curatorship shall commence as provided for in Article 28 (Declaration of commencement of curatorship) of this Code.

**Article 1137.- Selection of curator**

1-If the commencement of curatorship is declared, the court shall ex officio select a curator.

2-The provisions from paragraph 2 to paragraph 4 of Article 1105 (Selection of general guardian) and the provisions from Article 1106 (Resignation of general guardian) to Article 1109 (Persons ineligible to be general guardian) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to a curator.

3-In cases where there is a conflict of interest between the curator or the person represented by the curator and the person under curatorship, the curator shall request the court to select an acting curator. However, this provision shall not apply to cases where there is a supervisor of the curator.

4-The provisions of the preceding paragraph 3 shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where there is a conflict of interest between several persons under curatorship who are under the same curator.

**Article 1138.- Supervisor of curator**

1-If the court deems it necessary, it may select a supervisor of the curator upon the request of the curator, the person under curatorship, the spouse of the person under curatorship, a relative within the 4th (fourth) degree of the person under curatorship, the chief of the commune or

the ward under curatorship resides, the prosecutor, or any other interested person, or ex officio. This provision shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where there is a vacancy in the position of supervisor of the curator.

2-The provisions of paragraph 4 of Article 1105 (Selection of general guardian), Article 1106 (Resignation of general guardian), Article 1108 (Dismissal of general guardian), Article 1109 (Persons ineligible to be general guardian), Article 1111 (Persons ineligible to be supervisor of general guardian), Article 1120 (Cases where there are multiple general guardians), paragraph 2 of Article 1123 (Plan for amount of expenses and costs of general guardianship), and Article 1124 (Remuneration for general guardian) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to the supervisor of the curator.

3-The supervisor of the curator may consent to the person under curatorship performing an act in cases where there is a conflict of interest between the curator or the person represented by the curator and the person under curatorship.

4-The provisions of the preceding paragraph 3 shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where there is a conflict of interest between several persons under curatorship who are under the same curator.

**Article 1139.- Duties of curator**

1-A curator shall have the authority to consent to an act performed by the person under curatorship provided for in Article 30 (Right to rescind acts) of this Code, or to rescind such act performed by the person under curatorship without obtaining the consent of the curator.

2-In performing the duties provided for in the preceding paragraph 1, the curator shall respect the will of the person under curatorship and take into account

physical and mental condition and the living situation of such person.

3-The provisions of Article 1120 (Cases where there are multiple general guardians), paragraph 2 of Article 1123 (Plan for amount of expenses and costs of general guardianship), Article 1124 (Remuneration for general guardian), and Article 1125 (Supervision of duties of general guardian) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to the duties of the curator.

4-The provisions of Article 1060 (Statute of limitations for extinction of claims relating to property management between parents and children) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to claims arising in connection with curatorship between the curator or the supervisor of the curator and the person under curatorship.

#### Chapter 8
#### Support

**Article 1140.- Scope and order of persons under duty to provide support**

1-Relatives shall be under a duty to provide support in the following scope and order:

1st-Relatives who live together.

2nd-Lineal blood relatives.

3rd-Siblings.

2-In addition to the persons provided for in the preceding paragraph 1, the court may, under special circumstances, impose a duty to provide support on a relative within the 3rd (third) degree. In cases where a marriage has been dissolved by the death of one of the spouses, this provision shall apply mutatis mutandis to support

between the surviving spouse and the relatives of the deceased spouse.

3-If there is a change in the circumstances after a decision has been made pursuant to the provisions of the preceding paragraph 2, the court may rescind such decision.

**Article 1141.- Designation of persons to provide and receive support**

1-If there are several persons under a duty to provide support, the person or persons who are to provide support shall be determined by agreement between them.

2-If the agreement provided for in the preceding paragraph 1 is not reached or the agreement cannot be reached, the court shall determine the person or persons who are to provide support, taking into account the property situation, age, physical and mental condition of the persons under a duty to provide support, the personal relationship between the parties, and all other circumstances.

3-The provisions of the preceding paragraphs 1 and 2 shall apply mutatis mutandis to the determination of the persons entitled to receive support in cases where there are several persons entitled to receive support and the property situation of the person under a duty to provide support is insufficient to provide support to all of them.

**Article 1142.- Extent or method of support**

If an agreement is not reached or cannot be reached regarding the extent or method of support, the court shall determine it, taking into account the necessary living expenses of the person entitled to receive support, the property situation of the person under a duty to provide support, and all other circumstances.

**Article 1143.- Change or termination of support Relationship**

1-If there is a change in the circumstances after an agreement has been reached or a decision has been made regarding the designation of persons to provide and receive support, or the extent or method of support, the parties may change the support relationship by agreement.

2-If the agreement provided for in the preceding paragraph 1 is not reached or cannot be reached, a party may request the court to rescind or change the previous agreement or decision.

3-The provisions of paragraphs 2 and 3 of Article 1141 (Designation of persons to provide and receive support) above shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where the court orders the rescission or change pursuant to the provisions of paragraph 2 of this Article.

**Article 1144.- Prohibition of disposition of right to claim support**

The right to claim support is a personal right of the person in need of support and may not be the subject of assignment, gift, or creation of a security interest for a third party, or any other transaction.

### BOOK 8
### SUCCESSION

#### CHAPTER 1
#### GENERAL PROVISIONS

##### Section 1
##### Commencement of Succession

**Article 1145.- Grounds for commencement of succession**

1-Succession shall commence upon the death of the decedent.

2-Succession shall be conducted in accordance with the provisions of law or in accordance with the will of the decedent. Succession in accordance with the provisions of law is referred to as "statutory succession", and succession in accordance with the will of the decedent is referred to as "testamentary succession".

**Article 1146.- Place of commencement of succession**

Succession shall commence at the place of the decedent's domicile at the time of death.

##### Section 2
##### Effect of Succession

**Article 1147.- Succession to inheritance**

1-A successor shall succeed to all the rights and duties of the decedent's property from the time of commencement of succession. However, this provision shall not apply to rights and duties that are purely personal to the decedent.

2-With regard to the ownership of objects for religious belief and tombs, or movable objects that are family treasures of ancestors, if there is a person who is to succeed to such ownership, such as a person

who is to lead the ancestor worship rituals as designated by the decedent shall succeed to such ownership. If the decedent has not designated such person, the person who is to lead the ancestor worship rituals according to custom shall succeed to such ownership. If the custom is not clear, the court shall determine the person who is to succeed to such ownership.

**Article 1148.- Succession to rights and duties in case of several successors**

1-If there are several successors, each joint successor shall succeed to the rights and duties of the inheritance in proportion to their share of the inheritance. However, rights or duties that are indivisible by their nature shall be succeeded to indivisibly.

2-The inheritance shall be held in co-ownership by the joint successors.

##### Section 3
##### Qualification for Successor

**Article 1149.- Persons qualified to be successor, etc.**

1-Only a person who is alive at the time of commencement of succession may be a successor, except for persons provided for from Article 1150 (Ineligibility for succession, etc.) to Article 1152 (Disinheritance of successor by will) below.

2-Notwithstanding the provisions of the preceding paragraph 1, the provisions of paragraphs 2 and 3 of Article 9 (Fetus) of this Code shall apply to a fetus.

**Article 1150.- Ineligibility for succession, etc.**

The following persons may not be a successor or receive property by will:

(a) A person who has been sentenced for intentionally killing or attempting to kill the decedent or a person who has the same or higher priority in the order of succession, whether directly or indirectly.

(b) A person who, knowing that the decedent has been killed, fails to report or file a complaint. However, this provision shall not apply to cases where such person cannot distinguish between right and wrong, or where the person who killed the decedent is the spouse or a lineal blood relative of such person.

(c) A person who, by fraud or duress, has prevented the decedent from making, rescinding, or changing a will relating to succession.

(d) A person who, by fraud or duress, has caused the decedent to make, rescind, or change a will relating to succession.

(e) A person who has forged, altered, destroyed, or concealed the decedent's will relating to succession.

**Article 1151.- Disinheritance of successor**

1-During their lifetime, a decedent may file a petition with the court to disinherit a person who is presumed to be a successor from receiving the inheritance, if the person who is presumed to be a successor and who has a legally reserved share has committed any of the following acts:

(a) Mistreated the decedent.

(b) Seriously insulted the decedent.

(c) Failed to provide nursing or care for the decedent during illness despite being able to do so.

(d) Has been sentenced to imprisonment for

life.

(e) Has committed other serious offenses.

The decedent may also manifest their will to disinherit such person by will.

2-A person who is presumed to be a successor and against whom a petition for disinheritance has been filed may contest the existence or non-existence of the grounds for disinheritance in the decision provided for in the preceding paragraph 1.

**Article 1152.- Disinheritance of successor by will**

If a decedent has made a will as provided for in Article 1151 (Disinheritance of successor) above, the executor of the will shall, without delay after the will has taken effect, file a petition with the court to disinherit such successor. In this case, the disinheritance of the successor shall be effective retroactively from the time of the death of the decedent.

**Article 1153.- Possession of inheritance by ineligible person, etc.**

If a person who is ineligible for succession or who cannot receive property by will pursuant to the provisions of Article 1150 (Ineligibility for succession, etc.), or a person who has been disinherited pursuant to the provisions of Article 1151 (Disinheritance of successor) and Article 1152 (Disinheritance of successor by will) above, is in possession of the inheritance, such person shall be deemed to be a possessor in bad faith from the time of commencement of succession.

**Article 1154.- Rescission of disinheritance of successor**

1-During their lifetime, a decedent may file a petition with the court to rescind the disinheritance of a person who is presumed to be a successor at any time.

2-The provisions of Article 1152 (Disinheritance of successor by will) above

shall apply mutatis mutandis to the rescission of the disinheritance of a successor.

**Article 1155.- Cases where successor, etc. is a foreign national**

1-If a successor or a person who receives property by will does not have Khmer nationality, such person may not receive land by succession or legacy.

2-If one or more joint successors do not have Khmer nationality, the inheritance shall be partitioned taking into account the provisions of the preceding paragraph 1.

3-If all successors in the priority rank of succession do not have Khmer nationality, the land among the inheritance shall become a legal entity, and the management and disposition of such land shall be conducted by all such successors in the priority rank.

4-If all successors in the priority rank have sold the land in their capacity as the owners of the right to dispose of the inheritance provided for in the preceding paragraph 3, within 3 (three) months from the time when all such successors have given their full or limited consent, the proceeds of the sale of the land shall become the inheritance, and the legal entity of the inheritance provided for in the preceding paragraph 3 shall be dissolved.

5-If all successors in the priority rank have not sold the land within 3 (three) months from the time when all such successors have given their full or limited consent pursuant to the provisions of the preceding paragraph 4, the ownership of such land shall be succeeded to by the successors in the next rank who have Khmer nationality. In this case, the legal entity of the inheritance provided for in the preceding paragraph 3 shall be deemed not to exist.

#### CHAPTER 2
#### STATUTORY SUCCESSION

##### Section 1
##### Successors

**Article 1156.- 1st Rank Successors**

1-Children of the decedent shall become 1st rank successors.

2-Children of the decedent, whether biological or adopted, shall have an equal share of the inheritance.

**Article 1157.- Succession by representation**

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code.)

1-If a child of the decedent has died before or at the same time as the commencement of succession, or has lost the right of succession pursuant to the provisions of Article 1150 (Ineligibility for succession, etc.) above, or has been disinherited, the child of such person shall become a successor by representation. However, this provision shall not apply to persons who are not lineal descendants of the decedent.

2-The provisions of the preceding paragraph 1 shall apply successively to cases where there are grounds provided for in the same paragraph 1 on the part of the successor by representation.

**Article 1158.- Share of inheritance in case of succession by representation**

The share of the inheritance of a lineal descendant who becomes a successor by representation pursuant to the provisions of Article 1157 (Succession by representation) above shall be the same as the share that their lineal ancestor would have received. If there are several lineal descendants

if there are several lineal descendants, the share that their lineal ancestor would have received shall be divided equally among them.

**Article 1159.- 2nd Rank Successors**

1-If the decedent has no lineal descendants who are to become successors, the lineal ancestors of the decedent shall become successors. However, between persons of different degrees of relationship, the person of the closer degree shall have priority.

2-If there are several lineal ancestors who are to become successors, each such lineal ancestor shall have an equal share of the inheritance.

**Article 1160.- 3rd Rank Successors**

1-If the decedent has neither lineal descendants nor lineal ancestors who are to become successors, the siblings of the decedent shall become successors.

2-If there are several siblings, each sibling shall have an equal share of the inheritance. However, the share of the inheritance of a sibling who has only one parent in common with the decedent shall be one-half (1/2) of the share of the inheritance of a sibling who has both parents in common with the decedent.

3-The provisions of paragraph 1 of Article 1157 (Succession by representation) and the provisions of Article 1158 (Share of inheritance in case of succession by representation) above shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where a sibling of the decedent who would have become a successor pursuant to the provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2 above has died before the commencement of succession.

##### Section 2
##### Succession by Spouse

**Article 1161.- Succession by spouse**

1-The spouse of the decedent shall always be a successor.

2-In cases where the spouse of the decedent becomes a successor, if there are other persons who are to become successors pursuant to the provisions of Section 1 (Successors) above, the spouse shall have the same priority as such persons.

**Article 1162.- Share of inheritance in case of spouse**

In cases where the spouse of the decedent and other persons become successors, the share of the inheritance of the successors shall be as follows:

(a) If the spouse and lineal descendants of the decedent are the successors, the spouse and each child of the decedent shall receive an equal share of the inheritance.

(b) If the spouse and parents of the decedent are the successors, the share of the inheritance of the spouse shall be one-third (1/3) and the share of the inheritance of the parents shall be two-thirds (2/3). However, if only one parent of the decedent is alive, such parent and the spouse shall receive an equal share of the inheritance.

(c) If the spouse and lineal ancestors (other than parents) or siblings of the decedent or successors by representation are the successors, the share of the inheritance of the spouse shall be one-half (1/2) and the share of the inheritance of the lineal ancestors (other than parents), siblings, or successors by representation shall be one-half (1/2).

##### Section 3
##### Adjustment of Shares of Inheritance

**Article 1163.- Share of inheritance of person who received special benefit**

1-Among the joint successors, if any person has received a gift for marriage or other reasons or as capital for livelihood, or has received a legacy from the decedent, the total value of the inheritance that the decedent had at the time of commencement of succession plus the value of such gift shall be deemed to be the inheritance, and the amount remaining after deducting the value of such gift or legacy from the share of the inheritance calculated in accordance with the provisions of Section 1 (Successors) and Section 2 (Succession by Spouse) shall be the share of the inheritance of such person.

2-If the value of the gift or legacy is equal to or exceeds the value of the share of the inheritance, such joint successor may not receive such share of the inheritance.

**Article 1164.- Value of gift**

If the donee has disposed of the property received by gift before the commencement of succession, or if such property has been lost or destroyed before the commencement of succession, the value of the gift provided for in Article 1163 (Share of inheritance of person who received special benefit) above shall be calculated based on the value at the time of such disposal, loss, or destruction. If the value of such gift has changed as a result of the donee having partly changed the property, the value of such gift shall be calculated by assuming that it was in its original state at the time of commencement of succession. Interest and profits derived from the gift received by the successor shall not be included in the calculation of such value.

**Article 1165.- Cases where value of special benefit exceeds value of share of inheritance**

1-Even if the value of the gift or legacy received by a joint successor exceeds the value of their share of the inheritance, such joint successor shall not be required to return the property related to the gift for the benefit of other joint successors, except where it must be reduced to satisfy the legally reserved shares.

2-In the case provided for in the preceding paragraph 1, the share of the inheritance of other joint successors shall be the amount calculated by dividing the inheritance that the decedent had at the time of commencement of succession in proportion to the share of the inheritance of each joint successor determined in accordance with the provisions of paragraph 1 of Article 1163 (Share of inheritance of person who received special benefit) above.

**Article 1166.- Contributory portion**

1-Among the joint successors, if any person has made a special contribution to the maintenance or increase of the decedent's property by providing services for the decedent's business, or by providing investment in the property, or by providing nursing or care for the decedent, or by other means, the value remaining after deducting the contributory portion of such person determined by agreement between the joint successors from the value of the inheritance that the decedent had at the time of commencement of succession shall be deemed to be the inheritance, and the amount of the share in the succession calculated in accordance with the provisions of Section 1 (Successors) and Section 2 (Succession by Spouse) plus the contributory portion shall be the share of the inheritance of such person.

2-If the agreement provided for in the preceding paragraph 1 is not reached or cannot be reached, the court shall determine the contributory portion upon the petition of the person who has made the contribution provided for in the preceding paragraph 1, by

taking into account the time, method, and degree of such contribution, the amount of the inheritance, and all other circumstances.

3-The contributory portion may not exceed the amount remaining after deducting the value of legacies and the value of property that the decedent has designated as the share of the inheritance of a successor or as a method of division of the inheritance from the value of the inheritance that the decedent had at the time of commencement of succession.

4-The petition provided for in the preceding paragraph 2 may be filed in cases where a petition for partition by the court has been filed pursuant to the provisions of paragraph 1 of Article 1270 (Partition by court) or a lawsuit has been filed pursuant to the provisions of Article 1274 (Claim by person recognized after partition of inheritance).

**Article 1167.- Relationship with third parties**

The adjustment of shares of inheritance pursuant to the provisions of this Section 3 (Adjustment of Shares of Inheritance) may not be asserted against a third party unless the conditions for representation of the result of partition based on such adjustment are met.

#### CHAPTER 3
#### TESTAMENTARY SUCCESSION

##### Section 1
##### Capacity to Make a Will

**Article 1168.- Persons who can make a will**

1-A person who has reached the age of majority and a minor who has been emancipated from parental power or guardianship for minors may make a will.

2-The provisions of Article 26 (Right to rescind acts) and Article 30 (Right to rescind

acts) of this Code shall not apply to a will.

**Article 1169.- Capacity at time of making a will**

1-A testator must have capacity at the time of making a will.

2-A person who asserts that a testator lacked capacity must provide evidence thereof. However, this provision shall not apply to cases provided for in Article 1176 (Will of person under general guardianship) of this Code.

##### Section 2
##### Form of Will

**Article 1170.- Necessity of form of will**

1-A will may not be made except in a form provided for in this Code.

2-A will that does not comply with any form provided for in this Code shall be void. However, a will that does not have a date shall be deemed valid unless such will is inconsistent with another will made by the same testator. In cases where there are several wills that do not have a date and such wills are inconsistent with each other, the effect of such wills shall be reduced proportionally.

**Article 1171.- Validity of form**

A will that complies with any form provided for in this Code shall be deemed valid as to its form.

**Article 1172.- Persons disqualified from being witnesses, etc.**

The following persons may not be witnesses or observers to a will:

(a) Minors or persons under general guardianship.

(b) Persons presumed to be successors, legatees, and their spouses and lineal blood relatives.

(c) Where a notary is involved in the making of a notarial will or a secret will, the spouse, lineal blood relatives, clerks, and employees of such notary.

**Article 1173.- Notarial will**

1-A notarial will shall be made by the testator in the presence of a notary in accordance with the form provided for in paragraph 2 below.

2-A notarial will shall be made in accordance with the following form:

(a) At least 2 (two) witnesses must be present.

(b) The testator must orally state the contents of the will to the notary.

(c) The notary must write down the meaning of the testator's will in writing and read it aloud to the testator and the witnesses.

(d) After the testator and the witnesses have acknowledged that the writing is correct, each of them must clearly state on the document their name, age, and domicile, and must sign it. However, if the testator is unable to write or sign, the notary may substitute the writing and signing provided for above by writing the testator's name, age, and domicile, and must also state the reason therefor.

(e) The notary must write the date and sign it.

**Article 1174.- Holograph will**

1-A holograph will must be made by the testator writing the entire text and the date in their own hand, and signing it. A holograph will that is written by another person or by using a machine such as a typewriter, etc., shall be void.

2-Any addition, deletion, or other alteration in a holograph will shall be void unless the testator indicates the place of the alteration and additionally states the reason for the alteration and, in particular, unless they sign it.

**Article 1175.- Secret will**

A secret will must be made in accordance with the following form:

(a) The testator must sign the document.

(b) The testator must put the document into an envelope, seal it, and sign or write a mark or other means on the seal of the envelope whereby it can be judged whether the envelope has been opened or not.

(c) The testator must present the sealed envelope to a notary and at least 2 (two) witnesses and declare that it is their will, and if it was written by a person other than the testator, the testator must declare the name and domicile of such person.

(d) The notary must write the date on which the testator presented the envelope and the testator's declaration on the sealed envelope, and sign it together with the witnesses and the testator.

**Article 1176.- Will of person under general guardianship**

1-In order for a person under general guardianship to make a will during a temporary recovery of the capacity to understand and judge the legal consequences of their acts, at least 2 (two) physicians must be present.

2-The physicians present at the time of making the will must additionally state in writing on the will that the testator was not in a state of lacking capacity to understand and judge the legal consequences of their acts due to mental disorder at the time of making the will, and must sign it, certifying that they are physicians.

**Article 1177.- Will of person in imminent danger of death**

1-If a person who is in imminent danger of death due to illness or other reasons intends to make a will, such will may be made in the presence of at least 3 (three) witnesses by orally declaring the contents of the will to one of the witnesses. In this case, the person who received the oral declaration must write it down, and read it to the witnesses or let them see it, and after each witness has acknowledged that the writing is correct, each witness must sign the document.

2-A will made pursuant to the provisions of the preceding paragraph 1 shall not be effective unless one of the witnesses or an interested person files a petition with the court within 1 (one) month from the day on which the will was made and obtains confirmation thereof.

3-The court may not confirm a will unless

confident that the will expresses the true intention of the testator.

**Article 1178.- Will of person who is confined, etc.**

1-A person who is confined in a separate place by a judgment or an administrative measure may make a will in the presence of a police officer or an official of the confinement facility and at least 2 (two) witnesses.

2-A person who is on a ship or an aircraft may make a will in the presence of the commander or a person in charge of the ship or aircraft or a crew member and at least 2 (two) witnesses.

3-In making a will provided for in paragraph 1 or 2 above, the testator may orally declare the contents of the will to the police officer, the official of the confinement facility, the commander, the person in charge, or the crew member.

4-The provisions of items (b) to (e) of paragraph 2 of Article 1173 (Notarial will) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to the cases provided for in paragraph 3 above. In this case, the term "notary" shall be interpreted as the police officer, the official of the confinement facility, the commander, the person in charge, or the crew member.

5-A will made pursuant to the provisions of paragraphs 1 to 4 above shall not be effective if the testator continues to live for 6 (six) months from the time when they become able to make a will in any of the forms provided for from Article 1173 (Notarial will) to Article 1175 (Secret will) of this Code after being released from the confinement facility or disembarking from the ship or aircraft.

**Article 1179.- Making of will by person who is unable to speak, etc.**

1-In cases where a person who is unable to speak makes a will in the form provided for in Article 1173 (Notarial will), Article 1175 (Secret will), or Article 1177 (Will of person in imminent danger of death) of this Code, the testator may substitute the oral declaration by making a declaration of the contents of the will or a declaration that the document is their will through an interpreter or by writing it in their own hand in the presence of the notary or witnesses.

2-In cases where the notary or other person provided for in this Code must read the meaning of the will to the testator or witnesses after the contents of the will have been written down, if the testator or witnesses are deaf and unable to hear, the notary or other person provided for in this Code may substitute the reading by conveying the meaning of the writing through an interpreter to the testator or witnesses.

3-In the cases provided for in paragraphs 1 and 2 above, the notary or other person provided for in this Code must state the reason therefor in the document.

**Article 1180.- Writing of surname and given name and thumbprint in lieu of signature**

In cases where a notary or a witness must sign a document, it shall be sufficient for the notary or witness to write their surname and given name and affix their thumbprint.

**Article 1181.- Special provisions for wills of Khmer nationals residing abroad**

If a Khmer national intends to make a notarial will or a

the secret [will] at a place where there is a consulate of the Kingdom of Cambodia, the consul shall perform the duties of a notary.

##### Section 3
##### Matters to be Included in a Will

**Article 1182.- Determination of shares of inheritance**

1-A testator may determine the shares of inheritance of joint successors by a will, or may express an intention contrary to the provisions of Article 1163 (Share of inheritance of person who received special benefit) of this Code regarding the shares of inheritance of a person who received a special benefit. However, a determination of shares of inheritance that violates the legally reserved shares must comply with the provisions regarding legally reserved shares.

2-If the testator has determined the shares of inheritance for only one or several of the joint successors, the shares of inheritance of other joint successors shall be determined in accordance with the provisions of Section 3 (Adjustment of Shares of Inheritance) of Chapter 2.

**Article 1183.- Determination of method of partition by will, etc.**

A testator may by a will determine the method of partition or entrust a third party with such determination, or may prohibit partition for a period not exceeding 5 (five) years from the time of commencement of succession.

**Article 1184.- Disposition of property**

A testator may make a gift of all or a part of their property, or may make a testamentary gift by any of the methods provided for in Article 1194 (Definition of legacy) of this Code, provided that it complies with the provisions regarding legally reserved shares.

**Article 1185.- Disposition of property to successor**
