1-If a testator makes a will intended to dispose of one or more specific items of property to one or more joint successors, such will shall be presumed to be a determination of the method of partition, except in cases where the will clearly intends to make a legacy, such as a legacy with a burden, etc. If the value of the property so determined exceeds the share of the inheritance of such successor, it shall also be presumed to be a determination of the share of the inheritance.

2-In cases where a testator makes a will regarding only a portion of the inheritance, if the value of such property is less than the share of the inheritance to which the successor is entitled, it shall be presumed to be a determination of only the method of partition of the inheritance to be received by such successor, unless a contrary intention is expressed in the will.

**Article 1186.- Designation of executor**

A testator may by a will designate one or more executors or entrust a third party with such designation.

**Article 1187.- Other matters**

In addition to the matters provided for in this Code, a testator may by a will provide advice or opinions to ensure harmony among family members and other matters. In such cases, the successors, etc., must respect such will.

##### Section 4
##### Revocation of Will

**Article 1188.- Time and method of revocation of will**

A testator may revoke a will in whole or in part at any time in accordance with the provisions of this Section 4 (Revocation of Will).

**Article 1189.- Revocation by the form of a will**

1-A testator may revoke a will in whole or in part through a will.

2-If a later will is inconsistent with an earlier will, the later will shall be deemed to have revoked the earlier will to the extent of the inconsistency.

**Article 1190.- Revocation of will by inter vivos disposition**

If a testator makes a disposition or performs another act with respect to the subject matter of a will during their lifetime, the testator shall be deemed to have revoked the will to the extent that such disposition or act is inconsistent with the will.

**Article 1191.- Revocation by destruction of will, etc.**

If a testator intentionally destroys the original will, the testator shall be deemed to have revoked the will to the extent of the destruction. This provision shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where the testator intentionally destroys the subject matter of the will.

**Article 1192.- Waiver of right to revoke will**

A testator may not waive the right to revoke a will.

**Article 1193.- Revocation of legacy with a burden**

If a legatee fails to perform a duty that the testator has required the legatee to bear in connection with the legacy, the executor or, if there is no executor, the successors may demand that the legatee perform such duty within a reasonable period, and if the legatee fails to perform the duty within such period, the executor or the successors may apply to the court for the revocation of the legacy.

##### Section 5
##### Effect of Will

**Article 1194.- Time when will becomes effective**

1-A will shall become effective from the time of the testator's death.

2-In cases where a will is subject to a condition precedent, if such condition is satisfied after the death of the testator, the will shall become effective from the time the condition is satisfied.

**Article 1195.- Effect of determination of shares of inheritance, etc.**

1-If a testator has determined the shares of inheritance or the method of partition by disposing of one or more specific items of property to one or more successors by a will, such specific property shall automatically devolve to the successor who is to receive such property at the same time as the will becomes effective.

2-In cases where a will is subject to a condition precedent, if such condition is satisfied after the death of the testator, the property provided for in the preceding paragraph 1 shall be deemed to have devolved

the successor who is to receive such property retroactively from the time of the testator's death.

**Article 1196.- Death of person presumed to be successor, etc.**

1-In cases where a testator has determined the share of inheritance of a successor or the method of partition by a will, if the person presumed to be the successor has died before the commencement of succession or has lost the right to succeed pursuant to the provisions of Article 1150 (Ineligibility for succession, etc.) of this Code or by disinheritance, such determination shall be deemed to have been made for the successor by representation of such person.

2-In the case provided for in the preceding paragraph 1, if there is no successor by representation of the person presumed to be the successor, the determination for such person shall not be effective. This provision shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where a successor has renounced the succession.

3-Notwithstanding the provisions of paragraph 1 or 2 above, if the testator has expressed a contrary intention in the will, such intention shall be followed.

**Article 1197.- Limitation on will for guardian, etc.**

1-If a person under general guardianship makes a will for the benefit of the guardian or the spouse or lineal descendants of the guardian before the accounts of the guardianship are settled, such will shall be void.

2-The provisions of the preceding paragraph 1 shall not apply cases where a lineal blood relative, spouse, or sibling is the general guardian.

**Article 1198.- Right to receive property in case legacy is void, etc.**

If a legacy does not become effective because the legatee is ineligible or has died or for other reasons, or if a legacy does not become effective because of renunciation, the property that the legatee was to receive shall devolve to the successors of the decedent. However, if the testator has expressed a contrary intention in the will, such intention shall be followed.

##### Section 6
##### Legacy

**Article 1199.- Definition of legacy**

A universal legacy is a gift made by a testator to one or more persons of all or a portion of their property determined by a certain proportion. A specific legacy is a gift of specific property to one or more persons by a will.

**Article 1200.- Death of legatee**

1-A legacy shall not become effective if the legatee dies before or at the time of the testator's death. This provision shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where a legacy is subject to a condition precedent and the legatee dies before the condition is satisfied.

2-Notwithstanding the provisions of the preceding paragraph 1, if the testator has expressed a contrary intention in the will, such intention shall be followed.

3-In cases where a successor is designated as a legatee, the provisions of the preceding paragraph 1 shall not preclude the application of the provisions of paragraph 1 of Article 1196 (Death of person presumed to be successor, etc.) of this Code.

**Article 1201.- Renunciation of legacy**

1-A legatee may renounce a legacy at any time after the death of the testator. However, a renunciation of a universal legacy must comply with the provisions regarding renunciation of succession.

2-A renunciation of a legacy shall have retroactive effect from the time of the death of the testator.

**Article 1202.- Demand by person interested in the performance of duty**

A person who has a duty in connection with a legacy or an interested person may demand that the legatee accept or renounce the legacy within a reasonable period. If the legatee fails to express an intention to the person who has the duty in connection with the legacy within such period, the legatee shall be deemed to have accepted the legacy.

**Article 1203.- Renunciation or acceptance by successor of legatee**

If a legatee dies without having accepted or renounced the legacy, the successor of the legatee may accept or renounce the legacy within the limits of their own right of succession. However, if the testator has expressed a contrary intention in the will, such intention shall be followed.

**Article 1204.- Revocation of acceptance or renunciation**

1-Acceptance or renunciation of a legacy may not be revoked.

2-The provisions of paragraph 2 of Article 1252 (Revocation of acceptance or renunciation) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to the acceptance and renunciation of a legacy.

**Article 1205.- Acceptance or renunciation in cases where legatee is a successor**

1-In cases where a legatee is also a successor, if the legatee renounces the succession, they shall be deemed to have renounced the legacy as well. The legatee may not accept the legacy while renouncing the succession.

2-Even if they have accepted the succession, the legatee provided for in the preceding paragraph 1 may renounce a specific legacy.

**Article 1206.- Right of legatee to receive fruits**

A legatee may receive the fruits of the legacy from the time when they become entitled to demand the performance of the legacy. However, if the testator has expressed a contrary intention in the will, such intention shall be followed.

**Article 1207.- Legacy of right not included in inheritance**

A legacy shall not be effective if the right that is the subject matter of the legacy is not included in the inheritance at the time of the testator's death.

**Article 1208.- Legacy of thing or right subject to third party's right**

If a thing or right that is the subject matter of a legacy is subject to a right of a third party at the time of the testator's death, the legatee may not demand that the person who has a duty in connection with the legacy extinguish such right.

**Article 1209.- Subrogation of subject matter of legacy**

1-If a testator has a right to demand compensation from a third party for the loss or damage of a thing that is the subject matter of a legacy or for the loss of possession of such thing, such right shall be presumed to have been made the subject matter of the legacy.

2-In cases where a thing that is the subject matter of a legacy has been joined, mixed, or

or mixed with other things, if the testator has become the sole owner or a joint owner of the newly formed thing pursuant to the provisions of Article 186 (Accession between movable and immovable) or Article 198 (Accession or mixture of movables) of this Code, it shall be presumed that the testator has made such entire ownership or joint ownership the subject matter of the legacy.

**Article 1210.- Subrogation of subject matter of legacy of claim**

1-In cases where a claim has been made the subject matter of a legacy, if the testator has received performance of the claim and the thing so received is still in the inheritance, it shall be presumed that such thing has been made the subject matter of the legacy.

2-With respect to a claim for a sum of money, it shall be presumed that the money or bank deposits have been made the subject matter of the legacy to the extent of the amount of money in the inheritance and the amount of money deposited in the bank after the performance has been received.

**Article 1211.- Liability of legatee of legacy with a burden**

1-A person who has received a legacy with a burden shall be liable for the performance of the duty to be borne only to the extent of the value of the subject matter of the legacy.

2-If the value of the subject matter of a legacy with a burden is reduced due to acceptance of succession with a limitation or due to a claim for reduction to supplement a legally reserved share, the legatee shall be released from the duty to be borne in proportion to the reduction. However, if the testator has expressed a contrary intention in the will, such intention shall be followed.

**Article 1212.- Rights and duties of universal legatee**

1-A universal legatee shall have the same rights and duties as a successor.

2-If the entire inheritance is given as a universal legacy to a person who is not a successor, the successors shall be deemed not to have succeeded to the inheritance, except for the right to a legally reserved share.

##### Section 7
##### Execution of Will

**Article 1213.- Probate and opening of will**

1-A person who has custody of a will must submit it to the court for probate without delay after they become aware of the commencement of succession. This provision shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where there is no custodian and a successor or an interested person has found a will.

2-The provisions of the preceding paragraph 1 shall not apply to a notarial will.

3-A will that is sealed must be opened only in the court in the presence of successors, interested persons, or their representatives as observers.

4-A person who opens a will outside of the court in violation of the provisions of the preceding paragraph 3 shall be subject to a civil fine not exceeding 1,000,000 (one million) Riels.

5-A will shall not lose its effect even if it was opened outside of the court in violation of the provisions of paragraph 3 above.

**Article 1214.- Designation of executor**

1-If a testator has entrusted a third party with the designation of an executor, the person so entrusted must designate the executor and notify the successors without delay.

2-If the person entrusted as provided for in the preceding paragraph 1 intends to decline the entrustment, they must notify the successors of such fact without delay.

**Article 1215.- Assumption of office by executor**

An executor who has consented to assume the office must perform their duties immediately.

**Article 1216.- Demand to assume office of executor**

1-A successor or other interested person may demand that a person designated as an executor express whether they will or will not consent to assume the office within a reasonable period set by the successor or interested person. If the designated person fails to express a clear intention within such period, they shall be deemed to have declined to assume the office.

2-The provisions of the first sentence of the preceding paragraph 1 shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where the third party entrusted with the designation of an executor fails to designate an executor promptly. In this case, if the third party fails to designate an executor within such period, they shall be deemed to have declined the entrustment.

**Article 1217.- Persons who may not become executors**

A person under general guardianship, a person under curatorship, a minor, or a person

bankrupt person may not become an executor.

**Article 1218.- Appointment of executor**

If there is no executor or if the executor is lost, the court may appoint an executor upon the application of a successor or an interested person.

**Article 1219.- Preparation of inventory of property**

1-An executor must prepare an inventory of the property and deliver it to the successors without delay.

2-Successors may be present when the executor prepares the inventory of the property.

**Article 1220.- Authority and duties of executor**

An executor shall have the right and duty to manage the inheritance and to perform all other acts necessary for the execution of the will.

**Article 1221.- Loss of right of disposition by successor**

1-If there is an executor, the successors may not dispose of the inheritance or perform any other act that would obstruct the execution of the will.

2-Any disposition made in violation of the provisions of the preceding paragraph 1 shall be void.

**Article 1222.- Execution of will regarding specific property**

In cases where a will relates to specific property, the provisions from Article 1219 (Preparation of inventory of property) to Article 1221 (Loss of right of disposition by successor)

(Loss of right of disposition by successor) of this Code shall apply only to such property.

**Article 1223.- Status of executor**

1-An executor shall perform acts in the name of the executor with respect to the inheritance they are to execute.

2-Acts performed by an executor within the scope of their duties shall have direct effect on the successors.

**Article 1224.- Right of executor to appoint a substitute**

1-Unless there is an unavoidable reason, an executor may not have a third party perform their duties. However, this provision shall not apply if the testator has expressed a contrary intention in the will.

2-In cases where an executor has a third party perform their duties in accordance with the provisions of the preceding paragraph 1, the executor shall be liable for the appointment and supervision of such person to the legatees and successors. If the executor has a third party perform the duties in violation of the provisions of paragraph 1 above, the executor shall compensate for any damage arising therefrom to the legatees and successors.

**Article 1225.- Joint executors**

1-In cases where there are two or more executors, the performance of their duties shall be decided by a majority vote. However, if the testator has expressed a contrary intention in the will, such intention shall be followed.

2-Notwithstanding the provisions of the preceding paragraph 1, each executor may perform acts of preservation.

**Article 1226.- Remuneration of executor**

1-An executor may receive the remuneration provided for in the will.

2-If the will does not provide for remuneration, the court may determine a reasonable amount of remuneration upon the application of the executor, taking into account the condition of the inheritance, the relationship between the testator and the executor, and other circumstances.

**Article 1227.- Resignation and dismissal of executor**

1-If there is a justifiable reason, an executor may resign from the office with the permission of the court.

2-If an executor has neglected their duties or if there is any other justifiable reason, an interested person may apply to the court for the dismissal of the executor.

**Article 1228.- Expenses of execution of will**

The expenses related to the execution of a will shall be borne by the inheritance. However, the legally reserved shares may not be reduced by such expenses.

**Article 1229.- Mutatis mutandis application of provisions regarding mandate**

The provisions regarding mandate shall apply mutatis mutandis to the duties of an executor, except to the extent inconsistent with the nature of the executor's office.

#### Chapter 4
#### Legally Reserved Shares

##### Section 1
##### General Provisions

**Article 1230.- Persons entitled to legally reserved shares**

1-Lineal descendants, parents or grandparents, and the spouse of a decedent may acquire property as follows:

(a) One-third (1/3) of the decedent's property in cases where only parents or grandparents are successors.

(b) One-half (1/2) of the decedent's property in other cases.

2-In cases where there are two or more successors, each person entitled to a legally reserved share may receive a legally reserved share in proportion to their share of the inheritance.

3-The provisions of Article 1157 (Succession by representation) and Article 1158 (Share of inheritance in case of succession by representation) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to legally reserved shares.

**Article 1231.- Property forming the basis for calculation of legally reserved shares**

1-A legally reserved share shall be calculated by taking the property owned by the decedent at the time of commencement of succession, adding the value of special benefits provided for in Article 1163 (Share of inheritance of person who received special benefit) and the value of gifts provided for in Article 1232 (Gifts forming the basis for calculation of legally reserved shares) of this Code, and then subtracting the total amount of obligations.

2-A right subject to a condition or a right whose duration of existence is uncertain shall be valued based on an appraisal by an appraiser who

appointed by the court.

3-The obligations provided for in paragraph 1 above shall also include funeral expenses according to the status of the decedent.

**Article 1232.- Gifts forming the basis for calculation of legally reserved shares**

1-The following gifts shall be included in the property forming the basis for calculation of legally reserved shares, except for the special benefits provided for in Article 1163 (Share of inheritance of person who received special benefit) of this Code:

(a) Gifts made within one (1) year before the commencement of succession.

(b) Aside from the gifts provided for in point (a) above, any gifts made by both parties with knowledge that such gifts would cause damage to the persons entitled to legally reserved shares.

2-In this Chapter 4 (Legally Reserved Shares), a release of an obligation without consideration shall be deemed to be a gift.

**Article 1233.- Property subject to reduction to supplement legally reserved shares**

A legacy, a designation of a share of inheritance by a will, a special benefit, and a gift provided for in Article 1232 (Gifts forming the basis for calculation of legally reserved shares) above shall be subject to reduction to supplement legally reserved shares. However, in cases of a legacy, a designation of a share of inheritance, or a special benefit to a successor, such property shall be subject to reduction only to the extent it exceeds the amount of the successor's legally reserved share.

**Article 1234.- Amount of claim for reduction to supplement legally reserved share**

The amount that a person entitled to a legally reserved share may demand by way of reduction to supplement the legally reserved share shall be the amount calculated by taking the amount of the legally reserved share, subtracting the total amount of property received by the person by way of succession and special benefits, and then adding the amount of obligations borne by such person by way of succession.

##### Section 2
##### Method of Reduction to Supplement Legally Reserved Shares

**Article 1235.- Claim for reduction to supplement legally reserved share**

A person entitled to a legally reserved share and their successors may demand reduction of legacies, etc. from persons who have received property or benefits that are subject to reduction to supplement a legally reserved share, to the extent necessary to preserve such legally reserved share.

**Article 1236.- Order of reduction**

Gifts and special benefits may not be reduced until legacies and designations of shares of inheritance in a will have been reduced.

**Article 1237.- Reduction of legacies, etc.**

1-First, legacies and designations of shares of inheritance for successors shall be reduced simultaneously, and if a deficiency still remains, legacies for persons other than successors shall be reduced.

2-Legacies shall be reduced in proportion to the value of their subject matter.

These provisions shall also apply to the designation of a share of inheritance by way of a specific legacy of one or more items of property. However, in cases where a legacy or a designation of a share of inheritance provided for a successor is subject to reduction to supplement a legally reserved share, the portion exceeding the amount of the successor's legally reserved share shall be deemed to be the value of the subject matter.

3-A designation of a share of inheritance in a will that is determined as a proportion of the inheritance shall be reduced to supplement a legally reserved share by changing such proportion.

4-Notwithstanding the provisions of paragraphs 1, 2, and 3 above, if the testator has expressed a special contrary intention in the will, such intention shall be followed.

**Article 1238.- Order of reduction of gifts, etc.**

1-Reduction of gifts or special benefits shall start from the most recent gift or special benefit and then proceed to previous gifts or special benefits in reverse chronological order.

2-If a gift or special benefit was made more than twenty (20) years before the commencement of succession, the donee or the successor who received such special benefit may refuse the claim for reduction to supplement a legally reserved share.

**Article 1239.- Return of fruits**

A co-successor, legatee, or donee must return, in addition to the property or compensation to be returned, the fruits arising since the day on which the claim for reduction was made.

**Article 1240.- Burden of loss due to insolvency**

Any loss arising from the insolvency of a co-successor, legatee

the loss to be borne by the co-successor, legatee, or donee subject to reduction shall be borne by the persons entitled to legally reserved shares.

**Article 1241.- Reduction of gift with a burden**

With respect to a gift with a burden, a claim for reduction may be made for the value of the subject matter after deducting the value of the burden.

**Article 1242.- Reduction of contract, etc. deemed to be a gift**

1-A contract concluded for an inadequate consideration shall be deemed to be a gift only if both parties concluded it with knowledge that it would cause damage to the persons entitled to legally reserved shares. In this case, if the person entitled to a legally reserved share demands its reduction, such person must return the consideration.

2-The provisions of the preceding paragraph 1 shall apply mutatis mutandis to a release of an obligation for an inadequate consideration.

**Article 1243.- Reduction in case of disposition of subject matter of gift**

1-If a co-successor, legatee, or donee subject to reduction has assigned the subject matter of the gift to another person, such person must pay its value at the time of assignment to the person entitled to a legally reserved share. However, if the assignee knew at the time of the assignment that it would cause damage to the person entitled to a legally reserved share, the person entitled to a legally reserved share may also demand reduction against such assignee.

2-The provisions of the preceding paragraph 1 shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases where a co-successor, legatee, or donee has created a right over the subject matter of the gift.

**Article 1244.- Compensation by payment of value**

1-A co-successor, legatee, or donee may be released from the duty of return by paying the value of the designated share of inheritance, the subject matter of the legacy, or the subject matter of the gift to the person entitled to a legally reserved share to the extent of the reduction.

2-The provisions of the preceding paragraph 1 shall apply mutatis mutandis to cases provided for in the second sentence of paragraph 1 of Article 1243 (Reduction in case of disposition of subject matter of gift) above.

##### Section 3
##### Extinction of Claim for Reduction

**Article 1245.- Statute of limitations for extinction of claim for reduction**

A claim for reduction shall be extinguished by prescription if one (1) year has elapsed from the time when the person entitled to a legally reserved share became aware of the commencement of succession and the designation of a share of inheritance, legacy, gift, or special benefit subject to reduction. A claim for reduction shall also be extinguished by prescription if five (5) years have elapsed from the time of the commencement of succession.

**Article 1246.- Renunciation of legally reserved share**

A person may renounce all or a portion of their legally reserved share. However, a renunciation of a legally reserved share before the commencement of succession shall be effective only if the permission of the court is obtained.

**Article 1247.- Cases of co-successors**

The extinction by prescription of the right of any of the co-successors to demand reduction to supplement a legally reserved share, or the renunciation of a legally reserved share by any of the co-successors, shall not affect the legally reserved shares of the other co-successors.

#### Chapter 5
#### Acceptance and Renunciation of Succession

##### Section 1
##### General Provisions

**Article 1248.- Period for acceptance or renunciation**

1-A successor must accept or renounce the succession in its entirety or with a limitation within three (3) months from the time they become aware of the commencement of succession in their favor. However, this period may be extended by the court upon the application of the successor.

2-A successor may investigate the inheritance before accepting or renouncing it.

**Article 1249.- Special case for period where successor dies**

If a successor dies without having accepted or renounced the succession, the period provided for in paragraph 1 of Article 1248 (Period for acceptance or renunciation) above shall be calculated from the time when the successor's own successor becomes aware of the commencement of succession in their favor.

**Article 1250.- Special case for period where successor is minor, etc.**

If a successor is a minor or a person under general guardianship, the period

the period provided for in paragraph 1 of Article 1248 (Period for acceptance or renunciation) above shall be calculated from the time when the legal representative becomes aware of the commencement of succession in favor of the minor or the person under general guardianship.

**Article 1251.- Special case for period where successor is unaware of existence of obligations**

1-In cases where the decedent's obligations are in a large amount and cannot be fully satisfied by the inheritance, if the successor was unable to know the existence of such obligations based on the decedent's standard of living, the condition of the decedent's property, or other circumstances, the period provided for in paragraph 1 of Article 1248 (Period for acceptance or renunciation) above shall be calculated from the time the successor becomes aware of the existence of such obligations.

2-In cases provided for in the preceding paragraph 1, if the successor has disposed of the inheritance to satisfy other obligations received by way of succession before becoming aware of the existence of the obligations provided for in the same paragraph 1, the provisions of point (a) of Article 1255 (Constructive absolute acceptance) of this Code shall not apply.

**Article 1252.- Revocation of acceptance or renunciation**

1-Acceptance or renunciation of succession may not be revoked, even within the period provided for in paragraph 1 of Article 1248 (Period for acceptance or renunciation) of this Code.

2-The provisions of the preceding paragraph 1 shall not preclude the revocation of an acceptance or renunciation of succession on the grounds of a defect in the expression of intention. However, such right of revocation shall be extinguished if it is not exercised within six (6) months

from the time when ratification becomes possible. This provision shall also apply to cases where the right of revocation is not exercised within five (5) years from the time of commencement of succession.

3-A person who intends to revoke an acceptance with a limitation or a renunciation of succession as provided for in the preceding paragraph 2 shall apply to the court for such revocation.

**Article 1253.- Prohibition of action against successor before acceptance**

A claim against the inheritance may not be asserted in an action against the successor before the successor accepts the succession.

##### Section 2
##### Acceptance

**Article 1254.- Absolute acceptance**

If a successor accepts the succession absolutely, they shall succeed without limitation to the rights and duties of the decedent.

**Article 1255.- Constructive absolute acceptance**

In the following cases, a successor shall be deemed to have accepted the succession absolutely:

(a) If the successor has disposed of all or a portion of the inheritance. However, this shall not apply to acts of preservation and acts of mere management.

(b) If the successor fails to accept the succession with a limitation or to renounce it within the period provided for in paragraph 1 of Article 1248 (Period for acceptance or renunciation)

renunciation of succession) of this Code.

(c) Notwithstanding that the successor has accepted the succession with a limitation or renounced it, if the successor has concealed all or a portion of the inheritance or has used the inheritance for their own benefit. However, this provision shall not apply after the person who has become a successor due to the renunciation by the previous successor has accepted the succession.

**Article 1256.- Qualified acceptance**

1-A successor may accept the succession with the reservation that they shall satisfy the decedent's obligations and legacies only to the extent of the property that they acquire through the succession.

2-If there are two or more successors, an acceptance with a limitation may be made only if all co-successors do so jointly.

**Article 1257.- Method of qualified acceptance**

A successor who intends to accept the succession with a limitation must prepare an inventory and apply for the acceptance with a limitation to the court within the period provided for in paragraph 1 of Article 1248 (Period for acceptance or renunciation) of this Code.

**Article 1258.- Effect of qualified acceptance**

1-In cases where a successor has accepted the succession with a limitation, the decedent's creditors may not demand that the successor be held liable with their personal property.

2-In cases provided for in paragraph 1 above, the rights and duties of the successor toward the decedent shall be deemed not to have been extinguished by confusion.

**Article 1259.- Cases where grounds for constructive absolute acceptance arise after qualified acceptance**

1-In cases where a person who has succeeded alone has already made a qualified acceptance, if such successor has in bad faith failed to list all or a portion of the inheritance in the inventory, or if any of the grounds provided for in point (a) or (c) of Article 1255 (Constructive absolute acceptance) of this Code arise with respect to such successor, the successor shall be deemed to have made an absolute acceptance, and the declaration of qualified acceptance shall be void ab initio.

2-If any of the grounds provided for in the preceding paragraph 1 arise with respect to one or more co-successors who have made a qualified acceptance, the decedent's creditors may exercise their rights against such co-successor(s) for the amount of the claims that cannot be satisfied by the inheritance in proportion to each person's share of the inheritance.

##### Section 3
##### Renunciation

**Article 1260.- Renunciation of succession**

A person who intends to renounce the succession must apply for the renunciation of succession to the court.

**Article 1261.- Effect of renunciation**

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code)

A person who has renounced the succession shall be deemed to never have been a successor to that succession.

#### Chapter 6
#### Management and Partition of Inheritance

##### Section 1
##### Management of Inheritance

**Article 1262.- Custody and management of inheritance**

1-A successor who is in possession of the inheritance at the time of the decedent's death must keep custody of and manage the inheritance until the partition of the inheritance. However, such custody and management shall follow the provisions of Article 1263 (Management by executor) below.

2-A successor must manage the inheritance with the same care they exercise over their own property, even before they have accepted or renounced the succession. This shall also apply even after the successor has renounced the succession, until the person who becomes a successor due to such renunciation can begin to manage the inheritance.

**Article 1263.- Management by executor**

An executor who has assumed their office shall manage the inheritance. However, if the will pertains to specific items of property, the executor shall manage only such property.

**Article 1264.- Temporary administrator of inheritance**

1-If there is no executor who manages the entire inheritance, a successor, a legatee, or a creditor of the decedent may apply to the court for the appointment of a temporary administrator of the inheritance until the partition of the inheritance.

2-Notwithstanding the provisions of Article 1253 (Prohibition of action against successor before acceptance) of this Code, a temporary administrator appointed under the provisions of paragraph 1 above shall be the party to any action involving claims related to the inheritance, in their capacity as the representative of the decedent's successors, and shall also specify the name of the decedent, until the successor accepts the succession.

3-The provisions of paragraphs 1 and 3 of Article 40 (Rights of administrator of property, etc.) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to a temporary administrator.

**Article 1265.- Expenses related to inheritance**

Expenses related to the inheritance shall be paid out of the inheritance. However, expenses arising from the fault of a successor shall be borne by such successor.

##### Section 2
##### Partition of Inheritance

**Article 1266.- Discussion for partition of inheritance**

(Amended by the Law on the Implementation of the Civil Code)

1-Co-successors may commence discussions for the partition of the inheritance at any time after one (1) month has elapsed from the commencement of succession. However, with respect to the change of the name of the holder of the right to the inheritance as a result of the partition, the provisions of Article 1277 (Prohibition of change of name of holder of right) of this Code shall be followed, and in cases where the decedent has prohibited the partition of the inheritance in a will, the partition of the inheritance may not be made during the period of such prohibition.

2-In order to transfer the rights over the objects according to the partition of the inheritance, the partition shall be made according to

the formalities necessary for the transfer of each right, such as a written document, etc.

**Article 1267.- Basis of partition**

1-If the method of partition is not specified in the will, the partition of the inheritance shall be made by taking into account the types and characteristics of the objects or rights belonging to the inheritance, or the age, occupation, physical and mental condition, and standard of living of each co-successor, and other circumstances.

2-In cases where the partition would result in a severe decrease in value, if it is deemed reasonable, the co-successors may, through discussion, allow one of the co-successors to acquire such property on the condition of paying a reasonable amount to the other co-successors.

**Article 1268.- Priority right of spouse**

In cases where a spouse becomes a successor, if there is property that was owned in co-ownership with the decedent during the marriage, the spouse shall have priority over other co-successors in acquiring the co-owned share of such property through the partition of the inheritance, up to the full amount of their share of the inheritance.

**Article 1269.- Satisfaction of decedent's obligations, etc.**

If the decedent had obligations, the co-successors must partition the inheritance by taking into account the fact that such obligations must be satisfied, and they may not change the proportion of the burden of such obligations without the consent of the creditors.

**Article 1270.- Partition by court**

1-If the co-successors fail to reach an agreement through discussion for the partition of the inheritance, or if such discussion cannot be held, any co-successor may apply to the court for

partition of the inheritance.

2-In making a partition of the inheritance, in addition to the provisions of Article 1267 (Basis of partition) through Article 1269 (Satisfaction of decedent's obligations, etc.) above, the court shall take into general consideration the local customs and the opinions of the majority of the co-successors.

**Article 1271.- Conversion to money by sale**

With respect to property for which no agreement can be reached among the co-successors regarding its partition or the person to acquire it, the court may sell such property and partition the proceeds of the sale.

**Article 1272.- Property acquired based on disposition of inheritance before partition, etc.**

Property acquired as compensation for the loss, damage, or infringement of the subject matter of the inheritance, or under a contract relating to the inheritance based on a right belonging to the inheritance, shall belong to the inheritance.

**Article 1273.- Effect of partition**

1-A partition of the inheritance shall have retroactive effect from the time of commencement of succession. However, such partition may not infringe upon the rights of a third party that has been registered or has otherwise fulfilled the requirements for perfection.

2-Each co-successor shall receive the certificates of title relating to the property they have acquired.

**Article 1274.- Claim by person recognized after partition of inheritance**

In cases where a person who has become a successor due to being recognized

recognized after the decedent's death and intends to demand the partition of the inheritance, if the other co-successors have already partitioned the inheritance or otherwise disposed of it, such person shall only have the right to demand compensation by value.

**Article 1275.- Perfection of partition of claims, etc.**

1-In a partition of the inheritance, if claims belonging to the decedent are partitioned in a proportion other than the statutory shares of inheritance, such partition may not be asserted against a debtor unless all co-successors have given notice of such partition to the debtor, or unless the partition was made by an authentic act and such act is shown or a copy thereof is delivered to the debtor.

2-A debtor who has made payment to a co-successor in accordance with their statutory share of inheritance before receiving the notice provided for in paragraph 1 above may assert such payment against the co-successor who acquired the claim through the partition of the inheritance.

**Article 1276.- Warranty liability of co-successors**

Each co-successor shall, in proportion to their share of the inheritance, be liable for a warranty toward other co-successors, in the same manner as a seller.

##### Section 3
##### Adjustment with Creditors

**Article 1277.- Prohibition of change of name of holder of right**

A successor and an administrator of the inheritance may not change the name of the holder of a right belonging to the inheritance to that of a successor or a legatee until the period provided for in paragraph 1 of Article 1248 (Period for acceptance or renunciation)

renunciation) of this Code has elapsed. However, this provision shall not apply to cases where the name of the holder of a right belonging to the inheritance is changed as necessary to satisfy the decedent's obligations.

**Article 1278.- Decedent's creditors**

The decedent's creditors may not exercise their rights against the successor's personal property until the period provided for in paragraph 1 of Article 1248 (Period for acceptance or renunciation) of this Code has elapsed.

**Article 1279.- Successor's creditors**

The successor's creditors may not exercise their rights against the inheritance until the period provided for in paragraph 1 of Article 1248 (Period for acceptance or renunciation) of this Code has elapsed.

##### Section 4
##### Liquidation, etc. in Cases of Qualified Acceptance

**Article 1280.- Management of inheritance after qualified acceptance**

A person who has made a qualified acceptance shall continue to manage the inheritance with due care as a bona fide administrator.

**Article 1281.- Public notice and demand**

1-A person who has made a qualified acceptance shall, within five (5) days after making the qualified acceptance, give public notice to all of the decedent's creditors and legatees that they have made a qualified acceptance and that the creditors and legatees should present their claims within a certain period

specified period. However, this period shall not be less than two (2) months.

2-In the public notice provided for in paragraph 1 above, the person who has made a qualified acceptance must add that if a creditor fails to present their claim within the specified period, such claim shall be excluded from the liquidation. However, the person who has made a qualified acceptance may not exclude any creditors known to them.

3-The person who has made a qualified acceptance must personally demand each creditor known to them to present their claim.

**Article 1282.- Right to refuse performance during the demand period**

Before the expiration of the period specified in the public notice provided for in paragraph 1 of Article 1281 (Public notice and demand) above, the person who has made a qualified acceptance may refuse to make performance to the decedent's creditors and legatees.

**Article 1283.- Performance by proportional distribution**

After the expiration of the period specified in the public notice provided for in paragraph 1 of Article 1281 (Public notice and demand) above, the person who has made a qualified acceptance must satisfy the claims of the creditors who presented their claims within such period and any other creditors known to them out of the inheritance, in proportion to the amount of each claim. However, such performance may not infringe upon the rights of creditors who have priority rights.

**Article 1284.- Satisfaction of obligations, etc. before their due date**

1-Even if a claim is not yet due, the person who has made a qualified acceptance

who has made a qualified acceptance must make performance in accordance with the provisions of Article 1283 (Performance by proportional distribution) above.

2-With respect to claims subject to a condition or claims of uncertain duration, the person who has made a qualified acceptance must satisfy such claims according to the evaluation of an appraiser appointed by the court.

**Article 1285.- Performance to legatees**

A person who has made a qualified acceptance may not make performance to legatees unless they have made performance to all creditors in accordance with the provisions of Article 1283 (Performance by proportional distribution) and Article 1284 (Satisfaction of obligations, etc. before their due date) above.

**Article 1286.- Conversion of inheritance into money**

1-If it is necessary to sell the inheritance in order to make performance in accordance with the provisions of Article 1283 (Performance by proportional distribution) through Article 1285 (Performance to legatees) above, the person who has made a qualified acceptance must sell the inheritance by way of compulsory sale or sell it at a price higher than the evaluation of an appraiser appointed by the court.

2-The person who has made a qualified acceptance, a creditor of the decedent, or a legatee may become the purchaser themselves in the compulsory sale or the sale provided for in paragraph 1 above.

**Article 1287.- Liability for improper performance**

1-If a person who has made a qualified acceptance has been negligent in giving the public notice or demand provided for in Article 1281 (Public notice and demand) of this Code, or if they have failed to make performance to a creditor or

legatee, because they had made performance to another creditor or legatee within the period specified in the public notice provided for in paragraph 1 of Article 1281 above, the person who made such qualified acceptance shall be liable for the damages resulting therefrom.

2-The provisions of paragraph 1 above shall not preclude any creditor or legatee from demanding damages from a creditor or legatee who received performance with knowledge of its impropriety.

**Article 1288.- Case where claim was not presented**

Creditors and legatees who failed to present their claims within the period specified in the public notice provided for in paragraph 1 of Article 1281 (Public notice and demand) and who were unknown to the person who made the qualified acceptance may exercise their rights only with respect to the remaining property. However, this shall not apply to persons who have a real security right over a specific item or right belonging to the inheritance.

**Article 1289.- Cases involving two or more successors**

1-In cases where there are two or more successors, if a qualified acceptance is made, the court shall appoint an administrator of the inheritance from among the successors.

2-The administrator shall perform all acts necessary for the management of the inheritance and the satisfaction of obligations for themselves and on behalf of the other co-successors.

3-The provisions of Article 1281 (Public notice and demand) through Article 1288 (Case where claim was not presented) above shall apply mutatis mutandis to the administrator. However, the period for giving public notice specified in paragraph 1 of Article 1281 (Public notice and demand)

and demand) of this Code shall be ten (10) days after the appointment of the administrator.

#### Chapter 7
#### Absence of Successors

**Article 1290.- Inheritance as a legal person**

If it is not clear whether there is a successor, the inheritance shall be a legal person.

**Article 1291.- Appointment of administrator**

1-In the case provided for in Article 1290 (Inheritance as a legal person) above, the court shall, upon the application of an interested person, the head of the commune or sangkat of the decedent's last domicile, or a public prosecutor, appoint an administrator of the inheritance.

2-Upon appointing an administrator of the inheritance, the court shall give public notice thereof without delay.

**Article 1292.- Rights and duties of administrator**

1-The administrator must prepare an inventory of the inheritance without delay.

2-The administrator must preserve and manage the inheritance. If necessary for the preservation and management of the inheritance, the administrator may, with the permission of the court, convert the inheritance into money or otherwise dispose of it.

3-If a creditor of the decedent or a legatee makes a demand, the administrator must report the status of the inheritance to such person.

4-The administrator must comply with each point specified in the provisions starting

from Article 1282 (Right to refuse performance during the demand period) through Article 1288 (Case where claim was not presented) of this Code, which shall apply mutatis mutandis, subject to the provisions of paragraph 2 of Article 1296 (Public notice and demand to creditors, etc.) below.

5-Upon the order of the court, the administrator must investigate the facts and report the results thereof to the court.

**Article 1293.- Remuneration for administrator**

The court may grant reasonable remuneration to the administrator out of the inheritance, taking into account the status of the inheritance and other circumstances.

**Article 1294.- Case where successor is identified**

If a successor is identified, the legal personality of the inheritance shall be deemed to never have existed. However, this shall not affect the validity of acts performed by the administrator within the scope of their authority.

**Article 1295.- Extinction of administrator's authority**

1-The administrator's authority shall be extinguished when the successor accepts the succession.

2-If the successor accepts the succession, the administrator must render an account of the management to the successor without delay.

**Article 1296.- Public notice and demand to creditors, etc.**

1-If it is still not clear whether there is a successor two (2) months after the public notice provided for in paragraph 2 of Article 1291 (Appointment of administrator) above, the administrator must give public notice

without delay to all of the decedent's creditors and legatees that the creditors and legatees should present their claims within a certain period. However, this period shall not be less than two (2) months.

2-The provisions of paragraph 2 of Article 1281 (Public notice and demand) and the provisions of Article 1282 (Right to refuse performance during the demand period) through Article 1288 (Case where claim was not presented) of this Code shall apply mutatis mutandis to the case provided for in paragraph 1 above. However, the administrator may not become the purchaser in the compulsory sale or the sale provided for in paragraph 1 of Article 1286 (Conversion of inheritance into money) of this Code, which shall apply mutatis mutandis.

**Article 1297.- Public notice to search for successors**

1-After the period specified in paragraph 1 of Article 1296 (Public notice and demand to creditors, etc.) above has elapsed, if it is still not clear whether there is a successor, the court shall, upon the application of the administrator or a public prosecutor, give public notice that if there is any successor, such successor should present their claim within a specified period. However, this period shall not be less than six (6) months.

2-If it is estimated that no inheritance remains, the administrator or a public prosecutor may not make the application provided for in paragraph 1 above.

**Article 1298.- Deprivation of rights after public notice**

If no person presents a claim that they are a successor within the period specified in Article 1297 (Public notice to search for successors) above

successors, as well as creditors and legatees who were unknown to the administrator, may not exercise their rights.

**Article 1299.- Distribution of inheritance to persons with special connection**

1-In the case provided for in Article 1298 (Deprivation of rights after public notice) above, if it is deemed reasonable, the court may, upon application, distribute a portion of the inheritance remaining after liquidation to persons who lived with the decedent, persons who took care of the decedent, or other persons with a special connection to the decedent. However, the total value of property distributed shall not exceed one-half of the remaining inheritance.

2-The application provided for in paragraph 1 above must be made within three (3) months after the period specified in Article 1297 (Public notice to search for successors) above has elapsed, and must clearly state the grounds for the special connection.

3-In distributing the property as provided for in paragraph 1 above, the court may investigate the facts. In such investigation, the court may seek the opinion of the head of the commune or sangkat.

**Article 1300.- Escheat of inheritance to the State**

Inheritance that is not distributed in accordance with the provisions of Article 1299 (Distribution of inheritance to persons with special connection) above shall escheat to the State. In such case, the administrator must render an account of the management and submit it to the court without delay.

#### Chapter 8
#### Claim for Recovery of Inheritance

**Article 1301.- Purpose of claim for recovery of inheritance**

A successor whose right of succession has been infringed may demand the recovery of the inheritance in order to receive all or a portion of the inheritance.

**Article 1302.- Meaning of claim for recovery of inheritance**

1-A successor may demand that a person who has acquired the inheritance based on a right of succession that they do not possess return the property they have acquired.

2-Property acquired by the other party to the claim for recovery of inheritance under a contract having the inheritance as its subject matter shall also be the subject of the claim for recovery of inheritance.

**Article 1303.- Substance of obligation to return**

1-The other party to the claim for recovery of inheritance must return the inheritance to the successor who made the claim. If the other party acted in good faith, they shall have the right to retain the fruits and interest received, the right to demand reimbursement for expenses that increased the value of the property, and the right to demand compensation for obligations acquired through succession that they have already satisfied.

2-If the other party acted in bad faith, they must return the fruits and interest received, and they shall not only have no right to demand compensation for obligations acquired through succession that they have satisfied but also have no right to demand reimbursement for expenses that increased the value of the property.

**Article 1304.- Extinctive prescription of claim for recovery of inheritance**

The extinctive prescription of a claim for recovery of inheritance shall be five (5) years from the time when the inheritance was transferred to the other party through a will or partition of inheritance. In cases where only one person has acquired the inheritance as a successor, the extinctive prescription shall be five (5) years from the date of the decedent's death.

### Book 9
### Final Provisions

#### Single Chapter
#### Final Provisions

**Article 1305.- Date of enforcement, etc.**

1-This Code shall be enforced from the date separately specified by law.

2-Transitional measures related to the enforcement of this Code and other necessary matters related to the enforcement of this Code shall be separately specified by law.

Phnom Penh, December 8, 2007
In the name of and by the Royal Command of His Majesty the King
