```
Index to Civil Law Section

#### Glossary
#### Civil Law Section

Extracted from

"Glossary of Legal Terms Khmer-English-French: Civil Law and Civil Procedure Section"

prepared by the Council of Jurists Technical Committee for the Approval of Legal Terms and published by the Office of the Council of Ministers in 2007.
```

Wait, `បណ្ឌិត្យសភា` usually means `Academy` or `Academic Council`. In Cambodia's legal context, this might be the `Council of Jurists` (គណៈកម្មការបច្ចេកទេស ឬ គណៈកម្មការបណ្ឌិត្យសភា?). Actually, MoJ's Glossary is often prepared by the `Council of Jurists` (គណៈអ្នកច្បាប់) or similar bodies. But the Khmer says `គណៈកម្មការបណ្ឌិត្យសភា`. Let's stick closer to the Khmer: `Academic Committee Council`.
Wait, there is a known publication "Glossary of Legal Terms Khmer-English-French: Civil Law and Civil Procedure Section" by the **Council of Jurists** (Office of the Council of Ministers).
Actually, the Khmer says `គណៈកម្មការបណ្ឌិត្យសភា` (Akademey Committee). I'll use `Academic Committee Council`.
Wait, let's look at the source "Council of Ministers, 2007".
Actually, it's the `Council of Jurists`.

But I'll translate the words as they are: `Academic Committee in charge of Approving Legal Terms`.

Wait, translation for `ទីស្ដីការគណៈរដ្ឋមន្ត្រី` is `Office of the Council of Ministers`.

Let's write it.

#### K

1. Active Member
Eng. active member
Fr. membre actif

1-General Sense: A member who voluntarily contributes labor or property or both to an association.

2-In the Civil Code, this refers to a person with authority to implement and manage the affairs of a legal entity.

2. Commission
Eng. commission
Fr. commission

A fee paid to a person for performing activities under a mandate contract.

3. Exclusive Duty
Eng. exclusive duty
Fr. devoir exclusif

A duty linked to a specific person's personality which cannot be transferred through succession.

Example: A child has a duty to provide for their parents when the parents are elderly. When the child dies, this duty is not transferred to the child's successors.

4. Obligation
Eng. obligation
Fr. obligation

A legal bond binding two persons, where one person called the "debtor" has specific duties towards another person called the "creditor".

5. Principal Obligation
Eng. underlying obligation / principal obligation
Fr. obligation principale

The original obligation under a contract created by the creditor and debtor.

6. Joint and Several Obligation
Eng. joint obligation
Fr. obligation solidaire

An obligation involving multiple debtors where one or more debtors must perform all or part of the obligation jointly or successively upon demand by the creditor.

7. Guaranty Obligation
Eng. guaranty obligation
Fr. obligation de garantie

The duty of a guarantor to perform all or part of an obligation alongside a debtor who has a principal obligation, in cases where the principal debtor does not perform their obligation.

8. Alternative Obligation
Eng. choice of obligation
Fr. obligation alternative

An obligation that gives the debtor the freedom to choose one or more performances among multiple types of performance provided.

9. Self-Defence
Eng. self-defence
Fr. légitime défense

An action performed to avoid unavoidable harm, in order to protect one's own body or property, or those of others, against an act

of unlawful infringement, and which falls under circumstances where the time of infringement and the defensive act occurred simultaneously, and there is proportionality between the method of protection and the degree of infringement.

10. Compound Interests
Eng. compound interests
Fr. intérêts composés

Interest that can be added to the principal in cases where the debtor is in delay and has not paid for a period of one year or more, even if the creditor has reminded them to pay.

11. Tacit Agreement
Eng. tacit agreement
Fr. accord tacite

An act demonstrating intent through silence, without writing or explicit declaration, which is recognized by law.

12. Special Clause
Eng. special clause
Fr. clause spéciale

An agreement between parties in accordance with provisions, except as provided by law, which creates legal effects.

13. Workmanship
Eng. workmanship
Fr. ouvrage

Any result arising from the handiwork of a craftsman or an artist, etc.

14. Due Date
Eng. due date
Fr. délai de rigueur

A fixed time period considered valid which the parties cannot modify later.

15. Contract
Eng. contract
Fr. contrat

An act where two or more persons have matching intentions to create, modify, or terminate an obligation.

16. Life Annuity Contract
Eng. life annuity contract
Fr. contrat de rente viagère

A contract where one party, as the debtor of the annuity, promises to have the duty to provide periodic monetary performances at regular intervals to another party, who is the creditor or a third-party beneficiary of the annuity, until the death of the debtor themselves, the other party, or the third-party beneficiary; and the other party promises to have the duty to provide performances as the value of that property.

17. Bailment
Eng. bailment
Fr. contrat de dépôt

A contract where one party, as the depositary, promises to accept an object from another party, as the depositor, to be kept for a specified period, and at the end of the storage period, that object must be returned to the depositor, either for a fee or free of charge.

18. Rent-Leasehold
Eng. rent-leasehold
Fr. contrat de fermage

A contract where a property owner lends land or livestock to a lessee to extract produce, and those products are then shared between them.

19. Arbitration Agreement
Eng. arbitration agreement
Fr. compromis d'arbitrage

A contract entered into by parties in a dispute to select a third person as an arbitrator to resolve the dispute.

20. Works Contract
Eng. works contract
Fr. contrat de louage d'ouvrage / contrat d'entreprise

A contract where one party has the duty to complete a work as agreed, and the other party has the duty to provide a fee for the result of the work done.

21. Synallagmatical Contract
Eng. synallagmatical contract
Fr. contrat synallagmatique

A contract where the parties have mutual obligations to each other, such as a works contract, a lease contract, a sale and purchase contract, etc.

22. Mandate
Eng. mandate
Fr. mandat

A contract where a mandator grants power to a mandatee to manage affairs for the mandator.

23. Quorum
Eng. quorum
Fr. quorum

1-The minimum number of members with voting rights required to make a meeting or general assembly of a legal entity valid.

2-In the Civil Code, this refers to the minimum number of members with voting rights, being 1/10 or more of the total number of members with voting rights, required to demand the convening of a general assembly of a legal entity.

24. Debtor of the principal obligation
Eng. debtor of the principal obligation
Fr. débiteur principal / débiteur

A debtor who must perform the original obligation, which is guaranteed by a guaranty contract.

25. Balcony
Eng. balcony
Fr. balcon

A platform or small porch without supporting columns underneath, but with railings surrounding it.

26. Engagement
Eng. engagement
Fr. fiançailles

A traditional custom ceremony recognized by law, through which a man and a woman promise to marry each other in the future.

27. Bankruptcy
Eng. bankruptcy
Fr. faillite

A situation where a debtor cannot fulfill their obligations towards all creditors due to the collapse of their financial state, which is declared by a court.

#### Kh K

28. Clause
Eng. clause
Fr. clause

A provision or article contained in a contract.

29. Accounting
Eng. accounting
Fr. comptabilité

The work of maintaining, managing, or auditing account books.

30. Juridical
Eng. juridical
Fr. juridique

-Following the course of law.
-Relating to law that is correct according to law.

31. Principle
Eng. principle
Fr. principe

1-A general rule or a theory that provides the basis or the origin of legal norms.

2-A legal rule created by any legal text that has a general nature for the purpose of ensuring various implementations and having high legal value.

32. Natural Disaster
Eng. natural disaster
Fr. catastrophe naturelle

Damage caused by natural elements, such as storms, floods, earthquakes, etc.

#### Ch Chh Ch

33. Movable Property
Eng. movable
Fr. meuble

All things other than immovable property.
(See Immovable property)

34. Perpetrator
Eng. perpetrator
Fr. auteur

A person who unlawfully infringes upon the rights or interests of others through intentional or unintentional fault.

35. Shrubs
Eng. shrubs
Fr. arbrisseau

Refers to small trees.

36. Discretion
Eng. discretion
Fr. pouvoir discrétionnaire

1-Power granted to a judge to choose any means based on their own assessment to resolve any issue.

2-Power of a judge to take action in lawsuit proceedings without requiring a request from the parties.

37. Persons with special relationships
Eng.
Fr.

A person who is not an heir but shared a livelihood with the decedent, or a person who has put in effort to care for or medically treat the decedent as a profession.

38. Liquidation
Eng. liquidation
Fr. liquidation

Preliminary operations to facilitate the arrangement of property relations in cases where a legal entity or company is dissolved.

39. Betrothal Gift
Eng. betrothal
Fr. présent / cadeau

Tangible gifts or offerings provided by one party to the other party during the engagement ceremony.

#### Ny D Th

40. Relatives
Eng. relatives
Fr. lien familial / lien de famille

Blood relatives within 6 degrees, spouses, and relatives by affinity within 3 degrees.

41. Affinity
Eng. affinity
Fr. alliance

The legal bond between one spouse and the blood relatives of the other spouse (in-laws).
Ex. Parents-in-law and children-in-law, brothers-in-law, sisters-in-law (on the husband's or wife's side), etc.

42. Direct Affinity
Eng. direct affinity
Fr. alliance en ligne directe

Direct blood relatives by affinity who arise from each other in order, such as grandparents-in-law, parents-in-law, and the biological children of the other spouse.

43. Consanguinity
Eng. consanguinity
Fr. parenté

Relatives who share the same blood, such as father and child or mother and child, etc.

44. Direct Consanguinity
Eng. direct consanguinity
Fr. parenté en ligne directe

Relatives who share the same blood and are born from each other in sequence, namely: father, mother, child, grandchild, great-grandchild.

45. Servient Land
Eng. servient land
Fr. fonds servant

Land belonging to another person that provides a benefit to the land of another person.

46. Dominant Land
Eng. dominant land
Fr. fonds dominant

Land that receives a benefit from the servient land.

47. Mental Reservation
Eng. mental reservation
Fr. restriction mentale

A declaration of intent by a party who is aware that it differs from their true intent.

#### T

48. Third Party
Eng. third party
Fr. tiers

A third person or a person who is not a party to a legal relationship. A person who is not a party in a civil case.

49. Balance Sheet
Eng. balance sheet
Fr. bilan

A table reflecting the current financial status (assets and assets [sic - assets and liabilities]) of a legal entity.

50. Prestation
Eng. prestation
Fr. prestation

An object or an act that constitutes the subject matter of an obligation.
Ex. The prestation of a buyer is to pay the price to the seller, whereas the prestation of a seller is to deliver the object to the buyer.
Ex. Rice that one person borrows from another is called the prestation of the loan.

51. Impliedly / Tacit
Eng. impliedly / tacit
Fr. accord tacite

The state of silence in the declaration of intent.

#### T

52. Discipline / Correction
Eng. discipline / correction
Fr. punition

Under the Civil Code, it refers to the right to discipline a child for the purpose of education with the parental mercy of those with parental power.

53. Donor
Eng. donor
Fr. donateur

A party to a gift contract who expresses the intent to provide property to the other party without charge.

54. Residence
Eng. residence
Fr. résidence

A place where a person stays or stays temporarily for a certain period which is not their domicile.

55. Principal Office
Eng. principal office
Fr. siège social / établissement principal

The location specified in the articles of incorporation of a legal entity and designated as the primary office of the legal entity.

56. Secondary Office
Eng. secondary office
Fr. établissement secondaire

Various offices of a legal entity that act as branches performing activities under the guidance or orders of the principal office.

57. Legal Relationship
Eng. legal relationship
Fr. rapports juridiques

A bond arising between two or more persons through a legal act or a legal provision.

58. Bigamy
Eng. bigamy
Fr. bigamie

The status of a person whose previous marriage has not yet been dissolved and who enters into a new marriage, or a wife having two husbands or a husband having two wives.

59. Assets
Eng. assets
Fr. actif

The property owned by a legal entity.

60. Capital
Eng. capital
Fr. capital

A specific amount of property for which a limited liability legal entity and a foundation legal entity are responsible as determined by law.

61. Separate Property
Eng. separate property
Fr. biens propres

Property owned by one spouse before marriage or received through gift, inheritance, or legacy during the marriage.

62. Community Property
Eng. community property
Fr. biens communs

All property acquired by both spouses or by either spouse during the marriage, except for separate property.

63. Universal Property
Eng. universal property
Fr. biens universels

All property of any person, including assets and liabilities.

#### Th N

64. Annuity
Eng. annuity
Fr. rente

Yield received regularly, such as interest, land rent, house rent, etc.

65. Life Annuity
Eng. life annuity
Fr. rente viagère

An annuity which the annuitant receives for the rest of their life.

66. Non-existence
Eng. non-existence
Fr. inexistence

The absence of a burden or the state of something not existing.

67. Novation
Eng. novation
Fr. novation

A contract between a creditor and a debtor that extinguishes the original obligation and creates a new obligation in place of that original obligation.

68. Employer
Eng. employer
Fr. employeur

A party to a labor contract who promises to provide wages for the labor services of the other party (employee).

69. Employee
Eng. employee
Fr. employé

A party to a labor contract who promises to perform labor services for the other party (employer).

70. Adult
Eng. adult
Fr. majeur

1-In general meaning: A person who has reached the age of majority as determined by law and has full capacity over their acts.

2-Under the Civil Code: Refers to a person aged 18 years and above.

71. Juridical Person
Eng. juridical person / legal person / artificial person
Fr. personne morale

A group of people or a foundation that possesses legal personality and is the subject of rights and obligations.
Ex. Associations, companies, etc.

72. Incorporated Foundation
Eng. incorporated foundation
Fr. fondation

A collection of property as a contribution that is granted rights and obligations.

73. For-profit Juridical Person
Eng. for-profit juridical person
Fr. personne morale à but lucratif

A legal entity that conducts activities to seek profit.

74. Incorporated Association
Eng. incorporated association(s)
Fr. société enregistrée

A group with members seeking profit that is granted rights and obligations.

75. Juridical Person under Public Law
Eng. juridical person under public law
Fr. personne publique / personne morale de droit public

An institution which is established and governed by public law and has the power to perform administrative affairs.

76. Private Law
Eng. private law
Fr. droit privé

Rules that govern legal relationships between private individuals and private individuals and do not fall under public law.

#### B

77. Descendant
Eng. descendant
Fr. descendant

A person or group of persons born from another group.
Ex. Children are direct descendants of parents. Grandchildren are direct descendants of grandparents.

78. Account Record
Eng. account record
Fr. registre comptable

Under the Civil Code, it refers to a table or report that records the flow of income, expenses, profit, or loss during a certain period.

79. Inventory
Eng. inventory / list of properties
Fr. inventaire

A list of all property.

80. Bailment
Eng. bailment
Fr. dépôt

1-In general meaning: An object.
2-Under the Civil Code: Refers to a bailment contract.

81. Sub-bailment
Eng. sub-bailment
Fr. sous-dépôt

A contract in which a party as the sub-bailee promises to accept an object to be bailed from another party who is the bailor and is also the bailee in another bailment contract, for safekeeping.

82. Donee
Eng. donee
Fr. donataire

A recipient of a gift. Under the Civil Code, it refers to a party to a gift contract who agrees to accept property from a donor without giving value in return.

83. Restitution
Eng. restitution
Fr. restitution

Restoration to the original state or returning of property.
Ex. Returning an object delivered by a debtor or repaying money paid by a debtor; or returning an object used or managed by a creditor to the debtor; or returning inherited property received by a person who has no right of succession, etc.

84. Certificate of Possession
Eng. certificate of possession
Fr. certificat de possession

A document in a specific format issued by a competent authority to certify the possession rights of a person or group.

85. Legal Norm
Eng. legal norme
Fr. norme juridique / règle de droit

-Legal rules such as laws and various regulations established by the State.
-Legal rules that carry compulsory value (required to be followed) regardless of the source (laws, treaties, etc.).

#### B

86. Captain / Commander
Eng. captain / commander
Fr. capitaine / commandant

A leader, supervisor, or commander.
Ex. A captain of a ship or aircraft, etc.

87. Defamation
Eng. defamation
Fr. diffamation

The act of making allegations or casting blame in bad faith that harms the honor or reputation of a person or institution by any act which is shown in public, or in a public meeting, or through letters or drawings of any kind which are disseminated to the public or displayed for the public to see, or through any audio-visual communication means intended for the public.

88. Fatherhood / Paternity
Eng. fatherhood / paternity
Fr. paternité

A lineage relating from a father to a child.

89. Legal Personality
Eng. legal personality
Fr. personnalité juridique

The state of full status as a subject of rights and obligations.

90. Private Person
Eng. private person
Fr. personne privée

A person who has rights and obligations within a legal relationship and who is governed by private law.

91. Affiliation / Filiation
Eng. affiliation
Fr. filiation

A bloodline relating from parents to their biological children.

92. Ascendant
Eng. ascendant
Fr. ascendant

A person born before who then produced another group of persons.
Ex. Grandparents and parents are ascendants of children, grandchildren, etc.

93. Ancestor
Eng. ancestor
Fr. ancêtres

1-In general meaning: Has the same meaning as ascendant.
2-Under the Civil Code: Refers to a person who is in a kinship class higher than oneself.
Ex. Grandparents, parents, aunts/uncles, etc. are ancestors of grandchildren, children, nephews/nieces, etc.

94. Cultural Heritage
Eng. cultural heritage
Fr. patrimoine culturel

Every work of human beings and every product of nature with a scientific, historical, artistic, or religious character which reflects the stages of evolution of civilization or of nature.

95. Calendar Year / Solar Calendar
Eng. calendar year / solar calendar
Fr. année civile

A list or table recording the number and names of days and months for use in a year, calculated according to the movement of the sun.

#### B

96. Successor
Eng. successor
Fr. ayant cause

A person who succeeds to the rights or obligations of another person.

97. Particular Successor
Eng. particular successor
Fr. ayant cause à titre particulier

A person who succeeds to one or more specific rights based on each specific cause.

98. Universal Successor
Eng. universal successor
Fr. ayant cause à titre universel / ayant cause universel

A person who succeeds to general rights and obligations from another person.

99. Donation
Eng. donation
Fr. donation

A contract in which one party expresses the will to provide property to another party without charge and the other party agrees to accept it.

100. Force Majeure
Eng. force majeure
Fr. force majeure

An event that occurs beyond one's will, cannot be foreseen, and cannot be overcome.

101. Security
Eng. security
Fr. sûreté

1-A guarantee to a creditor for the recovery of their credit.
2-A means for guaranteeing the performance of an obligation.

102. Earnest Money / Deposit
Eng. earnest / deposite
Fr. arrhes / caution

1-Money paid by a buyer to a seller at the time when a contract is formed, in order to guarantee their right to terminate the contract.
2-Money paid for guaranteeing the rental of an object, which must be returned upon the return of such object without damage.

#### P B Ph

103. Natural Fruits / Natural Produce
Eng. natural fruits / natural produce
Fr. fruits naturels

1-Objects that are produced and separated through normal use.
2-Products arising from land or offspring of domestic animals born from their mothers.

104. Special Benefit
Eng. special benefit
Fr. avantages spéciaux

Property that a person (successor) has received as a donation on the occasion of marriage or other events, or as capital for living expenses, or received as a gift from the decedent.

105. Lawful Fruit / Income of Capital
Eng. lawful fruit / income of capital
Fr. fruits civils

Money or other objects received as a reward for the use of a certain object.
Ex. Interest or house rent, etc.

106. Usufruct
Eng. usufruct
Fr. usufruit

The right to use and profit from immovable property belonging to another person for a specified period that does not exceed the lifetime of the usufructuary.

107. Usufructuary
Eng. usufructuary
Fr. usufruitier

A person who receives a usufruct.

108. Civil Fine
Eng. civil fine
Fr. amende civile

A type of sanction which requires a person who violates a provision of civil law to pay a fine of a specific certain amount.

109. Foresee
Eng. foresee
Fr. prévoir

To make sure, to make clear, to predict.

110. Merger of Rights / Confusion
Eng. merger of rights
Fr. confusion

1-A case where ownership or other real rights in the same object fall to the same person, or a case where real rights other than ownership and other rights that have such real rights as their object fall to the same person.
2-A means of extinguishing an obligation where a person combines in themselves the capacity of both creditor and debtor.

111. Lessee
Eng. lessee
Fr. preneur / locataire

A party to a contract who promises to pay rent in exchange for obtaining the right to use and profit from an object belonging to the lessor.

#### Ph

112. Lessor
Eng. lessor
Fr. bailleur

A party to a lease contract who promises to allow a lessee to use and profit from an object in exchange for rent.

113. Lease
Eng. lease
Fr. bail

A contract in which one party allows another party to use and profit from a certain object in exchange for rent.

114. Legally Secured Portions
Eng. legally secured portions
Fr. réserve héréditaire / réserve légale

The portion of a decedent's property that the direct descendants, parents, grandparents, and spouse of the decedent may claim as determined by law.

115. Chargé d'affaires
Eng. chargé d'affaires
Fr. chargé d'affaires

1-A head of a third-class diplomatic mission who is permanently accredited to the Ministry of Foreign Affairs.
2-A member of a diplomatic mission in charge and responsible as the interim head of the diplomatic mission in case of the absence of the head of the diplomatic mission.
3-Under the Civil Code, it refers to a diplomatic official who performs the duties of a registrar at an embassy.

#### M

116. Estate / Heritage
Eng. estate / heritage
Fr. héritage

Inherited property that a person (decedent) has left behind after death.

117. Deceased
Eng. deceased
Fr. défunt

A person who has already died.

118. Testament / Will
Eng. testament / will
Fr. testament

A testament made by a person in a form determined by law to express their final will concerning the disposal of their property, etc. after death.

119. Destruction
Eng. destruction
Fr. destruction

Destruction, loss.

120. General Meeting of Members / General Assembly
Eng. general meeting of members / general assembly
Fr. assemblée générale

1-A plenary meeting of a non-profit organization which is held among members to approve important affairs of that legal entity.
2-The highest decision-making body of a non-profit organization in which members participate to decide on the important affairs of that legal entity.

121. Criteria / Standard
Eng. criteria / standard
Fr. standard / échelle

Measure, dimension, scale, level, form, or type which is the basis for judgment.

122. Security
Eng. security
Fr. obligation

A certificate that represents the ownership rights of the holder in a company (e.g., share certificate), represents a credit relationship between the holder and a company or the government (e.g., bank bond), or represents other rights of the holder.

123. Negotiable Instrument
Eng. negotiable instrument
Fr. valeurs mobilières

1-Under the Civil Code, it refers to a security that represents a property interest (right) and of which the transfer or exercise of such right must be made by means of the security.
2-Under the Law on Banking and Financial Institutions: A certificate issued by a banking and financial institution or an investment company to borrow money from the public for a specified term and interest rate, which is a certificate representing credit (represented by a debt to be claimed) that the holder can trade on a market regulated by law.

Credits include bank bonds, bond certificates, treasury bills, etc.

124. Legal Basis
Eng. legal basis
Fr. motifs légaux / cause juridique

A basis which makes a certain action valid under the law.
Ex. The right to lease is the legal basis for the lessee's possession.

125. Nullity
Eng. nullity
Fr. nullité

1-The status of having no legal effect for a legal act that is contrary to a provision on the procedures for a lawsuit.
2-A sanction declared by a judge to retroactively invalidate a legal instrument which does not fulfill the conditions determined by law or any action which is contrary to the procedures of the law.

#### R L S

126. Ancestral Heirloom
Eng. ancestral heirloom
Fr. souvenir de famille

1-General meaning: A valuable object.
2-Under the Civil Code, it refers to family movable property which is inherited from ancestors.

127. Natural Person
Eng. natural person
Fr. personne physique

A real human being as determined by nature, which is the opposite of a legal person.

128. Money
Eng. money
Fr. monnaie

An instrument determined by law which is used as a medium of exchange.
Ex. The Riel is the currency of the Kingdom of Cambodia.

129. Memorandum and Articles of Association / By(e)-laws
Eng. memorandum and articles of association / by(e)-laws
Fr. statut

An instrument creating a legal entity which contains essential and indispensable descriptions concerning important points such as the purpose, the name, the location of the head office, etc.

130. Acquisition
Eng. acquisition
Fr. acquisition

1-Obtaining.
2-Becoming the owner of a right to an object through one's own work, purchase, or exchange.

131. Authentic Deed
Eng. authentic deed
Fr. acte authentique

A deed made in legal form by a public official who is authorized to perform such functions. In a narrow sense, it refers to deeds prepared by a notary.

132. Private Deed
Eng. private deed
Fr. acte sous-seing privé

Any deed created by a private person with a signature or thumbprint.

#### V

133. Principal Thing
Eng. principal thing
Fr. chose principale

A thing which is the principal part of an accessory thing.

134. Accessory Thing
Eng. accessory thing
Fr. chose accessoire

A thing which is not a component of a principal thing and which the owner of the principal thing has attached to the principal thing for the continuous economic purpose of the principal thing.

135. Defect
Eng. defect
Fr. vice / défaut

A deviation from the normal state.

136. Occupation
Eng. occupation
Fr. profession

A position or task for earning a living.

137. Scope
Eng. scope
Fr. étendue

Size, breadth, extent.

138. Sequestrator / Sequestration
Eng. sequestrator / sequestration
Fr. séquestre

1-A person who keeps an object which is in dispute.
2-The act of delivering an object which is in dispute to any third party for safekeeping.

#### S

139. Activity / Act
Eng. act
Fr. acte

According to the Civil Code, it means the manifestation of will through a contract or at least by a party.

140. Valid Act
Eng. valid act
Fr. acte valuable

An act performed in accordance with the requirements prescribed by law and having legal effect.

141. Unilateral Act
Eng. unilateral act
Fr. acte unilatéral

An act that creates, modifies, or extinguishes an obligation by the manifestation of will to dispose of property by only one party, or by exercising a right acquired under a contract or a provision of law.

142. Ratification
Eng. ratification
Fr. ratification

1- Approval that has retroactive effect.

Ex: A minor (17 years old) enters into a contract; that contract is not fully legal. However, if that minor gives ratification (gives approval) to that contract after reaching the age of majority (18 years old), that contract becomes a fully legal contract.

2- Recognition by a party of a litigation act performed by a person lacking the authority of representation by mandate, and such recognition must have retroactive effect from the time the act was performed.

143. Succession
Eng. succession
Fr. succession

The transfer of rights and obligations of a decedent to one or more successors according to will or by law.

144. Successor
Eng. successor
Fr. héritier

A person who succeeds to the rights and obligations of a decedent through succession.

145. Person of Full Capacity
Eng. person of full capacity
Fr. capable / personne capable

All persons other than those under general guardianship, those under curatorship, and minors, except for minors who have been emancipated.

146. Capacity
Eng. capacity
Fr. capacité

The qualifications of a person required by law include:

- Qualifications that enable one to become a holder of rights and obligations (capacity to hold rights).

- Qualifications that enable one to understand and judge the results of

legal effects of one's own acts (capacity of will).

- Qualifications that enable one's own acts to have legal effects (capacity to act).

147. Treaty
Eng. treaty
Fr. traité

An international agreement concluded in writing between States and States, between States and international organizations, or between international organizations and international organizations, and governed by public international law, regardless of the designation of the agreement (Constitution, Treaty, Convention, Covenant...) to create legal effects.

148. Components of a thing
Eng. components of a thing
Fr. beans composants / composant d'une chose

A part of a thing that is integrated into a single thing and that cannot be separated without dismantling that thing or without changing the original character of that thing.

Ex: A building constructed on land and which cannot be moved, or seeds sown on land, are components of the land.

149. Community
Eng. community
Fr. communauté

1- Proceeding together, a village meeting, or an area where residents conduct common activities.

2- A group of persons connected by territory, blood relations, customs, traditions, or common religions.

Ex: Fishing community, ethnic minority community in remote mountainous areas.

150. Spouse
Eng. spouse
Fr. conjoint(e)

A husband or a wife, or both husband and wife, who acquired the status of spouse after having duly registered their marriage according to law.

151. Co-successor
Eng. co-successor
Fr. co-héritier

A person who is to participate in the distribution of any inheritance (See Successor).

152. Collateral relative
Eng. collateral relative
Fr. collatéraux

A relative who branches out to the side from a common ancestor, such as siblings, aunts, uncles, nieces/nephews, or cousins.

153. Solvency
Eng. solvency
Fr. solvabilité

The state of being able to pay all debts according to the payment schedule.

154. Notary public
Eng. notary public
Fr. notaire

A public official whose function is to prepare authentic documents certifying the truth and accuracy, ensuring knowledge of precise dates, keeping, and issuing authentic copies of documents that must be certified.

155. Fundamental / Essential
Eng. fundamental
Fr. fondamental

That which is beneficial, fundamentally important, and substantial.

156. Right of retention
Eng. right of retention
Fr. Droit de rétention

A type of real security right of a person who possesses a thing belonging to another, which gives that person the right to retain said thing until the debt owed to them is paid.

157. Perpetual lease right / long lease right
Eng. Perpetual lease right / long lease right
Fr. droit sur le bail emphyteotique / emphyteose

A right to lease immovable property with a term of 15 years or more.

158. Real right
Eng. real right
Fr. Droit réel

A right of direct control over a thing that can be asserted against any other person.

159. Real security right
Eng. real security right
Fr. sûreté réelle

A type of real right including the right of retention, preferential rights, pledge, mortgage, and real security rights by transfer, which are created to secure an obligation.

160. Right of use
Eng. right of use
Fr. droits d'usage

A right to enjoy the fruits of an immovable property to the extent of the needs of the right holder and their family.

161. Pledge
Eng. pledge
Fr. Droit de gage / nantissement

A right to possess a thing received from a debtor or a third party to secure a debt, and to receive compensation for that debt from said thing with priority over other creditors.

162. Right of residence
Eng. right of residence
Fr. Droit d'habitation

A right to occupy a part of a building to the extent of the needs for the residence of the right holder and their family.

163. Workmanship / Works of art
Eng. workmanship
Fr. ouvrage d'art

All results that arise from human craftsmanship.

164. Validity
Eng. validity
Fr. validité

The state of having the power to be enforced according to law.

165. Easement / Servitude
Eng. easement
Fr. servitude

1- A type of real right referring to the right to use the land of another for the benefit of one's own land, in accordance with the purpose specified in the contract creating it.

2- A burden imposed on one immovable property (servient land or lower land) for the benefit of another immovable property (dominant land or upper land) which belongs to an owner

another. Ex: The lower field has an easement to receive water from the upper field. The lower field is called the servient field, and the upper field is called the dominant field.

166. Administrative governmental institution
Eng. administrative governmental institution
Fr. institution administrative

1- An institution responsible for implementing public administrative affairs.

2- In this Civil Code, it means a public institution to which the law delegates power.

167. Simple adoption
Eng. simple adoption
Fr. adoption simple

The adoption of a child, who can be a minor or an adult, without severing the relationship with the biological parents, and such adoption must be decided by a court.

168. Full adoption
Eng. full adoption
Fr. adoption plénière

The adoption of a minor who is under 8 years old as an adopted child, by terminating the relationship with the biological parents, and such adoption must be decided by a court.

169. Acceptance
Eng. acceptance
Fr. acceptation

The manifestation of will by which a person expresses their agreement to an offer made by another person in order to create a contract.

# H

170. Risk
Eng. risk
Fr. risque

Facing damage or loss.

171. Partial guardianship / Curatorship
Eng. partial guardianship / curatorship
Fr. curatelle

Management of a person who has too little capacity to understand and judge the legal consequences of their own actions due to a mental crisis.

172. Curator
Eng. curator
Fr. curateur

A person appointed by the court to implement curatorship (See Curatorship).

173. Hypothec
Eng. hypothec
Fr. hypothèque

A type of real security right. A hypothec grants the creditor the right to receive payment for their debt with priority over other creditors in relation to the immovable property provided as security for an obligation by the debtor or a third party without transferring possession.

174. Hypothec for floating debt
Eng. hypothec for floating debt
Fr. hypothèque garantissant la dette flottante

A type of hypothec that can be created to secure indefinite obligations arising between a creditor and a debtor due to continuous trading, which has a type set within the limit of a maximum amount.

# A

175. Fact
Eng. fact
Fr. fait

An event that occurs and leads to specific legal consequences.

176. Immovable / Real estate
Eng. immovable / real estate
Fr. immeuble

- Land and things attached to the land and which cannot be moved, such as buildings, crops, plants, etc.

- Real rights or [ownership/possessory] rights over immovable property, and all movable property that is defined by law as immovable.

177. Testamentary gift
Eng. testamentary gift
Fr. legs

A gift made by a testator to one or more persons of their property through a will.

178. Specific testamentary gift
Eng. specific testamentary gift
Fr. legs à titre particulier

A gift with specific property identified for one or more persons through a will.

179. Residuary testamentary gift / Universal testamentary gift
Eng. residuary testamentary gift / universal testamentary gift
Fr. legs à titre universel / legs universel

A gift made by a testator to one or more persons of all or a part of their property, determined by shares through a will.

180. Testamentary donee
Eng. testamentary donee
Fr. légataire

A person who has received a testamentary gift.

181. Impending danger
Eng. impending danger
Fr. danger imminent

A danger that is about to occur with seeming certainty.

182. Indivisible joint-ownership
Eng. indivisible joint-ownership
Fr. mitoyenneté

The status of persons owning adjacent lands who have joint ownership with undivided shares over a wall or fence, such as a ditch, or a hedge, etc., which is located at the boundary of the lands or buildings on that land.

183. Emancipation
Eng. emancipation
Fr. émancipation

A status that allows a minor to be released from guardianship for a minor or from parental power and have the same capacity as an adult.

184. Civil status
Eng. civil status
Fr. état-civil

The legal bond of nationality to the State and the status within the family, which creates rights and obligations.

Ex: Civil status documents include birth certificates, marriage certificates, and death certificates.

185. Added value
Eng. added value
Fr. la plus-value

An increase in the value of any property from its previous value.

186. Inspector
Eng. inspector
Fr. inspecteur

In the Civil Code, it means a person appointed by the court at the request of the director to conduct research on property provided as contributions in-kind or property that any person has promised in the articles of incorporation of a legal person that they will transfer to said legal person after its creation.

187. Minor
Eng. minor
Fr. mineur

A person who has not yet reached the age of majority specified by law, or a person under the age of 18.

188. Secondary representative
Eng. secondary representative
Fr. sous-représentant

A person appointed by a representative who has rights and obligations toward the principal similar to those of the representative.

189. Transfer / Assignment
Eng. transfer / assignment
Fr. cession

The transfer of rights or property from one person, who is the assignor, to another person, who is the assignee.

Ex: Assignment of shares (sale of shares).

190. Person disqualified for succession
Eng. person disqualified for succession
Fr. indigne

A person who cannot become an heir or cannot receive property through a will.

191. Manager / Management
Eng. manager / management
Fr. gérant / gestion

1- Manager, management.

2- In the Civil Code, it means an entity meant to implement the affairs of a legal person.

192. Public prosecution / Prosecution department
Eng. public prosecution / prosecution department
Fr. parquet / ministère public

An institution that has the right to initiate criminal prosecution and protect the public interest.

193. Indivisible
Eng. indivisible
Fr. indivisible

That which cannot be divided.

194. Insolvent
Eng. insolvent
Fr. insolvable

Having no capacity to pay all debts.

195. Unjust enrichment
Eng. unjust enrichment
Fr. enrichissement sans cause

Receiving a benefit through the property or labor of another person without any legal basis, and which causes a loss of benefit to the other person as a result of such receipt.

196. Prescription
Eng. prescription
Fr. prescription

A period of time determined by law. The lapse of this period creates the effect of acquisition or the extinction of any right.

197. Mandate
Eng. mandate
Fr. mandat

A contract by which one party, called the mandator, grants the power of attorney to another party, called the mandatary, to manage affairs for the mandator.

198. Guardianship
Eng. guardianship
Fr. tutelle

Management, care, and maintenance of the property and person of a minor who has no person exercising parental power, or of an incompetent person.

199. Priority
Eng. priority
Fr. priorité

The state of being earlier, first, or receiving preference over others.

200. Effect
Eng. effect
Fr. effet

A legal consequence arising from a legal instrument such as a contract, law, court decision, administrative decision, etc.

201. Retroactive effect
Eng. retroactive effect
Fr. effet rétroactif

1- An influence on past activities through activities that comply with legal conditions.

2- The effect of a legal condition that applies to activities in the past.

202. Alimentary obligation
Eng. alimentary obligation
Fr. obligation alimentaire

An obligation that must be fulfilled according to law or a court decision, requiring one or more persons to provide money or property to support the livelihood of another person with whom they have a blood relationship.

203. Parental power
Eng. parental power
Fr. autorité parentale

The rights and obligations to care for a child who is a minor and to manage the property of that child.

204. Tort
Eng. tort
Fr. délit civil / acte dommageable extra-contractuel

An act that unlawfully infringes upon the rights or interests of another person through intentional or negligent fault.

# U E

205. Quasi-indivisible property
Eng. quasi-indivisible property
Fr. quasi-indivision

A situation in which multiple persons have rights over property other than ownership.

Ex: Usufructs, rights of use and residence, perpetual leases, etc., held by multiple persons.

206. Quasi-holder of claim
Eng. quasi-holder of claim
Fr. créancier apparent

A person who is not a creditor but appears to be a creditor.

207. Accession
Eng. accession
Fr. accession

The acquisition of ownership of a thing due to its being attached, mixed, or processed.

208. Subrogation
Eng. subrogation
Fr. subrogation

A status where a person legal substitutes for another person according to law (subrogation can also refer to things).

209. Subrogee
Eng. subrogee
Fr. subrogé

A person who succeeds to rights according to law or succeeds to the claims of another person through subrogation.

210. Unanimous
Eng. unanimous
Fr. unanime

Having the agreement of everyone.

211. Privilege
Eng. privilege
Fr. privilège

A right to receive payment from property that is the subject of the privilege with priority over other creditors.
